## M2b design drift to feed back into the design package
- `schema_reference.sql` makes `tenant_boundary` RESTRICTIVE for every role, which hides every row from `machvon_outbox_relay` despite its permissive policy `outbox_relay_access`. V003 narrows the restrictive policy on `outbox_event` to the other roles. Change request needed so the design SQL matches.

## M2b carry-overs
- Lên lịch job (seal audit mỗi 5 phút, verify hằng giờ, relay, purge idempotency hằng giờ): Temporal schedules, ST-KERNEL-044; định tuyến cảnh báo AUDIT_CHAIN_BROKEN và OUTBOX_LAG sang kênh vận hành: ST-KERNEL-051. M2b chỉ có hàm gọi được cùng MetricsSink/AlertSink.
- Endpoint đọc get_resources_audit_event*, get_resources_outbox_event*: chờ PDP (ST-IAM-004) và web shell.
- ST-KERNEL-028 TK-4: chạy bộ conformance trên 10 lệnh thuộc các phân hệ khác nhau, chỉ làm được khi các slice M3 có lệnh thật (hiện 3 lệnh mẫu + cổng commands:check).
- CR cho audit_event: lưu hash từng dòng để định vị chính xác dòng bị sửa (hiện báo theo cửa sổ seal).
- Mọi migration sau V003 viết tay trong db/migrations; db:generate chỉ sở hữu V001/V002.

## M2f lane A design gaps (change request entries needed)
- SAS-03 (SAS.md section 8) lists "Vòng đời, Lý do trạng thái, Owner" but `TenantDetail` of `getSystemTenant` has no `lifecycle_reason`, `suspended_at` or owner: add the fields (CR on openapi) so the screen can show why a tenant is suspended; until then it shows the status and the version only.
- `TenantSuspend.access_policy` (openapi) is a free choice, but the database function `tenant_runtime` derives the policy from the stored reason (SECURITY_INCIDENT is NO_ACCESS, any other reason READ_ONLY_EXPORT): the API refuses a pair that disagrees with 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` on `access_policy`. Decide: drop `access_policy` from the contract, or store it on the tenant and read it in `tenant_runtime`.
- `suspendTenant` and `resumeTenant` list `sas.entitlement.changed.v1` in `x-events`, but suspending changes no entitlement version (the features stay): the commands emit only `sas.tenant.status_changed.v1`, and its consumer drops the entitlement and access caches. Remove the event from `x-events` or define what version it would record.
- The live tenant state is read per request with a 1 second process-local cache and dropped by the command (and by the status event in other processes); a shared cache (Redis) is not available here.



## M2f lane B design gaps (change request entries needed)
- `acceptInvitation` is declared `x-plane: tenant` with permission `invitation.accept`, but the person who accepts holds an identity token and no tenant token yet (the invitation is what gives them a membership). It is implemented like `selectTenantContext`: an identity command (`@Public` plus the invitee guard), idempotent and audited by its own pipeline. Change the contract to say so (no tenant plane, no permission).
- IAM.md 3.3 answers an already used token with 409 `INVITATION_EXPIRED` (there is no used-invitation code); a token that names no invitation, a wrong secret and a token of another tenant are all the same 404 `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, so the answer never says whether an invitation exists.
- The seat limit comes from the latest valid `license_revision`: `seats` for staff, `quota_json.guest_seats` for guest contractors (IAM.md 3.3 names `guest_seats` but SAS never defines the key). A tenant with no revision has no limit. Define the key in the licence claims.
- `MemberInvite` has no `reason`, so the audit row of `inviteMember` has none; without `initial_grants` the person receives the MEMBER role on their unit (what `session.self` needs), which no document states.
- The invitation message goes through the `InvitationMailer` port; the only adapter is the in-memory MOCK one until the OMNI email channel exists (R1), so no invitation mail leaves the system yet. The events `iam.membership.invited|activated|suspended|ended.v1` are not in the notification catalogue.
- The hourly job that expires pending invitations (IAM.md section 9) is not implemented here; an expired invitation is expired on its next acceptance attempt. It belongs to the scheduler of Task 12.
- The last-owner guard (BR-IAM-07) runs before the rank rule on purpose: no one outranks a TENANT_OWNER (rank 99), so after the rank rule it could never be reached.
- `EffectiveAccess` (openapi, `getMyEffectiveAccess`) has `additionalProperties: false` and free-form `grants` and `delegations_in` items, and no `mode` or permission list at the top: the API puts role, scope (units with breadcrumb, projects, contracts), permissions, window and `state` (ACTIVE, EXPIRED, FUTURE) inside each grant, and the screen derives the effective permissions as the union of the ACTIVE ones (the property test proves it equals `effectivePermissions`). The session `mode` stays with the tenant selection answer. Change request: describe the grant and delegation items, and decide whether `mode` belongs in this answer.
- IAM-09 of the mockup has four tabs (Tổng quan, Số liệu, Tải liệu, Lịch sử) and a chart that no operation backs; they are not built, so the visual case compares the frame and the heading only.
- The unit-tree scope predicate wrapped the unit path in `rtrim(path, '/') || '/'`, which made the planner scan `organization_unit` for every scoped list (units, members, audit reads); it now tests the bare column with `starts_with`, which uses `organization_unit_path_idx` (p95 2 ms on 5,000 units and 50,000 grants). No migration was needed. `membershipsAffectedByUnits` (the holders to notify when a unit moves) still costs about 0.2 s at p95 for a department of 259 units, because it compares every subtree grant with the changed units; it runs on a reorganisation, not on a read. Two set-based rewrites were measured and the planner chose scans of `role_grant` for both, so the original query stays.
- License reads (`get_resources_license_revision(_id)`) are tenant-plane (`license_revision.read`, a DTO of the own tenant): a System Admin has no operation to list the licenses of all tenants (SAS-12). Add a control-plane read (for example `listSystemLicenses`, permission `system.license.read`, tenant filter) before the SAS-12 screen can work for the control plane. The id form answers a list envelope in the contract although it is a detail; it answers the item itself, as the legal hold read does.
- A license command names the license, not its tenant (`/licenses/{id}/revoke`), and a license is protected by the row-level policy of its tenant: V012 adds `license_tenant_of(uuid)`, which looks through the tenants one by one. A lookup table of license ids to tenants would make it O(1); decide when licenses are numerous.
- The review focus says a window that "starts tomorrow" is refused; SAS 3.8 and the Scheduled state say a license may start later (Issued until `valid_from`). Issue accepts a future start and refuses a window that ended or overlaps another revision that is not revoked; confirm.
- The license status job (Issued to Active to Grace to Expired labels each minute, the `sas.license.expiring.v1` reminder thirty days before `valid_until`) is not part of Task 2: V012 registers the event type, the entitlement store already follows the clock, and the job belongs to the jobs lane.
- Registering the public key of the signing key in `license_signing_key` is a bootstrap step (`LicenseStore.registerSigningKey`, `EnvironmentLicenseKeyStore.publicKeyPem()`); no command or CLI does it yet, and a key rotation (a Retiring key still verifies until `not_after`) has no operation.
- Object storage (Task 15): the story ST-KERNEL-024 says keys live under `tenants/{tenant_id}/`, the plan text said `t/<tenant>/`; the code follows the story (`objectKey(tenant, kind, id)` gives `tenants/{tenant}/{kind}/{id}`). The cache keeps `t:{tenant_id}:` as AC-ST-KERNEL-024-3 says. The real S3 compatible adapter and the antivirus deployment inside Vietnam (TK-KERNEL-024-3) do not exist; the MOCK adapters (file system, EICAR scanner) stand in behind the same ports and contract suites. Nothing yet stores `scan_status` or the key in a module table: DOC (`document_version`) and SAS (`tenant_theme_asset`) must call `FileService` and keep the key.
- A presigned link that fails (forged, altered, expired) answers 401 `SIGNATURE_INVALID`, the nearest registered code; the design registers no code for a storage URL. Oversized uploads use the registered `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (413). Office files are accepted on their zip signature only; whether to look inside the container (macros, embedded objects) is left to the scanner.
- The search projection (`search_document`, migration V018, the next free platform number) has no writer yet: nothing projects projects, contracts or documents, and the events that should feed it (`iam.access.changed.v1`, module events) are not consumed. Only units are projected in the isolation suite. PROJECT and CONTRACT scoped grants add nothing to a search (the owner unit is the only scope column) until the projection carries project and contract ids. `searchGlobal` returns the hit, never a total; Vietnamese search uses the `simple` configuration without accent folding, so a query without diacritics does not match a title with them (needs `unaccent` or a folded column).
- Scheduled exports (ST-KERNEL-025): `runScheduledExport` is the re-authorisation helper; no export job table or scheduler registration exists (BI exports belong to ST-BI-008), so the job audit that records `scopeHash` is the caller's to write. Cross-tenant vector retrieval (LLM-007) has no channel yet; add one when the retrieval store exists.
- Contract compatibility (`contracts:compat`) runs in CI against the pull request base; on a push to main it compares with `github.event.before`, and a base that cannot be found is reported SKIPPED, not passed. The Dart client (`packages/mobile-api-client`) has the `HttpTransport` port only: no `dart:io` adapter, token refresh or offline queue (MOB-1).
- Supply chain: the repository keeps caret ranges in its manifests, so `supply-chain.json` uses `rangePolicy: lockfile` (the lockfile is the pin, verified for every workspace); moving to exact pins is a one line policy change plus pinning. The dependency audit gates production dependencies at `high`; development dependencies have a critical `tinypool` advisory through `vitest` 4.x whose fix is the breaking `vitest` 5. Upgrade, then set `auditScope: all`.
- Test data platform (ST-KERNEL-034): `maskRow` needs a secret `MACHVON_MASKING_KEY` (or an explicit key) and throws without one, instead of masking with a default; the data dictionary marks only a few columns PERSONAL in prose (`affected_household`, `compensation_payment.bank_account_enc`, `land_complaint.description`, `qms_signature`), so the rule list also covers columns named like contact or identity data (`user_account`, `contact`, `document.signer_name`). Classify every PERSONAL column in the dictionary as data, not prose, so the test can read it.
- Test data refresh, invitations (M2f triage of f81f224): `membership_invitation.email_hash` is the SHA-256 of the invited address, and accepting compares it with the verified email. `maskRow` is row by row, so it masks the hash as an unrelated pseudonym; the row alone cannot recompute the hash of the masked address (it holds the hash, not the address). When the refresh runner is written it must mask an invitation through its account (invitation, membership, user account): set `email_hash` to the SHA-256 of the masked `user_account.email_normalized`. No runner exists yet, and a masked address (`@example.invalid`) can not be verified by any identity provider, so nothing breaks today.
- Test data: `maskRow` now also masks personal values inside JSON columns (`PERSONAL_JSON_FIELDS`, today `tenant_provisioning_run.steps_json._request.initial_owner_email`). The dictionary check reads column names and descriptions only, so a new personal value inside another JSON column (an audit `diff`, an outbox payload) is not found by it: reviewers of such a column must add an entry there. Audit diffs and outbox payloads written today carry no email (members.controller, tenant events), so none is listed.
- Legal hold and idempotency (M2f triage of 3f9dc42): the idempotency record of a command points at ONE audited resource (a tenant-plane command prefers one that a hold contains at completion time, else the first; a control-plane command takes the first, because the control role may not read holds). A hold placed later on a second resource of a multi-resource command does not protect the record; a TENANT hold and a hold on the first resource do. Reusing an expired key whose record is held sets the held record aside (its key becomes `retired:<id>`, everything else is kept) and starts a new record.
- Search projection (M2f triage of 3a7abe3): the scope of a search is judged on the CURRENT path of the owner unit (`left join organization_unit`), so a moved unit changes what its documents show at once; the `owner_unit_path` column of `search_document` is only a record of the path at projection time and could be dropped when the writer exists. A document whose owner unit no longer exists is visible to a reader of the whole tenant only.
- Maker-checker: the commands that have an approver are declared by hand in `apps/api/test/authz/maker-checker.ts` (today only `releaseLegalHold`); the contract has no `x-approver` marker to derive them from.

## M2f lane E2 design gaps (change request entries needed)
- The plan names `infra/**/*.yaml`; the files are JSON (`infra/**/*.json`): Node reads JSON without a dependency, and the repository has no YAML parser in its lockfile. The checks and the runbooks use the JSON names.
- Every infrastructure component is `PLANNED` with `restoreEvidence: null`: no restore test has been run, because no PostgreSQL primary and standby, object store, Keycloak, Temporal, collector, Redis, PowerSync or LiteLLM exists to restore. `npm run infra:check -- --require-entered <component>` fails until a real restore is recorded; the first stories of each component (listed by the check) wait for it.
- The design does not say how long restore evidence stays valid for a component whose version did not change; the check only rejects evidence for an older major version than the component (`EVIDENCE_STALE`). A maximum age (for example one year, or the quarterly drill of the backup file) is a decision for the Data Owner.
- `db/migrations/expand-contract.json` sets `baselineThrough: 19`: V003 to V019 were never released (V016 adds a NOT NULL column without a default), so they are exempt from the expand-contract rule. The value freezes at the first production release; a migration up to that number written later would also be exempt, so the number must be raised only by the release manager at that moment.
- The N versus N-1 compatibility harness (KERNEL-014: the previous release's client against the current API and the previous API against the current schema) and the feature-flag service are not part of Task 20 and do not exist; `infra/pipelines/production.json` lists `n-minus-1-compatibility` as a gate that no job runs yet.
- The pipelines are declared in `infra/pipelines/` and `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` runs them, but the deploy command itself is the repository variable `DEPLOY_COMMAND_STAGING` / `DEPLOY_COMMAND_PRODUCTION`; with no variable nothing is deployed and the job says so. The two required reviewers of the `production` environment and the cohort choices (`pilot`, `cohort-1`, `all`, copied from `infra/pipelines/production.json`) are repository settings, not files.
- The KERNEL-044 orchestrator pattern runs on the in-process adapter (`apps/api/src/modules/orchestration`); instances live in the memory of one process, so a restart forgets them. The Temporal adapter must keep instances durable and pass the same contract tests.
- Two codes are used for the orchestrator that the plan did not name: a signal the state does not accept is 409 `WORKFLOW_STATE_INVALID`, and an instance of another tenant is 404 `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` (the existence of foreign instances is not revealed). `TASK_ALREADY_DECIDED` stays the code of the approval task command (ADM), not of the engine.
- Spikes (Task 21): the acceptance criterion of KERNEL-001 says no touch target below 48 dp, while the generated theme asks 44 pt on Apple platforms (the platform guideline). Either the criterion names Android, or the iOS minimum becomes 48; a design decision for the mobile lead. The spike code is in `packages/mobile-api-client/spike/` (the plan said `packages/mobile-tokens/spike/`), because the queue and the upload build on the generated client.
- Spikes (Task 21): no resumable-upload endpoint exists in the API or the e2e stack (begin, held chunks, put chunk, complete with SHA-256); the Dart spike runs against a test double of that port. The upload module (DOC or a platform slice) must define the operations in the contract before MOB depends on them.
- Spikes (Task 21): the AI evaluation record (`route`, `kind`, `items`, `pages`, `score`, `p95LatencyMs`, `costPer1000Pages`) is the harness's own shape; the LLM module has not defined the eval record, so `EVAL_MISSING_FOR_ROUTE` is enforced by `checkProfilePublish` in the harness only until the gateway profile publish exists.
- ADM-09 CXN-SEC-01 (Task 11): the MFA role list of the security policy is DONE_UNCONFIRMED. It is enforced when a tenant is selected (a published policy replaces the default list LEADER, SECURITY_ADMIN, TENANT_ADMIN, TENANT_OWNER); the business owner must confirm the floor roles.
- ADM-09 (Task 11): the session limits (`session_idle_minutes`, `session_max_hours`), the IP allowlist, the download TTL and the watermark flag are validated and versioned but NOT enforced yet: the session core has no idle tracking or absolute start time, no sign-in IP check and no download-link TTL setting. ADM-14 says so on screen. Enforcement needs a session heartbeat and an absolute-expiry column (a platform migration).
- ADM-08 (Task 11): the 30-day certificate expiry warning is computed when the register is read (ADM-08 banner and badge) and the daily `certificate-expiry` job marks ended certificates Expired, but the reminder notification through the notification core is not sent: it needs a registered event type (control-plane migration) and a "reminded" marker for deduplication.
- CR-ADM-06 (Task 11): ADM-08 and ADM-14 list data, and openapi.json had no read operations for `signing_certificate` or `security_policy_version`. The four reads and the permissions `adm.signature.read` and `adm.security.read` were added in place (ADM.md section 7 and implementation_plan.json updated, contracts regenerated); the business owner must confirm that SECURITY_ADMIN should also read the certificate register (today only TENANT_ADMIN and TENANT_OWNER hold `adm.signature.*`).
- Visual cases (Task 11): ADM-08 and ADM-14 have component tests but no Playwright visual case against `04_Design_Assets/screens/ADM-08.png` and `ADM-14.png` yet; both mockups are the generic prototype form, not the register or the policy form, so the page-frame regions have to be agreed first.
- API runtime (Task 13): the outbox relay and the scheduled jobs are started by `npm start` since the workers wiring (see "Workers in the API process" below); the OpenTelemetry SDK packages are not added (OTLP/HTTP JSON and console exporters sit behind a port); `build` is a type check; the `/health/ready` ledger check needs `V020__ledger_read_access.sql`.
- Alerts (Task 14): the metric `access_revocation_lag_seconds` is emitted by `reportAccessRevocationLag` (kernel) but no production probe measures revocation lag yet (only the NFR test does); `raiseExpiringLicenses` is not called by a job yet; `createAlertSink` is handed to the relay, the seal job, the job runner and the consumers by the API process (see "Workers in the API process").

## M2f lane B2 design gaps (Task 7: ADM-02, ADM-17, guest contractors)
- CR-ADM-07 (ADM.md section 3.3, openapi.json): `get_resources_membership` got the `membership_type` filter, and its items carry `version`, `role_codes`, `invitation_status`, `contractor_id`, `contractor_name` and `contracts` (the items are untyped objects in the contract, so only the parameter is a schema change). `get_resources_contract` is implemented as the minimal contract read ADM-17 picks from (id, code, status, contractor, project, owner unit, completion date) until the CTR module (R1) defines the full resource view.
- The system role matrix is PENDING SIGN-OFF and does not hold what ADM-02 and ADM-17 need: TENANT_ADMIN (`iam.*`, `adm.*`, `audit.read`) and UNIT_ADMIN (`iam.member.*`, `iam.role.grant`, `iam.unit.manage`) lack `membership.read` (the list read) and `contract.read` (the contract picker, and rule (c) of BR-IAM-05 for the GUEST_CONTRACTOR role); UNIT_ADMIN also lacks `iam.guest.invite`. Only TENANT_OWNER can use both screens fully; the tests use custom roles. The matrix must say which role reads the directory and invites guests.
- BR-IAM-08 gives the guest bound as "latest contract end + 365 days [assumption]" without saying what the contract end is: the code uses `contract.completion_due`, counted from today when it is empty or past, so a guest of a completed contract can still be invited for up to a year. Confirm the figure and the base date.
- A guest has one `organization_id` but may hold contracts of several units: the membership is placed in the owner unit of the first contract by id, so the unit-scoped member list shows the guest to the administrator of that unit only. The design says nothing about where a guest lives.
- ADM-17 says a guest "expires when the contract closes": the grants end at `valid_until`, but nothing expires them on `contract.closed` (BR-IAM-08), because the CTR events do not exist yet.
- The acceptance of an invitation has no screen in the catalogue (IAM-01 to IAM-09 do not include it), so a person who received a token has no page to enter it; the API (`acceptInvitation`) works and the development session shows the data.
- The seat check of a guest runs at acceptance (`assertSeatFree`), not at invitation: a tenant can invite more guests than the licence has guest seats and only the acceptance fails with QUOTA_EXCEEDED.
- Visual cases of ADM-02 and ADM-17 compare the shell frame and the heading only (the mockups are the generic list); the sidebar region has 88 differing pixels (ratio 0.00037, under the 0.001 target).

## M2f lane D2 design gaps (change request entries needed)
- BR-DOC-15 says the trust anchors are configuration data per tenant, but no table exists: every tenant verifies against the anchors in the directory named by `MACHVON_TRUST_ANCHORS_DIR` (`PUBLIC_CA/` and `GOV_SPECIALIZED/`, PEM or DER). Add a per-tenant table (legal approval, CXN-DOC-07) and an ADM screen; until then nothing is trusted when the variable is unset.
- `document_signature` is meant to be append-only (a new row per verification) but nothing in the database enforces it: Task 18 had no migration number. `SignatureStore` only inserts and reads; a trigger or revoked UPDATE/DELETE grant needs a migration from the platform block.
- `DocSignatureReport` has `additionalProperties: false` with only `version_id`, `overall`, `signatures`, and the overall result depends on the policy chosen (STRICT or LENIENT_TIME), which the table does not store. The overall result is kept in a JSON report object that `report_object_key` points to (fallback: the strict reading of the rows); `verification_no`, `verified_at` and the registered certificate id are members of each signature. Propose a verification table with `overall`, `policy` and `verified_at`.
- A verification that finds no signature stores no row, so `getDocumentSignatureReport` cannot tell "never verified" from "no signatures": both answer INDETERMINATE with an empty list.
- `DocVerifyRequest.revalidate_revocation` has no stated default: false (or absent) leaves revocation NOT_CHECKED, true asks the revocation port. No OCSP or CRL adapter exists, so the bound port answers UNKNOWN and a verification with it is INDETERMINATE.
- BR-DOC-14 f9 (the signer matches the certificate registered for `signer_user_id`, else `SIGNER_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`) needs the signer of the document, which exists with Task 19. Task 18 only links a signature to the registered certificate with the same fingerprint (`registered_certificate_id`).
- The error registry has no code for a cancelled or unknown signing session: the port answers `SIGNING_SESSION_EXPIRED` (409) for an expired or cancelled one and `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` (404) for an unknown one.
- The answer of `verifyDocumentSignatures` carries `overall`, `verification_no` and `signature_count` next to the `CommandResult` members, as `renderDocumentPreview` does.
- PAdES long-term (B-LT): the profile is reported when a DSS exists, but the OCSP and CRL answers inside the DSS are not read yet to decide revocation at the time of signing.
- The verifier reads classic cross-reference files without object streams (what the renderer writes with `useObjectStreams: false`); a later revision that touches an object it cannot read is refused with `INCREMENTAL_CHANGES_NOT_ALLOWED`, and signer identifiers by subject key identifier or RSA-PSS signatures are not accepted. Decide whether files from other producers must be accepted before the external-upload path is the primary path.
- `npm run migrations:check` reports the missing V017: it is the block of Task 19 (outgoing documents), not allocated to Task 18.

## R10 baseline approvals (Task 22)
- Product owner: decide per screen whether the real UI becomes the new visual baseline, stays bound to the mockup, or is accepted as the gated regions only (`docs/r10-baseline-approvals.md`, 24 screens, whole-page ratios 0.9 to 4.3 percent against the 0.1 percent target). The baseline PNGs are unchanged until then.
- After an approval (R10 steps 8 and 9): draw the real screens and their real fields (for example the field "Mã đơn vị" of ADM-01) into `02_Prototype` and `screen_catalog.json` with a change request each, export the new PNGs with the existing export script, then gate the whole page. Not started: it needs the product owner's answer first, and a catalogue edit without the prototype and the image would make the three disagree.
- ADM-08 and ADM-14 have no Playwright visual case (the mockups are the generic prototype form); draw them or accept the component tests as their only check.
- The tool that runs the `UI-<screen>` screen-contract test cases (`test_cases.json`, type `screen-contract`) is not identified; `docs/r10-baseline-approvals.md` maps the existing checks that stand in for it.
- The `Browser` workflow has not run on a hosted runner; the baselines come from Chrome on Windows, so font differences on Ubuntu may turn text regions red. Decide at the first run whether to re-export baselines on the CI image or keep Windows as the reference machine.

## Workers in the API process (N1 fixed; what remains)
- The provisioning saga that the relay's consumer runs uses `MockProvisioningPorts` (identity provider organisation, DNS, certificates, storage, placement) and the MOCK invitation mailer: a tenant request is processed end to end in the database, but nothing outside it is created and the owner's invitation is not delivered. Replace the ports with the real adapters of Task 20 (`BootstrapOverrides.provisioningPorts` is the seam) and the mailer with the OMNI email channel before the first real tenant. `apps/api/src/provisioning/mock-ports.ts` must not reach production unnoticed: consider a startup refusal when `NODE_ENV=production` and a MOCK adapter is bound.
- The pilot license step needs a registered signing key: `LICENSE_SIGNING_KEY_*` is read, but no command registers its public half in `license_signing_key` (`LicenseStore.registerSigningKey` has no caller outside tests), so the LICENSE_PILOT step fails with `SIGNING_KEY_UNAVAILABLE` (the store refuses a key that is not registered and in its validity window) until a bootstrap step exists. `bootstrap.ts` also does not register the license routes (`AppModule.register({ licenses })`); only the e2e stack does.
- A provisioning run that was in flight when its process died is not resumed: `resumeUnfinishedRuns` exists but is not called, because several processes would resume the same run (the in-process workflow runner is not a single owner). The Temporal adapter (KERNEL-044) is the owner; until then an operator restarts the run with the retry command.
- The scheduler arms every job one interval after the start. A process that restarts more often than a job's interval (daily `certificate-expiry`, hourly `audit-verify`) never runs it; the advisory lock makes an extra run harmless, so an optional run at start (`JobDefinition.runOnStart`) can be added when deployments are frequent.
- Nothing exports the metrics of the process (`job_runs_total`, `outbox_*`): the runner keeps its history in memory and the relay and jobs use the no-op metrics sink. `ACCESS_REVOCATION_LAG` and `OUTBOX_LAG` rules therefore only fire on alert events (`OUTBOX_LAG` is raised by the relay), not on gauges.
- A shutdown does not wait for detached workflows (a saga in its retry back-off): they are cut with the process.
- `raiseExpiringLicenses` and the license status job are still not scheduled (N8).

## M2f triage: deploy, tenant theme, security screens (what is left after the fixes)
- Production approvals: `infra/pipelines/production.json` asks for two approvers of two roles (release manager and security administration), but a GitHub environment releases the job when any ONE of its required reviewers approves, so the workflow cannot count roles. `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` now refuses a commit without a green CI run and a green staging deployment, checks out the SHA preflight resolved, and enforces the canary order and the 30-minute bake (`scripts/deploy-gate.mjs`), but the two-role approval needs either an approval service the workflow can query or two chained environments (one reviewer role each); decide which, then wire it. Until then the second approval is a runbook step (`docs/runbooks/infra/rollback.md`).
- Canary abort: the workflow cannot evaluate `abortOn` (error rate, p95 latency, isolation violation) because no metrics source is wired to it. `deploy-gate.mjs` only checks that the previous cohort was promoted and baked for `bakeMinutes`; the health evidence of that cohort has to come from the monitoring stack (record it as a commit status such as `deploy/health/<cohort>` and require it in the gate).
- Deployment evidence for releases that predate the commit statuses: `deploy-gate.mjs` now runs from the pinned workflow revision (`.deploy-tools`), so an older ref no longer fails with a missing script, but a release deployed before `deploy/staging` and `deploy/production/<cohort>` existed has no such status and is refused (`STAGING_NOT_GREEN`), so a rollback to it through this workflow is blocked. A verified migration needs an owner decision (a signed attestation by the release manager recorded as the status, or a one-time back-fill from the deployment history of the target environment); until then roll back with the manual runbook step (`docs/runbooks/infra/rollback.md`).
- ADM-14 (security policy): the roles an MFA policy can name are read from `get_resources_role_definition`, which needs `role_grant.read` and hides roles the reader may not manage, so an author who holds `adm.security.manage` without those grants cannot pick roles (the screen now says so and refuses to save instead of saving an empty list). The contract has no operation that lists the roles a policy may name; add one with an `adm.security.read` permission (a change request on `openapi.json`), or let `get_resources_role_definition` answer the role codes for that permission.
- SAS-04 (tenant theme): publishing a draft clears it (publish step 9), so the draft revision restarts at 0 and an editor that still holds the old revision 1 could save over a newer draft that reached revision 1 again. The ETag of the draft is its revision only; a draft generation (or a revision that never restarts) in the contract would close it.

## M2f triage: tenant lifecycle, licenses, members, guests (what is left after the fixes)
- Guest baseline rights: a guest invitation now also grants the `MEMBER` role (self-service: `session.self`, `profile.self`, `notification.self`, `tenant.theme.read`) on the same contracts and for the same time as the guest role, because without it the guest gets 403 from `/me/effective-access` after accepting. `ADM.md` (inviteGuestContractor) names only the CONTRACT grant of the chosen role; the business owner should confirm the baseline grant and the design document should say it (a change request on ADM.md).
- Provisioning runs resumed by two processes: the pilot license and entitlement steps now take their advisory lock before they look at what exists, and the other steps are idempotent through unique indexes and retried, but nothing serialises a whole run across processes. The owner of that is the workflow engine (Temporal adapter, KERNEL-044); until then `resumeUnfinishedRuns` must stay un-wired for several processes (see "Workers in the API process").
- Effective license of a tenant with several serials: serials have no order of their own (every first revision is 1), so the license in force is chosen by dates and labels (started, then not revoked and before its grace end, then the latest start). If SAS wants an explicit successor chain (`supersedes_id` set on every renewal), the choice should follow it instead; today `supersedes_id` is only filled when a revision supersedes another.
- Accepting an invitation in a tenant that is still Provisioning is refused as `TENANT_SUSPENDED` (`tenant_runtime` maps every state except Active, Suspended and Offboarding to no access); the owner invitation of step 11 is mailed a moment before the tenant becomes Active, so an owner who clicks within that window must retry. No contract code names "tenant not open yet"; add one if the window matters.

## M2f triage: documents and files (what is left after the fixes)
- Certificate path validation (`cms-verification.ts`) checks signatures, `basicConstraints` (CA flag and `pathLenConstraint`), `keyCertSign` and validity at the proven time. It does not evaluate name constraints, policy constraints or `AIA`/`CRL distribution points`, and an OCSP/CRL adapter still does not exist (the bound port answers UNKNOWN). Owner: integration lead (D05); needed before a real CA sandbox is verified (UAT-AC430).
- Revision analysis reads a signed prefix leniently: where a cross-reference entry of an earlier revision names an object by offset, the named object wins, otherwise the last definition in the text does (linearized and hybrid files keep working). A file whose signed part already carries a deliberately inconsistent cross-reference is not flagged as such, only the revisions after the signature are held to the strict rule. Objects inside object streams of the signed part are not resolved, so a later change to one of them is refused as a new object (conservative).
- Signable rendering: the form fields `doc_number`, `doc_symbol` and `doc_date` are empty regions now, and the numbering revision writes the bare number, the symbol and `dd/mm/yyyy`. The state document form of Decree 30/2020 prints "Số: 05/QĐ-UBND" and "Cao Bằng, ngày 15 tháng 6 năm 2026" (label, place and the long date). Either numbering writes those full texts, or the profile gets static label regions beside the fields; a design decision on `number_field_layout` (BR-DOC-13/14) and the legal confirmation CXN-DOC-01.
- The place and date line is wrapped to the width of its profile box (60 mm in `ND30_A4_STANDARD`), so the usual line takes two lines. A wider box (the right column is about 95 mm) needs a new profile revision and legal confirmation.
- A signing session whose database transaction fails after the prepared PDF was stored (the file is written last, after the provider session and the session row) still leaves an unreferenced object; the clean-up job `doc.orphan-object-cleanup` named in DOC.md does not exist yet, nor does a delete on `DocumentFilePort`.
- DOC-05/06: the document type revision is picked in the browser from the types the first page of registers carries (same rule as `docTypeFor`); a read that returns the revision in force for a date would remove the copy of the rule.

## M2f triage round 2 (Codex review of the fix commits): what is left
- Cache invalidation is delivered to the API instance that consumed the outbox event only (`apps/api/src/workers/outbox-consumers.ts`): with several API processes the other instances keep their access, entitlement and tenant-status caches until the entry expires. A broadcast transport (Redis pub/sub or a Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY` channel) belongs to the access-cache transports of ST-IAM-004 (the Redis and Keycloak transports do not exist yet).
- Object storage layout: the file-system adapter changed its layout (hashed folder per object, one file per version) inside the unpushed M2 milestone, so no deployed store holds the old layout and no migration was written. The real S3-compatible adapter replaces this MOCK before any data exists (Task 20).
- A theme draft that was published is deleted, so its revision restarts at 1 for the next draft and a stale editor that still holds revision 1 of the earlier draft can overwrite a newer draft of another administrator. The web editor now restarts at revision 0 after its own publish; a revision that survives the publication (a generation column on `tenant_theme_draft`) needs a platform migration and a change request.
- The signable rendering leaves the number and date regions empty and the numbering revision writes only the bare values, so the final PDF lacks the "Số:" label and the place name: a design decision on BR-DOC-14 (see "documents and files" above), not a defect of the renderer.

## DGV-1 (data governance) open points

- ST-DGV-001 (decision): who sees a dataset (BR-DGV-11, AC55). The owner, the steward and the registrant always see their dataset; `dataset.allowed_role_codes` (V1020) narrows a dataset to the holders of the listed roles; with an empty list PUBLIC and INTERNAL datasets are open to every holder of `data.catalog.read` and the other three classes (CONFIDENTIAL, PERSONAL, IMPORTANT_CORE) only to the owner, the steward and the registrant. The default fails closed. A dataset the caller may not see is not in the answer, not in `total`, and an id that names one is 404 (also for `assignDatasetOwnership`).
- ST-DGV-001 (contract gap): no operation of openapi.json writes `allowed_role_codes` (`DgvDatasetRegister` and `DgvOwnership` do not carry it), so today only the owner, the steward and the registrant see a restricted dataset and the role list is set by the database. DGV-15 (classification and data rights) needs an operation, for example `setDatasetAccess` with `data.catalog.manage`, step-up and an audit reason, before a PERSONAL dataset can be shared with a role such as LND from the UI. The orchestrator owns the contract change.
- ST-DGV-001 (decision): the roles that count for visibility are those of every active grant of the caller whatever its scope, because a dataset belongs to no organisation unit. If DGV later scopes datasets to units, `viewerOf` in the DGV controller must use the scope of each grant.
- ST-DGV-001 (decision): an owner and a steward must be active members of the staff (`membership_type = STAFF`, Active, inside their validity); a guest, a service account or a suspended member is refused with 422 VALIDATION_FAILED naming the field. The seats are checked in the store and by composite foreign keys to `membership` (V1020). The same dataset code in another case is the same code (unique index on `lower(dataset_code)`), and the application role cannot delete a dataset (it is retired).
- ST-DGV-001 (decision): a change of the pair that changes nothing is 422 VALIDATION_FAILED (`owner_user_id`), a retired dataset cannot change owner (409 STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED), and a manager who cannot see a restricted dataset cannot change its owner. An orphaned restricted dataset (its owner, steward and registrant have left) therefore needs a break-glass reassignment by the platform support path; not built.
- ST-DGV-001: the quality badge of the catalogue (DGV-04) is already computed from `quality_run` by BR-DGV-04 (NOT_ASSESSED without a completed run, STALE after the freshness SLO, otherwise the overall result); the runs themselves belong to the quality story, so every dataset shows NOT_ASSESSED until then. The contract column shows the revision of `current_contract_version_id`, null until a contract is published (ST-DGV contract story activates the dataset: DRAFT to ACTIVE).
- ST-DGV-001: the owner and steward pickers of DGV-03 read `get_resources_membership` (permission `membership.read`, limited to the units the caller's grants reach); a data steward without it sees a notice and cannot register. A directory read for the catalogue (names of active staff, no personal data) would remove that dependency; it needs a contract operation.
- ST-DGV-001: `searchCatalog` answers `total` (the visible matches) beside the page; the DGV-04 screen loads every page and counts what it loaded, filters on the server by text, domain and classification, and does not page by scroll yet (a catalogue of thousands of datasets needs a page control).

- ST-DGV-002 (decision): a rule and a run are written against a `dataset_contract` (the contract of openapi.json: `contract_id`, `dataset_contract_id`); the dataset is the one the contract names (`dataset_contract.dataset_id`), and the catalogue offers the highest revision as `working_contract_id`. No operation writes `dataset_contract` until ST-DGV-005 (`createDatasetContractRevision`); until then the rows come from that story or from a fixture. A contract without a dataset is refused (422 `contract_id`).
- ST-DGV-002 (decision): the R1 source of rows is the lines of a frozen DIS `period_snapshot`; datasets of other producers (PAY, CAP, MDM imports) get a source with their snapshots (`QualitySource` in `dgv-quality-source.ts`). `snapshot.frozen.v1` is consumed by `SnapshotFrozenConsumer` (payload: `snapshot_id`, optional `tenant_id`); DIS-009 does not emit it yet. Import-applied triggers (`IMPORT_BATCH`) and the `SCHEDULE` creator job (a daily re-run) are not built: `SCHEDULE` is accepted from the API only.
- ST-DGV-002 (decision): a run is requested RUNNING and executed by the job `dgv-quality-run` (202 as the contract says); one run per (dataset, snapshot) may be RUNNING (409 `RUN_ALREADY_RUNNING`) and one per (dataset, snapshot) is started by the event (partial unique indexes of V1021).
- ST-DGV-002 (decision): ACCURACY rules are refused (422 `EXPRESSION_NOT_ALLOWED`, database CHECK) until ST-DGV-011; an INTEGRITY rule needs a threshold of 100 and cannot only warn; a failed rule fails the run, a warning or an unmeasured rule makes it WARNING at best; scores are cut to hundredths, never rounded up.
- ST-DGV-002 (decision): the three commands (create, approve, run) need the permission on the whole tenant (`holdsOnWholeTenant`, 403 `SCOPE_DENIED` for a unit grant); reads and the scorecard follow the dataset visibility of BR-DGV-11 (a rule, a run or a scorecard of a dataset the caller may not see is 404).
- ST-DGV-002 (decision): QUARANTINE and HARD_BLOCK are recorded on the run (`quarantined_count`, `certified_blocked`, rule result sample keys `sample_refs` = opaque row ids, never returned by a read); the per-record `quality_exception` rows are created by ST-DGV-003, which reads those keys. BI (ST-BI) must read `certified_blocked` of `dgv.quality_run.completed.v1` to exclude the snapshot from certified figures.
- ST-DGV-002 (verified): db lane test/dgv, test/authz, test/isolation 334 of 335 pass; the one failure is the matrix generator listing the ISS commands acceptIssueEscalation and escalateIssue that are not yet in matrix-commands.json (ISS escalation work, not DGV). Browser lane: dgv-flows and dgv-quality-flows 5 passed, visual DGV-01/03/04/06/07 5 passed; web vitest screens/dgv 136 passed.
- ST-DGV-002: the DGV-07 run form takes the id of the frozen snapshot as text; a picker needs the list of period snapshots (`listPeriodSnapshotLines` is ST-DIS-009 and lists lines, not snapshots): ask for a `listPeriodSnapshots` read when DIS-009 is built.
- ST-DGV-002: the DGV-06 form writes one check per rule (no and/or/not); compound expressions are accepted by the API. The field names are typed by the steward (the server validates them against the source); a list of the fields of the source would need a read operation that the contract does not have.
- ST-DGV-002: `searchCatalog` items gained `quality_score`, `quality_hard_fail`, `quality_run_at`, `working_contract_id`, `open_incidents`, `overdue_incidents` and the answer a `summary` (its response schema is the open `object`, so openapi.json is unchanged); `get_resources_quality_rule_version` and `get_resources_quality_run` accept `dataset_id` and `status` filters beside `limit` and `cursor`.
- ST-DGV-002 (needs the PO, see the assumption of the story): the vocabulary NOT_ASSESSED / NO_DATA, the SLA hours of the incidents (4/24/72/240) and the criticality weights are working assumptions until the data owner and the accountant sign the financial-critical thresholds (completeness 100%, duplicates 0).

## MDM-1 (master data) open points
- ST-MDM-002 AC-2 "only the tenant alias (label, order) can be overridden": the lock is built (a tenant cannot write the PLATFORM namespace through propose, change or retire; type policy in `master_type_policy` with a baseline in the master store). CLOSED: the alias is written through `createMasterCrosswalk` (ST-MDM-007) as a crosswalk row of the reserved system `TENANT_ALIAS` (code type `LABEL` = display label, `ORDER` = position; method MANUAL; target an entry of the PLATFORM namespace only; one ACTIVE alias of each kind per entry, V1089 holds the rules and gives the alias its own unique index so two entries may share a label or an order). `searchMasterRecords` answers `alias_label`, `alias_order`, `display_label`, `display_order` (the platform `label` is untouched; the response schema is an open object, so no contract change); MDM-27 shows the alias, MDM-24 explains it when the system is `TENANT_ALIAS`, MDM-33 states the rule. An import mapping may not use `TENANT_ALIAS` as its translation system. The story's three criteria are covered; docs/story-status.json is the orchestrator's to update.
- ST-MDM-001 task 4 (cache key `t:{tenant}:mdm:{type}:{key}:{date}` invalidated on `master.published.v1`): no master read cache exists yet, so there is nothing to invalidate; the first consumer that caches a lookup (PRJ-1) registers the invalidation on that event.
- The control plane has no screen or operation yet to write `master_type_policy` rows (a tenant-specific policy); today a row is written by the platform operator in the database, and the baseline in `BASELINE_POLICIES` applies to every tenant without one.
- Tenant stewards and checkers are custom roles (no system role holds `master.*` but TENANT_OWNER, which holds every tenant permission): the system role matrix of IAM.md has no DATA_STEWARD or DATA_CHECKER; a decision of the business owner is needed before a system role is added.
- ST-MDM-003: no screen assigns the approvers of a rule type (master_type_approver is written by the control plane only), so a tenant that holds a rule type cannot approve it until someone does; the control-plane UI belongs to SAS.
- ST-MDM-003: the baseline pack is built into the code and sealed with its digest; the signed pack from the control plane (MDM 3.1 section 9) replaces it when SAS delivers packs. Until then a change of the baseline is a code change with a new pack id.
- ST-MDM-003: CAP does not read the rule port (MasterRules) yet: CAP must call nationalCode before it publishes a plan and show the awaiting-confirmation badge while the rule is flagged awaiting. PRJ reads `suggestGroup` since ST-PRJ-001 (see "PRJ-1" below).
- ST-MDM-003: the seeds of the rule data (group thresholds, appraisal criteria, national code format and stage) are working assumptions flagged awaiting CXN-32, CXN-01 and ASSUMPTION-APPRAISAL-60; the owners confirm them by publishing a new version of each rule.
- ST-MDM-007: the adapters of the four default connectors are PROPOSED: no connector reads a file yet (the file adapters belong to the owners of each format: NATIONAL_PIM_FILE and TREASURY_STATEMENT_FILE need the sample files and the contract from the Ministry of Finance and the State Treasury; PROCUREMENT_FILE and BUDGET_CODE_FILE the same). When a connector is built it must advance its own cursor and set `adapter_status` only after the owner confirms the format.
- ST-MDM-007: a translated attribute of an import is judged by the type (the entry must exist), but the crosswalk does not check that its target is of the type that the attribute refers to; only the parent is checked against the type of the file. Add the check when an attribute with a reference to another type is imported through a crosswalk in production.
- ST-MDM-007: the crosswalk list is read whole by MDM-24 and filtered in the browser; a tenant with tens of thousands of mappings needs server-side filters on the screen (the operation already filters by system, code type, code, entry and status).
- ST-MDM-006: the dry run, the apply and the publication of a batch run in one transaction each, so a batch is limited to 5,000 rows (about a minute for the apply); a larger file needs the streaming import of ST-KERNEL-070 (quarantine table, reconciliation items with an owner), which this story does not build. The file is read from the object store at stage and at apply and its hash is checked each time. Excel dates stored as serial numbers are converted when their style is a date style (built-in or custom format, 1900 or 1904 system); a date typed as text must be YYYY-MM-DD, and a cell whose style is not a date style stays a number. A batch that was refused as empty (`IMPORT_EMPTY`) stays VALIDATED: there is no command to cancel a dry run, so such a batch remains in the list as a record of the attempt.
- ST-MDM-006: the review of a batch is by the whole batch; there is no "return" of a few rows and no partial approval. The rows of a PARTIAL batch that were left out are listed on the batch and exported as CSV; importing them again is a new file.
- ST-MDM-005: the priorities of MDM-18 (response and resolution hours, working calendar, escalation) are working assumptions; ISS owns the SLA policy per category and severity (`issue_sla_policy`), so ISS-1 had to decide whether it reads the priority entry or keeps its own table: decided in ST-ISS-001, ISS keeps `issue_sla_policy` and does not read the priority entry (see "ISS-1 (issues and field work) open points"); the issue groups of MDM-17 are the ones ISS reads. The retention periods are awaiting CXN-05, and MDM-22 shows the languages and the texts but the translations are written as JSON.
- ST-MDM-004: DIS must store `calendarVersionId` (and the revision) with every period it generates from `MasterRules.calendarPeriods`, and judge `is_late` with `isLate` from that version; the unit and currency rounding (`roundDecimal`, `convertDecimal`) is for PAY and CAP to use when they handle amounts.
- ST-MDM-004: the week numbering (the year and the number of a period are those of its last day) and the working calendar of D03 (Saturday to Friday, due Friday at 10:00) are assumptions until the capital business owner signs them; both are data except the numbering rule, which is code.
- ST-MDM-003: the forms of MDM-25/26 type the attributes of the catalogue types; the rule types (bands, criteria) are still written as JSON because only the platform changes them by default.
- ST-MDM-008: the five DISBURSEMENT terms (GIAI_NGAN_PERIOD, GIAI_NGAN_YTD, GIAI_NGAN_PTD, MAU_SO_KH_TTG_GIAO, MAU_SO_KH_TONG) are seeded per tenant IN_REVIEW by the baseline job, never published by the platform. Their owner (`owner_membership_id`, the only person who may decide and publish them) is the earliest Active TENANT_OWNER of the tenant who is not the job's own membership; with no such owner nothing is seeded and the next 6-hour run seeds them once one exists. The capital business owner is a person the tenant names: if that person is not the tenant owner, the tenant owner rejects the seeded term (a rejection is final for that revision) and a steward proposes the next revision with the capital owner as `owner_membership_id`; the baseline never re-seeds a code that exists in any status. A command to reassign the owner of a term in review needs a contract change (a new operation), which this story does not make. If the owner of a term leaves the tenant while it is IN_REVIEW, nobody can decide it (only the named owner may): the same reject-and-repropose path applies, and it needs another owner to reject, so the control plane must name a second TENANT_OWNER first.
- ST-MDM-008: the definitions in the seed (DIS BR-DIS-05, 06, 12 and the delta of BR-DIS-03) are the working assumption of the design until the capital business owner signs D03; the formulas in `formula_ref` are documentation of the rule, not an expression the system evaluates. The disbursement metrics (DIS-1) must read `GlossaryStore.definitionStatus(tx, 'DISBURSEMENT')` when they build a snapshot: SIGNED only when all five terms have a PUBLISHED revision, PROPOSED_PENDING_SIGNOFF otherwise. DIS has not been built yet, so nothing reads it today; the consumer of `glossary.published.v1` (DIS, DGV-02) registers itself when it exists.
- ST-MDM-008: the decision `return` of the shared MdmDecision contract sends a glossary term back to DRAFT with the reason, but no operation edits a term, so the author can only submit the same text again. Either add an `updateGlossaryTerm` operation for a DRAFT (contract change) or drop `return` for the glossary; until then the owner uses `reject` and the steward proposes the next revision.
- ST-MDM-008: `listGlossaryTerms` also accepts `term_code` (not declared in the contract; the store and the screen filter by it, and the openapi parameter list should gain it with the next contract change).
- ST-MDM-008 (web, MDM-34): the screen reads every term of the tenant (`readAll('listGlossaryTerms')`, no filter) and filters by domain and status in the browser, so the D03 badge always counts the whole glossary; like MDM-24 it needs server-side filters when a tenant has thousands of terms. The five required DISBURSEMENT term codes are mirrored in `model.ts` (`DISBURSEMENT_REQUIRED_TERMS`) because no operation exposes `GlossaryStore.definitionStatus`; a read of the definition status (a field on the list or a new operation, a contract change) would remove the copy and the risk that the two lists drift.
- ST-MDM-008 (web, MDM-34): the owner of a proposed term is typed as a membership id (UUID) because no operation lists the members a steward may name as owner; a picker needs a read of members (`membership.read`) that a steward does not hold. The screen offers decide and publish to every holder of `glossary.approve` and shows the refusal of the server (`SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`, `SCOPE_DENIED` as "only the owner named on the term") instead of guessing who the owner is.
- ST-MDM-008 (browser flow): the API stack of the e2e tests applies the baseline before it grants the tenant owner role, so the DISBURSEMENT terms are not seeded in the e2e tenant (no owner exists yet); the flow checks the pending badge and a term of its own domain, and the signed badge is covered by the vitest suite of the screen.
- ST-MDM-009 (decision): `recordMasterSuccession` opens a SUCCESSION change request in DRAFT anchored on the predecessor (the contract body has no change request id); the edges are written to `master_succession` only when the request is published, so what is in the table is always a fact. Review uses the generic submit, decision and publish operations. Documented in `docs/mdm-succession.md`.
- ST-MDM-009 (decision): a split is recorded edge by edge into the same open draft (same predecessor, date and legal basis) because the contract carries one edge per call; its shares must add up to 100 when it is submitted and published (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`, detail `share_pct`; no new error code), and the database refuses a split that does not add up when the transaction ends.
- ST-MDM-009 (contract gap): no operation lists the successions of a master type, so MDM-37 reads `getMasterRecordSuccessors` once for every entry that is in force the day before the chosen date and not on it (one read each). A tenant with thousands of ended communes needs a list operation, for example `GET /master-successions?master_type&effective_date` with `master.read`.
- ST-MDM-009 (contract gap): no operation reverses a published succession and the runtime role cannot update or delete edges (V1087); a wrongly published succession is corrected by the platform operator in the database. A governed reversal needs an operation with its own permission, a reason and a second person.
- ST-MDM-009 (baseline): no province, commune or agency list of the 2025 reform is seeded; they and the legal basis wait for CXN-34. The screens start from 01/07/2025 as a working assumption and say so. A PLATFORM-namespace predecessor is refused (403 `POLICY_LOCKED`), so the platform's own baseline successions need a writer in the baseline job (they also need a change request because `master_succession.change_request_id` is NOT NULL), which belongs to the release of the baseline after CXN-34.
- ST-MDM-009: `createMasterChangeRequest` with `change_kind` RETIRE and `successor_record_id` keeps the successor in the request only; it writes no `master_succession` row, so an entry retired that way is not found by the as-of and successor reads. Decide whether a retirement with a successor should publish an edge (kind RENAME or MERGE) as well.
- ST-MDM-009: an entry that ended without a succession has no name on MDM-02/03 for a date after it ended (a search as of the date returns no version for it); the name is taken from the succession that handed it over. Reading the last version of an ended entry needs a search parameter or a bulk read of versions.
- ST-MDM-009: `RESTRUCTURE` stays refused by `createMasterChangeRequest` (a change of parent is an UPDATE of `parent_record_id`); the contract enum keeps the value.

## ADM-1 ST-ADM-010 (working calendar versions and addWorkingTime) open points

- Relationship with the MDM reporting calendar (decision): the two are different data with one shared arithmetic. The MDM `CALENDAR` rule says which reporting week a day belongs to and when its report is due (`reporting-calendar.ts`, `MasterRules.calendarPeriods`); the ADM working calendar says which days are worked (`work_calendar_version`, `working-time.ts`). Neither holds the other's data. They share `dayOf`/`isoOf`/`weekdayOf`/`DAY_MS` and `CALENDAR_TIMEZONES` (the only edit to the MDM file is that these helpers are now exported). The contract note for module owners is `docs/adm-working-time.md`.
- Working hours (decision): the schema (`work_calendar_version`) and `WorkCalendarDraft` carry no hours of the day, and the contract is not mine to change. The default window is the constant `DEFAULT_WORKING_INTERVALS` (08:00-12:00, 13:00-17:00, BR-ADM-08); `addWorkingTime` takes the window of the caller as a parameter, because ISS counts 07:30-17:00 without a break. Tenant-specific hours need a new column and a contract field (a change request for ADM-07: "Giờ làm việc" is on the screen catalogue's field list).
- ISS and the working calendar (closed): the second copy of the arithmetic (`iss-working-hours.ts`) is gone; `IssSlaDeadlines` passes `ISS_WORKING_INTERVALS` to `addWorkingTime` and reads the years with `WorkCalendarStore.published`, so the calendar arithmetic exists once. A draft revision lower than a revision that is or was in force cannot be published (409 `STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED`, detail `revision`): a stale draft would take the newer holidays back.
- Error code owner: `CALENDAR_NOT_EFFECTIVE` is registered under module DIS ("Sinh kỳ từ lịch MDM-11") but `addWorkingTime` raises it for "no published working calendar for a year the deadline passes through". The registry (error_codes.json) should name ADM as a second owner; the code and status (422) are unchanged.
- Four-eyes (decision): publishing a calendar needs no second person (BR-ADM-01 lists DoA, workflow, retention, security policy, reorganisation, migration apply and legal hold release, not the calendar; the table has no `approved_by <> created_by` check). Both the author and the publisher are audited. To require a second person, `WorkCalendarsController.publish` calls `assertFourEyes(draft.createdBy, scope.tenant.membershipId)` and the maker-checker matrix gets a `publishWorkCalendar` row.
- Holiday data for 2026-2027 is a placeholder until the official notice is signed (CXN-CAL-01); the screen says so. The story is not DONE for the business owner until a signed notice is entered as the published version.
- Telemetry (TK-ADM-010-6): the commands are covered by the request telemetry of the API (tenant id, no personal data; the audit row carries the calendar code, year and counts only). The failure mode that matters (a deadline needing a year without a calendar, 422 `CALENDAR_NOT_EFFECTIVE`) is raised by the module that counts the deadline; an alert for "the next year has no published calendar" belongs to the scheduler (a yearly job in December that raises the alert when `source(tx, code, nextYear)` is empty) and is not written yet.

## KERNEL-1 ST-KERNEL-071 (provider callback kernel) open points

- Scope (decision): the story has no operation, table or screen in the plan, so there is no controller. The kernel offers `verifyProviderCallback` (pure, `provider-signature.ts`), `defineStatusTable` / `reduceStatus` (pure, `provider-status.ts`) and `ProviderCallbackStore` (`packages/db/src/provider-callback-store.ts`): `receive` (verify, then write the inbox row), `track`, `applyEvent`, `markUncertain`, `resolveTask`, `openTasks`, `failEvent`. The provider controllers of NOT, OMNI and PAY (`receiveChannelWebhook` and the like) call `receive` and `applyEvent` in one tenant transaction, after they have found the tenant from the channel account and read the secret from the vault.
- Tables (decision): `provider_status_item`, `provider_status_history` (append-only) and `provider_reconciliation_task` are new in V1000 because the status of a send, its history and the reconciliation task have no generic table in V002 (`reconciliation_item` is PAY's, tied to a project and a contract). A module that already keeps a status column may use `reduceStatus` alone and keep its own history; the generic tables are for the modules that have none. The existing `integration_event_inbox` is reused as is; V1000 only adds a guard (identity of an event is fixed, a processed event stays processed).
- Monotonic rule (decision): a status table is an acyclic graph; a status is applied when it is later than the current one (skipping statuses is allowed), ignored as a repeat when equal, ignored as late when earlier, and ignored with a reconciliation task (`STATUS_CONFLICT`) when neither (FAILED after DELIVERED). Order comes from the graph, never from the provider's timestamp. The NOT, OMNI and PAY stories define their own tables (for example SENDING, UNCERTAIN, ACCEPTED, DELIVERED, READ, FAILED).
- Uncertain results (decision): `markUncertain` moves the item to UNCERTAIN, opens one `RESULT_UNCERTAIN` task (due after 24 hours) and writes the outbox event `provider.result_uncertain`; the module that owns the item subscribes and asks the provider. A late callback moves the item out of UNCERTAIN on its own, but the open task stays until a person closes it with `resolveTask` and a note (the status found at the provider; a status that is earlier or sideways is refused with 409 `STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED`). Closing the task automatically when a callback resolves the uncertainty needs a worker identity for `resolved_by`; not done.
- Not done (needs the owners): the screens and operations to list and close reconciliation tasks (OMNI-11 shows the inbox and the reconciliation; no operation of the plan reads `provider_reconciliation_task`), the per-provider signing schemes (`TIMESTAMPED_HMAC_SHA256` and `BODY_HMAC_SHA256_PREFIXED` are the two shapes known so far; ZNS, WhatsApp, VNPAY and the signing providers add theirs), and an alert rule for `webhook_signature_failures_total` (OMNI telemetry asks for a warning above 10 failures in 5 minutes; the counter is emitted by `verifyProviderCallback`, the rule and its runbook belong with the OMNI story).

## DOC-1 ST-DOC-006 (template library with effective dating and separated legal confirmation) open points

- Contract gap (needs the orchestrator): `DocTemplateRevision.body_blocks` is typed `object` in `openapi.json`, but a body is a list of blocks (`{kind, text}`) and the render engine of ST-DOC-001 reads a bare array. Decision: the API carries `body_blocks` as `{ "blocks": [...] }` (read and written the same way) and the table keeps the bare list. Needed: type it as an array of `{kind: heading|paragraph, text}` (then drop `blocksOf` in `templates.controller.ts`). The responses of `listDocumentTemplates` and `getDocumentTemplate` are untyped (`type: object`); the shapes are `{items, next_cursor}` and the detail with `body_blocks`, `placeholder_schema`, `content_sha256`, the actors and the confirmation fields, `revisions` (history of the code), plus `cxn_refs`, `layout_confirmation_status`, `renders_watermarked`, `created_by` and `approved_by`, which the contract should list.
- Contract gap: `createDocumentTemplateRevision` lists `TEMPLATE_RANGE_OVERLAP` in its x-error-codes, but UAT-AC423 creates the overlapping revision and expects the 409 on publish. Decision: drafting never checks the period against other revisions (only a published period is exclusive); the overlap is refused at publish. The code stays in the list only as the answer of the exclusion constraint.
- Period (decision): `effective_until` is exclusive everywhere in DOC (templates, layout profiles, document types, `templateFor`, the EXCLUDE constraint): the first day the revision no longer applies. "Valid to 2026-12-31" of AC-ST-DOC-006-2 is therefore stored as `effective_until = 2027-01-01`; the library prints "from X until the day before Y".
- Succession (decision): publishing an open-ended revision that starts after the one in force closes that one on the new start date (shortening only, trigger of V1220, audit `closed_revisions`, version of the closed row +1), as `defineDocType` does; any other overlap (a closed period, or a start before the one in force) is 409 `TEMPLATE_RANGE_OVERLAP`. Without this a successor could never be introduced, because no operation edits a published period. `SUPERSEDED` and `IN_REVIEW` stay in the status list of the table but nothing produces them (there is no submit operation; a closed revision stays PUBLISHED so renders of its dates still find it).
- Separation of duties (decision, deepest governance): the legal confirmer is neither the drafter nor the publisher (`assertFourEyes` twice in the store, and the trigger of V1220 for the application role); publishing needs a person other than the drafter (table CHECK `document_template_sod_chk`). The platform pack has no approver, so its legal officer is only different from the loader. The `doc.template.manage` role can retire a published template without a second person (the contract gives `retireDocumentTemplate` that permission); if the legal owner wants a second person for retiring a confirmed template it is a contract change.
- Legal text is data: the legal basis, the watermark text and the start date are columns of the revision; the pack (`apps/api/src/modules/doc/template-pack.json`, `pack_version` 2026.10.1) carries them, no code holds a legal sentence. The CXN label of the library is read from the `[CXN-nn]` tags of the legal basis and the watermark (`cxn_refs`), or is `[CXN]` when the text names none.
- Pack content (not done): 22 templates are loaded (9 decisions, 4 letters, MNE-TT44-M02 to M10). The disbursement reports (DIS-TT37-*, DIS-CV3665-*), treasury forms (PAY-ND254-*), settlement forms (SET-TT91-*) and the inspection report (AUD-BC-*) of the catalogue in DOC.md are for the owners DIS, PAY and AUD to add to the same file when their forms are drafted (the loader upgrades by `pack_version`). The bodies are skeletons (`{{title}}`/`{{grounds}}`/`{{body}}`, `{{subject}}`/`{{greeting}}`, `{{period}}`): the real texts of the forms need the legal officer. The start dates are provisional (2026-01-01, except Decree 30/2020 on 2020-03-05) until the legal officer enters the effective date of each instrument; every template is PENDING_CONFIRMATION with its watermark. No template is CONFIRMED by the pack.
- Provisioning (not done, needs the orchestrator): `loadPlatformTemplates(tx, actor)` (layout profile of Decree 30/2020, then the pack) is idempotent and tested, but the provisioning saga of SAS 3.1 has eleven fixed steps and none of them calls it. A new step (for example `DOC_TEMPLATE_PACK` after `ROLE_SEED`, with its place in `ProvisioningStep`) is a SAS contract change; until then a new tenant has no templates.
- Template cache (TK-DOC-006-2): there is no template cache to invalidate; `DocumentRenderService` reads the published row inside the render transaction, so the next render sees a publish, a confirmation or a retirement at once. If a cache is added later, register the invalidation with `scope.afterCommit` in the four commands.
- Layout profile: a render stays watermarked while the layout profile is PENDING_CONFIRMATION, although the template is CONFIRMED (BR-DOC-10 applies to both). The library says so on the row (`renders_watermarked`, "the layout waits for its confirmation"). No operation of the plan confirms a layout profile, so the tests confirm it as the owner; until DOC-16 (layout revisions with legal confirmation) exists no tenant renders without a watermark.
- Screens: DOC-14 offers publish, confirm and retire. Drafting a revision is the operation `createDocumentTemplateRevision` without a screen in this story (DOC-15 "detail and versions of a template" owns the editor for blocks, the placeholder schema and the period); DOC-15 and DOC-16 are other stories.
- AC-ST-DOC-006-3 says "an issue request is rendered": `doc_issue_request` / `requestIssue` is a later DOC story. The test renders MNE-TT44-M02 through `renderDocumentPreview` (the same render service); re-run the criterion through the issue request when it lands. `unconfirmed_override_reason` of `issueOutgoingDocument` (BR-DOC-10) is not touched.
- The evidence document of a legal confirmation is any document of the tenant (`document` row); a rule on its kind or classification belongs to the legal owner. The proposed `legal_confirmation_ref` of the create body is stored as the drafter's proposal and never makes a revision CONFIRMED; the confirmation command overwrites it.
- Scope (decision, review): drafting, publishing, legal confirmation and retiring count only through a grant on the WHOLE tenant (403 `SCOPE_DENIED` for a grant on a branch of the tree), as DOC 2 gives the template administrator and the legal officer the whole tenant; the two reads still accept any scope of `doc.template.read`.
- Shared files touched by this story: `apps/api/src/request-validation.ts` (the `FIELD_PROBLEMS` map: empty reference is `CONFIRMATION_REF_REQUIRED`, empty legal basis is `LEGAL_BASIS_REQUIRED`, both 422), the authorization matrix (`matrix-commands.json`, `negative-matrix.ts`, `negative-matrix.test.ts`, `maker-checker.ts`: two approver commands, `publishDocumentTemplate` and `confirmDocumentTemplateLegalBasis`), the isolation suite (`suite.ts`, `channels/api.ts`, `channels/database.ts`, `isolation.test.ts`), `routes/screens/index.ts` and the two i18n catalogues (`doc.templates.*`).

## ISS-1 (issues and field work) open points
- ST-ISS-001 decision, where the SLA comes from: ISS keeps its own table `issue_sla_policy` (versioned, effective-dated, one row per category, severity and unit) and does NOT read the PRIORITY entry of MDM-18. A priority has no severity and no unit, it cannot be versioned per issue (BR-ISS-02: an issue keeps the version it was created under), and its `working_calendar` is only ALL_DAYS or WORKING_DAYS, not a calendar with holidays and make-up days. The policy follows the category record (`category_record_id`, not one version of it: a rename of the category must not orphan its policies), and `category_version_id` stays as the evidence of what the category was called. The `sla_priority_key` of an ISSUE_CATEGORY is therefore not read by ISS; if the business owner wants the priority as the starting values of the policy form (a suggestion the person confirms), it is a screen feature of ISS-09, not a second source of truth.
- ST-ISS-001 ISS-09 read: closed. `listIssueSlaPolicies` (CR-ISS-13) is in the contract and implemented (`IssSlaPolicyReadsController`, the container `IssueSlaPolicyScreen`, the route entry, the isolation lists); the browser flows are in `apps/web/e2e/iss-flows.spec.ts` and the visual region of ISS-09 (shell frame and title) in `visual.spec.ts`. The e2e stack seeds two issue categories, the published calendar WORKDAY_VN and the user `slaManager`.
- ST-ISS-001 working window: ISS.md (BR-ISS-01) counts 07:30-17:00 and ADM.md (BR-ADM-08) 08:00-12:00 and 13:00-17:00. The calendar tables carry no hours of the day, so ISS passes its own window (`ISS_WORKING_INTERVALS`) to the one function that counts working time (`addWorkingTime`, XM-ADM-01). The capital business owner must confirm the window; when the hours move into the calendar data, `ISS_WORKING_INTERVALS` is the only thing to remove.
- ST-ISS-001 the default policy values and the content of the working calendar WORKDAY_VN (holidays, make-up days) need the approval of the PO and the capital business owner (ISS.md section 12, 2 hours of review); no default policy is seeded.
- ST-ISS-001 the policy of a unit is matched on the unit of the issue exactly, then the default of the tenant (ISS.md 3.1 step 1); a policy written for a parent unit does not reach its children. Say so if the business wants the tree walked.
- ST-ISS-001 a policy cannot be longer than 2400 working hours and cannot begin in the past (a published version is never put in force for days that are gone); a correction of a wrong policy is a new revision from today. The database refuses a changed or deleted version (trigger `issue_sla_policy_guard`, no DELETE grant) and an overlap (exclusion constraint), whatever the application checked.
- ST-ISS-001 scope (review decision): ISS 2 lists `issue.sla.manage` for the SLA administrator on the TENANT scope, so `upsertIssueSlaPolicy` and `retireIssueSlaPolicy` need the permission through a grant on the whole tenant (403 `SCOPE_DENIED` for a subtree grant, like the ADM commands). A unit administrator who may write the policy of their own unit needs a rule on who may write for which unit; not defined.
- ST-ISS-002 (createIssue) must call `IssSlaDeadlines.calculate` in the same transaction and keep with the issue the policy id (`sla_policy_id`), `response_due_at`, `due_at` and the ids of the calendar versions the count used (`calendarVersionIds`); the issue table has no column for the calendar versions, so ISS-2 decides where they are kept (the `issue_event` payload is the natural place). Recounting an old deadline is a deliberate act, never a side effect of publishing a policy or a calendar.

## PRJ-1 (project register) open points and decisions

- ST-PRJ-001 (decision): `createProject` calls `MasterRules.suggestGroup` with the Vietnam date of the creation as the proposal date and keeps the answer (group, rule version id and revision, date) in `after_json.group_suggestion` of revision r1 INITIAL; the workspace read (`get_resources_project_id`) calls it again with the creation date (`proposed_on`) and the current amount and returns `group_suggestion` with `awaiting_confirmation` and `cxn`, which PRJ-02 (after the creation) and PRJ-03 show as the badge "Chờ ký" / "Awaiting confirmation". The group itself is not stored: it is decided by `decideProjectAppraisal` (PRJ-2), the column `project_group` stays null until then.
- ST-PRJ-001 (contract gap): PRJ-02 cannot preview the suggested group before the project is saved, because no operation takes an amount and a sector and answers the suggestion (a read such as `suggestProjectGroup` with `project.create`, or a `group_suggestion` member in the answer of `createProject`). The suggestion is shown from the read of the created project. The orchestrator owns the contract change.
- ST-PRJ-001 (decision): revision r1 INITIAL is written in status DRAFT (the CHECK `project_revision_decision_chk` lets APPROVED only with a decision document and an effective date, which a concept does not have). The story that decides the admission (`decideProjectAppraisal`) approves r1 with the policy decision document; a revision proposal (`proposeProjectRevision`) must count only IN_REVIEW/DRAFT revisions of the ADJUST_* kinds as pending, never r1 INITIAL.
- ST-PRJ-001 (decision): `updateProjectProfile` is allowlisted by the generated request schema (unknown fields are 400 FIELD_NOT_ALLOWED before the handler). Stage rules (STAGE_LOCKED_FIELD, 422): a closed, rejected or cancelled project takes no change; the dossier fields (investor, area, sector, project type, programme, start and end year) change only in CONCEPT, later only by a revision decided by the authority (the ADJUST_INVESTOR, ADJUST_SCOPE and ADJUST_SCHEDULE kinds of PRJ-2); the name and the classification stay open in every other stage. A request that changes nothing is recorded in the audit trail as `project.profile_unchanged` and writes no event and no new version (the command pipeline requires an audit row for every command).
- ST-PRJ-001 (decision): scope. A project is in scope when its owner unit or its investor unit lies in a scope of a grant that carries the permission, or a PROJECT-scoped grant names it (`projectScope` in `prj-project-store.ts`, built on `scopePredicate`). A project outside the scope of `project.read` is 404 on every path; one the caller can read but not update is 403 SCOPE_DENIED on the update; creating needs `project.create` on the owner unit (403 otherwise), and an owner unit that is not in the tenant is 404. The investor unit and the deciding authority only have to be active units of the tenant.
- ST-PRJ-001 (decision): the master versions of a project must be published, in force on the creation date, of the type of the field (area: AREA or ADMIN_UNIT, sector: SECTOR, project type: PROJECT_TYPE, programme: PROGRAM) and not retired or merged away: a version that is unknown or of another type or tenant is 422 VALIDATION_FAILED naming the field, one that is no longer in force is 422 MASTER_NOT_EFFECTIVE. `MASTER_REFERENCES` already lists the four project columns, so the impact preview and the usage list of an entry count the projects (tested).
- ST-PRJ-001 (decision): the amount is stored as whole dong: `1000` and `1000.00` are the same amount, a fraction or more than 24 digits is 422 VALIDATION_FAILED (`total_investment_vnd`). The amount never changes through the profile (BR-PRJ-13); the revision story (PRJ-6) is the only writer after creation.
