- ST-PRJ-001 (decision): V1200 adds the CHECKs on the trimmed code and name and on the year range, the CHECK that r1 INITIAL is revision 1, the trigger that keeps `project.code` for good (BR-PRJ-01), the defaults of `version` and `status_changed_at`, and `prj_legacy_project_status` (CR-PRJ-01 mapping Draft to CONCEPT, InReview to APPRAISAL, Approved to PIPELINE, Active to BUDGETED or IMPLEMENTATION, Closed to CLOSED; tested on synthetic values until D04 delivers data). `created_by` and `updated_by` of `project` stay nullable because many fixtures of other slices insert projects without them; the NOT NULL of the design (after a backfill) waits for the slice that owns those fixtures. No project exists before V1200, so no backfill of r1 was written.
- ST-PRJ-001: `prj_national_code_missing_gauge` is reported by the list read (first page only, per tenant, labelled with the tenant id) through the optional `PRJ_METRICS` sink; a scheduled job over all tenants would be the exact form, and needs a handler in `packages/db/src/jobs.ts` and the workers (shared files of the platform) once the exporter of KERNEL-050 exists.
- ST-PRJ-001: PRJ-01 filters (text, stage, missing national code) run on the loaded register: the contract of `get_resources_project` has no filter parameters (only `limit` and `cursor`), so the screen reads every page. A register of thousands of projects needs `status`, `q` and `missing_national_code` query parameters.
- ST-PRJ-001: the leader in charge of a line and of the workspace is read from `project_accountability` (ACTIVE `LEADER_IN_CHARGE`) and the display name from the `tenant_user_directory` view; the assignment commands belong to PRJ-17 (a later story), so the column reads "not assigned" until then. The tabs Mục tiêu, Kế hoạch, Giải ngân and Vướng mắc of PRJ-03 are a shell with a notice each: they fill when the revision, capital plan, disbursement and issue modules land.
- ST-PRJ-001 (test hygiene): the isolation negative control (b) used `get_resources_project_id` as its "uncovered id-bearing operation"; it now uses `get_resources_drill_context_id` (an operation nobody implements), because PRJ implements the project read. The isolation suite test got a 120 s timeout (it exceeded the 5 s default once the fixtures grew).
- ST-PRJ-001 (not mine, seen while running the shared suites): `apps/api/test/conformance/command-conformance.test.ts` expects 574 write operations and the contract now has 577; `upsertIssueSlaPolicy` answers 422 instead of 403/404 in the wrong-tenant matrix case.

## KERNEL-1 ST-KERNEL-072 (money-integrity harness) open points

- Scope (decision): the story has no operation, table or screen in the plan, so there is no controller. The kernel offers exact money helpers (`addDecimal`, `subtractDecimal`, `compareDecimal` in `decimal.ts`; `debitAmount`, `creditAmount`, `orderLockTargets`, `reverseEntry`, `reversalCancels`, `netOfEntries`, `entriesInForce` in `money-ledger.ts`) and a concurrency harness (`concurrency-harness.ts`); the db package offers the ledger registry (`ledger-registry.ts`), the ordered row lock and balance operations (`ledger-lock.ts`), the unique-claim translator (`unique-claim.ts`) and the ledger lint (`ledger-lint.ts`). Rules for the modules are in `docs/money-integrity.md`.
- Ledger lint (decision): a table named `*_ledger`, `*_ledger_entry` or `*_balance` must be declared in `LEDGER_TABLES`, and `ledgerReport` fails on a missing validated non-negative CHECK, business key, `(tenant_id, id)` lock key, duplicate lock rank, or an entry table the application role can UPDATE or DELETE. It runs as a db test on the migrated schema (`packages/db/test/ledger-lint.test.ts`), not as an npm script, because it needs the migrated database. The five existing ledger tables are declared (allocation_balance, cumulative_ledger, capital_ledger_entry, cumulative_ledger_entry, advance_ledger_entry).
- Migration V1001 (decision): adds `CHECK (>= 0)` to `advance_ledger_entry.outstanding_after_vnd` and `cumulative_ledger_entry.advance_outstanding_after_vnd`, the only amount columns of the declared ledgers without one. CAP and PAY: any new ledger table needs its declaration (next free lock rank) in `ledger-registry.ts` in the same change, or the lint fails.
- Spend semantics (decision): the reference spend `debitBalance` works on one declared non-negative column of a BALANCE table (the test uses `allocation_balance.allocated_vnd`); the CAP rules that combine columns (available = allocated - reserved - committed - disbursed) stay in CAP and call these helpers, with the table CHECKs as the last guard. The double-claim helper `withUniqueClaim` is exercised on the real `payment_request_active_acceptance_uq`; PAY should use it for its submit command so the loser answers 409 `ACCEPTANCE_ALREADY_CLAIMED`.
- Test seeding (decision): the concurrency suite seeds balances and payment requests with the privileged role and `session_replication_role = replica` (foreign keys off), because building the contract, project and acceptance parents would test the parents, not the ledger.
- Not done: no HTTP-level concurrent-command test (there is no spend operation in the plan yet; CAP and PAY add theirs with `runLoops` and `assertOneWinner`); the exact response is 409 `BALANCE_INSUFFICIENT` or `VERSION_CONFLICT`, as in AC-ST-KERNEL-072-1.

## KERNEL-1 ST-KERNEL-070 (idempotent file-import framework, kit K6) open points

- Scope (decision): the story has no operation, table or screen in the plan, so there is no controller. The kernel offers `planFileImport` and the format types (`packages/kernel/src/file-import.ts`, pure); the db package offers `FileImportStore.run` (claim the file, quarantine, claim rows, apply, open items; `totals`, `quarantined`, `list`, `find`) and `FileImportItemStore` (`list`, `resolve`, `reassign`), and V1002 adds `file_import`, `file_import_row`, `file_import_quarantine`, `file_import_reconciliation_item`. How a module plugs a format in: `docs/file-import.md`. No concrete layout is built (the Treasury layout waits for the anonymised samples, R-PAY-04).
- Reference adapter: the reference implementation is the test adapter in `packages/db/test/file-import-store.test.ts` (CSV, project check in `examine`, a write in `apply`, an `AMOUNT_MISMATCH` difference with an owner). PAY, DIS, CAP and BID copy that shape.
- Reading of AC-ST-KERNEL-070-1 (decision): "a file of 120 rows, 117 load, 2 quarantined, 1 amount mismatch" is read as 120 lines of CSV with the header, so 119 data rows: 117 loaded (the mismatching row is one of them, with its item) and 2 quarantined (UAT-NFR-OPERABILITY-06: "117 dòng nhận, 2 quarantine, 1 hạng mục đối soát"). A row with a difference is applied and has an open item; it is not held back. If PAY wants a mismatching amount held out of the ledger until the item is resolved, `examine` can refuse it with its own code, or `apply` can write it with a status that the module's totals leave out; the core counts it as applied.
- Idempotency (decision): a file is identified per tenant and connector by the SHA-256 of its bytes (`file_import_file_uq`, unique unless the try FAILED); a row by tenant, connector and business key (`file_import_row_key_uq`) with a row hash of its normalised fields. Same key and same hash is a repeat (skipped, counted); same key and another hash is a conflict (the original is untouched, one `DUPLICATE_CONFLICT` item per connector and key, joined by later conflicts). A correction of an already applied row is therefore a person's decision on the item, not an overwrite. The file claim is an `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so of two concurrent imports one waits for the other and answers `ALREADY_IMPORTED`; a rolled back winner lets the waiting one proceed (tested).
- One transaction per file (decision, limit): a file is judged and applied in one tenant transaction, at most 5,000 rows (the limit of `readTabularFile`), so a defect leaves nothing behind and the same file can be tried again. This is the same limit as ST-MDM-006. A larger file needs a chunked variant (stage the plan, then apply in chunks with a resumable cursor on `file_import`); not built, because the plan's 120-row scenarios and the Treasury daily statement fit, and chunking changes the all-or-nothing guarantee. Rows are claimed in the order of their keys so two files with the same keys in opposite orders do not deadlock (tested).
- Owner and due date (decision): every item is created with `owner_membership_id` (NOT NULL, foreign key to `membership`) and `due_at`. `ownerFor` of the adapter names the officer; an owner who is not an active member falls back to the `fallbackOwnerId` of the run, which must be an active member (422 `owner_membership_id` otherwise). Default time to settle is 3 days (`DEFAULT_ITEM_DUE_SECONDS`); the run and each difference can set another. A handover (`reassign`) keeps the due time and needs the version. The rule that chooses the owner (project officer, treasury reconciliation officer) belongs to each module; the PAY rule needs the business owner's confirmation.
- Not done, needs the owners: (a) who may start an import, read the quarantine, resolve or hand over an item: the permissions, scopes and audit rows are the controllers' (PAY-13 and the like); the stores only record the people; (b) the notification to an owner when an item opens (the outbox event `file_import.difference_opened` carries the item id, owner and due time; NOT subscribes), and the escalation of an overdue item (PAY's `reconciliation_item.issue_id` creates an ISS issue; a scheduler job over `file_import_reconciliation_item` with `dueBefore = now` is not written, nor is its alert rule); (c) retention of the four tables: they hold business keys, hashes and masked previews, no personal data by design, and nothing deletes them; the ADM retention rule for them is not defined; (d) the Temporal workflow of NFR-OPERABILITY-06 ("Workflow import trên Temporal") is not built; `FileImportStore.run` is one transaction and can be the body of an activity; (e) no operation of the contract reads `file_import`, so there is no import report screen; the data is there (`find`, `list`, `quarantined`, `totals`).
- PAY-006 and the other consumers (decision to confirm): PAY's `treasury_import`, `treasury_import_row` and `reconciliation_item` (V002) are PAY's tables, tied to a project and a contract. They can stay as the module's own record (written in `apply`, with `import_id` from the context pointing at `file_import`), or PAY can drop `treasury_import` for `file_import` and keep only `treasury_transaction`. The framework is built so that the second is cheaper; the first loses nothing. PAY's `reconciliation_item` (project, contract, amount columns, `EXPLAINED` and `WRITTEN_OFF_APPROVED` states with approval) is richer than the generic item and stays PAY's; its adapter can create it from the `Difference` it returns, or use the generic item and link it.
- MDM bulk import (decision, not refactored): `master-import-store.ts` (636 lines, ST-MDM-006) is left as it is. Its unit is a reviewed batch, not a file: stage, dry run, ATOMIC or PARTIAL mode, change requests with four-eyes, publication of the batch as one, a hash check at stage and at apply. None of those is a file-import concern, and it keeps its own `file_sha256` on `master_import_batch` and its row errors in `errors_json`. Putting it on the core would mean either quarantining rows that the batch screens already show, or changing the ATOMIC guarantee (the core claims a key once and never replays it), so the work is larger than the duplication it removes and it would reopen a reviewed, tested slice. What is already shared: the tabular reader (`readTabularFile`, lines and XLSX dates), which the core's `sourceRowsOfTable` also uses, and the file hash. Revisit when MDM-24 connector files (`NATIONAL_PIM_FILE`, `BUDGET_CODE_FILE`) get a real feed: those are plain file imports and use the core.
- Cursor (decision): `cursorScope` is opt-in. With it the connector must be `ACTIVE`, a read file sets `last_file_sha256` and `last_success_at`, an unreadable file sets the connector `FAILED` with `last_error_code` (only the reset of MDM-24 starts it again, as MDM-24 defines) and the cursor row is locked so imports of one connector take turns. A tenant without that row is not touched.
- Shared files touched with small edits: `packages/kernel/src/index.ts`, `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `packages/db/test/retained-history-grants.test.ts` (the four tables), this file. Two db suites outside this story were red when run (`rls-catalog.test.ts`: `project_stage_change` has a non-standard policy set and `issue`, `outgoing_document_event`, `project_stage_change` have `_id` columns without a `(tenant_id, column)` index); none of the four new tables is in those reports.

## ADM-1 ST-ADM-011 (delegation-of-authority matrix with resolver) open points

- Overlap in a draft (decision): `doa_rule` has an EXCLUDE constraint on the overlap of bands of one document type and unit, so a draft with an overlap cannot be stored. `createDoaMatrixDraft` and `updateDoaMatrixDraft` therefore answer 422 `DOA_MATRIX_INVALID` with `OVERLAP` where the overlap is written; `submitDoaMatrix` answers `GAP`, `NOT_FROM_ZERO`, `NOT_TO_INFINITY` (and `OVERLAP` again as a fence). A reading of UAT-AC127 that expects the overlapping draft to be accepted and refused only on submission would need the EXCLUDE constraint moved from the table to the submission (it is the designed second fence in the schema, so it is kept).
- Four eyes beyond the author (decision, deepest governance): the table keeps `created_by` only. Migration V1301 adds `last_edited_by` (set by create, update and submit) and a CHECK that the approver is neither; the store checks both before the database does. A colleague who edited someone else's draft can therefore not approve it. BR-ADM-01 says "other than the maker"; whoever changed the content made it.
- A matrix without a rule (decision): `NO_RULES` blocks a submission and an approval, since an approved matrix with nothing in it would leave every document without an approver. The request schema already asks for at least one rule.
- Start date (decision): a version must start after the one in force (`NOT_AFTER_CURRENT`, 422 at the approval). Approving it ends the old one on the new first day (`effective_until = least(old end, new start)`), as ADM 3.6 says, so the days between are covered by exactly one version and a past day is still judged by the version that covered it. A version dated between two published ones (back-dating, or inserting) is not supported; it needs a rule for what happens to approvals already pinned to the later version.
- Rule order: V1301 adds `doa_rule.position` (the row of the rule in the draft). The findings of a submission and of an approval name rules by that position (`rules[3].amount_min_vnd`), the row the administrator sees; reads return the rules in that order.
- Permission scope (decision): the matrix is tenant administration, so all four commands need the permission on the whole tenant (like the security policy); a grant on a subtree is `SCOPE_DENIED`. ADM.md names TENANT_ADMIN and UNIT_ADMIN "in the subtree" for the screen as a whole; rules for a subtree only would need a scoped write (who may write a rule for a unit) that the story does not define.
- Resolver (decision): `DoaMatrixStore.resolve` is the in-process contract for the approval engine (ST-ADM-012); there is no HTTP operation for it in `openapi.json`, and the screen previews with a client copy of the rule held to the same vectors. Documented in `docs/adm-doa-resolver.md`.
- `CXN-DOA-01`: the thresholds are the tenant's; the story is not DONE for the business owner until the pilot province regulation is entered as the first published version.
- Telemetry (TK-ADM-011-6): the commands are covered by the request telemetry of the API (tenant id, no personal data; the audit rows carry the revision, the dates and the counts, never the amounts). The failure mode that matters (HOME warns on `DOA_RULE_NOT_FOUND`) belongs to the caller of the resolver; an alert for "a document type has no approver" is the preview of ADM-15 for now.

## PMT-1 (permits) open points

- ST-PMT-001 (decision, storage): the permit type catalogue is a PMT-owned table (`permit_type`, as the ERD, the contract and `permit.permit_type_id` need), not master types with `rule: true`. Master data would give four-eyes publication, but `publishPermitType` / `retirePermitType` are single-step commands, a permit and a gate rule point at a `permit_type` row by composite foreign key, and the contract's own errors (`PERMIT_TYPE_OVERLAP`, `PERMIT_TYPE_IN_USE`) belong to the table. What is reused from the rule-data pattern: a mandatory legal basis, effective dating with immutable versions (guard trigger `permit_type_guard`, exclusion constraint `permit_type_no_overlap`, no delete for the runtime role) and the working-assumption flag: `cxn_ref` is the confirmation a version waits for (the `awaiting_confirmation` / `cxn` of RETENTION_POLICY), and confirming is publishing the next revision without it. The dates use the new kernel helpers `packages/kernel/src/effective-period.ts`.
- ST-PMT-001 (governance gap, deliberately not closed): publishing legal data has no second person (the actor is audited with the legal basis; there is no maker-checker as in master data or the glossary). A four-eyes publication needs new operations (propose, decide) in the contract; if the business owner wants it, `PmtPermitTypeStore.publish` stays the last step and a proposal table is added in front of it.
- ST-PMT-001 (decision, dates): `effective_until` is the LAST day the version applies (inclusive), unlike master data and ISS where the end is exclusive: the design speaks of permits "valid until 31 December" and of a type ended on 2026-12-31 with the next revision from 2027-01-01 (UAT-AC371). `permit_type_dates_chk` keeps `effective_from < effective_until`, so a version spans at least two days.
- ST-PMT-001 (decision, revisions): a code's first revision may begin on any day (the regulation may already be in force); the next revision must begin after the last one began and after the last day of the one before (else 409 `PERMIT_TYPE_OVERLAP`), may leave a gap after a closed predecessor, ends an open-ended predecessor the day before it begins (status RETIRED, same transaction) and may not begin in days that are gone (422 `effective_from`). A permit keeps the id of the version it was written under; nothing is rewritten.
- ST-PMT-001 (decision, retiring): `retirePermitType` only moves the end earlier (a later end is 422), not into days that are gone, and asking for the end the version already has is a 200 that changes nothing (same version, no event; the audit row is still written, because every command is audited, with `changed: false`). `PERMIT_TYPE_IN_USE` means a permit of the version in PLANNED, SUBMITTED, IN_APPRAISAL or SUPPLEMENT_REQUESTED whose `expected_decision_on` is empty or after the new last day; issued permits never block it and are never touched.
- ST-PMT-001 (working assumption, `applies_to_rule`): the design names "project group, kind of works, scale" without a shape. The stored shape is a closed allowlist: `project_group_keys` (PROJECT_GROUP master keys), `work_types` (free keys, no catalogue yet), `min_total_investment_vnd` / `max_total_investment_vnd` (whole VND digits, inclusive); `{}` applies to every project; an unknown key is 422. ST-PMT-002 (`createPermitDossier`, which evaluates the rule on the day of creation) must read exactly these keys, or the shape is changed here first.
- ST-PMT-001 (working assumption awaiting CXN-EXE-03 / CXN-EXE-02): the starting catalogue (`PERMIT_TYPE_WORKING_ASSUMPTIONS`, eight types of PMT 5) has the names and categories of the design only. Everything the design documents do not give is left empty or marked: no default validity or processing time (PMT 3 names the fields but no numbers), the legal basis of LAND_ALLOCATION, TRAFFIC_CONNECTION and WATER_DISCHARGE says in words that no source exists, every type carries `cxn_ref` `CXN-EXE-03` (`CXN-EXE-02` for BUILDING_PERMIT, whose exemption follows the new construction law), the first day `2026-01-01` is a placeholder shared by all, and the authority is "authority named by the regulation (unconfirmed)". ST-PMT-002 decides what `createPermitDossier` does with a type that still has a `cxn_ref` (PMT 3.1 says it is flagged, PMT 7 lists `PERMIT_TYPE_UNCONFIRMED`); the catalogue gives it the fact (`cxn_ref` not null) and nothing more. The screen already says "awaiting CXN-..." on every such version and counts them in a banner. The story is not DONE for the legal owner until each type is confirmed by publishing its next revision without `cxn_ref` (review `legal-rule`, 2-3 hours).
- ST-PMT-001 (seed, wired): `PmtPermitTypeStore.seedWorkingAssumptions` runs for every tenant in the `master-baseline-sync` job (every 6 hours, `apps/api/src/workers/workers.ts`, in its own transaction after the master baseline): a tenant gets the starting catalogue once, a code that exists in any status is skipped, so a type the tenant confirmed or ended is never given back. Every type it gives leaves the audit row and the `pmt.permit_type.published.v1` event of a publication (actor: the job membership). It is the only per-tenant default hook that exists; a dedicated provisioning step would give the catalogue earlier than the first run of the job (up to 6 hours after the tenant is created).
- ST-PMT-001 (scope and permissions, decision): the two commands need `permit.catalogue.manage` through a grant on the WHOLE tenant (`holdsOnWholeTenant`, PMT 2: "Chủ nghiệp vụ / pháp chế tenant, scope TENANT"); a grant on a unit subtree is 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`. The two reads (`get_resources_permit_type`, `get_resources_permit_type_id`, CR-PMT-01) need `permit.read`, which the policy guard checks from the contract on the operation; the catalogue is legal data of the tenant, not of a unit or a project, so a `permit.read` grant of any scope reads all versions (an auditor or a project officer sees what the counsel publishes). The legal counsel role therefore needs BOTH permissions (manage does not imply read: a counsel without `permit.read` could publish but not see the catalogue; the screen then shows the denied state). No system role holds a `permit.*` permission today except TENANT_OWNER (`permit.*`): the tenant defines a custom role for the counsel, or the role seeds of ADM/IAM gain one.
- ST-PMT-001 (events): publishing a revision emits `pmt.permit_type.published.v1` with `superseded_permit_type_id`; the end given to the predecessor by that publication does not emit its own `pmt.permit_type.retired.v1` (consumers read the superseding id). The retired event is emitted only for an explicit `retirePermitType` that changed something.
- Telemetry (TK-PMT-001-*): the commands are covered by the request telemetry of the API (tenant id, no personal data; the audit rows carry the code, the revision, the legal basis and the dates). The failure mode that matters (a project needing a permit type with no version in force on its date) belongs to ST-PMT-002, which evaluates the catalogue.

## BID-1 (procurement) open points

- ST-BID-002 (decision, identity verification is a port): `ContractorIdentityPort` (`apps/api/src/modules/bid/identity-port.ts`) looks a tax id up in an official register and answers MATCH, NOT_FOUND, NAME_MISMATCH or UNAVAILABLE, with the status of its own adapter (VERIFIED, PROPOSED, MOCK, MISSING, as in `integration_cursor.adapter_status` and the signing sessions). Bound by default: `UnboundIdentityPort` (MISSING, always UNAVAILABLE); `MockIdentityPort` (MOCK) is for tests and the development stack. `verifyContractor` rests on a person, an evidence document and a second person (maker-checker); the lookup only adds to that. The stored `verification_basis` is `EVIDENCE_DOCUMENT` unless the answer was MATCH through an adapter whose status is VERIFIED (`EVIDENCE_DOCUMENT_AND_LOOKUP`); the table CHECK `contractor_basis_chk` refuses that basis for any other adapter, so a tax id can never be recorded as confirmed by a lookup that its adapter did not stand behind. What every lookup said is kept beside it (`lookup_adapter_status`, `lookup_outcome`, `lookup_checked_at`) and shown on BID-02. An adapter whose status is VERIFIED that answers NOT_FOUND or NAME_MISMATCH stops the verification (409 `TAX_ID_INVALID`, nothing written); any other adapter, or a register that cannot be reached, never stops it. Waiting for D05-D07: a real adapter must be bound in `bidModule` (provider `CONTRACTOR_IDENTITY_PORT`), with its own timeout, because the lookup runs inside the command transaction.
- ST-BID-002 (decision, personal data): the registry holds the tax id, the legal name, the legal form and the registration documents of an organisation, nothing about representatives or staff (`additionalProperties: false` in the contract; the web form says so). A tax id may be the personal tax id of a sole trader, so: it is covered (`010****567`, the four middle digits of the ten) for every reader without `bid.contractor.read` over the whole tenant and for every guest contractor; the documents, the people who decided and the decision and the note of a restriction are left out of the same readers' answers; audit rows and events carry the covered tax id (events carry no name). The list has no filter on the tax id (the contract has `cursor` and `limit` only), so a covered reader cannot confirm a full tax id by asking. A guest contractor sees only the contractor of their invitation (404 for any other); the system role GUEST_CONTRACTOR holds `contract.read` only, so a guest reads the registry only through a custom guest role that adds `contractor.read`.
- ST-BID-002 (decision, tax id format): ten digits, or ten, a hyphen and three for a branch (a branch written without its hyphen is normalised). The check digit is NOT tested: the rule is not confirmed (needs the tax authority's rule, CXN) and the tax id of the acceptance example (0101234567) does not satisfy the usual algorithm. `TAX_ID_INVALID` is 409 in `error_codes.json` (status basis: convention), as the contract has it, although 422 would be the natural status.
- ST-BID-002 (decision, master data link): `registerContractor` links `master_record_id` to the live master entry of type `CONTRACTOR` whose business key is the tax id (the tenant's before the platform's), or to the entry named in the request when it is a live CONTRACTOR entry with the same key; a name never links anything. MDM does not define a `CONTRACTOR` master type yet (`master-types.ts` has `CONTRACTOR_CRITERION` only), so for now no entry exists and `master_record_id` stays null; the link is tried again at the verification. Needs the MDM owner: the type definition (key = tax id, label = legal name) and the creation of the entry when a contractor is first registered.
- ST-BID-002 (decision, duplicates): two UNVERIFIED claims of one tax id may exist (the unique index is partial on VERIFIED); registering a third after one is VERIFIED is 422 `CONTRACTOR_ALREADY_VERIFIED`; verifying the second claim is 409 `TAX_ID_DUPLICATE_VERIFIED`, which answers with the problem extension `merge_suggestion { target: 'MDM', action: 'MERGE', verified_contractor_id, verified_master_record_id, contractor_id, master_record_id }` (no tax id in it). Nothing is merged and no change request is created in MDM: the failed command rolls back, and MDM-37 (succession) is where a person records the merge; the screen links to it. A decision is final (trigger `contractor_identity_guard`): a verified contractor stays verified and the tax id never changes, a mistaken registration is answered by a new one and the merge in MDM.
- ST-BID-002 (decision, restrictions): a restriction in force on the day (Vietnam date) blocks, whatever its kind: the contract says "còn hiệu lực → chặn" for DEBARMENT, SUSPENSION and WARNING alike. Whether a WARNING should block is a question for the procurement owner (it is one `where` clause in `BidContractorStore.restrictionsInForce`). `effective_until` is the LAST day the restriction applies (inclusive; the generated CHECK `effective_until > effective_from` is replaced by `>=`, so a one-day restriction can be written). Restrictions are legal records: the trigger `contractor_restriction_guard` refuses any rewrite except the end date and the note, and the runtime role cannot delete. Contract gaps (need the orchestrator): there is no operation to bring the end of a restriction forward or to lift a wrong one (the table allows it, a command `endContractorRestriction` with a decision document is missing), and the source `NATIONAL_E_PROCUREMENT_IMPORT` is written by `importProcurementFile` (another story), not by `recordContractorRestriction` (always source DECISION on a decision document that is EFFECTIVE, else 422 `DECISION_DOCUMENT_NOT_EFFECTIVE`).
- ST-BID-002 (hand-over to the stories that take a contractor in): `BidContractorStore.assertMayTakePart(tx, contractorId, on?)` is what `receiveProposal` (ST-BID proposals) and `submitProcurementResult` must call first: 422 `CONTRACTOR_RESTRICTED` (with the restriction in the problem extension) before 422 `CONTRACTOR_NOT_VERIFIED`. The second half of AC-ST-BID-002-1 (the 422 on the proposal and on the result) is proven on this guard in `packages/db/test/bid-contractor-store.test.ts` and in `apps/api/test/bid/contractor-registry.test.ts`; the HTTP proof on `POST /proposals` and `POST /procurement-results` belongs to the stories that add those endpoints.
- ST-BID-002 (decision, events): `bid.contractor.registered.v1`, `bid.contractor.verified.v1` (only for a VERIFIED decision; the contract names no event for a rejection, which is in the audit trail) and `bid.contractor.restricted.v1` (event key = the restriction, not the contractor's version). No event carries a tax id or a name.
- ST-BID-002 (decision, scope): the registry belongs to no unit or project: `bid.contractor.manage` and `bid.contractor.verify` count only through a grant on the WHOLE tenant (403 `SCOPE_DENIED` otherwise); `contractor.read` (the permission of the scaffold GETs) lets staff read every contractor, covered unless `bid.contractor.read` is held over the whole tenant.
- ST-BID-002 (not done, later releases): BID-01 has no column "Năng lực" and BID-02 no performance history or scorecard (they come from CTR in R2, TK-BID-002-3); BID-21 (the list of all restrictions) is another story. Server-side filters on the list (status, restricted) would need `status` and `restricted` query parameters in the contract; the screen filters the pages it has read.
- ST-BID-002 (telemetry, TK-BID-002-*): the commands are covered by the request telemetry of the API (tenant id, no personal data); the failure modes that matter are a lookup adapter that stays UNAVAILABLE (the verification then rests on the document only, which every contractor row shows) and a restriction decision that never reaches EFFECTIVE (422 `DECISION_DOCUMENT_NOT_EFFECTIVE`).

### ST-CAP-001 (appropriation register by origin, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-001 (decision, table): `appropriation` came from the generated schema (V002); `V1360__cap1_appropriation_register.sql` adds what the register relies on: REJECTED in the status CHECK, amount above zero, a trimmed authority of 2 to 300 characters, years 1990 to 2100, source year = plan year unless CARRYOVER, a revision chain (revision 1 has no `supersedes_id`, a later one has), the approver required once the row leaves DRAFT, foreign keys of both documents to `document`, a unique decision line `(document, fund source version, program, year, origin, level)` and one live successor per appropriation, a BEFORE UPDATE guard (content is never edited, REJECTED and SUPERSEDED are final, the state machine, the approver is final) and no DELETE for the application role.
- ST-CAP-001 (decision, allocated total): `allocated_total_vnd` can change only inside a transaction that raised `app.cap_allocation_write = 'on'` (`set_config(..., true)`) and only on a PUBLISHED row. `CapAppropriationStore.decide` raises it for the one case of this story (a revision inherits the allocated total of the appropriation it replaces); the allocation command of ST-CAP-002 must do the same, lock the appropriation rows `FOR UPDATE` in the order of their ids, and never write the column otherwise. No API body can carry it (the allowlist answers 400 `FIELD_NOT_ALLOWED`).
- ST-CAP-001 (decision, revision): a revision is a new DRAFT row with `supersedes_id`, `revision + 1`, the same fund source ENTRY (a newer version of it is fine), program entry, plan year, source year, origin and level. It is refused below the allocated total at registration (nothing is written, 422 `APPROPRIATION_BELOW_ALLOCATED`) and again at the decision under the row locks. Publishing it supersedes the old row, moves the `allocation_line` rows and the allocated total to the revision, and posts to `capital_ledger_entry`: a REVERSAL of the old APPROPRIATE (`reversal_of_id`) and a new APPROPRIATE, so the net of the appropriation entries is always the amount in force. The first publication posts one APPROPRIATE. Reconcile (`capital.balance-reconcile`) only PUBLISHED rows: a SUPERSEDED row keeps the allocated total it had when it was replaced, as history.
- ST-CAP-001 (decision, decisions): `decideAppropriation` approve publishes at once (DRAFT to PUBLISHED, the APPROVED state of the schema stays unused); reject ends the draft (new status REJECTED, frees the decision line); `return` has no meaning for a draft that cannot be edited and is 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` (`decision`). The registrant never decides (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`, also a DB CHECK). The master data (fund source, program) and the documents are judged again at the decision.
- ST-CAP-001 (decision, rules): the fund source must be a FUND_SOURCE entry in force today that adds to the others: a `relation = SUBSET_OF` source (CTMTQG) is refused with 422 `SUBSET_SOURCE_NOT_ADDITIVE` (it is part of its parent; its amount is tracked on the allocation line, `ctmtqg_subset_vnd`, BR-CAP-03). A decision document must exist in the tenant and have an `effect_status` of PENDING, EFFECTIVE or CORRECTED (else 422 `DECISION_REQUIRED`; a missing `decision_document_id` is the same code through `FIELD_PROBLEMS`). A CARRYOVER needs its extension document (422 `EXTENSION_DOCUMENT_REQUIRED`), `extended_to` (not before 1 January of the plan year) and a source year before the plan year; the carry-over fields on any other origin are 422. A plan year whose `fiscal_year_control` row is LOCKED takes no capital (409 `FISCAL_YEAR_LOCKED`; ST-CAP-008 owns the table). New code `APPROPRIATION_DUPLICATE` (409) registered in `error_codes.json` for the unique decision line; a second live revision is 409 `REVISION_PENDING_EXISTS`.
- ST-CAP-001 (decision, money): amounts are strings of whole VND (`requirePositiveAmount` with scale 0, at most 24 digits, nothing is rounded); sums and remaining amounts are computed by PostgreSQL `numeric` or by BigInt in the web, never by a float. `appropriation` is not a declared ledger table (it is no `*_balance`); the ledger declarations of `capital_ledger_entry` (V002) are used as they are. The web types an amount in millions of dong and sends exact VND; only the decimal mark of the language is accepted (a point is a grouping mark in Vietnamese, so "10.000" is refused instead of being read as ten), and the line "Sẽ ghi N đồng" shows the exact dong before sending.
- ST-CAP-001 (decision, scope): the register belongs to no unit: `capital.appropriation.manage`, `capital.appropriation.approve` and `capital.read` are judged as tenant permissions (the contract says `tenant`). The list returns DRAFT and PUBLISHED rows (the register in force and what awaits a decision), newest first; the decision document is shown by its symbol and date, never its title.
- ST-CAP-001 (screens): CAP-16 is the book (filters, pinned fund and year columns, millions with the exact dong on the cell, Con lai, totals of the published lines, register, revise, approve and reject with a reason of at least 5 characters); CAP-04 is the tree category / fund source / origin of the published lines with the validity and the ODA on-lending, CTMTQG explained and never added; CAP-14 shows CENTRAL_SUPPORT and REVENUE_INCREASE as groups of their own. HOME-07 (the split on the home screen) belongs to ST-HOME and reads the same list.
- ST-CAP-001 (open, contract): `listAppropriations` has no `status` parameter, so the superseded and rejected rows cannot be listed (the history of a revision chain is in the audit trail only); a `status` filter on the operation is a change for the orchestrator. `listAppropriations` could also return `as_of`/a summary by origin, which the web computes today.
- ST-CAP-001 (open, policy): which origins count in the "Prime Minister assigned" denominator (CENTRAL_SUPPORT, REVENUE_INCREASE) is tenant policy waiting for CXN-03 and is not implemented here; the origin is stored, the inclusion is configuration (ST-CAP-017). The decision document is entered as its identifier (the document register has no picker yet).

### ST-CAP-002 (plan drafting with allocation lines under the appropriation ceiling, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, migration): `V1362__cap1_capital_plan_drafting.sql` lets a DRAFT version of a project have no decision and no effective date (`plan_version.decision_id` and `effective_at` lose NOT NULL; a PUBLISHED or SUPERSEDED one still needs both), backfills the legacy shape (below), and adds: well-formed code and years and the document FK on `capital_plan`; a guard trigger on `capital_plan` (code, kind, years, unit, revision and `supersedes_id` are never changed, REJECTED and SUPERSEDED are final, the state machine of CAP.md section 4 is the only way a status moves); guard triggers on `plan_version` (project, plan, year and revision are fixed; only a DRAFT version is deleted) and `allocation_line` (a line is edited or deleted only while its version is a DRAFT; the one exception is the move of `appropriation_id` that ST-CAP-001 does when a revision of the appropriation is published); CHECKs that a line which draws on an appropriation is complete (project, budget level, origin, audit columns) and that its origin and source year are known; the FK of `extension_document_id`; `REVOKE DELETE, TRUNCATE` on `capital_plan` and `REVOKE TRUNCATE` on `plan_version` and `allocation_line` (a draft line is removed by the command and the guard). `capital_plan` is listed in `retained-history-grants.test.ts`.
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, NOT NULL not applied): the plan says "backfill then SET NOT NULL" for `plan_version.capital_plan_id`, `created_by`, `updated_by` and `allocation_line.appropriation_id`, `budget_level`, `origin`, `created_by`, `updated_by`. It is not done: fixtures of other modules (PRJ `project-stage.test.ts`, `prj-stage-store.test.ts`; CTR `contract-register.test.ts`, `ctr-contract-store.test.ts`, `support/ctr-fixtures.ts`; the authz matrix) still insert plan versions and lines in the legacy shape, and this slice may not edit them. The drafting command always writes the complete row and `allocation_line_complete_chk` makes a line that names an appropriation complete. Once those fixtures write the complete shape: `ALTER ... SET NOT NULL` on the columns above (expand then contract; a legacy line has no appropriation to give, so those rows must be given one or removed first).
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, backfill): the migration runs as the owner role, to which FORCE row-level security applies, so a plain UPDATE would silently touch no row; the backfill is a DO block that sets `app.tenant_id` for each row of `machvon.tenant` in turn. Each legacy (fiscal year, decision, revision) becomes one ANNUAL capital plan (`LEGACY-<year>-<decision id>`) at the root unit of the tenant, its versions are linked to it and the rows that had no author name the system (`00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000000`); the id of a plan is the md5 of its key so the INSERT and the UPDATE agree without a work table. A decision that is no document of the tenant cannot make a plan PUBLISHED or SUPERSEDED (it is APPROVED), versions of one key in different states make a DRAFT, and among PUBLISHED revisions of one decision only the newest stays PUBLISHED. A tenant without an organisation unit is left as it was. The appropriation, budget level and origin of a legacy line are not invented (D04 supplies the documents); `allocation_line.project_id` is taken from the version. Tested by `packages/db/test/cap-plan-migration.test.ts` (it migrates to V1361, writes the old shape, applies V1362). It is rehearsed on synthetic data only until D04.
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, reads: what `plan_version` means): the table `plan_version` is the version of one project in one plan, but the scaffold reads `get_resources_plan_version[_id]` say "only PUBLISHED and SUPERSEDED for people who do not draft; DRAFT only for the author and the approver", which is the visibility of a plan REVISION. They answer the plan revisions (CAP-01 lists them; the by-id read adds the projects with lines in the scope of the caller). The version of a project is not addressable by its own id. A revision in work is shown to the people who hold `capital.plan.draft`, `capital.plan.submit`, `capital.plan.approve` or `plan.publish` on any scope, to nobody else (404 by id). The totals of a plan and its lines are those of the projects in the scope of the caller (owner unit, investor unit or the project itself, `projectScope`): a project outside the scope does not exist for the caller (reads are filtered, writes are 404).
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, drafting): `draftCapitalAllocation` is an upsert by the key (project, appropriation, bucket, source year) plus `remove_line_ids`, all or nothing. Order of refusals: shape 422, plan 404, `VERSION_CONFLICT` 409 (with `safe_diff`: status, `updated_at`, the number of lines and their total in the scope of the caller, never a line of another unit), `PLAN_NOT_DRAFT` 409, locked plan year 409, project 404 outside the write scope or `PROJECT_STAGE_INVALID` 422 (PIPELINE, BUDGETED, IMPLEMENTATION, SUSPENDED only; removal is allowed in any stage), the appropriation (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` when it does not exist or its year is not in the plan, new code `APPROPRIATION_NOT_PUBLISHED` 422 when it is not PUBLISHED), locked year of the appropriation 409, then the ceiling `APPROPRIATION_EXCEEDED` 422 with `available_vnd` and `appropriation_id` as problem members. A request that changes nothing writes nothing and keeps the version (audit `capital.plan_allocation_unchanged`). The line takes budget level, origin, fund source and the carry-over facts from the appropriation; `source_year` must be the appropriation's; carry-over capital goes in the CARRYOVER bucket and nothing else does; an annual plan draws on the appropriations of its year, a medium-term plan on those of the years of its period (both kinds count against the same ceiling; BR-CAP-09 warning is not here); a line is for a positive amount and its CTMTQG part is 0 to the amount (`SUBSET_EXCEEDS_PARENT`). A program on a line must be a PROGRAM in force and, when the appropriation names one, the same program.
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, ceiling and locks): in one transaction the plan row is locked first, then the appropriation rows `FOR UPDATE` in the order of their ids (a revision of an appropriation published meanwhile moves the lines: the removed lines are read again under the lock and a mismatch is a `VERSION_CONFLICT`, never a write to the wrong source). The allocated total moves with the line, written under `app.cap_allocation_write` as ST-CAP-001 asked, and the allocation is never written by anything else. The story that publishes a plan (ST-CAP-003), revises it (ST-CAP-004), extends or reallocates must lock in the same order (plan, then appropriations by id) or two commands can deadlock. `CapPlanStore.allocatedDrift` is the check of BR-CAP-02 ("the allocated total is the sum of the lines of the live plans: DRAFT, IN_REVIEW, APPROVED, PUBLISHED"); the tests assert it is empty after every race, and ST-CAP-006's reconciliation can use it. A DRAFT revision that copies the lines of a PUBLISHED one (ST-CAP-004) would count them twice under that definition: ST-CAP-004 must count only the difference.
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, concurrency evidence): two planners on 800 million of the last 1 billion, 30 loops (`runLoops` and `assertOneWinner`), five planners on 300 million each of 1 billion (exactly three win), two plans that name the same two appropriations in opposite order (no deadlock), two savers of one plan version (one `VERSION_CONFLICT`), a double creation of one plan code (one `PLAN_CODE_DUPLICATE`, through a savepoint, never a raw 25P02), and the same race through the HTTP API.
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, authority): `createCapitalPlan` needs `capital.plan.draft` on a grant whose scope contains the plan's unit (403 `SCOPE_DENIED`, 404 for a unit that does not exist in the tenant); the plan of a unit above a planner's scope is therefore not theirs to create, but the plan header is readable by every holder of the read permission and the lines are cut by project scope. `draftCapitalAllocation` is judged line by line by the project, not by the plan's unit (AC-ST-CAP-002-5: lines of U-BQL2 are 404 for a planner of U-BQL1 on a plan that holds both).
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, event): `capital.plan_published.v1` is built by `apps/api/src/modules/cap/plan-events.ts` (`planPublishedEvent`) from `CapPlanStore.publicationFacts` (revision, year, `effective_at`, decision document, the projects with a line of more than nothing) with the payload CAP.md section 9 and the PRJ consumer read. The operation that publishes a plan (`post_plans_id_publish`) is ST-CAP-003: it must call `publicationFacts` and `scope.emit(planPublishedEvent(...))` in its own transaction. `apps/api/test/cap/plan-published-event.test.ts` drives the real chain: plan and lines through the drafting command, the event into the real outbox, the real relay, the real `PlanPublishedConsumer`, and checks the projects become BUDGETED once with `actor` the system, a second delivery changes nothing, and an unpublished plan gives no event.
- ST-CAP-002 (decision, observability): `cap_allocation_conflict_total{code}` counts `VERSION_CONFLICT`, `APPROPRIATION_EXCEEDED` and `PLAN_NOT_DRAFT` refusals and the gauge `cap_draft_latency_ms{outcome}` holds the duration of the last draft (the sink has no histogram). `cap_publish_latency_ms` belongs to the publication story. Each saved draft has one audit row (`capital.plan_allocation_drafted`) whose diff lists every line (added, changed, removed with the old and new amounts and the CTMTQG part) and where each ceiling stood before and after; the optional `note` of the body is its reason. No event is emitted by a draft (the contract names none).
- ST-CAP-002 (web): CAP-01 (list, default filter medium-term; creating a plan), CAP-02 (a proposal: project, appropriation with what it has left, bucket, amount in millions with the exact dong, CTMTQG part; the remaining ceiling "after" follows the typing and the server's `available_vnd` is shown when another planner won), CAP-03 (annual plan table with the columns of CAP.md section 8, total without the CTMTQG part), CAP-06 (grid: editable amounts, live remaining ceiling per source, committed and reserved once published, the AVC threshold column says "not set" until ST-CAP-005, removal of lines). A locked year is learned from the refusal (`FISCAL_YEAR_LOCKED`) and turns the screen's writes off with a banner; no screen reads `fiscal_year_control` (ST-CAP-008). The picker of CAP-02 needs `project.read`, the unit choice of CAP-01 `organization_unit.read` (the screens say so when the read is refused).
- ST-CAP-002 (open, contract, for the orchestrator): (CR-CAP-01 made `lines` `minItems: 0` and added `APPROPRIATION_NOT_PUBLISHED` to the error codes of `draftCapitalAllocation`: the grid now sends an empty `lines` with the ids to remove and may remove every line of a plan; the store needs at least one of the two.) (2) `get_resources_plan_version` takes `plan_kind`, `status` and `plan_year` filters the contract does not declare (the screens read all revisions and filter in the browser). (3) The answers of `listCapitalPlanLines`, `get_resources_plan_version[_id]` and `get_resources_allocation_line[_id]` are `type: object`; the shapes are in `apps/api/src/modules/cap/plan-view.ts` (suggested schemas: CapPlanRevisionView, CapPlanLineView, CapPlanLinesPage with `plan`, `items`, `totals`, `next_cursor`, `as_of`). (4) There is no read of one plan by id apart from the scaffold; `listCapitalPlans` was not needed.
- ST-CAP-002 (not done here): SET NOT NULL (above); the AVC threshold and the reserved amount before publication (ST-CAP-005, ST-CAP-011); `fiscal_year_control` SOFT_CLOSED is not judged (only LOCKED refuses, as in ST-CAP-001); BR-CAP-09 (medium-term ceiling warning) and the `previous_line_id` rules of a revision (ST-CAP-004 copies lines; here the id is only checked to be a line of the same project); the submit, decide and publish commands (ST-CAP-003).

### ST-CAP-003 (plan approval, publication with ledger and balances, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-003 (decision, migration): `V1363__cap1_plan_approval_publication.sql` adds `submitted_at`, `decided_at`, `decision_reason`, `published_by` to `capital_plan` (a returned plan carries the reason to its planner), replaces the guard of `capital_plan` (each move needs the people it needs: IN_REVIEW a submitter, APPROVED a submitter and an approver, PUBLISHED an approver and a stamp; the approval and the publication are never edited), a CHECK that `ALLOCATE`/`REDUCE` entries name a line and a project, and `REVOKE DELETE, TRUNCATE` on `allocation_balance` (`allocation_balance` and `capital_ledger_entry` added to `retained-history-grants.test.ts`). No guard trigger on `allocation_balance.allocated_vnd`: the money harness tests of KERNEL-072 and the fixtures of PRJ write it directly, so the single writer is kept by the commands and checked by `balanceDrift`.
- ST-CAP-003 (decision, state): the `plan_version` rows follow the plan (IN_REVIEW, APPROVED, PUBLISHED, SUPERSEDED, REJECTED, back to DRAFT on a return), so the existing guard of the lines (editable only while the version is DRAFT) protects an IN_REVIEW or APPROVED plan in the database too.
- ST-CAP-003 (decision, four eyes): the person who created the plan and the one who submitted it never decide it, whatever the decision and the state (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED` is judged before the state, so an over-privileged planner is refused too), and never publish it; the approver may publish. Decision, state and unit are judged in this order: shape 422, plan 404, unit 403 `SCOPE_DENIED` (the grant that holds the permission must reach `scope_unit_id`; a sub-unit grant is refused), version 409, four eyes 403, state 409 (`PLAN_NOT_DRAFT`, `STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED` for a decision on a plan not in review, `PLAN_NOT_APPROVED`). The first version routes by one approver permission; the DoA matrix by value waits for CXN-DOA-01 (ADM resolver not wired here).
- ST-CAP-003 (decision, publication): one transaction (see `docs/capital-balances.md`): supersede, publish, balances created or moved to the new line (same row id, so committed and disbursed survive a revision), `ALLOCATE`/`REDUCE` for the difference, active reservations follow the balance, outbox `capital.plan_published.v1` once, drift check inside the transaction. A line is the same line by project + appropriation + bucket + source year (not by `previous_line_id`). A dropped line is reduced to nothing and keeps its balance row at 0; a dropped line with a commitment is `REVISED_BELOW_COMMITTED`. `effective_at` may not be earlier than the revision it replaces (BR-CAP-10). The national code rule (CXN-01) is applied to the funded projects that are still in PIPELINE (the ones this publication makes BUDGETED); WARN lets them through and lists them in the audit row.
- ST-CAP-003 (decision, allocated total): publication never writes `allocated_total_vnd`; a revision counts only its difference to the revision it replaces (the rule ST-CAP-002 asked ST-CAP-004 to follow). REJECT gives back the difference (the whole plan for a first revision), a RETURN keeps the ceiling. ST-CAP-004 must follow `docs/capital-balances.md` (revision contract) or publication refuses (`PLAN_CHANGED_SINCE_PROPOSAL`).
- ST-CAP-003 (decision, workflow): the approval as a workflow (`capitalPlanApprovalWorkflow`, AC-ST-CAP-003-6) runs the same steps as the commands (`CapitalPlanApprovalService`) inside activities under the signed tenant context; it is exported for `AppModule` `orchestration.definitions` and is not registered by default. The Temporal adapter (DONE_PENDING_ENV) replaces the in-process engine behind the same port; the commands stay the synchronous authority.
- ST-CAP-003 (decision, reads): `listCapitalPlanLines` gains `disbursed_vnd` (the impact view needs committed + disbursed to show the floor) and the plan reads gain `submitted_at`, `decided_at` and `decision_reason` (shown only for revisions in work, to the circle that already sees them). Not exposed: who submitted and approved (the directory read of people is not in the contract).
- ST-CAP-003 (observability): `cap_publish_latency_ms{outcome=first|revision}` and `cap_balance_drift_vnd` (0 after every publication; the total of the disagreement when the transaction is rolled back by `BalanceDriftError`); audit rows `capital.plan_submitted`, `capital.plan_decided` (reason, released ceilings) and `capital.plan_published` (totals before and after, entry ids, superseded plan). The decision reason is in the audit trail and on the plan view, never in an event.
- ST-CAP-003 (web): CAP-10 (`PlanApprovalScreen`: impact view against the revision in force with the floor of committed + disbursed, submit, approve/return/reject with a reason, publish with the decision document id and a moment; refusals explained, `REVISED_BELOW_COMMITTED` lists the lines) and CAP-05 (`DecisionDocumentsScreen`: one row per decision document and what it backs, appropriation or plan; a replaced plan stays listed with "Kế hoạch đã bị thay thế"). The status label SUPERSEDED reads "Kế hoạch đã bị thay thế".
- ST-CAP-003 (open, contract, for the orchestrator): (1) the decision document is entered as its identifier (no document picker yet, as in ST-CAP-001); (2) `post_plans_id_publish` answers `DECISION_REQUIRED` for a missing `decision_document_id` through `request-validation.ts` (`FIELD_PROBLEMS`), the contract could mark the field as required with that code; (3) no read exposes the balance of a line (reserved/committed/disbursed per line is only in `listCapitalPlanLines`); (4) the answers of the plan reads are still `type: object` (see ST-CAP-002).
- ST-CAP-003 (not done here): the revision command and the copy of lines (ST-CAP-004), the reservation, AVC and commitments (ST-CAP-005, ST-CAP-011), the nightly reconcile job and its incident (ST-CAP-006 calls `balanceDrift`), year close and reopen (ST-CAP-008), the DoA matrix by value, the Temporal adapter, an e-mail/notification for the approvers when a plan is submitted (the event exists).

### ST-CAP-004 (plan revision, as-of lookup, plan tracking, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-004 (decision, revision): `createCapitalPlanRevision` (`CapPlanRevisionStore`) copies the plan in force into a DRAFT revision (`supersedes_id` = the plan in force, revision = max + 1, `previous_line_id` on each line) in one transaction; it locks only the source plan, so it cannot deadlock with the commands that lock the plan and then the appropriations. The reason (10 to 2,000 characters) is written to the audit row `capital.plan_revision_created`. The permission is `capital.plan.draft` on the unit of the plan; a caller whose grant covers only a subtree below the unit of the plan gets 403 (the revision is the plan's, not a project's). The details are in `docs/capital-balances.md`.
- ST-CAP-004 (decision, as-of): `getCapitalPlanAsOf` answers the revision of the plan code in force at `at`. A date-only `at` means the end of that day in Vietnam (UTC+7); an instant is taken as given; both are validated (422 on `at`). The disbursed amount comes from the ledger as of the moment, not from the running balance, so a past report can be explained. The answer is scoped like the plan lines; a plan of another tenant and a moment before the first revision are both 404.
- ST-CAP-004 (web): CAP-07 (`PlanAdjustmentScreen`: ask for a revision with a reason, see the pending revision instead of a second button, and the capital a revision moves, "Nguồn giảm" beside "Nguồn tăng" with exact totals and the basis of each side) and CAP-15 (`PlanTrackingScreen`: decision, project, initial, adjustment, current and disbursed with "Xem tại ngày"; the plan is named by any revision of its code). The lines of a revision are edited on CAP-06.
- ST-CAP-004 (open, contract, for the orchestrator): (1) `CapPlanAsOf` declares `lines` as untyped objects and not `plan_code`, `totals`, `truncated`, and the lines' `initial_vnd`, `adjustment_vnd`, `as_of_disbursed_vnd`; the screens read them defensively, a typed schema would let the generator check them; (2) a tracking read that spans plans (all plans in force on a date) would need its own operation; (3) CAP-07 shows the transfer of one revision against its base, the proposals of ST-CAP-007 (`TRANSFER_UNBALANCED`, reallocation lines) will reuse `transferOf` in `revision-model.ts`.
- ST-CAP-004 (not done here): the `reallocation_line` transfer proposal and its approval (ST-CAP-007), the AVC and reservation (ST-CAP-005), the year close that supersedes the plan (ST-CAP-008), an e-mail to the approvers when a revision is made (the audit row exists).

### ST-CAP-009 (monthly disbursement registration, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-009 (decision, migration): `V1364__cap1_disbursement_schedule.sql` puts into `disbursement_schedule_line` what the command relies on: one current row for each key (project, year, grain, month or period, bucket, budget level, kind) and one row for each revision (unique indexes, `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`), a registration is monthly and a weekly commitment weekly, a forecast carries its assumption, the month or the period goes with the grain only, revision 1 has no predecessor, and a row is never edited except that the revision that replaces it takes `is_current` from it (guard trigger); the runtime role cannot delete or truncate the rows (`retained-history-grants.test.ts`).
- ST-CAP-009 (decision, command): `registerDisbursementSchedule` (`CapScheduleStore.register`) is judged in this order: shape 422, the project in the scope of `capital.schedule.register` 404 (outside it, or of another tenant, it does not exist), its stage 422 `PROJECT_STAGE_INVALID` (PIPELINE to SUSPENDED, as the plan drafting), the fiscal year 409 `FISCAL_YEAR_LOCKED`, the allocation line (a line of that project) 422, the period 409 `PERIOD_LOCKED` (a weekly row names its period; a monthly row stands for the MONTH period of that month when the calendar has one; an approved reopening that covers the project lets the registration through, as for the reports). One advisory lock per key orders the revisions; a registration of an unchanged figure is still a revision (the idempotency key covers a replayed request). It writes nothing to the ledger or the balances (BR-CAP-08), audit `capital.schedule_registered` carries the amount before and after, the outbox event `capital.schedule_registered.v1` once.
- ST-CAP-009 (decision, achievement): the paid of a month is the sum of the DISBURSE entries of the capital ledger (less those a reversal undid) on the project, the plan year, the month by Vietnam time, the bucket of the line and the budget level of the appropriation of the registration; the rate is the paid over the registered, exact to four decimals half up (BigInt), and absent when nothing is registered (the screen says "Chưa có đăng ký"). Month 13 is the adjustment period, taken as January of the next year [assumption, CXN-31]; payments of later months of that year belong to no registration. Until the payment stories post DISBURSE entries (the Treasury import of ST-PAY-006 reconciles, it does not post), the paid is 0.
- ST-CAP-009 (contract, done by the orchestrator): new read `listDisbursementSchedules` (GET /disbursement-schedules, `capital.read`, project scope, current row of each key or the whole history, filters project, year, kind, grain, month, keyset pages); a row of `CAP.md` section 8; the story lists it. `registerDisbursementSchedule` is as the contract had it.
- ST-CAP-009 (web): CAP-08 (`DisbursementScheduleScreen`: the thirteen months by source group "Vốn năm / Vốn kéo dài / Vốn khác" with the paid and the rate, the form that registers a month and offers the figure already registered, refusals explained). Forecasts (CAP-12, CAP-13) and the weekly commitments (DIS-24) use the same command and store and have their own stories.
- ST-CAP-009 (open, contract, for the orchestrator): (1) the answer of `listDisbursementSchedules` is an untyped object like the other reads of the capital; a `CapScheduleList` schema would let the generator check the screens; (2) `revision_reason` of `CapScheduleInput` is optional and goes to the audit row, the contract could require it from the second revision of a key; (3) the registration made from a weekly report (DIS-05, `source_report_revision_id`) has no entry point yet.
- ST-CAP-009 (not done here): CAP-12 and CAP-13 (forecast screens), DIS-24 (weekly commitments against the Treasury per week), the registration from the weekly report (DIS-05), an alert when a month's achievement is far below the registration.

### ST-ADM-019 (shared catalogue entry point)
- ST-ADM-019 (decision): ADM-05 (`SharedCatalogueScreen`, `SharedCatalogueWorkspace`) reads the master records of the tenant (`searchMasterRecords`) and shows type, code, name (the alias the tenant set, else the label), validity, namespace and version, with a card per type that links to the MDM screen that holds it (`catalogueRoute`, from the catalogue registry of MDM, or the hub filtered on the type) and a link to the detail of each entry. ADM has no command here: every change is an MDM command with its impact preview and approval. The permission is the one of the master data read (`master.read`): a person without it gets the denied state and nothing is requested, so there is no half-linked screen. No operation was added to the contract (the story lists none).
- ST-ADM-019 (not done here): a count of the entries waiting for a decision per type (the MDM hub shows it), a link from the administration menu groups (navigation owns them).

### ST-BID-003 (evaluation criteria, slice BID-1)
- ST-BID-003 (decision, versions): V1344 lets `criteria_version` begin as a draft (no approver, no effective time), adds the checks (a draft has neither approver nor time, an approved version has both, the approver is never the maker), the package foreign key, one draft and one approved version per package and a guard trigger: a version keeps its package, revision, criteria, legal basis, weights and maker for good (a change is a new revision), moves DRAFT to APPROVED or SUPERSEDED and APPROVED to SUPERSEDED only, is born with weights that add up to 100 (or no weighted criterion) and is never deleted (`criteria_version` joins the tables the runtime role cannot delete from). The next revision of a package replaces the draft in work (SUPERSEDED, never approved) and replaces the version in force only when it is approved, in the same transaction as the approval.
- ST-BID-003 (decision, criteria): `criteria_json` is `{criteria: [{code, name, kind SCORED|PASS_FAIL, weight, max_score, threshold, mandatory, required_evidence[]}]}`, read by `readCriteria` (the shape, 422 VALIDATION_FAILED with the path of the member, 400 FIELD_NOT_ALLOWED for a member that is not part of a criterion; then the criteria nobody may ask for; then the weights): the weights of the SCORED criteria are whole numbers that add up to exactly 100 (422 CRITERIA_WEIGHT_INVALID with `weights_sum`), a set with no SCORED criterion has no weights (`weights_sum` null). The contract keeps `criteria_json` an open object, so the shape is judged in code, as the items of the packages of a plan are.
- ST-BID-003 (decision, prohibited criteria): the list of what may not be asked (gender, ethnicity, religion, marital status, age, place of birth or residence, being a local person or business) is `PROHIBITED_RULES` in `bid-criteria-rules.ts`, each rule with its terms and its legal basis, matched on whole words without regard to case or accents; the refusal (422 PROHIBITED_CRITERION) names the criterion, the rule, its category and the legal basis, and saves nothing. The approval judges the list again, so a draft made before a rule was added cannot be approved. The basis names the principle of Luật Đấu thầu 22/2023/QH15 and NĐ 214/2025/NĐ-CP (fair competition, no restriction of participation); the article of each rule is for the legal department to confirm [CXN-BID-01]. Open: a text that states a principle ("không phân biệt giới tính") also falls under the rule because the match is on the words, not on the sense; moving the list into a tenant-managed master type with versions is the follow-up if legal wants to change it without a release.
- ST-BID-003 (decision, approval): `approveCriteriaVersion` refuses in order the version (404 outside the scope of the project of the package), VERSION_CONFLICT, the maker (403 SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED), a version that is not a draft (409 CRITERIA_LOCKED_BY_RUN when an analysis run used it, else the new 409 CRITERIA_STATE_INVALID), the package (409 PACKAGE_STATE_INVALID once its plan is no longer approved) and the criteria once more (PROHIBITED_CRITERION). Criteria are made for a package whose plan is approved (`PLANNED`) or that was awarded outside a plan; packages in DRAFT, IN_REVIEW or CANCELLED are refused (409 PACKAGE_STATE_INVALID with `package_status`).
- ST-BID-003 (contract): added the filters `package_id` and `status` to `get_resources_criteria_version` (scaffold list, `criteria_version.read`; a draft is read by those who hold `bid.criteria.manage` or `bid.criteria.approve`, anywhere, and the version in force and the ones it replaced by every reader of the scope) and the code CRITERIA_STATE_INVALID to the approval; events `bid.criteria.drafted.v1` and `bid.criteria.approved.v1` carry ids, the revision and the figures, never the text of a criterion. The detail read answers the same line as the list.
- ST-BID-003 (web): BID-04 (`CriteriaScreen`): the package picker, the versions with their state in words and the sum of their weights, the criteria of the version shown (the one in force by default), the approval of a draft with the refusals in words, and the editor with the running total of the weights, the start from the version shown and the refusal that names the criterion and the legal basis.
- ST-BID-003 (not done here): AC-ST-BID-003-2 is proved at the approval (a version an analysis run used answers CRITERIA_LOCKED_BY_RUN and the revision is the only way forward) and by the database (a version never changes its criteria); the run that takes a criteria version (`post_analysis_runs`, ST-BID-007) must refuse a version that is not APPROVED, and `compareProposals` the versions that differ (BR-BID-11, CRITERIA_VERSION_MISMATCH); the screen shows the figures of a version, not a score sheet (the scoring is the analysis).

### ST-BID-004 (sealed proposals and the declaration of conflict of interest, slice BID-1)
- ST-BID-004 (decision, the opening unseals): `proposal.access_released_at` is the effective `BID_OPENING` of the package (actual date, else planned) copied at the receipt and kept in step by a trigger on `procurement_milestone`, so a postponed opening keeps the sealed proposals sealed; a package with no opening takes no proposal (422 `BID_OPENING_UNSET`, fail closed), and none is received once the opening has come, whatever time the clerk gives. A proposal that was released is never sealed again: a later opening is 409 `OPENING_ALREADY_RELEASED` (`recordProcurementMilestone`, and a row error of the national file; the trigger is the last word).
- ST-BID-004 (decision, who sees what): one pure function in the kernel (`proposalView`) decides FULL / METADATA / DECLARATION_REQUIRED / HIDDEN from the status, the release, the reach of the caller over the project (`bid.evaluate`, `bid.proposal.receive`) and the standing of their declaration for the contractor of the proposal. HIDDEN is 404 (the same as a proposal that does not exist), so a member cannot learn that a bid was received; DECLARATION_REQUIRED is 403 `CONFLICT_UNDECLARED` on the detail and a line without price in the list. The clerk gets the receipt at all times and never the price or a file; replaced proposals are for the clerk only.
- ST-BID-004 (decision, the conflict): the spec table has one declaration per member per package and no contractor, but UAT-AC450 recuses a member from NT-1 only, so `bid_conflict_declaration.contractor_id` (nullable) was added. A conflict that is not decided yet already shuts the member out of what it covers (fail closed); RECUSED covers the named contractor (every contractor when none is named); BLOCKED covers the whole package; ALLOWED opens it. The decision can be taken again under the new version; the audit rows keep each one. RECUSED and BLOCKED take the member out of `evaluation_report.signed_off_by` of the reports that are not issued (a signature is a membership id or an object with `membership_id`) and send a fully signed report back to DRAFT; the sign-off command (a later story) must check `conflictStanding` for every contractor before it signs.
- ST-BID-004 (decision, the files): the files are PDFs through the shared upload door (`uploadProposalFile`, 25 MiB) and are named by key and digest; the receipt reads each again, checks the digest, takes a `document` upload only, and refuses a file that already stands for a document. Each becomes a CONFIDENTIAL `ATTACHMENT` document (type `BID-PROPOSAL-FILE`, subject `BID_PROPOSAL` + the id of the proposal), which the reads of the DOC module leave out (`SEALED_SUBJECT_MODULE`): their scope is a unit and says nothing about a bid. `document_version_ids` (baseline jsonb) is filled with the same ids and never returned.
- ST-BID-004 (contract): added `uploadProposalFile` (POST /proposals/files, `bid.proposal.receive`) and `listConflictDeclarations` (GET /procurement-packages/{id}/conflict-declarations, `bid.read`: whoever holds `bid.conflict.decide` over the project sees every declaration, a member their own; 404 outside the scope); `BidProposalReceive.documents` now has an item shape (`BidProposalFile`, listed in `packages/contracts/compat-allow.json` as ST-BID-004: the operation was a scaffold with no client); `BidConflictDeclare.contractor_id`; the scaffold read `get_resources_proposal` takes `package_id`; error codes `BID_OPENING_UNSET`, `NO_CONFLICT_DECLARED`, `OPENING_ALREADY_RELEASED` added to the registry.
- ST-BID-004 (kernel): `momentOf` accepted `2026-02-30T10:00:00Z` and `24:00` because the date reader rolls them over; it now rejects a moment that does not exist (this also tightens the milestone and the file import).
- ST-BID-004 (web): BID-05 (`ProposalScreen`: the package, the opening and whether the proposals are sealed, the proposals with what the caller may see and why, the intake dialog with one upload per file, the conflict panel with the declaration dialog for a member and the decision dialog for whoever decides); 141 i18n keys; users `proposalClerk`, `proposalEvaluator`, `proposalEvaluatorTwo`, `proposalAuthority` (790 to 793) for the browser flows.
- ST-BID-004 (assumption, open): the evaluation team of a package is whoever holds `bid.evaluate` over its project (no team table yet); a member cannot amend a declaration of no conflict (`ALREADY_DECLARED`, as the spec says); the independent authorization review the story requires was not run (no subagents were allowed) and the same goes for the migration review of V1346.
### ST-CTR-004 (review, signing and activation of a contract, slice CTR-1)
- ST-CTR-004 (decision, one graph): the statuses and the commands live in one table in the kernel (`CONTRACT_TRANSITIONS`); `cancelContractDraft` uses it too, so the draft store and the lifecycle store cannot disagree. The generic PATCH/PUT of a contract does not move the status (404 on the generic routes, tested for the 9 statuses × 5 commands).
- ST-CTR-004 (decision, 409 vs 422): a command from the wrong status is 409 `CONTRACT_STATE_INVALID`; a blocked permit gate is 409 `PERMIT_GATE_BLOCKED` (the state of another record), a missing guarantee, a failed commitment and a stage of the project that is not IMPLEMENTATION are 422, since the caller can fix them from the same screen. A refusal of the capital is 422 `FUND_COMMIT_FAILED` with the `cause` it gave (`NO_ALLOCATION_LINE` when the package has no line).
- ST-CTR-004 (decision, delegation): the limit of the signature and of the activation is the DoA rule of document type `CTR_CONTRACT` in force for the value (`DoaMatrixStore.resolve`); the caller must hold the permission with a rank that reaches the approver role (403 `SCOPE_DENIED` + `approver_role_id` below the band; 422 `DOA_RULE_NOT_FOUND` when no rule exists: fail closed). `CTR_CONTRACT` is a new document type name, not yet in the MDM catalogue.
- ST-CTR-004 (decision, guarantee): a performance guarantee is required unless the category is CONSULTING or LAND_CLEARANCE (`GUARANTEE_EXEMPT_CATEGORIES`); the permit gate `CONTRACT_ACTIVATION` applies to CONSTRUCTION and EPC (`PERMIT_GATED_CATEGORIES`). Both lists are an assumption to be confirmed with legal counsel (CXN-EXE-02).
- ST-CTR-004 (decision, conflict): the signer in conflict is blocked whatever is on record; a reviewer in conflict is covered only by a signature that carries `conflict_decision_ref`. The block writes a denied audit row that survives the rollback. `blockingDeclaration` matches a declaration on the contractor, or on the package with no contractor or with decision BLOCKED.
- ST-CTR-004 (migration): V1384 adds the people and the signature to `contract`, CHECKs that tie them together, the trigger `guard_contract_signature` and the append-only status history. The trigger enforces only the immutability of the signature and of the activation, not the path of the status: the fixtures of earlier stories flip the status directly, and the path is the store's job (one graph in the kernel).
- ST-CTR-004 (contract): the 5 operations `submitContractForReview`, `returnContractDraft`, `recordContractSigning`, `post_contracts_id_activate`, `getContractSummary` have their `x-error-codes` completed; the summary is cached 60 seconds per contract and version. Contracts regenerated, compat clean.
- ST-CTR-004 (web): CTR-03 (`WorkspaceScreen`: the facts, the checks before the activation, the history and one dialog per command; a button only for what `actions` allows and not in a read-only session). Reached from the draft screen by a link.
- ST-CTR-004 (open, UAT matrix): `UAT-AC300` (9 statuses × 10 commands) is covered for the 5 commands this story implements; the other 5 commands belong to ST-CTR-005 and later.
- ST-CTR-004 (open, reviews): the authorization review and the migration review the story requires were not run (no subagents were allowed); the conflicts, delegation and two-person paths are covered by tests, not by an independent reader.

### ST-CTR-005 (suspend, resume and terminate a contract, slice CTR-1)
- ST-CTR-005 (decision, the decision document): a version of a file of the tenant that is not infected and not a sealed bid, as the waiver of a permit gate takes it (PMT-005); it is not required to be an issued document (`effect_status`), since a decision often arrives as a scanned paper. A request without one is 422 `DECISION_DOCUMENT_REQUIRED` (the request validation names that code for the missing field of the three operations, `FIELD_PROBLEMS`).
- ST-CTR-005 (decision, the proposer): the people who proposed a termination are whoever made the decision document, drafted the contract or sent it to review (`assertNotProposer`); the approver is also within the DoA delegation limit (`CTR_CONTRACT`) and not in conflict of interest. The suspension and the resumption ask for the permission and the scope only (`contract.suspend`), but are blocked for a conflict of interest too.
- ST-CTR-005 (decision, the guarantee): `guarantee_action` acts on the ACTIVE PERFORMANCE guarantees only; the ADVANCE guarantees stay (the advance still owed stays on the contract for the settlement, and the existing release rule would refuse to release them anyway). CALL with nothing to call is refused (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `reason: NO_ACTIVE_GUARANTEE`); RELEASE keeps the four-eyes rule of `releaseGuarantee`.
- ST-CTR-005 (decision, the extension): `extension_days` of a resumption is a request kept in the history and the event; `completion_due` never moves (it changes only through an approved extension revision, ST-CTR-006 and later).
- ST-CTR-005 (decision, the violation suggestion): a termination writes one `contractor_violation` (`OTHER`, `MAJOR`, penalty `TERMINATION`, amount 0, RECORDED, resting on the decision document, source CTR); the kind and the severity are an assumption for a person to correct when they confirm it. V1385 keeps it unique per contract.
- ST-CTR-005 (migration): V1385 adds `terminated_by` (not the drafter), makes the termination unchangeable and adds the unique index of the suggestion; the columns of the decision were already in V002.
- ST-CTR-005 (contract): none added; the `x-error-codes` of the three operations are completed. `getContractSummary` adds `suspend`, `resume`, `terminate` to `actions` (the schema of the summary lists no fields of `actions`).
- ST-CTR-005 (web): CTR-22 (`StatusScreen`: the contract, the actions that apply, the reason, the document, the day, the extension or the guarantee action, the effect on payments, the irreversible warning and a confirmation for the termination); the workspace CTR-03 links to it for whoever may take an action.
- ST-CTR-005 (open, new work): AC-1 expects a measurement recorded while SUSPENDED to be 409; `recordMeasurement` is not implemented yet, so the rule is the kernel function `acceptsNewWork` (ACTIVE only) and the story that implements the measurement must use it. The payment-matrix effects (BR-PAY-09) belong to PAY, as the story says.
- ST-CTR-005 (open, reviews): the authorization review the story requires was not run (no subagents were allowed); the proposer, the delegation, the conflict and the scope paths are covered by tests, not by an independent reader.

### ST-LND-009 (site handover by section and km to an active construction contract, slice LND-1)
- ST-LND-009 (decision, the area): `handed_area_m2` of `recordSectionHandover` is the whole area handed over by now, not the area of the step (UAT-AC386: "to 30.000"); it has to grow (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` on `handed_area_m2` when it does not) and cannot pass `required_area_m2` (422 `HANDED_AREA_EXCEEDS_REQUIRED`). The status follows the area (`sectionStatusFor`: nothing is NOT_READY, all of it is HANDED_OVER, between is PARTIAL) and V1682 ties the column to it.
