- ST-LND-009 (decision, the contract): only an ACTIVE contract of a group that builds (CONSTRUCTION and EPC, `receivesSite`) receives land: 409 `CONTRACT_STATE_INVALID` (with `contract_status`) for any other status; a section with no contract (`reason: NO_CONTRACT`) or one whose contract does not build (`reason: NOT_A_CONSTRUCTION_CONTRACT`) is 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` on `contract_id`. Once land is handed over the contract of the section cannot change (V1682).
- ST-LND-009 (decision, the parcels): the operation lists `PARCELS_NOT_READY` and the spec does not say when. Assumption, to be confirmed: part of the area may be handed over before the compensation is all paid, but the last of it (the section becomes HANDED_OVER) needs every parcel of the section PAID or HANDED_OVER (422 with `parcels_not_ready`), and it hands the paid parcels over with it (status HANDED_OVER, version + 1).
- ST-LND-009 (decision, the record): each handover names a clean document (`handover_document_version_id`) and a day that is not in the future and not before the last handover. The table keeps the last handover only (area, day, document: V1682 requires the three together or none); every step is in the audit row `land.section_handed_over` and in the event `lnd.section.handed_over.v1`, which the contracts and the quality module read to show the site as ready.
- ST-LND-009 (decision, the ratio): the share of a project handed over is the sum of the handed areas over the sum of the required areas, to two decimals, rounded half up and computed in whole hundredths (`handedRatio` in the kernel, `ratioText` in the web), never an average of the shares of the sections: 30.000 of 58.000 is 51,72 % (UAT-AC386).
- ST-LND-009 (contract): none added; the `x-error-codes` of `recordSectionHandover` are completed with `DOCUMENT_NOT_CLEAN`, `VALIDATION_FAILED` and `SCOPE_DENIED`. The permission is `land.handover` over the project of the section; a section of another tenant or one the caller cannot see is 404.
- ST-LND-009 (web): LND-10 (`SectionScreen`: the sections of a project with the kilometres, the area needed and handed over with its share, the contract that receives it, the target and the status, the totals, a handover dialog and the creation of a section, which now offers the contracts of the project to name the one that receives the land). The contract codes come from the contracts the caller can read; one who reads none sees the start of the identifier.
- ST-LND-009 (open, reviews): the authorization review the story requires was not run (no subagents were allowed); the scope, the contract-state and the area paths are covered by tests, not by an independent reader.

### ST-BID-006 (submit and approve the result of the selection, slice BID-1)
- ST-BID-006 (decision, the evaluation): the story says that in R1 the evaluation prerequisite is a DOC-issued evaluation minutes document and that the structured report arrives in ST-BID-012. `evaluation_report_id` of the request is therefore the id of a document in force (409 `REPORT_NOT_ISSUED` otherwise), stored in the new column `evaluation_minutes_document_id` (V1347) because the column `evaluation_report_id` is a foreign key to the table of the structured reports. ST-BID-012 will accept the id of a structured report as well.
- ST-BID-006 (decision, the DoA): the story names "DoA resolution". There is no DoA band for results in the baseline matrix and the acceptance cases configure none, so the limit applies only when the tenant configured a rule for the document type `BID_RESULT`; a tenant that did, is always limited (a band left out is 422 `DOA_RULE_NOT_FOUND`). Assumption to confirm: whether results should be limited by default like contracts. The check is the shared `assertWithinDelegation` (`apps/api/src/doa-delegation.ts`), which the contract lifecycle now uses too.
- ST-BID-006 (decision, the proposer): the approver is not the proposer for a rejection either (a person who submits a result does not decide it, one way or the other).
- ST-BID-006 (decision, the package): an approval gives the package the status of the outcome (`AWARDED`, `NO_ACCEPTABLE_BID`, `CANCELLED`); the contract register already reads an APPROVED, AWARDED result of the package. The statuses of a package are not constrained by the database (a text), so the two new values are an assumption of this story. A result that is rejected leaves the package `APPROVED`.
- ST-BID-006 (decision, no winner restriction on the proposals): the winner is not required to have handed in a proposal for the package (the story does not say so, and UAT-AC446 awards to a contractor whose proposal was refused); the restriction and the verification of the tax id are judged at the submission and again at the approval.
- ST-BID-006 (migration): V1347 adds `evaluation_minutes_document_id`, `submitted_at`, `rejected_by`, `rejected_at`, `decision_comment`, the checks of the outcome (an award has a winner and a price, the others have neither), of the basis (a submitted or rejected result rests on an evaluation), of the rejection, and of the approval day, the unique index of one open result per package, a guard that keeps a result as it was written (a decided result is frozen, the status moves only along the edges) and no delete for the runtime role. The checks of the basis and of the submitter leave out APPROVED rows, because five fixtures of earlier stories insert approved results directly.
- ST-BID-006 (contract): added `listProcurementResults` (GET /procurement-results, `bid.read`, query `package_id`) for BID-18; the `x-error-codes` of the two commands are completed. `BidResultSubmit.evaluation_report_id` says what it means in R1.
- ST-BID-006 (web): BID-18 (`ResultScreen`: the package, the results, a dialog to submit and a dialog to decide; the note that the decision is a person's and that nothing here comes from an AI). The winner is chosen among the contractors the caller can read (those restricted are listed: the server refuses them).
- ST-BID-006 (open, reviews): the money review and the authorization review the story requires were not run (no subagents were allowed); the price, the restriction, the proposer and the delegation paths are covered by tests, not by an independent reader.

### ST-SAS-014 (license renewal and history, slice SAS)
- ST-SAS-014 (decision, the window): `renewLicense` creates revision n+1 of the same serial that starts at the earlier of the moment of the renewal and the end of revision n (`renewalWindow`, kernel), so the term has no gap whether the renewal comes early (revision n+1 takes over at once; a Superseded revision is ignored by the entitlement gate, so a start in the future would have left the tenant unlicensed) or in the grace period (the days since the end are covered, which is the spec's "valid_from mới = valid_until cũ"). The request has no `valid_from` (the contract is unchanged); the new end must be later than the old end and than now, and the grace period cannot end before it.
- ST-SAS-014 (decision, what is kept): the new revision keeps the serial, edition, quota, binding and issuer, takes the seats and the entitlement version of the request (or those of revision n), and keeps the offline lease of a private deployment while it lasts and never past the new grace period. Revision n changes in nothing but its status (SUPERSEDED) and version, its signed claims stay as they were, and both signatures verify (V012 already grants the control role UPDATE only on status, reason and version). Only a live revision (Issued, Active, Grace, not past its grace) is renewed; Suspended is reinstated first, Revoked and Superseded are 409 `LICENSE_STATE_INVALID`. `license_revision_one_current_uq` is the last word on a race: the replaced revision steps aside first and two renewals at once give one winner.
- ST-SAS-014 (contract): none added; `renewLicense` now lists `LICENSE_TENANT_MISMATCH`, `SIGNING_KEY_UNAVAILABLE`, `VALIDATION_FAILED` and the event `sas.license.renewed.v1`. The audit row `license.renewed` carries the reason, the new window and the replaced revision, never the claims or a key.
- ST-SAS-014 (web): SAS-14 (`LicenseRenewScreen`: the current revision beside the revision that would replace it, the new end, grace and seats, a reason of 10 to 2000 characters, the version as If-Match) and SAS-16 (`LicenseHistoryScreen`: the chain of revisions of a tenant with state, window, what each replaced, the signature check, the revision in force, the propagation objective of 60 seconds and a renew link for the revision in force); SAS-13 links to both. AC-ST-SAS-014-2 is proved by the suspension tests that already existed (their titles now name it).
- ST-SAS-014 (assumption, open): the renewal cannot change the edition or the quota (a change of edition is a new license, SAS-12, once the old one is revoked); the independent authorization review the story requires was not run (no subagents were allowed).
### ST-BID-005 (milestones and the national bidding file, slice BID-1)
- ST-BID-005 (decision, the layout): the adapter `PROCUREMENT_FILE` (the connector the baseline already declares; source system `MUASAMCONG`, adapter PROPOSED) reads columns `package_code`, `external_code`, `milestone_kind`, `actual_at` and optionally `project_code`; the layout `WORKING-ASSUMPTION-2026.1` is a working assumption (no sample, D05) and is kept with every import. Two live packages with one code are told apart by `project_code`, else the row is `PACKAGE_AMBIGUOUS`; a `YYYY-MM-DD` or `DD/MM/YYYY` is the first instant of the day in Vietnam.
- ST-BID-005 (decision, what a row does): an import writes the actual date of a milestone only where the register has none (source IMPORT, the batch); a date the register holds is never overwritten: the same Vietnam day is in step, another day is `MILESTONE_DATE_MISMATCH` kept in `procurement_external_link.diff_json` with an owner (the planner who made the package while they are a member, else the importer), and a package linked to another code is never relinked (`EXTERNAL_CODE_MISMATCH`, nothing of the row written). V1345 adds the guards (source and batch together, a date on every milestone, applied batch immutable, a link with a difference has an owner, no delete).
- ST-BID-005 (decision, dry run): BR-BID-13 says the dry run writes nothing, so it has no `procurement_import_batch` row (the table keeps the DRY_RUN mode for a later use, constrained to never be APPLIED); `rows_ok` and `rows_error` of the answer are the counts of valid and refused rows.
- ST-BID-005 (decision, the version): `recordProcurementMilestone` has the package as its path resource, so its `If-Match` is the version of the package, which moves with every milestone written by hand or by a file (the package guard of V1343 lets the version follow). The story gives no other versioned resource.
- ST-BID-005 (decision, authority): as for the national code file, the apply needs `bid.import` on the whole tenant and a planner of a unit tree may dry-run for their packages. A package of a plan not in force (DRAFT, IN_REVIEW, SUPERSEDED, CANCELLED) takes no milestone (409 `PACKAGE_STATE_INVALID`; `PACKAGE_STATE_INVALID` as a row error).
- ST-BID-005 (contract): added `uploadProcurementFile` (POST /procurement-imports/files, `bid.import`), `listProcurementMilestones` and `listProcurementExternalLinks` (GET, `bid.read`) with the schemas `BidProcurementFileUpload`, `BidMilestoneLine`, `BidMilestoneList`, `BidExternalLink`, `BidExternalLinkList`, `mapping_version` on `BidImportRequest`, and the error codes the commands answer. BID.md lists them.
- ST-BID-005 (web): BID-17 (`MilestoneTrackerScreen`: the packages of the plans in force with their milestones in order, dates and source, the record dialog under the version of the package) and BID-19 (`ExternalLinksScreen` with `ProcurementImportPanel`: the proposal adapter note, upload, dry run row by row, apply, the links with the code, the batch hash, the difference and its owner). New e2e users `procurementPlanner`, `procurementReader`.
- ST-BID-005 (assumption, open): the day of reconciliation is the Vietnam calendar day (a file that carries a time is compared by its day); `MISSING_EXTERNAL` is never set by an import (a file is not a full list of the packages); the length of time an owner has to settle a difference is the framework default. The layout waits for the owner of the national system (D05, ST-BID-015).
- ST-BID-005 (not done here): the migration review the story requires was not run (no subagents were allowed); the real feed of the national system (ST-BID-015); a screen action to resolve the reconciliation item itself (the framework's item store is read by MDM-24).
### ST-LND-010 (progress of the stages of land clearance, tracked from outside, slice LND-1)
- ST-LND-010 (decision, what is stored): `land_stage_progress` (V002) holds one row per section, stage and plan (the unique index with NULLS NOT DISTINCT) and V1681 adds `blocker_note` (the contract field `blocker_note` had no column), `land_stage_done_parcels_chk` (a DONE stage has every parcel done), the order of the dates, `land_stage_external_evidence_chk` (a TRACKED_EXTERNAL stage with parcels done or an end carries an evidence document), the pair last_reported_by/at, a trigger that keeps the place of a stage (project, section, plan, stage code, mode; a stage lies in a section and a plan of its own project) and no delete for the runtime role. Rows of mode NATIVE are not written by anything: the progress of such a stage is the parcels themselves, counted by the overview at the moment of the read, and `reportLandStageProgress` answers 422 `NATIVE_STAGE_READONLY` for `mode` NATIVE and for a stored NATIVE row.
- ST-LND-010 (decision, status): `stageStatusOf` (kernel) gives DONE when there is an actual end, else LATE when the planned end is before the Vietnam date (the planned day itself is not late), else BLOCKED when a blocker note is sent, else IN_PROGRESS once a parcel is done or the work has begun, else NOT_STARTED. A report whose status is DONE needs every parcel done and an actual end not in the future; more parcels done than the total is 422 `PARCELS_DONE_EXCEEDS_TOTAL`; progress or an end without a clean evidence document is 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` (`evidence_document_version_id`), a document that is not CLEAN is 422 `DOCUMENT_NOT_CLEAN`.
- ST-LND-010 (decision, authorization, the review the story asks for): `land.progress.report` is judged over the project (a grant on the unit trees of its owner or investor, on the project or on the tenant): those people give a stage to its executor with the first report and may reassign it. The executing unit (a grant on its own unit tree, the centre outside the tree of the project) may report only on a stage that the project already gave it, and cannot hand it to another unit or take one nobody gave it (403 `SCOPE_DENIED`, decided after the stage is locked, so a centre cannot probe stages by their answers). The caller who reaches neither gets 403 before any lookup of sections, plans or documents. A project of another tenant is 404. The overview (`getLandProgress`, `land.read`) is read over the project; a project outside the scope is 404, never 403. The independent authorization review the story requires was not run (no subagents were allowed).
- ST-LND-010 (decision, the late stage and ISS): a stage becomes LATE once: in the report that takes it there, or in the job `land-stage-late` (hourly) that takes the stages with a planned end behind the Vietnam date. Each emits `lnd.stage.late.v1` (event key per version of the stage) in the common contract of the issue intake (ids, a reference code `<section code>-<stage code>`, the owner unit of the project; no note, no name). The intake spec `lnd.stage.late.v1` (severity MEDIUM, category LAND_CLEARANCE) opens one issue (BR-ISS-07 holds a second event off while that issue is open) and `IntakeSpec.link` writes `blocker_issue_id` of the stage in the same transaction. A stage that goes back from LATE (a new planned end) and becomes late again asks again; the stage keeps the latest issue.
- ST-LND-010 (contract): none changed. `getLandProgress` keeps its free-form response; its members are listed in docs/lnd-parcels.md and returned by `stage-progress.controller.ts` (counts of sections, parcels, parcels with a notice, parcels paid or handed over, households (a count, never a name), plans posted, payments paid, late stages, the area handed over against the area needed, parcels by status, the sections and the stages with the name of the executing unit).
- ST-LND-010 (web): LND-01 (`OverviewScreen`: tiles from the counts of the server, the late stages with their executor, the sections, the parcels by status) and LND-11 (`ExternalProgressScreen`: the stages with executor, mode, parcels done / total, progress, deadline and status, the evidence and the issue, and the report dialog that starts from the report of a stage). The page titles are the names of the screens in LND.md. New e2e users `progressOfficer`, `progressReader`.
- ST-LND-010 (assumption, open): the external centre reports through the API with its own `land.progress.report` grant on its unit tree; it cannot read the overview (that is over the project), so no screen is offered to it. If the officers want the centre to see its own stages, a read over the executor unit has to be added. The severity (MEDIUM) and the deadline of the issue come from the SLA policy of the category in each tenant. Data from external units may arrive late (R-LND-03): a late report is judged against its own date, and the stage is LATE until a report moves its planned end or finishes it.
- ST-LND-010 (not done here): the independent authorization review; `listUpcomingLandDeadlines`, which LND-11 lists in the screen specification, belongs to another story; the plan (`plan_id`) of a stage is accepted by the report and shown by the overview but the screens do not offer it yet (the plans are drafted by ST-LND-003).
### ST-CAP-008 (fiscal-year close, lapse and time-boxed reopen, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-008 (decision, the states): a year without a control row is open with version 1 (the first close creates the row; the screen sends If-Match "1"). `closeFiscalYear` takes an open year to SOFT_CLOSED (the adjustment period) or LOCKED, and a soft-closed year to LOCKED; a locked year or a year soft-closed twice is 409 `YEAR_STATE_INVALID` (`reason`: ALREADY_LOCKED, ALREADY_SOFT_CLOSED). V1367 holds the moves (OPEN to SOFT_CLOSED or LOCKED, SOFT_CLOSED to LOCKED, LOCKED to OPEN only by a reopening), forbids DELETE and the change of the year, and keeps the reopening (reason, approver, window) together.
- ST-CAP-008 (decision, what a soft-closed year takes): the story says "SOFT_CLOSED allows only DISBURSE"; DISBURSE postings are not built yet (payments), so the rule is carried by the commands that exist: one shared guard (`cap-fiscal-year-guard.ts`) refuses the plan commands (create, draft, submit, approve, publish, revision), the appropriation register and the commitment of capital in a soft-closed or locked year with 409 `FISCAL_YEAR_LOCKED` and `state` SOFT_CLOSED or LOCKED, and lets the capital carried out of the year (ST-CAP-007) through in a soft-closed year; the year the capital is carried into must be open. The four private copies of the old "locked only" check now call the guard.
- ST-CAP-008 (decision, what stops a close): a plan of the year IN_REVIEW or APPROVED and not yet published (409 `PLANS_PENDING` with the plan ids; the story says IN_REVIEW, an approved plan waiting for its publication would be stuck forever behind the lock so it counts too; a DRAFT does not), and for the lock an ACTIVE fund reservation of the year (409 `RESERVATIONS_ACTIVE`; the officers release or use them first, nothing is released silently).
- ST-CAP-008 (decision, the lapse): locking posts, for every line in force of a published annual plan of the year, a LAPSE entry of allocated less committed and less disbursed (source `YEAR_CLOSE`, one reference per closing in `lock_ref_id`) and takes it off the balance; committed and disbursed capital stays. Requests to carry capital over that nobody decided become LAPSED. The ledger is compared with the balances before the transaction goes on (`balanceDrift`, which now counts LAPSE as -1) and nothing stays if they disagree. The ceiling of the appropriation keeps holding what was allocated, so lapsed capital cannot be allocated again.
- ST-CAP-008 (decision, a revision does not bring it back): the publication of a revision of a plan now judges a line against its amount less what left it without the plan saying so: carried over (EXTEND_OUT, ST-CAP-007) and lapsed (LAPSE); the capital that lapsed stays off the line even when a plan is revised in a reopened year (a revision that kept every other line the same would otherwise have re-allocated all lapsed capital). Putting lapsed capital back would be a reversal entry, which no story builds yet.
- ST-CAP-008 (decision, the reopening): `reopenFiscalYear` needs a reason of 20 characters (422 `REOPEN_REASON_REQUIRED`; the schema no longer carries a minimum of its own, so the specific code is the answer) and a window of 1 to 168 whole hours; the approver is anyone but the person who locked the year (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED` and the guard trigger). `locked_by` is kept through the reopening for that check; the job that locks again counts as a person of its own (the worker membership). The job `fiscal-year-relock` (every 15 minutes) locks again the years whose window ended and lapses what came back; the story names it `capital.year-reopen-expiry`, the product names jobs without a module prefix.
- ST-CAP-008 (contract): beyond the two operations of the story a read is added, `listFiscalYears` (GET /fiscal-years, `capital.read`) with the `FiscalYear` and `FiscalYearList` schemas: the years the control knows, the latest first, each with the version to send as If-Match; a year it does not list is open. `integer` path parameters (a year) are no longer taken for the id of an object by the isolation enumerator, which asked for a foreign-id case of every operation with a path parameter.
- ST-CAP-008 (web): CAP-11 (`YearCloseScreen`): the year (the years the control knows and those around today), its state with the facts of the closing and of the last reopening, a red banner for a locked year and a note for the adjustment period, the actions the state allows and the person holds (soft close, lock, reopen) each in a dialog that says what it does and asks the reason (and the window), the years under control. New e2e users `yearCloser`, `yearReopener`, `yearSolo`, `yearReader`.
- ST-CAP-008 (assumption, open): the length of the adjustment period and the moment of the lapse follow CXN-31 and are the choice of the officers, not computed; the lapse is of the whole undisbursed, uncommitted part of a line (the story says "unextended capital"); a reservation that is not active is not touched; the story keeps the status DONE_UNCONFIRMED until CXN-31 is confirmed.
- ST-CAP-008 (not done here): the independent money and authorization reviews the story requires were not run; the payments that post DISBURSE into a soft-closed year (the only thing it still accepts, per the story) belong to the payment stories and must call `assertYearsNotLocked`; the unused capital of a carry-over line after its last day lapses with the closing of its own year like every other line.
### ST-CAP-007 (carry-over extension of undisbursed capital with the approving document, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-007 (decision, what may be carried over): the undisbursed part of a line is its allocated amount less committed and disbursed and less what the other open requests of the line already ask for (a reservation is not a commitment; BR-CAP-06 judges it), whole VND with BigInt; it is judged at the request and again under the lock of the balance at the decision (422 `AMOUNT_EXCEEDS_UNDISBURSED`, UAT-PLAN-61). The last day of disbursement is typed from the document and lies in the year after the plan year: the widest window the law could give, because the rule of the day awaits CXN-31.
- ST-CAP-007 (decision, one transaction): the table has REQUESTED, APPROVED, APPLIED, REJECTED, LAPSED, and the contract has no "apply" command, so the approval applies the extension in the same transaction and the row goes from REQUESTED straight to APPLIED (APPROVED stays allowed by the guard for a later two-step flow). The decision carries who decided, when, why, the document, and the line and the appropriation the approval made; the approver is never the requester (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`, `allocation_extension_sod_chk` and `allocation_extension_decider_chk`).
- ST-CAP-007 (decision, the money): `EXTEND_OUT` leaves the line and the appropriation of the plan year Y (the balance falls, the appropriation keeps its amount and its allocated total, so the capital of Y is not increased and cannot be allocated again) and `EXTEND_IN` enters a new line of bucket CARRYOVER (`is_extended`, `extended_to`, `source_year` = Y, the document) in Y+1; both entries carry the extension as their source (`EXTENSION`). The balances and the ledger are compared (`balanceDrift`) before the transaction ends and nothing stays if they disagree. `capital_ledger_extend_line_chk` makes both entries name a line and a project.
- ST-CAP-007 (decision, where the new line lives): in a published plan of its own, `CARRYOVER-<year>-<unit code>`, for the year and the unit of the source plan, created by the first approval that needs it, so no published plan is edited (a line of a published plan is neither edited nor removed). It draws on an appropriation of origin CARRYOVER registered and published through the appropriation store (master data in force, document usable, year open, ledger APPROPRIATE), with the approving document as decision and extension document, the authority taken from the issuer of the document (the source authority when it names none), and the latest in-force version of the fund source and program. A second approval under the same decision, source year, fund source, program and level revises that appropriation (amount and allocated total grow, the first line follows it) instead of registering a second one (unique decision line); a project has one carried-over line per such appropriation (409 `APPROPRIATION_DUPLICATE`).
- ST-CAP-007 (decision, a revision of the plan does not put it back): the publication of a revision of the source plan judged a line by its plan amount, which still includes the capital that left. `CapPlanPublicationStore` now reads the `EXTEND_OUT` entries of the same line across revisions (same project, appropriation, bucket and source year), judges the line against its amount less that, writes the balance accordingly and refuses a line below it with `REVISED_BELOW_COMMITTED`, so the capital is never allocated twice.
- ST-CAP-007 (decision, the lapse): the hourly job `allocation-extension-lapse` makes a request that nobody decided LAPSED once its last day has passed (it can no longer be approved) and moves no money. The capital that an approved extension left unused after its last day lapses with the closing of the year (BR-CAP-04, ST-CAP-008).
- ST-CAP-007 (contract): beyond the two operations of the story a read is added, `listAllocationExtensions` (GET /allocation-extensions, `capital.read`, filters `status` and `allocation_line_id`, keyset by creation time) with the `AllocationExtension` and `AllocationExtensionPage` schemas, because CAP-20 has to show who asked, who decided, with what document and what line the approval made. The request needs no If-Match (it creates); the decision does. Both are judged for the unit of the plan the line belongs to, like the plan commands.
- ST-CAP-007 (web): CAP-20 (`ExtensionScreen`): the plan (published, annual) and the line to carry over (a line that is itself carried over is not offered), the figures of the line read by `getCapitalAvailability` with what is left to carry over, the form checked in the browser with the same window and rules as the server, a preview sentence of what the approval will do, the list of requests with approve and reject (with the document id and a reason; a request of the person themselves offers them nothing to decide). New e2e users `extPlanner`, `extApprover`, `extReader`.
- ST-CAP-007 (assumption, open): the CTMTQG subset of the source line is not carried (the new line has 0); only a line of an ANNUAL plan can be carried over, and a carried-over line cannot be carried again (the second year would need a rule for its source year); the approving document must be in force (PENDING, EFFECTIVE or CORRECTED) but is not checked against a list of authorities able to allow a carry-over (CXN-31); the story keeps the status DONE_UNCONFIRMED until CXN-31 is confirmed.
- ST-CAP-007 (not done here): the independent money and legal-rule reviews the story requires were not run; PAY choosing a line by the plan year of its source (BR-CAP-04) and the LAPSE of the unused carried-over capital at the closing of the year belong to their own stories.
### ST-SAS-009 (entitlement overrides with four-eyes approval, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-009 (decision, four eyes in three layers): the person who asks cannot decide, whichever way: the store answers 403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED` to the requester for an approval and for a rejection alike (the identity is the membership of the control-plane session on the platform tenant, so two administrators are two memberships), the table already had `approved_by <> requested_by` (V002), and V1484 adds `entitlement_override_approver_ck` (an override is Active exactly when someone approved it; a waiting or rejected one has no approver) so a hand-written UPDATE cannot make an Active override without a second person. The screen offers the requester nothing to decide (only to withdraw).
- ST-SAS-009 (decision, an exception is a record, not a setting): V1484 makes what was asked for immutable (feature, on or off, limit, window, reason, requester), lets the state move forward only (waiting: Active, Rejected, Revoked, Expired; Active: Revoked, Expired; the rest final), keeps the approver whatever becomes of the override, forbids DELETE and TRUNCATE (grants revoked and a trigger) and a limit on a feature that is off. A change of mind is a new request. Existing fixtures that deleted overrides now end them by revoking (usage, members, e2e).
- ST-SAS-009 (decision, when it takes effect): the entitlement gate already read the overrides in force from the database (approved, inside the window), so approval is felt by the next decision that reads the database; for the cached ones every change of what is in force (approval, revocation of an Active one, expiry) records one new entitlement version (`recordCurrentEntitlementVersion`, cause OVERRIDE or EXPIRY, `sas.entitlement.changed.v1`), which tells the sessions of the tenant, so it is in force within the minute the cache lives (AC-ST-SAS-009-1). A rejection, a withdrawn request and the lapse of a request that was never approved change nothing a tenant can do and record no version. A tenant with no entitlement version yet has nothing to copy and nobody to tell.
- ST-SAS-009 (decision, the clock): the new job `entitlement-override-expiry` (every minute, idempotent, through the control role acting as each tenant) lets an Active override whose window ended expire and records one version per tenant per pass; a request that waited past the end of its window expires without a version. The gate ignores an override outside its window whether or not the pass has run, so the minute is only the latency of the notification, not of the rule.
- ST-SAS-009 (decision, the rules of a request, kernel `overrideIssues`, the same ones in the screen): the feature must be one a plan sells (422 `FEATURE_UNKNOWN`), the limit a non-negative decimal of at most 6 places and only with a feature that is on, the window ends after it starts and in the future, lasts at most 366 days and starts at most 90 days ahead, the reason has 10 to 2000 characters (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` naming the first field, every issue in `issues`). A request whose window ended while it waited cannot be approved (422). The bounds (366, 90) are mine: the design says only that the end is mandatory.
- ST-SAS-009 (contract): beyond the three operations of the story a control read is added, `listTenantEntitlementOverrides` (GET /system/tenants/{tenantId}/entitlement-overrides, `system.tenant.read`, keyset by start then id, latest start first) with the `EntitlementOverride` and `EntitlementOverridePage` schemas, because SAS-25 has to show who asked, who approved and the version to decide against. The `Decision` body is shared by every approval and has a `return`; an override has no rework (a change of mind is a new request), so `return` answers 422 on `decision`. Revocation is `POST /system/entitlement-overrides/{overrideId}/revoke` (the contract path), body `ReasonOnly`.
- ST-SAS-009 (web): SAS-25 (`EntitlementsScreen`) gets the `OverridesPanel` for the control plane: the list with feature, setting, window, state, who asked and who approved (the person is shown as "you" or the first 8 characters of the membership id: there is no directory of platform people yet), the request form (checked in the browser with the same bounds, window read as UTC), approve, reject and revoke with a reason of 10 characters or more, against the version that was read. A reader (`system.tenant.read` only) sees the list and nothing to do. The table of entitlements above is read again after each command. New dev user `billingAdmin` (BillingOperator) in the e2e fixtures for the second administrator.
- ST-SAS-009 (assumption, open): any tenant status may have an override (a suspended tenant's exception is still the operators' to make); who may approve is any holder of `system.billing.manage` other than the requester (no approval-authority matrix for the control plane exists); no notification to the requester of the decision (NOT module) and no dedicated metrics or alert beyond the audit trail, the outbox event and the generic job-failure alert (TK-SAS-009-6 is covered by those only).
- ST-SAS-009 (not done here): independent authorization review (required by the story) was not run; the authorization tests are the matrix of roles, the cross-tenant case (an override of another tenant answers 404 on decision and revoke) and the DB constraints above.
### ST-SAS-008 (subscriptions, plan change preview and proration, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-008 (decision, the money): the proration is BR-SAS-08 exactly and lives in the kernel (`prorationOf`, `billingPeriod`): `floor((new - old) * remaining / days)` in whole VND with BigInt, the days counted with both ends and today in the remaining ones (the year of 2026, day 46, 365,000,000 to 730,000,000 is 320,000,000, UAT-AC155, tested in the kernel and over HTTP against an independent calculation). A change **now** is only an upgrade (the target plan costs more); a downgrade, or a plan of the same price, can only take effect at the next cycle with proration 0 and no money given back (the assumption of the story, pending D08). Periods are counted from the billing anchor (31 January plus a month is 28 February, the next period is 28 February to 30 March, and the anchor never drifts); `CONTRACT_TERM` is billed by the year because the contract term is not modelled yet. A plan's `base_price_vnd` is read as the price of one period of whichever cycle the plan sells.
- ST-SAS-008 (decision, the preview and its token): the preview is not a state change (no row, no entitlement version); its token is a sha-256 of the facts it was judged on (the subscription and its version, both plans, the effect, the day, the period, the proration, the day it takes effect, the quota violations and the hash of the tenant's current entitlements), so it goes stale when an override or a licence changes the entitlements, when the subscription changed, or the next day (the proration is another). The server works the proration out again at apply and never trusts the client's token for money: 409 `PREVIEW_STALE` and nothing changes. The version of the subscription (If-Match) is checked first (409 `VERSION_CONFLICT`), under a row lock, so two applications race to exactly one.
- ST-SAS-008 (decision, what a change does): **now** moves the subscription to the plan, takes a trial out of the trial (the customer is paying), and records the entitlements of the new plan as one new version (`sas.entitlement.changed.v1`, cause SUBSCRIPTION_CHANGE); **at the next cycle** only sets the pending plan and the day it takes effect (the day after the period ends), and a later change replaces it (the earlier row is marked superseded and never applies). Every change is kept in the new table `subscription_plan_change` (V1483: from, to, effect, day, proration, the preview token, the reason, who asked; never edited or deleted; applied, superseded and invoiced are each set once). A downgrade takes nobody away: the preview lists the quotas the tenant is over (`member.seats` today) and, from the day the plan takes effect, the invitations and the activations are refused (the seat limit of ST-SAS-010 is the plan's quota), 120 people still work in a plan of 100 (AC-ST-SAS-008-2, UAT-AC156, tested in the store with the job and over HTTP).
- ST-SAS-008 (decision, the clock): the new job `subscription-cycle` (hourly, idempotent, runs through the control role acting as each tenant because the plan catalogue is a control-plane table) turns a trial whose end has passed into Active, applies the plans that waited for their day (with their entitlements), rolls a period that has passed to the one that holds today, and ends a subscription that was set to end with its period; the provisioning trial's period is now exactly one billing year (it was a year and a day). Cancelling ends a trial at once and lets a paid subscription run to the end of its period.
- ST-SAS-008 (decision, contract): beyond the five operations of the story a control read is added, `getTenantSubscription` (GET /system/tenants/{tenantId}/subscription, `system.tenant.read`): the live subscription (absent when none, not an error), the plan it is on and the one that waits with their prices, and the latest 10 changes; and the generic reads `get_resources_subscription` and its id form give a tenant its own subscriptions by plan id (the tenant plane has no privilege on the plan catalogue, so it cannot show plan names). The commands name the tenant (create) or only the subscription (the rest): the tenant of a subscription is found by `subscription_tenant_of` (V1483, a function that answers one uuid) and the command acts as that tenant while it writes, audits and emits.
- ST-SAS-008 (assumption, open): turning a trial into Active at the end of its days and when a paid upgrade is applied is mine (the table has the states, the design says no rule); `PastDue`, `Paused` and `Expired` have no way in yet (payments and billing, ST-SAS-011 and the payment reconciliation). The "Số ghế" field of SAS-07 has no place in `SubscriptionCreate` (seats come from the plan's quota and the licence), so it is not on the form.
- ST-SAS-008 (not done here): the **draft invoice with its ADJUSTMENT line** of UAT-AC155 (320,000,000) belongs to the billing run (ST-SAS-011): here the proration is kept as the applied change that billing will take onto an invoice (`invoiced_invoice_id`), because an invoice line needs a pricing version and an invoice a buyer snapshot that only the billing story defines. The tenant-side read-only view of SAS-07/08 for the owner and the billing viewer is the API only (ids of plans, no names). The independent review the story marks required (money) has not been done.

### ST-SAS-010 (usage metering and quota counters, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-010 (decision, the record): `recordUsageEvent` (the internal plane) writes one event per (meter, `external_event_key`) and feeds one counter; the unique key decides, so a key sent again, also by many at once, answers 200 with the first event and adds nothing (201 for the event that was recorded), and the counter is 101 and not 102 (UAT-AC168, tested over HTTP and in the store with eight concurrent senders). An event is insert-only in the database (V1481: a trigger refuses update and delete, also for the owner; the runtime role has the privileges taken away). Every command leaves an audit row, so a repeat is told apart (`usage.event_deduplicated`) and a sender that repeats its keys can be found.
- ST-SAS-010 (decision, the catalogue): the meters and the counters they feed are in the kernel (`USAGE_METERS`): the three message channels feed one counter (`notification.messages`, limited by the campaign feature of the plan), `ai.tokens` (limited by the AI feature), `storage.gb_day` and `bi.queries` (reported, no plan limit yet); an unknown meter is 422 `METER_UNKNOWN`. A correction is its own meter (`<meter>_correction`, the assumption of the story): its quantity is positive, it takes away from the counter, and it is refused (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `quantity`) when the counter of that month holds less. Counters are kept per calendar month (UTC) of the time the event **happened**, not the time it was recorded; an event more than five minutes ahead of the clock has not happened (422). The meters are my proposal where the design names only `notification.zns`: the product owner owns the list.
- ST-SAS-010 (decision, quota): the counters of the events do not refuse anything (the thing was done, and the event is its record); a limit refuses the *next* thing before it is done. Seats are not counted from events: they are the Active staff memberships (service accounts excluded, guests apart), counted by the database under the existing advisory lock of the seat decisions, which already serialises concurrent accepts. What changed is the limit and the moment: the staff seats are the **lower of the licence and the plan's `member.seats` quota** (an override in force replaces the plan's number), so a plan downgrade applies at once, and the invitation itself (`inviteMember`, `inviteGuestContractor`, whose contract already listed `QUOTA_EXCEEDED`) is refused when no seat is free, as well as the acceptance and the reactivation. Nobody is taken away: a tenant at 120 of 100 keeps its 120 working and cannot add a 121st (AC-ST-SAS-010-2, UAT-AC156 at its member level; the subscription downgrade itself is ST-SAS-008).
- ST-SAS-010 (decision, reads): the story names the generic reads of the events but the screen also needs the counters against the limits, so two reads are added to the contract: `getMyUsage` (GET /me/usage, `subscription.read`, so the billing viewer reads it) and `getTenantUsage` (GET /system/tenants/{tenantId}/usage, `system.tenant.read`, the Billing Operator's, with the latest 20 events). The summary shows every counter of the catalogue (0 where nothing was counted), the limit where a plan holds one (a switch has none, whatever number the plan stores for it), and the seats against the effective limit. The control plane reads the tenant's counters and events inside a transaction naming the tenant (V1482 grants it select on the two tables) and its seats from a function that counts them: it reads no membership.
- ST-SAS-010 (decision, events read): `get_resources_usage_meter_event` and its id form list and read the events of the caller's own tenant for whoever holds `usage_meter_event.read` (the tenant owner; the billing viewer does not), newest first by the time the event happened, keyset-paged; they do not hide `source_reference`. The design says the tenant sees the usage aggregated by meter and period and the detail only on the control plane; the aggregate is `getMyUsage`, and the permission, not a second DTO, is what keeps the detail from the people who do not need it. A decision for the product owner.
- ST-SAS-010 (assumption, open): the internal plane has **no authentication path** yet: no verifier produces an `internal` audience, so `recordUsageEvent` is reachable only where one does (the tests use the test guard). The service account is an ordinary `SERVICE_ACCOUNT` membership of the tenant holding a role with `internal.usage.write`; TENANT_OWNER now holds `internal.*` so that the owner can create such a role (a role cannot hold what its maker does not), and the plane check keeps an owner's own token from calling it. How the platform services obtain such a token (mTLS, signed service tokens) is not built.
- ST-SAS-010 (not done here): the invoice lines that bill the events once (BR-SAS-13, the billing run of ST-SAS-011); the retention of events for 10 years and the counters for 2 (the jobs); notification, AI and storage as senders of events (each module calls `recordUsageEvent` when its story needs it); a limit that refuses the next message or token before it is sent (`consume` on a counter: no consumer exists yet, so none was written). The independent review the story marks required (money) has not been done.

### ST-SAS-007 (plan catalogue authoring with immutable published versions, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-007 (decision, contract): the story names `createPlanVersion` and `publishPlanVersion` only, which gives SAS-06 nothing to list, so the read `listPlanVersions` (GET /system/plans, `system.billing.manage`, plan_code and status filters, keyset by plan code and newest revision first, each version with its features and its `version` for the If-Match of publication) is added to the contract (a change request in the baseline) rather than reading the tables from the screen. There is no read of one version; the list carries everything.
- ST-SAS-007 (decision, what a plan may be sold with): the rules live in the kernel (`planDraftIssues`) and judge both moments, the write of the draft and its publication, so what is published is what was validated: a name, one billing cycle at least, a non-negative integer price, terms, one feature at least and at most 100; a feature is one of the codes something enforces (`PLAN_FEATURE_CODES`: the features that gate permissions plus `member.seats`; selling a code nothing enforces would be a promise nobody keeps, so a new feature is added there together with the code that enforces it); a boolean takes 0 or 1 and no meter, a quota needs a limit and no meter, a metered feature needs a meter and may have a cap; after the end a feature never gives more than in the grace window (ALLOW 3, READ_ONLY 2, DENY 0 against READ_ONLY 2, EXPORT_ONLY 1, DENY 0). Every refusal is 422 `PLAN_INVALID` with the first field in `detail` and all of them in `issues`.
- ST-SAS-007 (decision, numbering): a plan code is matched without case and keeps the spelling that exists (the seeded plan is `STANDARD`, the contract writes `standard`, so `standard` gives revision 2 of `STANDARD`, UAT-AC154); the next revision is the greatest plus one under an advisory lock, so concurrent drafts get distinct revisions without gaps.
- ST-SAS-007 (decision, immutability in three places): the store answers 409 `PLAN_VERSION_IMMUTABLE` to the publication of a version that is not a Draft whatever If-Match the caller holds (the finer answer first); a stale If-Match is 409 `VERSION_CONFLICT`; V1480 makes the database refuse any change of a Published version other than its retirement, the features of a version once it is not a Draft, a way back to Draft, and any delete, also for the owner. The seeded STANDARD revision is guarded like any other.
- ST-SAS-007 (decision, audit): the plan catalogue is shared by all tenants, so its audit rows (`plan.draft_created`, `plan.published` with the reason) and the event `sas.plan.published.v1` belong to the platform tenant; the audit diff has the terms and the feature codes and nothing of a person.
- ST-SAS-007 (assumption): the story says prices come from decision D08 (entitlement matrix) and ships with placeholder prices. None are seeded beyond the STANDARD plan at 0 VND; the screen is where D08 is entered, and its numbers are not invented here.
- ST-SAS-007 (open): publishing a revision does not retire the one before it, and there is no operation to retire a plan (the table has the `Retired` state, the contract no command): tenants can still be provisioned on any Published revision (`PLAN_NOT_PUBLISHED` only refuses drafts and retired ones). Whether new tenants must get the latest revision, and who retires the old, is a decision of the product owner.
- ST-SAS-007 (open): publication has one actor (the contract has no maker-checker for it, unlike the overrides of SAS 3.x); for a price list a second approver would be the safer rule. The review the story marks required (money) has not been done by an independent reviewer.
- ST-SAS-007 (not done here): SAS-06 also names read-only access for the Tenant Owner and BILLING_VIEWER of their own tenant; there is no tenant-plane read of plans in the contract (the tenant sees its own entitlements in SAS-25), so the screen is the control plane's. A draft cannot be edited or deleted (the contract has no such command): a mistake is a newer revision.

### ST-KERNEL-090 (UAT evidence by screen and requirement)
- ST-KERNEL-090 (decision, two documents): `docs/UAT-COVERAGE.md` (`uat:trace`, since ST-KERNEL-010) covers the acceptance criteria of the stories by the titles of the tests that exist and stays as it is; the new `docs/UAT-EVIDENCE.md` (`scripts/uat-evidence.mjs`, `npm run uat:evidence`) covers the 478 screens and the 478 requirement rows of `traceability.json` by the **results** of the tests of `test_cases.json` read from a run store. The story title names UAT-COVERAGE.md; merging the two would have made one 250 KB file answer two questions, so the second has its own name and the runbook (docs/runbooks/uat-evidence.md) says which is which.
- ST-KERNEL-090 (decision, the rule): a row is the worst state of its tests; PASS needs the recorded evidence (when it ran, build, schema version, dataset hash, request ids, a link), else UNPROVEN; FAIL, SKIPPED, NOT_RUN and UNPROVEN never count as PASS; a screen or requirement with no known test is a GAP; WAIVED needs a waiver with owner, reason, approver and a day that has not passed (expired or incomplete waivers fail the gate by themselves). The critical section (money, isolation, restore, by the requirement prefixes of `docs/uat-critical.json`, which the product owner owns; 28 tests today) counts PASS only: a waiver never counts there. `npm run uat:gate` is the release gate; it is red today and is not in `npm run lint`, because a repository that is still being built is honestly red; `uat:evidence:check` (keeping the document current) is in lint.
- ST-KERNEL-090 (decision, today): no run store exists, so the generated report says 0 of 478 screens complete, 478 rows NOT_RUN, 0 of 28 critical tests PASS: the truth about acceptance by run. The repository tests are real and green but are named by acceptance criteria, not by the ids of `test_cases.json`.
- ST-KERNEL-090 (not done here): the pipeline that runs the design tests against a staging build and writes `docs/uat-run-results.json`, and a reporter that maps Jest, Vitest and Playwright results to the design test ids with their evidence (the "evidence requirements" are documented, the capture is not built); the critical list is a first proposal.

### ST-KERNEL-060 (legal rules as dated data, the CXN gate)
- ST-KERNEL-060 (decision, the gate): `npm run cxn:check` (`scripts/cxn-gate.mjs`, in `npm run lint`) joins the stories of the plan with `legal_confirmation_ledger.json` and `docs/story-status.json`. A story that is DONE or DONE_PENDING_ENV while a CXN item it cites is not confirmed fails and lists the ids; DONE_UNCONFIRMED is the honest status for it and is itself refused when every item it cites is confirmed (stale) or when it cites none. A ledger row counts as confirmed only with who, when and the evidence (a status edited alone is INVALID_CONFIRMATION in the ledger and a failure of the gate). Turning the gate on relabelled 39 stories from DONE to DONE_UNCONFIRMED: none of the 58 CXN items has been confirmed (no `legal_confirmation_register.json` exists, the owner of the legal items has not signed any), so the count of stories that are DONE with nothing legal pending is 63 and the count that wait for legal is 41. This is the truthful number and not a regression: the work did not change, its label did. How a lawyer confirms an item: docs/runbooks/legal-confirmation.md.
- ST-KERNEL-060 (decision, the resolver): the kernel has `resolveRule(rules, code, asOf)` (the version of a business date with its legal basis and the watermark flag when it is not confirmed), `ruleOfRevision` (the version a record was bound to) and `assertRuleVersions` (no overlap, no reversed range, a legal basis; 409 RULE_RANGE_OVERLAP). It is the generic form of what `MasterRules` (MDM) and the template tables (DOC) already do over their own tables; those were not rewritten to call it (they read from SQL with the same rule: range [from, until), the latest revision of the day). The payment request of AC-1 is shown with the generic resolver: PAY does not exist yet, and PAY must bind the revision it used when it makes a request.
- ST-KERNEL-060 (decision, the lint): V1003 adds the exclusion constraint on the daterange of the key (published versions only, deferrable so that a command that retires one version and publishes the next in one transaction is not judged between its statements) to doc_type, doc_retention_rule, document_layout_profile, early_warning_rule and doa_matrix_version, and a check that a period ends after it starts to early_warning_rule, mne_obligation_rule, contract_party, master_crosswalk, membership and project_component. `packages/db/src/effective-range-registry.ts` declares every table that has a start and an end of validity (RULE with its guard, VALIDITY, SERIES, PENDING with the reason) and the db test fails for a table that is not declared, a guard that does not exist, a range with no period check. `metric_definition` is PENDING: the metric registry has no publisher yet, and the BI story that publishes one adds its guard. The tables classified VALIDITY (the period of a membership, a permit, a certificate, a grant...) are judged by me as facts of a record and not rule versions; a reviewer who reads one of them as a rule moves it.
- ST-KERNEL-060 (not done here): the template renders of DOC and the rules of MDM do not call the kernel resolver (see above); `license_revision` and `contract_revision` are classified VALIDITY (their flows set the period) and not given an exclusion; the status vocabulary has no state for "confirmed, waiting for the data to be switched" (the confirmation of the data is its own command).

### ST-CAP-005 (availability control, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-005 (decision, the migration): V1365 holds what the policy needs as a record spending can rely on. The shape of the scope (a tenant policy has no reference, a unit policy a unit, a fund source policy the business key of the fund source, a project group policy one of QTQG, A, B and C), the code and the legal basis as written, a period that never ends before it starts, `approved_by` set exactly when the policy is published (V002 already had warn <= block, block <= 100 and maker <> approver). A policy is never deleted; what it says never changes (a guard trigger, owner included); it moves DRAFT to PUBLISHED and no other way (RETIRED exists in the vocabulary and no command reaches it: a policy ends by its end date, the contract has no "retire" command); the one thing that moves is its end date, once, and only earlier. Two published policies of one scope and object never overlap in time (the store judges first and names the other policy, the trigger is the backstop, SQLSTATE 23P01). `capital_ledger_entry.source_ref_kind` gains CONTRACT for the commitment of a contract.
- ST-CAP-005 (decision, what governs): the most specific published policy in force on the Vietnam day wins: a project group before a unit (the exact owner unit of the project, not its subtree) before a fund source before the tenant, within the control object (ALLOCATION_LINE for a line, APPROPRIATION for an appropriation). Without a published policy a LINE falls back to the tenant default of 90 and 100 [assumption of BR-CAP-06] and names no policy; an APPROPRIATION with no policy of its own is not controlled at all (assumption: applying the default there would warn on every appropriation that is two thirds spent, which the design does not ask for). A code is one policy over time: its revisions keep the scope and the object (422 on `policy_code` otherwise), and a later revision that starts after an earlier one of the same code in force takes over (the earlier one ends the day before); any other overlap is 409 AVC_POLICY_OVERLAP naming the other.
- ST-CAP-005 (decision, the rule): the use after a request is reserved (when the policy counts reservations, the default) plus committed plus disbursed plus the amount; OK below the warning threshold, WARN from it up to and including the block threshold, BLOCK above it; an allocation of nothing blocks everything (ZERO_ALLOCATION). The comparison is made on whole numbers by cross-multiplying, never on the rounded percentage that is shown (rounded down, two decimals), so a block of 100 lets exactly the allocation through and not one VND more. The thresholds are written with at most two decimals (numeric(5,2)).
- ST-CAP-005 (decision, the port): `CapitalCommitments.checkAndCommit` (CAP module provider, built on `CapAvailabilityStore.checkAndCommit`) is the one place that commits capital against the policy. It locks the balance of the line (and, when a policy controls the appropriation, the balances of all its lines, in the one lock order of the product), reads the figures under the lock, judges the line and the appropriation, moves `committed_vnd` and appends the COMMIT entry in the same transaction, then audits (`capital.commitment_posted`) and emits `capital.commitment_posted.v1`. AVC_BLOCKED carries the reason, the shares and the policy; AVC_WARNING_ACK_REQUIRED asks the caller to say they accept the warning, and the acknowledgement is recorded on the ledger entry (`AVC warning acknowledged at 95.00%`) and in the audit row and the event. The same business fact posts once (the dedupe key of the ledger): asking again with the same amount answers what was done and writes nothing, another amount is 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT. Nothing calls it yet: the activation of a contract (CTR) and the payment are the stories that will. 100 loops were asked for the race of two requests of 100 on a balance of 100; 25 are run in the suite (and 15 for two lines of one appropriation) because each loop is a real transaction.
- ST-CAP-005 (decision, the reads and the authority): the policy is a rule of the whole tenant, so drafting it, publishing it and previewing it need the right on the whole tenant (403 SCOPE_DENIED for a grant on one unit); anyone who reads capital lists the policies and reads the availability. A line is read in the scope of the caller (outside it, 404); an appropriation by the permission, like the register of appropriations. The preview counts, over the whole tenant, the lines in force (published plans) or the published appropriations of the scope of the policy that its thresholds would put in OK, WARN and BLOCK; a project group or a unit policy over appropriations counts none (those are properties of a project, not of an appropriation).
- ST-CAP-005 (contract): added `listAvcPolicies` (GET /avc-policies) and `previewAvcPolicy` (GET /avc-policies/{id}/preview, schema CapAvcPreview) for CAP-17, `appropriation_id` and `avc_reason` to CapAvailability, and the codes AVC_POLICY_STATE_INVALID and AVC_POLICY_OVERLAP (409). `getCapitalAvailability` is not in the plain-list sweep of the isolation suite because it names the line or the appropriation in its query.
- ST-CAP-005 (authorization matrix): `publishAvcPolicy` is in the maker-checker list (each case drafts a policy of a fund source scope of its own so that one published policy per scope does not make the cases clash); both commands are in the negative matrix and the isolation lists.
- ST-CAP-005 (web): CAP-17 (`AvcPolicyScreen`): the versions with scope, object, thresholds, reservations, period and state in words, the form with the two threshold bars (three zones, each said in words below, with a hatch so that colour never carries the meaning alone) and the refusals in the form before sending (a warning above the block, above 100, not a percentage), the next revision started from a version, the publication offered to a person who holds the right and did not draft it, and the preview counts. The names of the units come from the organisation unit read, so a user without it sees the unit by its id and cannot pick one.
- ST-CAP-005 (not done here): the reservation (`createFundReservation`, CAP-18) and its release are R2 and the stories that spend call the port; a policy cannot be retired by a command (see above); the preview is a tenant-wide count and says nothing about which lines are affected (the list of lines is CAP-06); the effect of a policy that ends or is replaced on the commitments already made is none (a policy judges a request, it does not re-judge the past); the default of 90 and 100 for a tenant with no policy is an assumption to be confirmed by the Sở Tài chính (R-CAP).

### ST-DOC-012 (recall of issued documents and voiding of numbers, slice DOC-1)
- ST-DOC-012 (decision, the migration): V1224 adds what the two commands rest on. V002 already gave the columns (`outgoing_document.recall_*`, `document_number_allocation.voided_*`, `document_relation` with RECALLS) and V016 already keeps the allocation log append-only (the only change is the approved void), so no second guard was written for the allocation. New: a recalled or cancelled outgoing document is final and its recall (reason, instant, person) or its cancel reason is never rewritten; the effect RECALLED of the anchor is never in force again; `document_distribution.recall_notice_at` (the instant the recall notice was issued for a recipient) is written once; `document_relation` can no longer be deleted by the runtime role (retention is permanent). The guards of the outgoing document and the effect apply to the table owner too (no maintenance exception): a privileged migration that has to touch a recalled document must lift the trigger itself.
- ST-DOC-012 (decision, what a recall does): ISSUED, DELIVERED, ACKNOWLEDGED, FILED or ARCHIVED to RECALLED with a trimmed reason of at least 10 characters (a second recall is 409 DOCUMENT_STATE_INVALID); the effect of the anchor becomes RECALLED (`resolveDocumentReference` then answers DOCUMENT_NOT_EFFECTIVE); the request of the source module is RECALLED; the sendings not yet made (PENDING, FAILED) are CANCELLED so nothing goes out for a recalled document; when `notify_recipients` is true (the default when it is not sent) every recipient who has the document (SENT, DELIVERED, ACKNOWLEDGED) gets `recall_notice_at`. The notice itself is carried by `doc.recalled.v1` (it lists the recipients: distribution id, kind, unit, name, channel) for the notification module and the channels to send; no adapter sends the recall by e-mail or paper here. The number stays ALLOCATED and the ledger shows it as RECALLED (the entry status is derived from the effect of the document).
- ST-DOC-012 (decision, the basis): `recall_basis_document_id` is the recalling document: the relation RECALLS goes from it to the recalled document (with the reason as note). A basis must be another document of the tenant in force (EFFECTIVE); anything else is 422 RECALL_BASIS_REQUIRED, and so is a recall of an ARCHIVED document without one (assumption: the design says "căn cứ thu hồi khi áp dụng" and does not say when it applies; archived records leave current use, so the recall must rest on a document). The error carries `basis` (SELF, NOT_IN_FORCE, REQUIRED_FOR_ARCHIVED).
- ST-DOC-012 (decision, voiding): NUMBERED or SEALED to CANCELLED. The caller is the one who voids, `approver_user_id` the second person: the approver is neither the caller nor the person who allocated the number (403 SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED, judged by the store before anything else changes), and must be an active member who holds `doc.outgoing.void_number` over the drafting unit of the document (422 VALIDATION_FAILED on `approver_user_id`, reason APPROVER_NOT_AUTHORISED; judged after the void inside the same transaction, so a refusal leaves nothing). The clerk who allocated the number may therefore start a void when another person approves; naming themselves is refused. The design does not say who executes: DOC.md calls the records manager "người duyệt hủy số" and the clerk may not void a number they allocated on their own. The signing sessions still open are ended (the provider is told after commit), the request of the source module is CANCELLED, `doc.number_voided.v1` goes out once and, for a sourced document, `doc.rejected.v1` with the reject code NUMBER_VOIDED. The next number is the next one; a voided number is not a gap (`auditRegisterVoids` lists them next to the gap audit).
- ST-DOC-012 (decision, authorization matrix): `voidOutgoingDocumentNumber` is not in the maker-checker list of the matrix test: its second person is named in the body and the allocator is not "the maker who may not approve" but a person who may start a void with another approver, so the framework (maker as caller, same body for the other holder) cannot express it; the rules are proved by the API tests (the allocator naming themselves, naming the allocator, naming the caller, an approver out of reach, a stranger). Both commands are in the negative matrix and in the isolation lists.
- ST-DOC-012 (web): DOC-19 (`RecallScreen`): the document picker (issued, delivered, acknowledged, recalled, numbered, sealed), the status and effect in words, a banner with the reason, the person and the time for a recalled document, the notice each recipient got and the sendings called off, the recall form (reason, basis chosen from the other documents in force, notice on or off), the void form (reason, approver) and the timeline of the document (events ISSUED, RECALLED, NUMBER_VOIDED added to the labels). Gated by the grants and by a suspended tenant.
- ST-DOC-012 (not done here): replacement and correction (`createReplacementDocument`, `createCorrectionDocument`, relations REPLACES and CORRECTS, effect REPLACED and CORRECTED) are ST-DOC-015 (R2); the screen name says so but only the recall and the void are built. FILED and ARCHIVED are recallable in the store but no flow produces those states yet, so the picker does not list them (a document opened by its address is recallable). The approver is typed as the member id (a UUID): the contract has no list of the members who may approve and DOC-19 has no picker for people (a design gap to settle with ADM: a read of members by permission). The notice of the recall is only recorded and carried by the event; the e-mail and the paper handover of the notice, and the acknowledgement of the notice by the recipient, are not built. The source-module consumers of `doc.recalled.v1` (MNE, DIS, CAP) are theirs to build.

### ST-IAM-007 (support tickets, slice IAM-1)
- ST-IAM-007 (decision, the table): V1240 keeps the metadata of a ticket in `support_ticket` and what the member wrote in `support_ticket_body` (the ticket points at it with `description_ref`), so a view of the metadata for the platform operator never reads the description (NFR-PRIVACY-02); attachments are rows of `support_ticket_attachment` (document versions of the tenant, not infected, at most five). The code SUP-YYYY-NNNNNN is counted per tenant and Vietnam year by `support_ticket_counter` (the row of the counter is the lock, so two tickets never share a number). The ticket keeps what the member asked for and its SLA for good (guard trigger), starts Open, is never deleted; its status, assignee and version are what the operator moves (the operator side is SAS-19 and the control plane, not built here).
- ST-IAM-007 (decision, priority and SLA): the priority comes from the category, not from the member: INCIDENT P1, ACCESS and DATA P2, BILLING P3, HOWTO and OTHER P4 (`PRIORITY_OF_CATEGORY`, an assumption until the business owner signs it). The time for an answer is 4 hours, 1 day, 3 days and 10 days of working time on the calendar `WORKDAY_VN` of the tenant with the ADM window of 8 working hours a day (so 4, 8, 24 and 80 working hours), and 4, 24, 72 and 240 elapsed hours when the tenant has no published calendar for a year the count runs into (a ticket must always be raised, so the missing calendar never refuses it; `on_calendar` tells which way it was counted in the audit row).
- ST-IAM-007 (decision, who may): `support.ticket.create` and `support.ticket.read` are given to the MEMBER system role (and backfilled into the MEMBER roles that exist by V1241, as V010 did for `session.self`), because help is asked for by every member; the table of roles did not name it (PENDING SIGN-OFF). A member lists their own tickets; someone who holds `support.ticket.manage` on the whole tenant (TENANT_OWNER through `support.*`) lists every ticket of the tenant, with `own` telling which are theirs. The list never carries the description or the requester.
- ST-IAM-007 (decision, the operator): AC-ST-IAM-007-2 asks that an operator with only a ticket is refused the business data of a tenant with SCOPE_DENIED and that an audit row exists. The refusal was already the rule (a control-plane principal on a tenant-plane operation is denied for its plane) but only a refused command was audited; the policy guard now audits a refusal for the wrong plane for a read as well (`authz.denied:<operation>`, reason PLANE, in the tenant of the token). Auditing every refused read would be noise and is not done.
- ST-IAM-007 (contract): added the scaffold-style list `GET /resources/support_ticket` (`support.ticket.read`; filter `status`) because IAM-08 lists the tickets; `createSupportTicket` was in the contract. Event `iam.support_ticket.created.v1` and audit `support.ticket_created` carry the code, the category, the priority and the SLA, never the subject or the description.
- ST-IAM-007 (web): IAM-08 (`SupportScreen`): the tickets with the code, the group, the priority and the state in words and the time the answer is due in Vietnam time (overdue in words, never by colour alone), the form (group, subject, description) and the privacy sentence. The code of the new ticket is told once the list has it.
- ST-IAM-007 (not done here): the attachments are in the API but the form has no file chooser (the upload of files is the files module; the form would send the ids of versions it uploaded); the link to the support access request waiting for approval ("Cho phép truy cập hỗ trợ", SAS-19 `requestSupportAccess`); the operator side (the control-plane view of the metadata, the assignment, the moves of the status, the reminders when the SLA is near or missed); the SLA does not pause while the ticket waits for the member.

### ST-PMT-005 (permit gate waivers, slice PMT-1)
- ST-PMT-005 (decision, the requester): the contract has one command, `grantPermitGateWaiver`, and no step that asks for a waiver, so "approved by its requester" cannot rest on a request that is typed into the command (the approver would choose who the requester is). The people who asked for a waiver are read from the record: whoever made the decision document the waiver rests on (the maker recorded as `created_by` of the waiver, which V002 requires to differ from the approver), whoever is in charge of the dossiers of the project (`owner_membership_id`), and whoever made, last changed or answers for a permit of that type in them. The approver is none of them (403 SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED). If the product wants a request step with its own record, it is a new command (`requestPermitGateWaiver`) and the maker becomes its author; nothing here would have to change but where `requestersOf` reads from.
- ST-PMT-005 (decision, the longest): `MAX_WAIVER_DAYS = 90` counts from the day of the grant, the last day included (the longest last day is today plus 89), a working assumption until the tenant confirms it (R-PMT-03); 422 WAIVER_TOO_LONG names `max_days`, `requested_days` and `latest_day`, a last day in the past or that is not a date is 422 VALIDATION_FAILED. It is a constant, not tenant data: making it a rule of the tenant (as the PMT document says, "theo rule") needs a place for tenant parameters that the stories so far have not built.
- ST-PMT-005 (decision, the table): V1184 adds the code of the permit type (copied by a trigger, so the waiver covers every version of the type, as the gate rule does), `revoked_at`, `revoked_by` and `revoked_reason` (a revoked waiver tells who and why, checked), the foreign keys to the project, the permit type and the decision document, one waiver in force per project, gate and permit type code, and a guard trigger: a waiver keeps what was decided (project, gate, type, reason, document, last day, approver, maker, times), is born ACTIVE, ends once as EXPIRED or REVOKED and never changes again, and is never deleted. A waiver is not extended: after it ends a new decision is a new waiver. The evaluation already read the last day, so a lapsed waiver lets nothing through before the job runs.
- ST-PMT-005 (job): `gate-waiver-expiry` (`PmtWaiverExpiry`, hourly tick that works in the 00:00 hour of Vietnam time; `docs/runbooks/jobs.md`) ends the ACTIVE waivers whose last day has passed (EXPIRED once, version + 1, audit `pmt.gate_waiver_expired`, event `pmt.gate_waiver.expired.v1`) and sets the gauge `pmt_waiver_active` to the waivers in force; a waiver a command is acting on is left to the next pass.
- ST-PMT-005 (contract): added the scaffold-style list `GET /resources/permit_gate_waiver` (`permit.read`, project scope; filters `project_id`, `gate_code`, `status`) because PMT-06 needs the waivers and their ids; `grantPermitGateWaiver` and `revokePermitGateWaiver` were in the contract. The list answers the project, the permit type, the reason, the document version id, the dates and the state, never who asked or who approved (the audit trail has them). Authz matrix (grant is also a maker-checker case: the maker of the decision cannot approve), isolation lists, the workers and the jobs tests follow. Refusals carry their figures as extensions (`max_days`, `latest_day`, `active_waiver_id`, `waiver_status`); an extension named like a member of the problem (`status`) is dropped by the problem filter, which is why the state is `waiver_status` (and `criteria_status` for ST-BID-003).
- ST-PMT-005 (web): PMT-06 (`PermitWaiverScreen`): the waivers with the project, the gate, the permit type, the reason, the last day with the days left, the state in words and the revocation words, a filter by state, the form that grants one (the last day is limited by the input to the longest and by the form before sending) and the revocation with its reason. The exemption of a permit itself stays on the permit (PMT-02); the screen says so and links to the legal files.
- ST-PMT-005 (not done here): the document is typed as the id of its version, as in the other PMT forms (a document picker is a cross-module story); the notification of the expiry to the person in charge (the event is not registered with the NOT workflow yet, like the other PMT events); the cache of the evaluation (60 seconds, invalidated by `pmt.*`) is not built: the evaluation is computed on every read.

### ST-PMT-004 (permit gate rules, slice PMT-1)
- ST-PMT-004 (decision, rules): `permit_gate_rule` (V1183) gains `permit_type_code` (filled from the type by a trigger, FK to `permit_type`), so a rule names the permit TYPE by code and every version of the type is covered. A rule is dated legal data like a permit type: a gist exclusion `permit_gate_rule_no_overlap` (tenant, gate, type code, days) refuses two rules of the same gate and type on one day (409 `GATE_RULE_OVERLAP`), the next revision takes over on its first day and ends the one before the day before (`PmtGateStore.publish`, advisory lock like `PmtPermitTypeStore`), `LEGAL_BASIS_REQUIRED` (422) when there is no legal basis, delete and truncate are revoked and a guard trigger lets a rule only end earlier. `retirePermitGateRule` shortens the end, it never reopens one. Publishing and retiring need `permit.gate.manage` and `holdsOnWholeTenant` (legal data of the tenant, not of a unit).
- ST-PMT-004 (decision, evaluation): `PmtGateStore.evaluate(tx, projectId, gate, asOf?)` answers PASSED, PASSED_WITH_WAIVER or BLOCKED with one verdict per rule (SATISFIED, WAIVED, BLOCKING). A permit counts by the type code and by its days (EXPIRED and SUPERSEDED permits were valid on the days before, so an evaluation in the past is right; REVOKED never counts); `VALID_OR_EXEMPT` also takes an exemption, `SUBMITTED_ONLY` a permit that was filed. Applicability (project group, work types, investment range, milestone) is `appliesTo`. `assertPasses` raises `PERMIT_GATE_BLOCKED` (409) with `gate_code`, `as_of` and `blocking[]` in the extensions: the contract of the consumers.
- ST-PMT-004 (contract): added the scaffold-style list `GET /resources/permit_gate_rule` (`permit.read`); the publish, retire and evaluate operations (`publishPermitGateRule`, `retirePermitGateRule`, `evaluatePermitGates`) were in the contract. Authz matrix, isolation lists and the e2e user `permitGateManager` follow.
- ST-PMT-004 (web): PMT-04 (`PermitGateScreen`): the rules by gate with their phase (waiting, in force, ended) and legal basis, the evaluation of a project for one gate or all five on a day, and the form that publishes a rule (the revision hint when the gate and type already have one) or ends it.
- ST-PMT-004 (not done here): AC-ST-PMT-004-2 is proved at the guard every command calls (`assertPasses`: PERMIT_GATE_BLOCKED 409 unless a waiver is active) because the commands it names (CTR `activate` and `recordMeasurement`, QMS `saveSiteDiaryDraft` and `requestInspection`, PRJ start of construction, AST `createHandover`, PAY `requestAdvance`) do not exist yet; each of those stories must call `assertPasses` in its own transaction and add its own test of the refusal (the PMT.md list of dependent commands is the checklist); the waiver commands belong to ST-PMT-005 (the evaluation already reads a valid waiver); no cache of the evaluation (it is computed on every read); the event types `pmt.gate_rule.published.v1` and `pmt.gate_rule.retired.v1` have no consumer yet.

### ST-PMT-006 (permit expiry alerts, slice PMT-1)
- ST-PMT-006 (decision, job): `permit-expiry` (`PmtPermitExpiry`, hourly tick, works in the 06:00 hour of Vietnam time; `docs/runbooks/jobs.md`) is the template of `guarantee-expiry`: an ISSUED permit with an end that is 60, 30, 15 or 0 calendar days (Vietnam) from its last day gets one reminder for the level it has reached (`alert_level_sent` 1 to 4, the column V002 gave the permit), so a second pass the same day sends nothing and a missed day sends the level of today, not the stale ones. A reminder does not change the version of the permit. The reminder event `pmt.permit.expiring.v1` carries the notification member (resource, deep link `/pmt/pmt-05?permit=`, the person in charge as explicit recipient); the NOT workflow that turns it into an in-app notification is a cross-group story (the event type is not registered with the payload validator yet, like `ctr.guarantee.expiring.v1`).
