- ST-PMT-006 (decision, expiry): the day after the last day the permit becomes EXPIRED (state machine of V1181: ISSUED to EXPIRED), once; the same transaction counts the readiness of its dossier again (an expired permit no longer covers its type), writes the audit row `pmt.permit_expired` and the event `pmt.permit.expired.v1` with the members of the common contract of the issue intake (`source_ref_id`, `reference_code` = the permit number, `project_id`, `owner_unit_id`); `readIntake` reads it as a well formed request, so the intake of ISS opens one issue. The job never renews or extends: a renewal is the existing command, a new permit that names the old one (`supersedes_id`), the old one SUPERSEDED, and the valid_until of an issued permit is protected by the database.
- ST-PMT-006 (contract, nothing added): `listExpiringPermits` (GET /permits/expiring, `permit.read`, `within_days` 0 to 365, default 60, keyset pages by last day and id) was in the contract; `PmtExpiringPermits` answers the issued permits within the window (and past their last day until the job marks them), and the expired ones no renewal has replaced, the soonest first, in the scope of the caller (`dossierScope`). Gauge `pmt_permit_expiring{days}` (sum over the tenants of the ISSUED permits within 60, 30, 15 and 0 days).
- ST-PMT-006 (web): PMT-05 (`PermitExpiryScreen`): window of 15 to 365 days, days left, level of alert in words, the person in charge, a link to the permit (to renew when expired), and the note that the system never renews.
- ST-PMT-006 (not done here): the NOT workflow and event type registration for `pmt.permit.expiring.v1` (in-app notification), a runbook alert for a permit that expired without an issue being opened, a check that the issue category `PERMIT` exists in the tenant's master data (the intake retries, then dead-letters, when it does not).

## DIS-1 (disbursement reporting) open points and decisions

- ST-DIS-001 (decision): V1040 does not create `reporting_period` (V002 has it with the lock check, the overlap exclusion and the unique code); it adds `calendar_version_id` (FK to `master_record_version`, NOT NULL by CHECK, indexed, and listed in `MASTER_REFERENCES` so the impact preview of a calendar change counts the periods bound to it), the CHECKs that tie `soft_closed_at` and `locked_by` to the status, `REVOKE DELETE` for the app role, and the trigger `reporting_period_guard`: the dates, deadline and calendar version are fixed for good, the status only moves OPEN to SOFT_CLOSED to LOCKED with the version raised by one, a locked period never changes (reopening is a `period_reopen` for a scope, ST-DIS-002).
- ST-DIS-001 (decision): `generateReportingPeriods` binds the periods to the calendar in force on the later of the Vietnam date of the request and 1 January of the fiscal year (`MasterRules.calendarPeriods`), all or none; a year that overlaps an existing period of the same calendar and type is 409 PERIOD_OVERLAP (a pre-check names it; the exclusion constraint and the unique code are the last word under concurrency). Only `period_type` WEEK and `calendar_code` DEFAULT can be generated: the port of MDM-11 holds one calendar and counts weeks. MONTH, QUARTER, HALF_YEAR and YEAR answer 422 VALIDATION_FAILED (`period_type`), another calendar code 422 CALENDAR_NOT_EFFECTIVE (`calendar_code`), until the business owner signs the month and quarter deadlines and MDM-11 holds more than one calendar (a contract-visible change for the orchestrator: the enum of `DisPeriodGenerate` already lists the other types).
- ST-DIS-001 (decision): `softCloseReportingPeriod` is refused before the deadline of the period (409 PERIOD_STATE_INVALID, detail `deadline_at`, DIS.md state table: guard "sau deadline"); a lock needs no wait when a person does it (guard "-"), the job waits three working days. The check order is: period exists (404), If-Match version (409 VERSION_CONFLICT), status (409 PERIOD_STATE_INVALID), deadline.
- ST-DIS-001 (decision, spec conflict): the module document and AC-ST-DIS-001-1 say a submission to a locked period is 409 PERIOD_LOCKED, the canonical text of `error_codes.json` says "(422)". `DisPeriodStore.judge` throws 409 as the story and the module document say; the registry text should be corrected by the orchestrator (the problem filter takes the status of the thrown error, so nothing breaks meanwhile).
- ST-DIS-001 (interface for ST-DIS-003, the submit): `DisPeriodStore.judge(tx, periodId, at)` returns `{ period, late, deadlineAt }`, where `late` is `MasterRules.isLate` by the calendar version the period is bound to (never the calendar in force today), and refuses a LOCKED period with 409 PERIOD_LOCKED; the submit story lifts that refusal for a scope with an APPROVED `period_reopen` window (ST-DIS-002) and counts `dis_submit_total` with `submitLabels(late)` (the base labels). It runs in the transaction of the submit, so a period locked a moment later cannot be missed.
- ST-DIS-001 (decision): the job `dis-period-autoclose` (every 15 minutes, `packages/db/src/dis-period-autoclose.ts`) is a job of `standardJobs` on the existing runner and scheduler (the Temporal runner named in the cross-group request is not needed for it): it calls `DisPeriodStore.softClose` and `lock` as the commands do, writes the same audit row and event (`periodMoveRecord`), skips a period that a command holds (`for update skip locked`) and counts `dis_period_transition_total{to, trigger}`. The lock time is the deadline plus 3 working days = 24 working hours on the published working calendar `WORKDAY_VN` (08:00-12:00 and 13:00-17:00, ADM-07); with no published calendar for the year the period stays soft closed and is counted as `skipped` (nothing is guessed). The 3 days are a D03 assumption held as `DEFAULT_LOCK_AFTER_WORKING_DAYS` in code because the CALENDAR type of MDM-11 is `strict` and has no attribute for it: moving it to data needs a `lock_after_working_days` attribute on the CALENDAR type (an MDM change), after which the job reads it from the calendar version bound to the period. The calendar code of the working calendar (`WORKDAY_VN`) is the one ISS uses; ADM decides whether DIS needs a code of its own.
- ST-DIS-001 (decision): `reporting_period.read` is the only permission of the two reads; the list answers `fiscal_year`, `status`, `period_type` and `calendar_code` filters that the contract does not declare (like `term_code` of the glossary) and the period carries `reopens` (read from `period_reopen`, written by ST-DIS-002) so DIS-14 can show the history of reopening before that story exists. The screen reads every period (`readAll`, no filter) and picks the year and the status in the browser; a tenant with many years needs the server filters declared in the contract. The detail read returns the period itself (not the `items` list of the scaffold schema), like the other `get_resources_*_id` reads.
- ST-DIS-001 scope (review decision): DIS 2 gives the period manager the whole tenant, so `generateReportingPeriods`, `softCloseReportingPeriod` and `lockReportingPeriod` need their permission through a grant on the whole tenant (403 `SCOPE_DENIED` for a subtree grant). The reads stay open to every holder of `reporting_period.read`.
- ST-DIS-001 (decision): generating a year answers the first period as `resource_id` with status OPEN and version 1 (CommandResult has no room for a count); the count and the first and last code are in the audit row `dis.periods_generated`. There is no event for the generation (the contract names none).
- ST-DIS-001 (web, DIS-14): a soft close is offered only after the deadline (the screen knows the clock; the server still refuses it before), the lock only on a SOFT_CLOSED period, each in a dialog that asks for the reason (5 to 2000 characters) the audit trail keeps. Times are shown in Vietnam time (the columns say UTC+7) and the instant stays in the `datetime` attribute. The "Closed" word never appears: the three statuses are Open, Soft closed and Locked in words and in colour.
- ST-DIS-001 (not done here): the operations `requestPeriodReopen` and `decidePeriodReopen` (ST-DIS-002) and the screen DIS-22; the submit that counts `dis_submit_total` (the labels are ready); no Playwright flow or visual region (apps/web/e2e belongs to the orchestrator).

## DIS-1 ST-DIS-002 (reasoned reopening of a locked period) decisions and open points

- ST-DIS-002 (decision, migration): V1041 adds `requested_window_hours` (1 to 168, NOT NULL by CHECK), `decided_by`, `decided_at` and `decision_reason` to `period_reopen`, the shape CHECK of each status (REQUESTED has no decision; REJECTED has a decision and no window; APPROVED, EXPIRED and CLOSED have an approver who is the decider and a window that starts after the decision and lasts at most 168 hours), `decided_by <> requested_by` (so a rejection is also never by the requester), the reason stored trimmed (20 to 2000), the partial index the expiry job reads, `REVOKE DELETE, TRUNCATE` for the app role (retention 10 years; listed in `retained-history-grants.test.ts`), an insert guard (born REQUESTED at version 1, only for a LOCKED period, each project once) and an update guard (what was asked and decided never changes, the status machine REQUESTED to APPROVED or REJECTED and APPROVED to EXPIRED or CLOSED, version plus one). The period is never written: a reopening never changes `reporting_period.status`.
- ST-DIS-002 (decision, window): the window starts at the moment of the approval (`decided_at`) and lasts the hours the approver grants. The approver may grant any 1 to 168 hours (the contract gives `window_hours` to the decision and the AC says "an approver grants 48 hours"); the hours the requester asked for are kept in `requested_window_hours` and are the default of an approval without hours. The grant is deliberately NOT capped at the request (assumption: the approver is the authority, the contract maximum of 168 h is the only cap; capping would be a one-line change in `DisPeriodReopenStore.decide`). The audit row of the approval keeps both numbers.
- ST-DIS-002 (decision, authority): `requestPeriodReopen` for named projects needs, for every project, a grant of `period.reopen.request` whose scope contains the owner unit or the investor unit of the project (403 SCOPE_DENIED), and projects that do not exist in the tenant are 422 on `project_ids`; a request for the WHOLE period and every decision (`decidePeriodReopen`) need the permission through a grant on the whole tenant (403 SCOPE_DENIED for a subtree grant), because a period belongs to no unit and the window can open other units' projects (DIS 2). Self-approval is 403 SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED and is answered before the version check, so the requester learns nothing about the state of the request; the table refuses it too.
- ST-DIS-002 (decision, expiry, job versus lazy): both, with one authority. The rule is lazy and judged by the clock: `DisPeriodStore.judge` lets a locked period take a report only for an APPROVED request with `decided_at <= now < window_until` that names the project (or the whole period), and the reads show an approved window that has ended as EXPIRED from its last instant. The job `dis-reopen-expiry` (5 minutes, `docs/runbooks/jobs.md`) stores APPROVED to EXPIRED and writes the audit row `dis.period_reopen_expired` so the history ends with a stored, audited fact and reports and exports that do not run the clock logic read the same status. A late, skipped or failed job therefore changes what is stored, never who may submit. No event is emitted for the expiry (the contract names only `period.reopened.v1`, emitted by the approval with ids and times and never the reasons). The stored status CLOSED has no operation (the contract has none): nothing closes an approved window early.
- ST-DIS-002 (interface for ST-DIS-003, the submit): `DisPeriodStore.judge(tx, periodId, projectId, at)` now takes the project of the report and returns `{ period, late, deadlineAt, reopen }` where `reopen` is `{ id, windowUntil }` when the period is LOCKED and a window covers the project (otherwise 409 PERIOD_LOCKED); a report accepted under a reopening is late (the period is past its deadline). The submit story passes the project and records `reopen.id` with the revision if it wants the evidence.
- ST-DIS-002 (decision, reads, contract gap): the contract has no list of reopening requests. DIS-22 reads the periods (`get_resources_reporting_period`, as DIS-14 does) and takes the requests from their `reopens`, so nothing was staged. The detail of a request (reason, requester, approver, decision reason, hours, version, projects by code) is in the answer only for the approvers (`period.reopen.approve` on the whole tenant) and for the person who asked; every other reader of the calendar sees the scope, the window and the status. A dedicated `listPeriodReopens` (filters by status, project and requester, a cursor, and the detail rules above) would stop the screen from reading every period to build the approval queue; it is a contract change for the orchestrator.
- ST-DIS-002 (decision, validation): `REOPEN_REASON_REQUIRED` (422, field `reason`) answers a missing, short (under 20 once trimmed) or over-long (over 2000) reason of `requestPeriodReopen`: the generated body validator maps it through `FIELD_PROBLEMS` in `request-validation.ts`, the store repeats the check after trimming. A decision reason under 5 characters is 422 VALIDATION_FAILED `reason`. A request for a period that is not LOCKED, and a decision on a request that is no longer REQUESTED, are 409 PERIOD_STATE_INVALID (detail `status`); no new error code was needed.
- ST-DIS-002 (web, DIS-22): the screen has the request form (locked periods only, scope PROJECTS or PERIOD, projects from the register with a search, reason of at least 20 characters, hours 1 to 168) and the queue (requests that wait first, oldest first, then the decided ones newest first). A request the approver made is shown with approve and reject DISABLED and the separation rule as the tooltip and as visible text (never hidden). Every project of an open window carries "Đang mở lại tới hh:mm" (Vietnam time); a window that has ended is Expired on the screen whatever the server stored. The back link goes to DIS-21 (the parent of the route table; the screen is not built yet).
- ST-DIS-002 (not done): DIS-22 lists projects through `get_resources_project`, so a requester needs `project.read` to name projects (without it the chooser says the register cannot be read); a project picker scoped by the request permission would need a read of its own. The browser flows cannot make a window expire by time, so the expiry is checked by the API and database tests (clock moved 49 hours) and, in the browser, by a request decided in the past.

## MNE-1 ST-MNE-001 (report forms, obligation rules, filing calendar) open points

- ST-MNE-001 (decision, storage): the tables are those of V002 (`mne_report_form`, `mne_obligation_rule`, and `mne_report` for the shells); V1700 adds `origin`, `pack_version`, `published_at`, `retired_at` (both) and `watermark_text` (forms), the maker-checker and period checks, the exclusion constraints on published periods per code, and one guard trigger (`guard_mne_dated_revision`): a revision never changes its content, is published once by someone other than its maker, and is only ever ended by the takeover of a successor. The plan lists only the form and rule tables for this story, but `generateMneObligations` writes `mne_report` shells (MNE F2), so this story inserts DRAFT rows there (payload `{}`, no snapshot) and ST-MNE-002 owns every later change of those rows.
- ST-MNE-001 (decision, period): `effective_until` is exclusive everywhere (the first day the revision no longer applies), as in DOC and MDM; the catalogue prints "from X to Y inclusive" with Y one day before. A revision is data: a change is a new revision. A later open-ended revision takes over from the open-ended one in force (the earlier one becomes RETIRED on the new start date, version +1, audit `closed_revisions`); a closed period, a start on or before the one in force, or a closed new period over anything is 409 `FORM_RANGE_OVERLAP` (rules: `EFFECTIVE_RANGE_OVERLAP`, see gaps). The overlap is checked when the revision is drafted and again when it is published.
- ST-MNE-001 (decision, legal confirmation): there is no separate confirmation step as in DOC. A revision carries `legal_confirmation_ref` or it does not; without one it is a working assumption (`awaiting_confirmation`, label `[CXN-nn]` read from the tags of its legal basis, watermark text shown). Confirming is publishing the next revision with the reference. The reviewer of the publication (maker-checker, `mne.form.approve` / `mne.calendar.approve`) is the control on the reference a maker types; if the legal owner wants the separation of DOC F12, a `confirmMneForm` operation is a contract change.
- ST-MNE-001 (decision, DOC template): `DOC_TEMPLATE_MISSING` is raised when `DocumentStore.templateFor` finds no published template of `doc_template_code` in force on the first day of the form, both when the revision is drafted and when it is published (a template retired in between refuses the publication). The catalogue row carries `doc_template_id` and links to DOC-14.
- ST-MNE-001 (decision, who owes a report): the units of a role are read from the data model: `INVESTOR` = `organization_unit.is_investor`, `PROJECT_OWNER_AGENCY` = the distinct `project.owner_unit_id`, `PROVINCIAL_PEOPLE_COMMITTEE` = `unit_type = GOVERNING_BODY`; only Active units. `PLANNING_AGENCY` has no unit in the model (nothing marks the planning agency), so a rule of that role yields no obligation and the answer of `generateMneObligations` lists it in `unresolved_roles`; MNE-01 says so. Needed: a way to designate the unit of that role (a unit flag or a role-to-unit table), which is an ADM/IAM model change.
- ST-MNE-001 (decision, deadlines): `due_rule_json` is validated and evaluated by `packages/kernel/src/mne-due-rule.ts` (keys outside the rule are refused so a typo never falls back to a default). SEMIANNUAL/ANNUAL: `{period: H1|H2|YEAR, month, day, year_offset}` with the deadline strictly after the period; WEEKLY: `{weekly: MON..SUN, time}` on the weeks of the MDM reporting calendar in force today (`MasterRules.calendarPeriods`), due on the first such weekday on or after the week start (the time is shown, the shell stores the day); EVENT: `{event: PROJECT_START|PROJECT_ADJUSTED|PROJECT_COMPLETED, days_after}` is parsed and evaluated (`eventDueDate`) but not generated here (ST-MNE-008 makes it on the project event). `shift_on_holiday: true` needs `calendar_code` and moves the day to the next working day of the published ADM working calendar of that year (422 `CALENDAR_NOT_EFFECTIVE` when there is none); without it a day off never moves the deadline (BR-MNE-03). "Before the 10th" is stored as the 10th.
- ST-MNE-001 (decision, generation): one run writes every period of the year, for every unit of the role, with the rule revision and the form revision in force on the last day of the period (BR-MNE-02); the unique index of `mne_report` with `on conflict do nothing` makes it idempotent and race safe; a rule in force whose form is not in force on that day fails the whole run with 422 `FORM_NOT_EFFECTIVE` (nothing is written). A rule of the same form and role under another code would hit the same unique key and be deduplicated, not duplicated. The caller's scope (`mne.calendar.manage` grants) narrows the units; `scope_unit_id` outside it is 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`, an unknown one 422. The assumption "draft creation 30 days before the due date" is not used here: the run creates the whole year (the daily job `mne.obligation-window` belongs to a later story).
- ST-MNE-001 (decision, calendar read): `listMneObligations` reads the shells that have a rule, in the scope of the caller (`mne.read` grants over the unit), ordered by due date; `days_late` is the days between the due date and today (Vietnam) for a report that is not filed (BR-MNE-04) and `is_late` is `days_late > 0`; for SUBMITTED_EXTERNAL and SUPERSEDED the stored `is_late` is used and `days_late` is 0 (not late) or null (late), because the filing day is not kept in `mne_report` (ST-MNE-004 stores `submitted_at` on the package). The weekly deadline is not bound to the calendar version (`mne_report` has no column for it), so lateness of a weekly report is judged by `due_date` alone.
- ST-MNE-001 (working assumption awaiting CXN-10, CXN-11, CXN-13): the platform pack `apps/api/src/modules/mne/form-pack.json` (`pack_version` 2026.10.1) carries ten forms (TT44-M02..M10, CV3665-WEEKLY) and three rules (OVERALL_SEMIANNUAL 10 July, OVERALL_ANNUAL 10 February of the next year, WEEKLY_QUICK Friday 16:00) with the legal basis, the watermark "Mẫu chờ xác nhận pháp lý [CXN-nn]" and the shared start date 2026-01-01 as data. Everything the design documents do not give is a proposal: the mapping of M04..M10 to report kinds (M08 as PROJECT_PERIODIC, M10 as EVALUATION), the recipients, the empty `schema_json` (the structure of the cells waits for the circular), the roles that file each report. None is CONFIRMED; the story is not DONE for the legal owner (review `legal-template`, 1 to 2 hours) until each is confirmed by publishing the next revision with its reference.
- ST-MNE-001 (not done, needs the orchestrator): `loadMnePack(tx, actor)` is idempotent and tested, but no provisioning step calls it (it needs the DOC pack first, like `loadPlatformTemplates`); a new step is a SAS contract change. Until then a new tenant has no forms and no rules.
- ST-MNE-001 (cross-module, DOC): the DOC pack has MNE-TT44-M02..M10 but no template for CV3665-WEEKLY: the weekly form and its rule of the pack are skipped (`DOC_TEMPLATE_MISSING`, `FORM_NOT_EFFECTIVE` in the load result) until DOC adds `MNE-CV3665-WEEKLY`. The NOT reminders at 7, 3 and 1 days (cross-group request) need the event `mne.obligation.due_soon.v1` and the job of a later story; nothing emits them here.
- ST-MNE-001 (CR-MNE-01, rules read): `listMneObligationRules` (GET `/mne-obligation-rules`, `mne.read`) is implemented in `MneObligationRulesController`: every revision of the tenant newest first (creation time and id keyset, `next_cursor`), filters `reporting_role`, `form_code`, `status` (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` naming the parameter, `cursor` for a malformed cursor), items shaped by `ruleViewOf` (rule, form, level, recipient, frequency, `due_rule_json`, legal basis, `awaiting_confirmation`, `cxn_refs`, period, status, origin, version, maker, approver). MNE-02 has the rules panel: list, status, publish by a second person (`mne.calendar.approve`, the maker is told another person has to publish) and the form that drafts a revision (`mne.calendar.manage`, `due_rule_json` as JSON text that the server evaluates and refuses naming the key). The panel reads the whole list with `readAll` and no query. Contract mismatch left for the next batch: openapi.json declares the query `effective_on` and `report_kind` for this operation (copied from `listMneForms`), the handler implements the filters of the change request (`reporting_role`, `form_code`, `status`); the answer is an untyped `object`, and `limit` and `cursor` are not declared. `due_rule_json` of `MneObligationRule` is a bare `object` too (one cast in `apps/web/src/screens/mne/hooks.ts`, as for `schema_json`). Still open from the same batch: `MneObligationStatus` (`listMneObligations`) allows only nine members per item (`additionalProperties: false`) but MNE-01 needs more, which the API returns: `mne_report_id`, `rule_code`, `reporting_role` (the level), `recipient`, `form_title`, `form_revision`, `report_year`, `period_label`, `unit_code`, `unit_name`, `legal_basis`, `awaiting_confirmation`, `cxn_refs`, `form_id`, and `as_of` beside `items`. `listMneForms` has an untyped answer (`type: object`) and no `limit` and `cursor` parameters (the screen reads it with `readAll`); the shape is `{items, next_cursor}` with the fields of `formViewOf` (`awaiting_confirmation`, `cxn_refs`, `doc_template_id`, `watermark_text`, `origin`, `pack_version`, `created_by`, `approved_by`, `version`). `MneFormVersion.schema_json` is a bare `object`, which the generator renders as `Record<string, never>` (one cast in `apps/web/src/screens/mne/hooks.ts`), and the body has no `watermark_text`, so a tenant form has none.
- ST-MNE-001 (contract gap, error codes): no MNE code exists for publishing a revision that is not a draft (`REPORT_STATE_INVALID` is used) nor for the overlap of two rule periods (`EFFECTIVE_RANGE_OVERLAP`, the MDM code, is used); `createMneObligationRule` lists `FORM_NOT_EFFECTIVE` and `VALIDATION_FAILED` only. Proposed: `FORM_STATE_INVALID` and `RULE_RANGE_OVERLAP`.
- ST-MNE-001 (shared files touched): `apps/api/src/modules/registry.ts`, `apps/api/src/request-validation.ts` (`LEGAL_BASIS_REQUIRED` for the two create commands) and its test, `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `packages/kernel/src/index.ts`, the authorization matrix (`matrix-commands.json`, `negative-matrix.ts`, `negative-matrix.test.ts`: fixtures `mneFormId`, `mneRuleId`, the template `MX-TEMPLATE`, three fingerprint tables; `maker-checker.ts`: the two publish commands), the isolation suite (`suite.ts` with `MNE_FIXTURES`, `channels/api.ts`, `channels/database.ts`, `isolation.test.ts`), `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts` and the two i18n catalogues (`mne.forms.*`, `mne.calendar.*`).
- ST-MNE-001 (not done): no Playwright flow or visual region (apps/web/e2e belongs to the orchestrator); MNE-09 progress (ST-MNE-006) and the report screens; the navigation entries of the MNE group in the prototype.
- ST-MNE-002 (decision, schema of a form): `schema_json.fields[] {key, label, kind MONEY|PERCENT|INTEGER|TEXT, required, source?}` is the one structure the report reads (`packages/kernel/src/mne-report.ts`); sources are `SNAPSHOT_METRIC`, `PROJECT_COUNT`, `WARNING_COUNT`, `APPROPRIATION_TOTAL`. It is validated when a form revision is drafted (`createMneFormVersion`). MNE.md does not fix the structure (`schema_json` is free JSON in the contract), so this is the working design; the pack still ships none (CXN-10), and a form with no fields gives an empty shell.
- ST-MNE-002 (decision, figures and scope): the figures are those of the whole unit subtree for the unit-level forms (`rateSums` gained an `underUnitId` filter) and of the project for a project-level form, and are not narrowed by the reader's project scope: the right to prepare for the unit was judged first, and a report that depends on who edits it would not agree with the dashboards. Rates use basis CURRENT_YEAR (the Treasury over the year plan, as DIS-19), `meta.rate_basis` says so; a carry-over basis cell is not offered.
- ST-MNE-002 (decision, sources of counts): `PROJECT_COUNT` and `WARNING_COUNT` read the live register, not the snapshot (the snapshot keeps no stage and no warnings), so they can move after the report was filled; the cells are filled again when a snapshot is attached, and a report that left DRAFT keeps what it has. If the report must freeze them as of the snapshot day, the snapshot needs to carry them (ST-DIS).
- ST-MNE-002 (contract gaps): `createMneReport` accepts `obligation_rule_id` (checked to exist, kept on the row) but a report made for a calendar obligation is opened by `editMneReport` on the shell, because the shell already holds the unique key (409 `REPORT_EXISTS` names it); `getMneReport` is an untyped object in openapi.json; `FORM_NOT_EFFECTIVE`, `SNAPSHOT_REQUIRED`, `SNAPSHOT_NOT_FROZEN`, `FIELD_SOURCE_LOCKED`, `REPORT_EXISTS` and `REPORT_STATE_INVALID` are in the registry already.
- ST-MNE-002 (shared files touched): `packages/db/src/dis-snapshot-store.ts` (`underUnitId`, scope parameter now `$7`), `packages/db/src/mne-form-store.ts` (the schema is read when a revision is drafted), `apps/api/test/mne/mne-app.ts` (members 7 and 8, role 6; `mne-scope.test.ts` moved to 9, role 7), the authorization matrix (`createMneReport`, `editMneReport`, `submitMneReport`; fixture `mneReportId`), the isolation suite (the four operations; `editMneReport`, `submitMneReport`, `getMneReport` against the foreign report), `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts`, the two i18n catalogues (`mne.report.*`).
- ST-MNE-002 (not done): attachments are accepted by the API but MNE-03 does not offer them; no creation form in MNE-03 for a report outside the calendar (the calendar shells are the way in; `createMneReport` is exercised by the API tests); no Playwright flow or visual region.
- ST-MNE-002 (decision, snapshots a preparer may choose): `getMneReport` carries `snapshot_options` for a draft (the FROZEN close or MNE snapshots of a day not after the end of the period, newest first, 50 at most), so MNE-03 needs no read of the disbursement module (`listPeriodSnapshots` needs `disbursement.read`, which a preparer may not hold). It is an additive member of an untyped answer; if the contract is typed later it must keep it.
- ST-MNE-002 (pending destination): every locked cell links to HOME-10 (context of a figure and trace to its source) with `source`, `snapshot`, `detail` and `field`; HOME-10 is a HOME screen not implemented yet (the HOME stories are next in the queue), so the link shows the not-implemented page until then. The query names are the contract the HOME-10 story should read.
- ST-MNE-003 (decision, approval): the table keeps `approved_by` different from `created_by` (V002) and, new in V1703, from `submitted_by`; the command refuses both as 403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`. A return clears `approved_by` and keeps `decided_by`, `decided_at` and the reason, so that the record of who sent the report back survives; the decision is shown by `getMneReport` (`decision`). The operation has no workflow version binding (ST-ADM-012 is not implemented): a single level of approval, as the plan's AC asks.
- ST-MNE-003 (decision, payload for DOC): the templates MNE-TT44-M02..M10 ask for `title`, `period` and `body` (and DOC adds `agency` and `place` to every payload). The payload is generic (one line per cell of the form, in Vietnamese: "label: value"), because the real layout of the forms awaits CXN-10; when the legal layout is confirmed the placeholders and this builder (`mneReportPayload` in the kernel) change together. The hash is of the payload as the kernel builds it, frozen when issue is asked for, and never changes afterwards (trigger `guard_mne_report`).
- ST-MNE-003 (decision, recall): `doc.recalled.v1` sets `recalled_at` and raises `MNE_REPORT_RECALLED`; it does not change the status, because MNE 3.1 section 4 has no state for it and `correctMneReport` (ST-MNE-007) is what replaces an issued report. MNE-05 and MNE-10 should show the day.
- ST-MNE-003 (gap, notification): the configuration alert for the form administrator (`mne.configuration_alert.v1`, audience `FORM_ADMINISTRATOR`) is an event and an audit row; nothing turns it into a notification yet (NOT-004..006 and the catalogue of notification events). Until then the alert is visible in the audit trail and the outbox.
- ST-MNE-003 (gap, metric): `mne_issue_latency_seconds` is a gauge of the last answer (the `MetricsSink` port has no histogram); a histogram needs the port to grow.
- ST-MNE-003 (shared files touched): `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `packages/db/src/mne-report-store.ts` (the decision and issue columns; `lock` and `assertUnitInScope` public), `packages/kernel/src/{mne-report,index}.ts`, `apps/api/src/workers/outbox-consumers.ts` (+ its test), `apps/api/test/mne/mne-app.ts` (members 10 and 11, roles 8 and 9, grants 9 and 10, the file port of DOC), the authorization matrix (`decideMneReport`, `requestMneReportIssue`), the isolation suite (the two operations, against the foreign report), `alerts/rules/MNE_REPORT_*.json` and their runbooks, `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts` and the two i18n catalogues (`mne.approval.*`).
- ST-MNE-003 (cross-module, DOC): the drafter may complete the place of issue in the draft, which changes the hash of the draft but not what the module asked for. DOC now adds `source_payload_sha256` (the hash of the request) and `source_event_id` (the event the module asked with) to `doc.issued.v1`, `doc.recalled.v1` and `doc.rejected.v1` (apps/api/src/modules/doc: issuance, recall, outgoing-drafts, issue-request.service; `DocIssueRequestStore.sourceOf`); the consumers of MNE and AUD match the hash the module asked for (falling back to `payload_sha256` when the member is absent) and MNE ignores an answer that names an earlier attempt (a refusal DOC repeats must not undo the retry now in hand). The existing members are unchanged. The payload of MNE is stored under a key that carries its hash (an object is written once), and a retry after a refusal asks again for the frozen payload instead of building a new one.
- ST-MNE-003 (not done): MNE-05 does not list the history of versions of the report (MNE-10, ST-MNE-007) and has no Playwright flow or visual region; the upload of a signed document when in-system signing is not ready (the R8 spike of the plan) is DOC's.
- ST-MNE-004 (decision, files): the XLSX, PDF and CSV are made from the cells of the form with the shared builders of DIS (`xlsxOf`, `csvOf`, `pdfOf`), one row per cell (label, value, unit, source); money in million dong without a float. The national system's file specification does not exist (CXN-05), so this is a proposal with the legal watermark, and the XML is refused (`EXPORT_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`). "Totals recomputed on export" has nothing to recompute: the cells of a form are the figures of the snapshot as they are. When the specification is written the builder (`mneExportDocument` in the kernel) changes and the packages of the old layout stay as they were (they are told apart by hash).
- ST-MNE-004 (decision, same content, same package): the bytes are deterministic (the zip has a fixed time, the PDF carries the day of issue: `pdfOf` takes an optional stamp), so the unique key (report, target, format, hash) answers a repeated request with the existing package; a change of the form's schema, the report's text or the code that writes the file makes a new hash and so a new package.
- ST-MNE-004 (decision, adapter): the adapter status of a package is read from the integration connector of the tenant whose code stands for the target (`national_pim_file`, `recipient_agency_file`, `lgsp_file`, compared without case; the table of connectors keeps its codes in lower case), and is MISSING when the tenant has none. The plan names the connector `NATIONAL_PIM_FILE`; the code check of the table forces lower case, and the ingestion cursor keeps the upper case name (a different table).
- ST-MNE-004 (decision, download): there is no operation in the contract to fetch a package. The links come with `getMneReport` (five minutes, one for each package that is offered, only to who holds `mne.report.export` over the unit) and the file is served by a public signed route that is not in openapi (as `report-exports/{id}/file` of DIS). The first serving marks the package DOWNLOADED by the worker; a failure to record it never refuses the file.
- ST-MNE-004 (gap, egress): the proof that nothing leaves for the national system is a test that records every outbound `fetch`, `http` and `https` request of the process while the files are made and the receipt is recorded. The allowlist of the egress of the environment (the infrastructure) has no entry for the national host, and none is added.
- ST-MNE-004 (shared files touched): `apps/api/src/modules/dis/export-pdf.ts` (optional stamp), `apps/api/src/request-validation.ts` (`RECEIPT_REQUIRED`), `packages/db/src/{jobs,index,mne-report-store}.ts`, `packages/kernel/src/index.ts`, `apps/api/src/workers/workers.ts` and the three test lists of the jobs (`jobs.test.ts`, `workers.test.ts`, `workers-runtime.test.ts`), `docs/runbooks/jobs.md`, `apps/api/test/mne/mne-app.ts` (the object store of the tenant), the authorization matrix (`generateMneExportPackage`, `recordMneSubmissionReceipt`, fixture `mnePackageId`), the isolation suite (`MNE_FIXTURES` gain `packageId` and `packageSha256`, the table `mne_export_package` joins the snapshot tables), `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts` and the two i18n catalogues (`mne.export.*`).
- ST-MNE-004 (decision, PDF and links): the PDF of a report is written with the table wrapped (`pdfOf` takes `{ stamp, wrap }`; a cell of 4000 characters is written over as many lines and pages as it needs, and a paragraph keeps its line breaks), while the table of DIS keeps cutting a long cell with an ellipsis. A signed link lives five minutes: MNE-06 reads the report again every four minutes while it holds a link, and does not follow a link that is about to run out (it reads again and the new link is the one to follow). A package that expired is made again as a new one (the unique key of a file is held until the package expires; the expired one stays as the record).
- ST-MNE-004 (not done): MNE-06 does not offer the document of the receipt (the API takes `receipt_document_id`), nor the refusal of a package (`REJECTED` has no command in the contract); no Playwright flow or visual region.
- ST-MNE-007 (decision, supersede on issue): the revision that is corrected becomes SUPERSEDED when DOC says the correction was issued, not when the correction is started or approved (the report DOC issued stays in force until DOC issues the one that replaces it). The consumer does it in the transaction of the answer of DOC; a correction whose predecessor is not in a state to be superseded (it was recalled and corrected twice, for example) leaves it as it is.
- ST-MNE-007 (decision, what a correction copies): the cells, the attachments and the snapshot of the report it corrects, the form revision, the obligation rule and the due date. The snapshot of the form revision is not changed even when a later revision of the form is in force: a correction corrects the report, not the form it was made on. `is_late` starts false for the new revision (its own filing is judged when it is filed).
- ST-MNE-007 (gap, screen): the plan lists no screen for this story (MNE-10, the history of the versions and the corrections, is not assigned to a story that is open); `getMneReport` already answers the chain (`history`). No button starts a correction in MNE-03, MNE-05 or MNE-06 yet.
- ST-MNE-007 (decision, the day an obligation was filed): `mne_report.filed_on` (the Vietnam day of the first receipt) is written once, on the report that was filed and on the revisions it corrected (`settleSuperseded`), and the calendar judges an obligation by it: a report with a `filed_on` is settled (late or not by the day it was filed, `days_late` counted from it, which also closes the gap of ST-MNE-001 where the filing day was not kept); a superseded report with none is still open and late by today. A later filing never changes it. The migration backfills it from the first receipt recorded of each report. The calendar lists the first revision of an obligation; its status shown there is the status of that revision (SUPERSEDED after a correction was issued).
- ST-MNE-007 (shared files touched): `packages/db/src/{mne-report-store,mne-report-issue-store,mne-obligation-store,mne-export-store,index}.ts`, `packages/kernel/src/{mne-report,index}.ts`, `apps/api/src/request-validation.ts` (`CORRECTION_REASON_REQUIRED`), `apps/api/src/modules/mne/{mne-reports.controller,mne-export.controller,mne-views,report.consumer}.ts`, the authorization matrix and the isolation suite (`correctMneReport`), `apps/api/test/mne/mne-flow.ts` (the steps the tests of the monitoring report share).
- ST-MNE-006 (decision, no new operation): MNE-09 reads \`listMneObligations\` (the story lists no operation), which now answers \`filed_on\`; the days late of a filed report are counted from it (the gap noted in ST-MNE-001, "the filing day is not kept", is closed by \`mne_report.filed_on\` of ST-MNE-007). A weekly report is judged by its due date as before.
- ST-MNE-006 (decision, the rule version shown): it is the version bound to the shell when the calendar made it (the rule in force at the end of the period), shown as "code, revision n"; a report whose period belongs to r1 would be judged by r1 even if r2 took over later, and the screen says which one.
- ST-MNE-006 (not done): no filter by unit or by recipient on MNE-09 (the list is narrowed by the scope of the reader), and no export of the progress; no Playwright flow or visual region.

## CTR-1 (contracts) open points
- ST-CTR-001 (decision, "tendered package" and "approved capital plan"): neither BID nor CAP exposes a read for them yet, so `packages/db/src/ctr-contract-source.ts` (the one place that reads other modules) asks the tables: a package is awarded when its latest `procurement_result` with `outcome = AWARDED` is `APPROVED` (else `PACKAGE_NOT_AWARDED`), and the capital plan is approved when a `plan_version` of the project is `APPROVED` or `PUBLISHED` (else `CAPITAL_PLAN_NOT_APPROVED`). Replace the two queries with the ports of BID and CAP when they exist; nothing else in CTR changes.
- ST-CTR-001 (decision, ceiling): the ceiling of a contract is `procurement_package.budget_vnd` (BR-CTR-01: the price of the package), stored as `estimate_ceiling_vnd`; a request may name a lower ceiling and never a higher one (`VALIDATION_FAILED` on `estimate_ceiling_vnd`). The award price of the result is not a ceiling: the spec is silent on a contract above the awarded price, so it is not enforced. `CONTRACT_VALUE_EXCEEDS_CEILING` carries `ceiling_vnd` as a top-level member of the problem (the platform puts extensions there; UAT-AC57 says "details").
- ST-CTR-001 (decision, who may be the contractor): the contractor of `createContract` must be the winner of the approved result (`VALIDATION_FAILED` on `contractor_id` otherwise), verified and free of restrictions (`assertMayTakePart`: `CONTRACTOR_NOT_VERIFIED`, `CONTRACTOR_RESTRICTED`). A package may carry more than one live contract: no rule in the spec forbids it, so none is enforced (the code is unique per tenant).
- ST-CTR-001 (decision, joint venture): members exist only for a contract created with `is_joint_venture = true` (a contract of one contractor has no `contract_party` rows; `SOLE` is not written). `replaceContractParties` replaces all members together while the contract is a DRAFT: at least two, exactly one `LEAD`, and the lead is the contractor of the contract; shares add up to exactly 100 (`JV_SHARE_NOT_100`), the work adds up to the contract value (`BOQ_TOTAL_MISMATCH`, as UAT-AC306 says). A member that leaves is `WITHDRAWN`, never deleted, and a returning member gets its row back. A later `reviseContractDraft` of the value does not re-check the members (the register shows `parties_check.work_matches_value`); `submitContractForReview` (a later story) must re-check both sums.
- ST-CTR-001 (decision, database guards, V1380): the identity of a contract (tenant, project, package, contractor, code, type, currency) never changes; the status moves only along the state machine of CTR section 4 (`CONTRACT_STATE_INVALID`, 23514); the deferred share trigger now also fires on delete of a member and on the status change of the contract, and on leaving the draft states it checks that the work adds up to the value (`BOQ_TOTAL_MISMATCH`). `contract.created_by` / `updated_by` stay nullable (SET NOT NULL would break fixtures; backfill belongs to the data migration).
- ST-CTR-001 (decision, cancel): `cancelContractDraft` (DRAFT or IN_REVIEW) writes `contract_status_change` (reason, `effective_on` = today in Vietnam, command), sets `status_reason`, and marks the draft revision REJECTED; the creation itself writes no history row (there is no previous status).
- ST-CTR-001 (decision, reads): `get_resources_contract` was the placeholder `ContractPickerController` of ADM (ST-ADM-007); CTR now owns the route. The line keeps every field the picker read, and a contract grant now reaches its contract (a guest or an inviter bound to one contract sees it, as UAT-AC301 requires): two assertions of `apps/api/test/guest-contractors.test.ts` were changed to say so, and `apps/api/src/modules/adm/contract-picker.controller.ts` was deleted. A reader without `ledger.read` gets no ledger total at all; a guest contractor gets no ceiling, no history and no creator. The scope of a contract is the unit tree of the owner or the investor unit of its project, the project, or the contract itself.
- ST-CTR-001 (contract gap, `replaceContractParties`): `parties` items are `additionalProperties: true` with no declared fields, so the generated validator cannot reject a bad member; the six fields (`contractor_id`, `role`, `share_pct`, `work_value_vnd`, `valid_from`, `valid_until`) come from the data model of `contract_party` and are checked in `readParties` (`FIELD_NOT_ALLOWED` for a seventh). openapi needs a `CtrPartyInput` schema.
- ST-CTR-001 (contract gap, master data): `createContract.type` is a closed enum of price mechanisms (LUMP_SUM ... ESTIMATE_BASED, also `contract_type_chk`), while the master type `CONTRACT_TYPE` holds document classes (CONSTRUCTION, SUPPLY, CONSULTING, ANNEX). No field of the contract carries a `CONTRACT_TYPE` version, so no column and no `MASTER_REFERENCES` row was added. Decide whether the contract needs both.
- ST-CTR-001 (contract gap, error codes): the operations do not list what they raise: `BOQ_TOTAL_MISMATCH`, `CONTRACTOR_RESTRICTED` and `VALIDATION_FAILED` on `replaceContractParties`; `CONTRACTOR_RESTRICTED` on `createContract`; `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` for a project outside the tenant; `VALIDATION_FAILED` on `cancelContractDraft` (empty reason). The problem members `ceiling_vnd`, `share_total`, `work_value_total_vnd` and `contract_value_vnd` are not in the Problem schema. UAT-AC317 expects extra fields to answer 400 `INVALID_REQUEST`; the platform answers 400 `FIELD_NOT_ALLOWED` (request-validation.ts, unchanged).
- ST-CTR-001 (contract gap, reads): the GET scaffolds are `{items: object[]}`; the DTO shapes of `get_resources_contract(_id)` and `get_resources_contract_revision(_id)` are defined by `contract-view.ts` (the detail carries the revisions, the members with `parties_check`, and by right the status history and the ledger totals). No read lists packages or document versions, so CTR-02 takes the package and the document version as identifiers (`?project=` and `?package=` prefill the form for the BID screens to link to).
- ST-CTR-001 (not done): CTR-25 shows no "advance granted", "recovered" or "guarantee" column (the data comes with ST-PAY and the guarantee stories); CTR-02 has no "signed on" / "effective" fields (they belong to signing and activation, ST-CTR-002/003); the dossier of the draft is not linked to a DoA matrix check (the draft needs none); no Playwright flow or visual region (apps/web/e2e belongs to the orchestrator).

## ADM-1 (audit viewer, retention, connectors: ST-ADM-016, 017, 018) open points
- ST-ADM-016 (contract gap, the answer): the items of the audit read also carry `seal_hash` (the seal that covers the event, null while it waits), and the answer carries `seal` = `{status: OK|BROKEN|STALE|UNKNOWN, checked_at, seals, broken: {seal_id, from_seq, to_seq, reason} | null}` (the kept verdict of the hourly verification, V1302 `audit_seal_check`; STALE after three missed runs). Both are undeclared (`items` is `type: object`). Needed: declare `AuditEventView` and the `seal` member; ADM-11 reads them today.
- ST-ADM-016 (contract gap, no way to read an export): `exportAuditLog` answers 202 with the id only. Needed: `GET /audit-exports/{exportId}` (permission `audit.export`; status, size, sha256, counts, and a presigned link of at most 5 minutes) and a route that redeems a presigned grant (`FileService.redeemDownload` has no controller anywhere). The file is `tenants/{tenant}/audit_export/{exportId}`, written as CLEAN. Until then ADM-11 tells the person the id of the export and that the file is kept; nothing downloads it.
- ST-ADM-016 (gap in IAM, not mine): the seeded system role AUDITOR holds `audit.read` (TENANT_ADMIN too) but the operations need `audit_event.read` and `audit.export`; as seeded only TENANT_OWNER can read the audit trail and only TENANT_OWNER and SECURITY_ADMIN (`audit.*`) can export. The sign-off of the AUDITOR / TENANT_ADMIN rows of IAM section 2 (PENDING SIGN-OFF in `system-roles.ts`) must name `audit_event.read` and `audit.export`. I did not touch the role catalogue.
- ST-ADM-016 (decisions): the export needs a grant on the whole tenant (the seal windows are tenant wide and a file that proves a window complete carries the hash of every row of it; a subtree grant answers 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`); it is built inside the command (the answer is 202 `READY`, not a queued job) with a 64 MiB limit (422 `EXPORT_RANGE_TOO_LARGE` beyond it, as for a period of more than 366 days); the new upload purpose `audit_export` is in the kernel; the file carries `ip_hash` and `schema_version` because they are sealed, and a `safe_diff` that still held a sensitive value is redacted with `redacted: true`; CSV is for reading (formula cells are neutralised, types are lost), JSON lines is the verifiable form (`verifyAuditExport`, `docs/runbooks/audit-export.md`). The plan lists no table for the story: V1302 adds `audit_seal_check` (the verdict) and an index on `(tenant_id, occurred_at, seq)`.
- ST-ADM-016 (not done): an export whose command fails after the file was stored leaves an unreferenced object (nothing collects it yet); no alert rule for a STALE verdict (the viewer shows it); the web `exportAuditLog` has no download link (contract gap above).
- ST-ADM-017 (contract gaps, governance): no operation decides a review item (the council's decision: destroy, transfer, keep), so an item only has `PENDING_REVIEW`; no operation discards a draft (a newer draft of the class replaces the open one); `placeLegalHold`/`releaseLegalHold` have no UI yet (ADM-12 is their home: only the list of the active holds is built).
- ST-ADM-017 (decision, relation to master data): `RETENTION_POLICY` (DGV, platform only) is a baseline that no job executes; the rule the nightly job reads is the tenant's `data_retention_policy`. The two meet in one rule: a tenant policy for a class named in the catalogue (its key in lower case, for example `contract_record`) can never keep it for less than the catalogue (422 `RETENTION_POLICY_INVALID`, finding `BELOW_PLATFORM_BASELINE`). Documented in `docs/mdm-catalogue-dictionary.md` and `docs/runbooks/retention.md`.
- ST-ADM-017 (decisions, the run): `retention-purge` is an hourly tick that works in the 01:00 hour of Vietnam time (the scheduler only knows intervals); a technical class is kept at least its platform period (outbox 30 days, inbox payload 90 days and metadata 2 years, sessions 180, delivery attempts 365, idempotency keys 7) and a policy can only lengthen it (not `idempotency_record`: an expired key is never honoured, the policy cannot keep it); a business class is only listed (`retention_review_item`, once, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`); sources exist for `project_dossier` (`project`) and `audit_event` (by seal window; held when any row in the window is held); `payment_dossier`, `report_revision`, `contract*` have no records to read yet and are reported as `noSource`. A version whose `effective_from` has not come cannot be published (finding `DATE_FUTURE`), so a class never has a gap. The payloads of purged inbox events are removed from the object store through the new `FileService.remove`.
- ST-ADM-017 (CXN-RET-01..03): the periods are placeholders until the legal owner confirms them; ADM-12 says so on every screen, and the story is not DONE before the confirmation (plan assumption).
- ST-ADM-017 and ST-ADM-018 (the reads of CR-ADM-14, decisions): the reads of the retention rules, the review list, the connectors and the inbox are tenant wide (a grant on the whole tenant; a subtree-only grant gets an empty list and 404 on one id, as the legal holds do), page by keyset cursor with the declared `limit` and `cursor` only, so the filter of the inbox by status and provider is the screen's own, and the audit filters are the declared ones. The inbox view never carries the payload or the reference to it (the row type does not hold them; a test and the browser flow check it). ADM-09, ADM-10 and ADM-12 containers read everything with `readAll`; the legal holds and the MDM-24 cursors they show have their own read permissions, so a person without them still gets the screen with a warning instead of the list.
- ST-ADM-018 (gaps): no operation tests a connector (`last_test_at`/`last_test_result` are never written) and none changes the verdict on the adapter (a fact about the agency: today it is set in the database or by a migration; `updateIntegrationConnector` cannot do it, by the contract). ADM-10's columns "Job / Offset / Độ trễ" are the cursors (lag is not computed).
- ST-ADM-018 (decisions): a connector may claim `VERIFIED` only when the cursor that the platform recorded for the same connector (`lower(integration_cursor.connector_code) = connector_code`) says so; the same check runs at activation. `MOCK` is refused in `production`, `dr` and any environment that is not named: the environment is `MACHVON_ENVIRONMENT` (infra/environments names; unset means production; not yet in `config.ts` or the infrastructure check). Replay is "requeue plus an attempt right after commit" (202 `RECEIVED`); no provider registered an adapter yet (`ADM_INBOX_HANDLERS` is empty: PAY treasury callbacks, NOT and OMNI webhooks register theirs), so events of a provider with no adapter wait as `RECEIVED`. `failEvent` of the provider callback store now waits 1, 5 and then 30 minutes (was the outbox backoff) and a trigger moves the inbox version on every write by any writer.
- Shared files touched with small edits: `packages/kernel/src/object-storage.ts` (purpose `audit_export`), `packages/kernel/src/index.ts`, `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `packages/db/src/jobs.ts` (jobs `retention-purge`, `integration-inbox`), `packages/db/src/provider-callback-store.ts` (retry waits), `packages/db/src/audit-seal-job.ts` (keeps its verdict), `apps/api/src/workers/workers.ts`, `apps/api/src/bootstrap.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/files/file.service.ts` (`remove`), `apps/api/test/{authz,isolation,workers}/*`, `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts` (ADM-09, ADM-10, ADM-11, ADM-12), the two i18n catalogues, `docs/runbooks/jobs.md`, `alerts/rules` (two rules); for the browser flows (CR-ADM-14, authorised): `apps/web/e2e/visual.spec.ts` (four regions), `apps/web/e2e/support/e2e-fixtures.json` (the user `admReader`) and `apps/api/test/e2e-stack/serve.stack.ts` (the role of `admReader` and `governanceFixtures`: audit rows, retention rules, a review item, a hold, connectors, inbox events, a cursor).

## PRJ-2 (guarded PIMA lifecycle transitions and the project bank: ST-PRJ-002) decisions and open points
- ST-PRJ-002 (decision, a table the plan did not list): `getProjectLifecycle` must answer the history, the decision documents and who moved the project, and the story lists `tables: []`. Migration V1202 adds `project_stage_change` (append-only: guard trigger, no UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE for the runtime role, one row per project version, a member always gives a reason, the system always names its event). Alternative rejected: reading the audit trail back (a different retention, not a read model).
- ST-PRJ-002 (decision, K5): the transition table is domain code (`packages/db/src/prj-stage-machine.ts`, 15 edges) and a test holds it equal to the master data PROJECT_STAGE (`sequence`, `allowed_next`, `terminal`). It also carries the edges of the commands that other stories own (submit, decide, settlement.closed); this story makes only the six commands and the consumer of `capital.plan_published.v1`.
- ST-PRJ-002 (decision, guards): start needs `investment_decision_document_id` and the national code by the rule of the day (`MasterRules.nationalCode`: BLOCK refuses with 422 NATIONAL_CODE_REQUIRED or NATIONAL_CODE_FORMAT_INVALID, WARN lets it through and flags the audit row; a rule naming a stage this module does not know is read as required); suspend, complete and cancel need `decision_document_id`; resume and close take one if given. A document must exist in the tenant and be PENDING, EFFECTIVE or CORRECTED (else 422 DECISION_REQUIRED). A document field the command does not take is 422 VALIDATION_FAILED naming the field.
- ST-PRJ-002 (decision, obligations port): cancel is refused (409 OPEN_OBLIGATIONS_EXIST) while committed or disbursed capital exists or a reservation is ACTIVE; close is refused while capital is committed or reserved (money already paid is what a settled project is made of). The port `ProjectObligationsPort` reads `allocation_balance` and `fund_reservation` through `allocation_line.project_id`; CAP may replace the implementation behind the port when its own read exists (plan note of ST-CAP-003).
- ST-PRJ-002 (open, CAP producer): nothing emits `capital.plan_published.v1` yet. The consumer (`apps/api/src/modules/prj/plan-published.consumer.ts`, registered in `workers/outbox-consumers.ts`) takes the payload shape of CAP.md section 9 (`capital_plan_id`, `project_ids[]`), reads the truth from the plan (status PUBLISHED, a line of more than nothing in `plan_version` / `allocation_line`, the decision document of the plan) and not from the event, and fails a malformed payload (dead letter alert) instead of dropping it.
- ST-PRJ-002 (not done, ST-PRJ-011): the consumer of `settlement.closed.v1` (COMPLETED to SETTLED) is not built, as the plan says; the table has the edge.
- ST-PRJ-002 (contract gap, for the orchestrator): `getProjectLifecycle` and `listProjectPipeline` answer `type: object` in openapi.json, so the shapes are documented only in `apps/api/src/modules/prj/stage-view.ts`. Suggested schemas: PrjLifecycle (project_id, code, status, version, project_group, national_project_code, status_changed_at, history[], next[{operation, to, permission, allowed, guards[{key, state}]}], as_of) and PrjPipelinePage (items[card], total, next_cursor, as_of). The pipeline answers `total` (projects matching the filter in the scope of the caller) beyond what the operation lists.
- ST-PRJ-002 (decision, cache): the pages of the bank are cached in process under `t:{tenant}:prj:pipeline:{stage}:{digest}` for 60 s, the digest covering the scope predicate, the group, the size and the cursor, so two scopes never share a page. A committed move (command or consumer) clears the tenant; a process other than the one that moved the project may serve a page up to 60 s old, and a page read just before a move may be stored just after it (bounded by the same 60 s). A shared (Redis) cache replaces `InMemoryPipelineCache` behind the `PipelineCache` port.
- ST-PRJ-002 (decision, ranking): BR-PRJ-06 ranks by appraisal score (highest first, no score last), then by the number of milestones that are DONE (`project_milestone` has no GPMB or legal category yet, so every done milestone counts), then by how long the project has been in the stage. Refine when the milestone story adds the category. The cursor carries the rank key and the id of the last card.
- ST-PRJ-002 (decision, UI): the card of the board has no MetricTile (the component does not exist in the web application); the cards are plain collapsed cards. The document a move rests on is typed as its id (UUID) because no document picker exists yet; replace with a picker when DOC has a list read for the web. `capital_ready` is the national code the rule requires (the group is a condition of the PIPELINE stage in the database).
- ST-PRJ-002 (note, privacy): the reason of a move is free text kept in the audit row and shown in the history of `getProjectLifecycle` to everyone who can read the project; the event carries no free text, and no log carries the reason.
- ST-PRJ-002 (note, no four-eyes): the module document gives no maker-checker to these six commands (the table has none), so there is no self-approval refusal to test; the e2e flow covers the guard refusals, a stale version, the narrow cancel-only user and the read-only user instead.

## PRJ-3 (appraisal and admission to the project bank: ST-PRJ-003) decisions and open points
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, no read operation in the contract): the contract has no read of the appraisal sheets and no read of the criteria set (`getProjectLifecycle` is `type: object`). The lifecycle read now carries an `appraisal` block (`apps/api/src/modules/prj/appraisal-view.ts`), an explicit allowlist: the set of criteria in force (code, name, weight, threshold, awaiting flag and CXN), the sheets the caller may see, for a decision maker the mean, the recommendation of the threshold, which decisions need an override reason and the sheet a decision is addressed to (with the version for If-Match). It is `null` for a caller who neither holds `project.appraise` nor `project.appraisal.decide` on the project. A dedicated `listProjectAppraisals` (GET /projects/{id}/appraisals) would be cleaner; if the orchestrator adds it, move the block out of the lifecycle (the web reads one function, `toAppraisalView`).
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, sheets and rounds): `decideProjectAppraisal` is addressed to a SHEET id (`/project-appraisals/{id}/decision`) and its If-Match is the version of that sheet. The decision is taken for the round: every SUBMITTED sheet of the round becomes DECIDED together, with the same decision, reason, document, group and override reason (migration V1203 adds `decision_reason`, `override_reason`, `decided_group`, `note`). The round is the number of `submitProjectForAppraisal` moves of the project (at least 1, so a project that was placed in APPRAISAL without being sent is in round 1); a RETURN then a new submission opens round 2 and the sheets of round 1 stay as they were decided.
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, four eyes): the proposer of a project is whoever created it and whoever sent it to appraisal in any round. They neither score it nor decide on it (403 SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED), checked in the store and held by a database trigger (`project_appraisal_proposer_guard`) so a statement that bypassed the commands is refused as well. The module document does not forbid the appraiser from also being the decision maker (R1: one round, one decision maker); this is not enforced and would be a rule of the tenant.
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, override, BR-PRJ-04): a decision goes against the sheets when it differs from the recommendation of any sheet of the round, or admits a project whose mean score is under the threshold (the threshold recommends ADMIT from `pass_threshold`, REJECT below it, never RETURN). It then needs `override_reason` of at least 20 characters (422 OVERRIDE_REASON_REQUIRED); an override reason that is not needed is refused (422 VALIDATION_FAILED `override_reason`). An ADMIT recommendation is never produced by the server: the appraisers choose it, the threshold only marks the decisions that need a reason. The weight of a criterion is the `max_score` of the rule (the weights add up to 100); the threshold and the set are working assumptions awaiting ASSUMPTION-APPRAISAL-60 and the screens say so.
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, blind scoring): an appraiser sees only their own sheet (the decision maker sees all of the round), so nobody scores with another's score in front of them. The same appraiser scoring again replaces their sheet while the round is open (version +1; the audit row of each version keeps the previous total).
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, group): any group of QTQG, A, B, C is accepted for ADMIT (a value outside them is 422); the group the thresholds suggest (CXN-32) is shown on PRJ-09 and in the workspace but not enforced, because the table is a working assumption. Group and document are required for ADMIT (PROJECT_GROUP_REQUIRED, DECISION_REQUIRED) and the database repeats the group rule (`project_group_required_chk`).
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, authority): a project that names a `decision_authority_unit_id` is decided on only by someone who holds `project.appraisal.decide` over that unit (403 SCOPE_DENIED otherwise), in addition to the scope of the project.
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, cancel): `cancelProject` from APPRAISAL now voids the open sheets of the project (status VOID, version +1) in the same transaction; a decided or voided sheet is immutable (guard trigger) and no sheet can be deleted by the runtime role (REVOKE and `retained-history-grants`).
- ST-PRJ-003 (decision, If-Match of the record): the contract makes If-Match optional on `recordProjectAppraisal` (the generated client has it as not versioned), so the web screen does not send it (the second save of the same appraiser replaces the sheet); the API honours it when it is sent (409 VERSION_CONFLICT on another version).
- ST-PRJ-003 (shared code moved): `packages/db/src/prj-stage-support.ts` holds what the commands that move a project share (lock inside the scope, usable document, reason and date, the row of the history); `prj-stage-store.ts` imports it. `MasterRules.appraisalCriteria/appraisalCriteriaVersion` read the set in force or a given version. `reach.ts` (apps/api) holds the check of a grant over a unit that the project controller used privately.
- ST-PRJ-003 (open): PRJ-14 does not offer the submission (`STAGE_COMMANDS` keeps the six commands of ST-PRJ-002; the submission has its own screen PRJ-09 and its own store); the workspace PRJ-03 links to PRJ-09, PRJ-15 and PRJ-10 by the stage. `APPRAISAL_INCOMPLETE` is raised for a DRAFT sheet in the round, which the contract cannot create today (the check is tested with a sheet written directly).
- ST-PRJ-003 (open, evidence): PRJ-15 has no EvidenceViewer: the evidence of a score is the ids of documents of the tenant, typed in and checked by the server (a document of another tenant is refused); the viewer of the design needs the DOC reader.

## PRJ-4 (national project code: ST-PRJ-004) decisions and open points
- ST-PRJ-004 (decision, THE LAYOUT OF THE FILE IS A WORKING ASSUMPTION): no sample and no specification of the national system's file exists (CXN-01, D05), so `packages/kernel/src/national-code-file.ts` reads the smallest layout that names a project by what the register holds: a CSV/XLSX with the columns `internal_code` (`project.code`) and `national_project_code`, layout version `WORKING-ASSUMPTION-2026.1` (kept with every import). The adapter is PROPOSED (connector `NATIONAL_PIM_FILE`, the one the baseline gives each tenant); PRJ-21 says `Adapter: PROPOSED - nhập tệp thủ công` and calls nothing. Documented in `docs/prj-national-code-file.md`. When the real layout is confirmed only that kernel file and the version string change.
- ST-PRJ-004 (decision, crosswalk not used): the plan says "crosswalk translation of external codes", but the crosswalk of ST-MDM-007 maps external codes to MASTER RECORDS and a project is not one, so a code of the national system for a project has nothing to translate through. The file names a project by its internal code. If the national system ships its own project key, a project-level crosswalk is an MDM change request; nothing was invented.
- ST-PRJ-004 (decision, no overwrite): a project that holds a different code than the file says is never overwritten by an import: it is a difference `NATIONAL_CODE_MISMATCH` with an owner (the importer) and a due time (file import framework), settled by hand. A project without a code takes the file's. A code repeated inside a file is refused on the later rows (the first row keeps it), a code held by another project of the tenant is refused (both `NATIONAL_CODE_DUPLICATE`).
- ST-PRJ-004 (decision, dry run in the core): the framework had no dry run, so `packages/db/src/file-import-preview.ts` (`previewFileImport`) was added beside it, without touching `file-import-store.ts`: it plans and judges exactly as `run` does (same pure plan, same `examine`, same order of the keys) and writes nothing. Any adapter can use it; `docs/file-import.md` says so. The adapter adds `outcomeOf` (ASSIGN, UNCHANGED, MISMATCH).
- ST-PRJ-004 (decision, outcomes of the apply): ALREADY_IMPORTED is 409 `IMPORT_FILE_ALREADY_APPLIED` (the contract says so; the framework doc says the caller answers 200); a file that cannot be read is 422 with its code; a file none of whose rows can be taken is 422 `IMPORT_HAS_ERRORS` with the lines. The command rolls back on these, so a FAILED try is not stored (the DENIED audit row keeps the reason) and the connector is not marked FAILED by it; the framework records both when the caller commits, which this command cannot do and still answer 4xx.
- ST-PRJ-004 (decision, the contract has no `reason` on assignNationalProjectCode): "reason and audit when changing after IMPLEMENTATION" is read as: changing a code that was assigned, once the project is IMPLEMENTATION, SUSPENDED, COMPLETED or SETTLED, needs `evidence_document_id` (422 DECISION_REQUIRED); a first assignment does not; every change is audited with the previous and the new code. The same code again is recorded as unchanged (no version).
- ST-PRJ-004 (decision, rule port): `nationalCodeReading` (the one reading of NATIONAL_CODE_REQUIRED and NATIONAL_CODE_FORMAT, BLOCK or WARN) is exported, and `NationalCodeGate.assess` offers it to other modules. The start of the implementation answers `warnings: ["NATIONAL_CODE_REQUIRED"]` under WARN. Not done, CAP's story: the publication of a capital plan does not call the gate yet (nothing in CAP reads the rule today); it should refuse (BLOCK) or flag (WARN) the projects of the plan with `NationalCodeGate.assess(tx, projectIds, today)`.
- ST-PRJ-004 (contract, CR-PRJ-01, applied): `uploadNationalCodeFile` (POST /project-national-code-imports/files, multipart, `project.national_code.manage`) is in the contract; `national-code-file.controller.ts` is its door (the file is judged by its bytes, scanned, and only then its layout is read, so an infected file is FILE_NOT_CLEAN and one without the columns IMPORT_SCHEMA_INVALID). The import panel of PRJ-21 (`NationalCodeImportPanel.tsx`: upload, dry run row by row, apply once, the lines of IMPORT_HAS_ERRORS) uses it. No list of earlier imports is built: the register itself shows which projects have a code.
- ST-PRJ-004 (note): the national code column has a database CHECK (V1204: trimmed, not blank, at most 50, no control character); the unique index per tenant comes from V002 and the write turns a race on it into 409 under a savepoint.
- ST-PRJ-004 (decision, scope, changed after the wave-3 review): `importNationalProjectCodes` with `dry_run: false` requires `project.national_code.manage` on the WHOLE tenant (`holdsOnWholeTenant`, 403 `SCOPE_DENIED` otherwise): the national file spans all projects and its SHA-256 is consumed once, so a unit-scoped apply would quarantine the rows outside the scope and block the tenant-wide officer with `IMPORT_FILE_ALREADY_APPLIED`. The dry run may stay scoped (a unit-scoped officer previews the rows of their units; the rest look like projects that do not exist) and judges `NATIONAL_CODE_DUPLICATE` only against projects in the caller's scope, so it never reveals a code held outside it; the apply, being whole-tenant, judges all. `assignNationalProjectCode` stays scoped to the project. PRJ-21 disables the apply for a caller who does not hold the permission on the whole tenant and says why.

## PRJ-1 (leader in charge and task forces, ST-PRJ-005) decisions and open points
- ST-PRJ-005 (contract, CR-PRJ-02, applied): the reads `get_resources_project_accountability` (`project.read`; filters `project_id`, `status`) and `get_resources_task_force` (new permission `taskforce.read`; filter `status`; each force carries its terms) are in the contract; `accountability-reads.controller.ts` answers them from `PrjAccountabilityStore.list` and `PrjTaskForceStore.list` as allowlist DTOs, filtered by the unit tree before the page is cut (assignments by the project, forces by the scope unit). A holder of `taskforce.manage` without `taskforce.read` sees no force.
- ST-PRJ-005 (decision, who may administer): the five commands need the permission on the WHOLE tenant (`holdsOnWholeTenant`, 403 `SCOPE_DENIED` for a grant on a unit subtree), as the module document gives them to "Quản trị phân công (Văn phòng UBND/Sở): toàn tenant"; the screens say so and hide the forms from a caller who holds the permission on a subtree only (they read the grants with `getMyEffectiveAccess`).
- ST-PRJ-005 (decision, assignments): a period is `valid_from` to `valid_until`, both days inclusive; a new assignment starts today at the latest (so what the register shows as in force is in force) and an end lies between the start and today. The periods of one role of one project never overlap, ended ones included (BR-PRJ-10, database `EXCLUDE` in V1205); a second ACTIVE `LEADER_IN_CHARGE` also breaks the partial unique index of V002. Both are 409 `LEADER_ALREADY_ASSIGNED`, also for a race (the loser of the lock is told by the check, a writer that bypasses the lock by the savepoint). The next leader starts the day after the last day of the previous one. An assignment is never edited (guard trigger): it is ended (`STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED` when it has ended already) and another is made. The leader in charge of PRJ-01 is still read from the ACTIVE row; the pages of the project bank are cleared after both commands.
- ST-PRJ-005 (decision, LEADER_NOT_ELIGIBLE): the person must be a member of the tenant (another tenant's is 404), in force, on the staff (not a guest contractor or a service account) and hold `project.read` over the project through a grant in force on the owner unit, the investor unit or the project itself.
- ST-PRJ-005 (decision, task forces): a force has its code (unique in the tenant, 409 `TASK_FORCE_CODE_DUPLICATE`), purpose, lead, a scope unit (its subtree is the scope) and terms. The lead sits as the first member with the role LEAD; no other member takes LEAD (there is no command to change the lead). A member (and the lead) must be in force, on the staff, and hold a grant that meets the subtree of the scope unit: the whole tenant, a subtree on the same branch (above or below), a unit at or below it, or a project owned or invested in inside it; a grant on a contract does not count (422 `MEMBER_OUT_OF_SCOPE`, a member of another tenant 404). A followed project must belong to the tenant (404) and be owned by a unit inside the scope (422 `MEMBER_OUT_OF_SCOPE` on `project_id`). The same member for the same project twice at once is 409 `DUPLICATE_KEY` (no dedicated code in the registry). Adding a member raises the version of the force (who sits in it is part of what it is), so a dissolution made on a stale list is refused with 409 `VERSION_CONFLICT`. Dissolving ends the force and every open term today in one transaction; the reason is kept in the audit row (the table has no column for it); a dissolved force takes no member, in the command (409 `TASK_FORCE_DISSOLVED`) and in the database (trigger). `addTaskForceMember` answers the id and the new version of the FORCE.
- ST-PRJ-005 (decision, history): `project_accountability`, `task_force` and `task_force_member` cannot be deleted or truncated by the runtime role (retention permanent / ten years; `retained-history-grants.test.ts` lists them), and the columns that say who, what and since when never change (guard triggers of V1205).
- ST-PRJ-005 (open, CXN-33): the content of Directive 162/CD-TTg (the roles, the template of a task force) is not known; the three accountability roles and the three member roles are data of the contract. The default explanation deadline is for ST-PRJ-006.
- ST-PRJ-005 (browser): `prj-accountability-flows.spec.ts` runs the three flows against the real stack (users `accountabilityAdmin` and `accountabilityReader`, roles on the whole tenant on purpose); the visual regions of PRJ-17 and PRJ-18 compare the frame and the title with the mockup baselines.

## NOT-1 ST-NOT-002 (delivery engine) decisions and open points

- Scope (decision): the delivery engine is the kernel rules (`packages/kernel/src/delivery.ts`: status graph, retry schedule with jitter, circuit breaker, status of a notification from its attempts), the adapter port (`channel-port.ts`), the SQL (`packages/db/src/delivery-store.ts`, `delivery-engine.ts`), and in the API the dispatcher, the in-memory adapter, the webhook, the inbox adapter, replay, cancel and the two reads (`apps/api/src/notifications/delivery/`). Runbook: `docs/runbooks/notification-delivery.md`. Migration `V1440__not1_delivery_engine.sql`.
- Channel plan (decision): `channel_plan` is an ordered fallback chain of the external channels, not a fan-out. Only the first channel that has an Active account and an Active template is sent to; the next is tried when a channel is exhausted (five sends) or fails permanently. NOT 3.1 step 5 ("an attempt per channel of the plan") and BR-NOT-03 ("next channel of the plan") disagree; the chain never sends one message on two paid channels. The inbox (`IN_APP`) is independent of the chain: it is delivered by the insert and is first for CRITICAL and HIGH.
- Where the plan comes from (decision): `workflow_version.definition_json.channels` is a list of `{channel, templateCode}` (the inbox without a template). ST-NOT-004 (workflow designer) owns the editor and must write this member; `definitionOf` validates it. The template is the Active revision of that code for the channel (`vi-VN` first). ST-NOT-005 owns the template editor and the `content_ast` format; the engine renders `{subject?, body}` with `{{variable}}` for the variables declared in `variable_schema` and supplied by `payload.notification.variables`, and refuses a send whose placeholder is undeclared or missing (`TEMPLATE_RENDER_FAILED`, permanent).
- Honest adapters (decision): no real e-mail, SMS or Zalo provider exists (D06). A channel without an adapter is MISSING and its sends are suppressed with `ADAPTER_MISSING`; the in-memory e-mail adapter `smtp-relay` (MOCK) is registered only outside production (`MACHVON_ENVIRONMENT` named and not production or dr); MOCK in production and PROPOSED never send. The e-mail adapter of the story assumption (a domestic SMTP relay) is to be written when the relay is chosen: implement `ChannelAdapter`, register it in `defaultChannelAdapters`, give it its callback signing scheme.
- Not done, needs the owners: consent (BR-NOT-07), quiet hours (BR-NOT-08), digest (BR-NOT-09) and the channel budget and daily cap (BR-NOT-13) are ST-NOT-003, ST-NOT-008 and ST-NOT-010; they belong in `DeliveryDispatcher.preflight` (before a send is taken up) and in `DeliveryEngine.startChain` (when the plan is made). Until then an external send is made to an e-mail address of a member, which BR-NOT-07 allows without consent for a public-duty address; the SMS, Zalo and WhatsApp channels have no adapter, so none can send. The push channels need `device_registration` (ST-NOT-006) and the token vault; the dispatcher has no address source for them.
- Webhook secrets (decision): the vault of the platform does not exist, so `webhook_secret_ref` is resolved from the environment (`MACHVON_SECRET_<REFERENCE>` = `keyId:secret`, comma separated while a provider rotates). A reference with no value refuses every callback of the account. Replace `EnvironmentWebhookSecrets` when the vault adapter exists (KERNEL secret store).
- Callback storage (decision): the raw body is stored under the kind `webhook` in the same object root as the FILES module (`MACHVON_OBJECT_ROOT`), once per provider and event (the first body is the evidence).
