The default root is duplicated from `modules/files/index.ts` because FILES does not expose its storage port; FILES should export it (then `defaultWebhookPayloads` takes it from there).
- Callbacks for an unknown message id (decision, differs from BR-NOT-06): the spec holds them PENDING_MAPPING for one hour and then DEAD. The inbox processor of ST-ADM-018 retries after 1, 5 and 30 minutes and parks the event as DEAD after the tenth failure (about four hours). Closing that gap needs a handler outcome "park as DEAD at once" in `IntegrationInboxProcessor`.
- Tenant of a callback (decision): found from `channel_account.external_account_id` by the SECURITY DEFINER function `machvon.channel_accounts_by_external_id` (control role only; it walks the tenants of the registry like `memberships_of_user`). If two tenants hold the same external account id on one channel the callback is a 404, never delivered to one of them. Cost grows with the number of tenants; an index table (tenant, channel, external id) kept by a trigger is the way out if a deployment has thousands of tenants.
- Replay (decision): the contract types the body as `Decision` (approve, return, reject); a replay is a go-ahead, so only `approve` is accepted (422 otherwise). `ReasonOnly` would fit better. A channel that has used its five sends cannot take a sixth: the replay then creates a new notification (`replay_of`, `replay_seq`, dedupe key of `replay:<root>:<n>`, external channels only) and the original keeps its Failed or Unknown record with its version moved. The inbox listing of a person shows only notifications whose plan holds `IN_APP`, so a replay copy never appears twice.
- Permissions (decision): replay, cancel and the two reads need the permission on the whole tenant (403 `SCOPE_DENIED`, an empty list and 404 for a grant on a subtree): the queue spans the people of every unit. The system role NOTIFICATION_ADMIN holds `notification.*` only, which does not include `delivery_attempt.read`, so a NOTIFICATION_ADMIN cannot open NOT-11 to NOT-13 until the IAM matrix gives the role that read (IAM 2 table names "notification.*"); the owner and a custom role can.
- Contract (CR-NOT-01, done): `getMyNotificationPreferences` (GET /me/notification-preferences, `PreferenceView`) and `version` on the `ConsentPage` items are in openapi.json; NOT-03 (`PreferencesScreen`) is built on them, with the handler `MyPreferencesReadController`. **Deferred (needs a design decision on public operations):** the unsubscribe link (RFC 8058, `POST /unsubscribe/{token}`, no session) and the STOP/HUY word on an inbound channel message (OMNI). The pure parts are written and tested but not in the tree: `notification-unsubscribe.ts` (signed token with expiry, `listUnsubscribeHeaders`, `isStopKeyword`) and its test, kept in `not-003-pending-read` of the scratchpad with the operation sketch (`openapi-snippet.json` item 4). Open questions for the design: which key signs the link (platform vault), how a public operation finds its tenant (the token carries it), rate limiting and audit of an unauthenticated write. Until then a person withdraws only inside the application (NOT-17).
- Metrics: `MetricsSink` has no histogram, so `delivery_latency_seconds{channel}` is a gauge of the last observation (creation to acceptance). The exporter does not exist yet (KERNEL-050).
- Reconciliation tasks (`provider_reconciliation_task`) opened by a lost result are closed by the engine itself when the result is learnt (a callback, or the answer of `lookup`), with the worker identity as `resolved_by`. A task that is still open after the day has no screen: OMNI-11 or NOT-13 should list `ProviderCallbackStore.openTasks` and offer `resolveTask` with a note.
- Retention: delivery attempts are removed after 365 days by the retention job (ST-ADM-017); the status items, their history and the tasks of KERNEL-1 are kept, and the inbox payload files follow the inbox rules.

## NOT-1 ST-NOT-003 (preferences, quiet hours and consent history) decisions and open points

- Scope (decision): V1441 (`notification_preference_profile`, guards and checks of `contact_consent`), `packages/kernel/src/notification-policy.ts` (the consent decision table BR-NOT-07, the channel names, quiet hours by time zone BR-NOT-08), `packages/db/src/contact-consent-store.ts`, `notification-preference-store.ts`, `delivery-gate.ts`, the gate called by `DeliveryEngine.claim`, in the API `apps/api/src/notifications/consents/` and `preferences/`, and the screen NOT-17 (`ConsentHistoryScreen`). Digest (BR-NOT-09, ST-NOT-008) and the channel budget (BR-NOT-13, ST-NOT-010) are not done: `digest_mode` is stored and returned only.
- Where the gate runs (decision): once, in the transaction that takes a send up (`DeliveryEngine.claim`, after the checks of the notification and before the circuit), under the lock of the notification, so a consent withdrawn before that transaction can never be sent on and no cache can be stale (the reads go to the database; `not.consent.changed.v1` is still emitted for OMNI). NOT 3.1 step 5 also asks for a check at creation; it is not made there because the fallback decision must use the facts of the moment of sending anyway, and a check at creation would change the staged e2e of NOT-11 to NOT-13 (replay queues sends for a person with no consent). The dispatcher takes a new send up within one tick (5 s). Order: preference of the channel (`PREFERENCE_OFF`), consent (`NO_CONSENT`), quiet hours (the send waits: `next_retry_at` is the end of the hours, `error_code` is `QUIET_HOURS` until it is taken up). A suppressed send moves the plan to the next channel; one that waits does not.
- Consent (decision): BR-NOT-07 as data (`CONSENT_DECISION_TABLE`). The inbox and the duty messages (OPERATIONAL, SECURITY, REMINDER) on a push need none. **E-mail needs consent for every purpose**: the module cannot tell an address of the agency from a personal one (no registry of agency domains), and the brief of the story says no external channel without consent. When ADM gives that registry, add the public-duty row for e-mail to the table and pass the classification of the address to the gate (CXN-PDP-01). A SECURITY message is covered by an OPERATIONAL consent and a DIGEST by an ANNOUNCEMENT one (`consentPurposeOf`); the contract has no SECURITY consent. The latest consent decides (`Expired` and `Withdrawn` do not allow); the table forbids deleting or rewriting one (guard trigger, no DELETE for `machvon_app`), a regrant is a new row; one live consent per person, channel and purpose (409 `CONSENT_ALREADY_GRANTED`, also under a race). The evidence reference and the reason of a withdrawal stay in the database; the audit row is PERSONAL and carries neither.
- Identity of a channel (decision, partial): `CHANNEL_IDENTITY_UNVERIFIED` is raised when the account has no phone (SMS, Zalo, WhatsApp) or no live registered device (push). The phone is not verified by OTP yet (no such state exists), so "verified" means "present".
- Preferences (decision): the whole set is replaced under one version kept in `notification_preference_profile` (V002 had the quiet hours on every row, with no version for a PUT). Absent means on. A person who never saved has the default 21:00-07:00 in Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh; **a PUT without `quiet_start` and `quiet_end` turns the quiet hours off**. The category of an event is the first segment of its type (`report` of `report.overdue`), `*` is every category. The inbox of a category that holds a Published workflow of priority CRITICAL or HIGH cannot be switched off (422 `MANDATORY_CHANNEL_LOCKED`; the contract says CRITICAL, HIGH is included because the plan forces the inbox for HIGH as well). The consumer drops the inbox from the plan of an ordinary event for a recipient who switched it off, and creates no notification when the plan is then empty. The columns `quiet_start`, `quiet_end` and `time_zone` of `notification_preference` are no longer read (to be dropped by a contract migration).
- Quiet hours (decision): PUSH, SMS, Zalo and WhatsApp only, never the inbox or e-mail; on the wall clock of the person (DST safe). An urgent message breaks them only when its workflow has `quietHoursOverride: true` and priority CRITICAL (`definitionOf` refuses the flag for any other priority); each such send writes the audit row `notification.quiet_hours_override` in the transaction that takes it up and counts `quiet_hours_override_total`. A retry that falls in the quiet hours waits too. ST-NOT-004 (workflow designer) must write `quietHoursOverride` into `definition_json`.
- Push (open): the dispatcher has no address source for the push channels (ST-NOT-006), so a push that passes the gate fails with `NO_ADDRESS`. The tests use SMS for the quiet-hours rule (UAT-AC27, UAT-AC185 say push).
- Replay (open): NOT 3.6 wants 422 `CONSENT_DENIED` when a replay finds no consent; the replay queues the send and the gate suppresses it when it is taken up.
- Contract (needs the orchestrator): `getMyNotificationPreferences` (GET /me/notification-preferences) is missing, so NOT-03 is staged; `ConsentPage` items need `version`; the unsubscribe link (RFC 8058) and the STOP word have no public operation. All staged with the exact snippets in `not-003-pending-read` of the scratchpad (README there). The 38 keys `not.pref.*` and the files of NOT-03 wait there as well.
- Retention (open): the consent evidence is kept 5 years after withdrawal (CXN-PDP-02) and preferences until the membership ended plus one year: the retention job (ST-ADM-017) has no policy for these two tables yet; nothing deletes them.
- Terms (open): the screen sends `consent_text_version` = `consent-2026-1` (a constant of `consents/model.ts`); there is no page of the terms to link to yet.

## ISS-2 (ST-ISS-002: create, assign, work, comment and transfer issues) decisions and open points
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, where the calendar versions are kept): the `CREATED` row of `issue_event` carries `sla_policy_id`, `sla_policy_code`, `sla_policy_revision`, `calendar_version_ids` (one per year the count passed through), `response_due_at` and `due_at`; `createIssue` calls `IssSlaDeadlines.calculate` in its own transaction and the issue stores `sla_policy_id`, `response_due_at` and `due_at`. Nothing recounts them (a trigger refuses a changed `response_due_at`; `due_at` moves only by an extension, ST-ISS-003).
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, V1061): a unit transfer writes the `issue_event` kind `TRANSFERRED` (payload `from_unit_id`, `to_unit_id`, the reason in `note`), which the design list lacked; ISS.md section 6 now lists it.
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, V1061): `issue.client_operation_id` and `issue.client_request_hash` (unique per tenant, reporter and operation id; ISS.md section 6 and `ERD.mmd` list them). The same operation id of the same person answers the issue that exists (audit `iss.issue_open_replayed`, no event) and another request under that id is 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; `createIssue` keeps its idempotency record for 7 days (`ttl: extended`, offline phone). The hash covers the whole normalised request, position included.
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, BR-ISS-07): `issue_source_chk` (a manual issue has no source object and every other source names one) and the partial unique index `issue_open_source_uq` (one issue that is not closed per `(source_module, source_ref_id)`) are in the database; on the command the duplicate is 409 `DUPLICATE_SOURCE_OPEN`. The consumers of ISS-11 that "skip quietly" must catch that code; they are a later story.
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, scope): an issue is in the scope of a grant when its owner unit lies in the unit scope of the grant, or the grant names its project or its contract; the person who reported an issue also reads it and comments on it wherever it lies, provided they hold the permission somewhere. The owner and investor units of the project do not widen the scope. An issue outside the scope, or of another tenant, is 404; one the caller can read but not act on is 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`. `createIssue` needs `issue.create` over the place named in the body (unit, project, contract).
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, transfer): `transferIssueUnit` needs `issue.assign` over the issue where it lies now, not over the receiving unit (the point is to hand it to a unit the caller does not belong to). It keeps the status, the assignee, `due_at`, `response_due_at` and `overdue_since`; the person it is given to may not work issues in the new unit, and then the receiving unit reassigns (the assign command checks the assignee again). Nothing in the database refuses a person from another unit as the assignee of an issue that moved; if the business wants the transfer to clear the assignment the state machine needs ASSIGNED to OPEN.
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, assignee): the assignee must be an active membership that holds `issue.work` over the place of the issue (422 `ASSIGNEE_OUT_OF_SCOPE`, also for a stranger: the answer never says which). ISS-03 chooses from `listIssueAssignees` (CR-ISS-14, `issue.assign`), which lists exactly the people the command accepts, so the coordinator needs no `membership.read`.
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, days overdue): `overdue_days` of the reads is the number of working days after the Vietnam date of `overdue_since` up to the day of `closed_at` or today, counted on the working calendar of the policy; it is null when the count passes a year with no published calendar (never guessed). Nothing in ISS-2 sets `overdue_since` (the job `issue-sla` does, a later story); the database refuses to clear or move it once set.
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, privacy, NFR-PRIVACY-02): the position is stored only with consent (coordinates without `geo_consent` are 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` naming `geo_consent`, and `issue_geo_consent_chk` / `issue_geo_chk` hold it in the database); the audit row and the outbox events say `geo_recorded`, never where; the reads give the position to staff only, with the low-accuracy flag above 50 m (BR-ISS-06). A guest contractor sees the work, not the people, the source object, the position or the notes of others; the description and the notes of an issue from a land complaint (`source_module = LND`) are shown only to those who hold `land.household.read` over the issue (UAT-AC354); the title stays visible, so the consumers must keep personal data out of it (ISS 3.1 step 8).
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, events): `iss.issue.opened/assigned/started/commented/transferred.v1` carry ids, the state and the dates (`due_at`, `response_due_at`, `assignee_id`, `previous_assignee_id`, `from_unit_id`), never the title, the description, the notes or the position. A comment does not change the issue (its version stays); its event key is `issue:<id>:comment:<event id>`. The escalation clocks (job `issue-sla`) and the notifications (NOT) read these events and the dates of `opened`; ISS-2 emits only.
- ST-ISS-002 (decision, database): the trigger `issue_guard` holds the state machine of ISS 4 (OPEN to ASSIGNED, ASSIGNED to IN_PROGRESS or ESCALATED, IN_PROGRESS to ASSIGNED, ESCALATED or PENDING_VERIFICATION, ESCALATED to IN_PROGRESS, PENDING_VERIFICATION to CLOSED or ASSIGNED, CLOSED to REOPENED, REOPENED to ASSIGNED) and the parts of an issue that never change (code, project, contract, category, severity, source, SLA policy, reporter, `opened_at`, `response_due_at`, the position, the operation id); `issue_event` is append-only by trigger and by grant; DELETE and TRUNCATE are revoked on `issue` and `issue_event` (`retained-history-grants.test.ts`). The next stories that move an issue (escalation, verification, closing, reopening) use only these edges; ESCALATED to ASSIGNED and ASSIGNED to PENDING_VERIFICATION are not edges.
- ST-ISS-002 (not done, photos): the contract has no generic upload of a document, so ISS-02 and ISS-03 offer no photo or file; `commentIssue` already takes the id of a version scanned clean (422 `DOCUMENT_NOT_CLEAN` otherwise) and writes an `EVIDENCE_ADDED` row. The photo on ISS-02 belongs to the mobile flow (MOB) and to the document pipeline of ISS-003.
- ST-ISS-002 (not done, list filters): `get_resources_issue` declares only `limit` and `cursor`, so ISS-01 reads every page and filters in the browser. A server filter by status, project or unit is a contract change.
- ST-ISS-002 (not done, metrics): `iss_open_total`, `iss_sla_breach_total`, `iss_escalation_pending` and `iss_source_dedupe_total` (ISS 9) are not emitted; they belong with the job and the consumers.
- ST-ISS-002 (browser lane): `apps/web/e2e/iss-issue-flows.spec.ts` makes its own issue category and CRITICAL policy (the stack seeds two categories that `iss-flows.spec.ts` counts), so it runs after `iss-flows.spec.ts`; the visual regions of ISS-01 to ISS-03 are the frame and the title (the mockups are generic pages).

## ISS-3, ISS-4, ISS-5 (ST-ISS-003 verification and closure, ST-ISS-004 escalation, ST-ISS-005 intake) decisions and open points

- ST-ISS-003 (decision, V1063): the limit of extensions (2) and the window of a reopening (30 days) are working assumptions of the tenant rules (R-ISS-01) held once in code (`ISSUE_MAX_EXTENSIONS`, `ISSUE_REOPEN_WINDOW_DAYS` of `iss-issue-closure.ts`, sent to the screens in the issue read as `extensions` and `reopen_window_days`). The database holds the shape of the rule, not the number: `issue.extension_count` (new column) and the trigger `issue_guard` let `due_at` move only later and only together with a counted extension, and let `reopen_count` move only with CLOSED to REOPENED. Move the numbers to rule data when the pilot tenant signs them.
- ST-ISS-003 (decision, who "asks" for an extension): the contract has one command, `extendIssueDeadline` (`issue.extend`), and no command that records a request. The person who asks is the one who answers for the deadline, the assignee (UAT-AC353 names u.cv.hd, the handler, as the requester): the assignee cannot grant the extension of their own issue (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`). The event kind `EXTENSION_REQUESTED` of the design stays unused until the contract gets a command to propose an extension (a contract change for the orchestrator: `requestIssueExtension`, `issue.work`, writes `EXTENSION_REQUESTED`); then the approver must also differ from whoever proposed.
- ST-ISS-003 (decision, "adds the days on the working calendar"): one extension adds at most the resolution time of the policy of the issue counted on the working calendar of that policy (`IssSlaDeadlines.addMinutes`), from the later of the present deadline and now; the new deadline must be later than the present one and in the future (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` `new_due_at`). An extension never clears `overdue_since`; when the old deadline had passed and the job had not noticed yet, the extension writes it first (the deadline that was missed).
- ST-ISS-003 (decision, independence): the closer is never the assignee, whatever the policy says: `issue_verifier_chk` of the generated schema is unconditional, so `require_independent_verifier = false` is stored and shown but cannot let an assignee close their own issue (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`). The evidence the policy asks for is judged at the request for verification (422 `EVIDENCE_REQUIRED`, documents of the tenant scanned clean, at most 20) and again by the database: the trigger `issue_guard` refuses CLOSED without a VERIFICATION_REQUESTED event, and without evidence in it when the policy asks. `closeIssue`, `reopenIssue`, `requestIssueVerification` check the state before the version, so a command sent again under a new key after it succeeded is 409 `ISSUE_STATE_INVALID` (UAT-AC397); the same key answers the first body (the interceptor).
- ST-ISS-003 (decision, giving the result back): the verifier refuses the result with `assignIssue` (PENDING_VERIFICATION to ASSIGNED), which now needs the reason (`note`) and withdraws `resolved_at`; the event is REASSIGNED with `outcome: VERIFICATION_REFUSED`. The person who refuses holds `issue.assign`, not necessarily `issue.close`. Closing resolves the open escalations of the issue; a reopening clears the closure (`closed_at`, `closed_by`, `verifier_id`, `issue_closed_fields_chk`) and begins the escalation again (`escalation_level` back to 0).
- ST-ISS-003 (not done): the maker-checker matrix of the authorization tests (`APPROVER_COMMANDS`) lists approvals by "who made the object"; closing and extending are independent of the ASSIGNEE, which the matrix does not model, so they are covered by their own tests (store, HTTP, browser) and not added there. Evidence is typed as document version ids (the contract has no upload for an issue, see ST-ISS-002 not done, photos). The reopening is a screen of ISS-07; the weekly matrix (ISS-06, ISS-08) is not built.
- ST-ISS-004 (decision, levels): ISS.md 3.2 and UAT-AC352 give escalation levels 1 (unit), 2 (the person in charge, at `escalate_l2_pct`) and 3 (the task force, at `escalate_l3_pct`), while the acceptance text of the plan speaks of "level 1 at 100 percent and level 2 at 150". The module document wins: the job raises level 2 to the owner unit at 100 percent (the leaders of the project by `project_accountability` are the named recipients of the notification) and level 3 to the task force at 150 percent; level 1 is raised by hand only (`escalateIssue`, to any unit of the tenant, the owner unit by default). A manual escalation must be to a level above the one the issue has reached (422 `ESCALATION_LEVEL_INVALID`), levels 1 and 2 name a unit and level 3 an active task force that answers for the issue (422 `TASK_FORCE_NOT_ACTIVE` for a dissolved one, 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` `task_force_id` for an unknown or unrelated one), and the issue must be ASSIGNED or IN_PROGRESS (the only edges to ESCALATED).
- ST-ISS-004 (decision, thresholds): a threshold at or above 100 percent is counted from the current `due_at` (an extension moves it): 100 percent is the deadline, 150 percent is `resolve_hours` * 0.5 working hours after it; a threshold below 100 percent is counted from `opened_at`. The working time is that of the policy calendar and the ISS window 07:30-17:00 (`addWorkingTime`); a year without a published calendar is never guessed: the level waits for a later pass (`withoutCalendar`). `overdue_since` is the missed deadline itself (`due_at`), written once with the timeline event `SLA_BREACHED` by the first pass after it; the version of the issue does not move for it.
- ST-ISS-004 (decision, states): the job looks at OPEN, ASSIGNED, IN_PROGRESS, ESCALATED and REOPENED issues, not at an issue that waits for its verification (the delay is the verifier's, not the handler's). The escalation of an OPEN or REOPENED issue is recorded and raises `escalation_level`, but the status stays (the machine has no edge from them to ESCALATED); the acceptance puts an ESCALATED issue back to IN_PROGRESS and leaves any other status alone. One level 2 and one level 3 escalation exist per issue while open or accepted (`issue_escalation_open_uq`); closing resolves them, a reopening lets the job raise them again. The receiver has `ESCALATION_ACCEPT_HOURS` (8) working hours to accept, a working assumption (the design asks for "a determined deadline"); the board says when it is late, nothing escalates further on that account.
- ST-ISS-004 (decision, the task force): PRJ owns the task force tables and the interface `activeTaskForce(project, unit, at)` of R-ISS-03 has not been published, so `TaskForceDirectory` reads `task_force`, `task_force_member` and `project_accountability` (read-only, nothing else of PRJ). A task force answers for an issue when it is ACTIVE on the day (the last day of its term counts) and one of its members is assigned to the project of the issue, or its scope unit is the unit that owns the issue or one above it; a member of the project first, then the deepest scope unit, then the code. Without one the level 3 waits (`withoutTaskForce`). When PRJ publishes the interface, `TaskForceDirectory` is the only thing to replace.
- ST-ISS-004 (decision, who may accept and see): the receiver of a unit escalation is whoever holds `issue.escalation.accept` over the target unit; the receiver of a task force escalation is its lead or a member in force (a grant of the permission is also needed to reach the command). A person who is not the receiver gets 403 `SCOPE_DENIED` (404 when they cannot read the issue either). The board (`listIssueSlaBreaches`, `issue.read`) lists the open and accepted escalations of the issues that are not closed in the read scope of the caller plus the ones addressed to them whether or not their grants reach the issue; each line says whether the caller may accept it now. A guest contractor sees the level and the dates, not the title, the receiver or who accepted. The issue read gives the escalations and, to a reader who may escalate, the task force that answers for the issue (`active_task_force`) so the screen can offer level 3.
- ST-ISS-004 (events and notification): `iss.issue.escalated.v1` is registered in `notification_event_type` by V1064 (payload: ids, the level, the trigger, the dates, `notification.recipients`: the leaders of the project for levels 1 and 2, the lead and the members of the task force for level 3), `iss.issue.escalation_accepted.v1` is not registered (nobody is told). A recipient who does not hold `issue.read` over the issue is dropped by the notification core, so the people of a task force need that permission as well; no workflow is seeded, a tenant that has none gets no notification (`notification_no_workflow_total`).
- ST-ISS-004 (job and metrics): `issue-sla` every 15 minutes (`docs/runbooks/jobs.md`), at most `SLA_PASS_LIMIT` (500) issues per tenant and pass, the oldest deadline first, an issue a command is acting on is skipped (`for update skip locked`). Counters `iss_sla_breach_total{level=overdue|2|3}`, gauges `iss_open_total{severity}` and `iss_escalation_pending` (the first two metrics of ISS 9 that ST-ISS-002 left open). No alert is defined for a late acceptance yet. The timeline order of the events of one transaction is by the statement time (`clock_timestamp()`), not the transaction time.
- ST-ISS-004 (data model additions for the data dictionary): `issue.extension_count` (V1063); on `issue_escalation` (V1064): CHECKs `issue_escalation_force_chk`, `issue_escalation_reason_chk`, `issue_escalation_accept_chk`, the triggers `issue_escalation_insert_guard` and `issue_escalation_guard`, DELETE and TRUNCATE revoked from the runtime role (`retained-history-grants.test.ts`); the trigger `issue_guard` lets `escalation_level` fall only on a reopening.
- ST-ISS-004 (not done): the cache of ISS 9, the weekly snapshot job (`issue-weekly-snapshot`) and the weekly matrix; the late-acceptance alert; the escalation of an issue to a task force by a person who is not allowed to read the task force (no PRJ read exists, the issue read offers the one that answers for it).
- ST-ISS-005 (decision, the events): the intake reads `ctr.guarantee.expired.v1` (only when `issue_required`, its own members `source_reference`, `project_id`, `contract_id`, `kind`, `expires_on`) and, for the events of the other producers, the common members `source_ref_id` (uuid), `project_id` (uuid), `reference_code` (a code of the source object), optional `contract_id`, `owner_unit_id` and `severity`: `pmt.permit.expired.v1`, `pay.advance.overdue.v1`, `pay.reconciliation.overdue.v1`, `lnd.complaint.filed.v1`, `lnd.deadline.breached.v1` (`INTAKE_SPECS` of `iss-intake.ts` is the contract, tested in `iss-intake.test.ts`). The producers (PMT permit-expiry job ST-PMT-004, PAY, LND) do not emit these events yet; each producing story must send exactly these members and no personal data. `qms.ncr.raised.v1` and `ast.warranty_claim.overdue.v1` (R2) are not read.
- ST-ISS-005 (decision, privacy, NFR-PRIVACY-02): only the members above are read; every other member of a payload (a household name, an identity number, an address, a phone, free text) is never read, copied, logged or audited. A `reference_code` that is not a code (letters, digits, `._/-`, 3 to 40 characters, no spaces) or that is 9 or 12 digits is replaced by the first 8 characters of the source id and counted (`iss_intake_reference_replaced_total`). The title is `<kind of problem>: <reference code>` and the description repeats it with the source module and the category key, in English as stored data (a screen that wants the words of the user translates by `source_module`); the description and notes of an LND issue stay hidden from readers without `land.household.read` (ST-ISS-002).
- ST-ISS-005 (decision, category and unit): the tenant keeps one ISSUE_CATEGORY entry per source with the business keys `LAND_CLEARANCE` (LND), `PERMIT` (PMT), `PAYMENT` (PAY) and `CONTRACT_GUARANTEE` (CTR) (`INTAKE_CATEGORY_KEYS`), and an SLA policy for the severity of each event; the issue is filed in the unit that owns the project (or `owner_unit_id` of the event) with the default severity of its kind (HIGH for an expired permit, advance, deadline and guarantee, MEDIUM for a complaint and a reconciliation item) unless the event names one. A missing category (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` `category_key`) or policy (422 `SLA_POLICY_MISSING`) fails the delivery, which the relay retries and then dead-letters with the alert `OUTBOX_EVENT_DEAD` to the owner of the event; once the tenant defines them a replay opens the issue. An event for a project that is not the tenant's is counted and dropped (`iss_intake_ignored_total{reason}`). Seeding the categories belongs to the tenant baseline (MDM-3); none is seeded here.
- ST-ISS-005 (decision, dedupe): BR-ISS-07 holds in the database (`issue_open_source_uq`): the second event for a source object that has an issue that is not closed is a quiet duplicate (`iss_source_dedupe_total{source}`) and changes nothing, not even the title; a closed source gets a new issue. The issue opens in the name of the system (`reported_by` = the worker membership), its operation id is derived from the delivery, and the audit row `iss.issue_opened` (with `intake_event_type`) and `iss.issue.opened.v1` are written in the same transaction as the inbox row (`consumeOnce`). The consumer is wired in `createOutboxConsumers` (no flag).
- ST-ISS-005 (not done): ISS-11 as a screen of its own (the list of automatic issues, P1): ISS-01 already shows the source column and the source module on the card; the weekly matrix; the intake of QMS and AST events; a producer-side contract test in PMT, PAY and LND (the payload is tested here from the ISS side).
- Shared files touched by ISS-3 to ISS-5: `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `jobs.ts`, `apps/api/src/workers/workers.ts`, `outbox-consumers.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/iss/index.ts`, the two i18n catalogues (`iss.verification.*`, `iss.extension.*`, `iss.board.*`, `iss.escalation.*`, and the problems of `iss.issueProblem.*`), `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts`, the authorization matrix (`matrix-commands.json`, `negative-matrix.ts`, `negative-matrix.test.ts`), the isolation lists and fixtures (`isolation.test.ts`, `suite.ts`, `channels/api.ts`, `channels/database.ts`), `retained-history-grants.test.ts`, the worker tests (`workers.test.ts`, `workers-runtime.test.ts`, `outbox-consumers.test.ts`), `apps/api/test/e2e-stack/serve.stack.ts` (the roles `issueVerifier`, `issueExtender`, `issueHandler`, `issueEscalator`, `issueTaskForce`, `issueStranger` and the interval of `issue-sla`), `apps/web/e2e/support/e2e-fixtures.json`, `apps/web/e2e/visual.spec.ts` (ISS-04, ISS-07, ISS-10), `apps/web/package.json`.

## PMT-1 (permit dossier, ST-PMT-002) open points

- ST-PMT-002 (decision, storage): the tables `permit_dossier`, `permit` and `permit_milestone` come from the generated schema; `V1181__pmt1_permit_dossier_governance.sql` adds to `permit` the stable code of the type (`permit_type_code`, copied from the type version by a trigger when the permit is written), `is_required` (the dossier counts the types the rules required when it was opened) and `revocation_document_version_id` (revoking needs a reason AND the decision, the table had no column for the document). The guard trigger `permit_guard` holds the state machine of PMT 4 (PLANNED to SUBMITTED or EXEMPTED, SUBMITTED to IN_APPRAISAL or EXEMPTED, IN_APPRAISAL to SUPPLEMENT_REQUESTED, ISSUED or REJECTED, SUPPLEMENT_REQUESTED back to IN_APPRAISAL, ISSUED to EXPIRED, REVOKED or SUPERSEDED, EXPIRED to SUPERSEDED) and freezes what an issued permit says (number, days, scan: "no direct edit of valid_until"), the type version and the dossier it names; `permit_milestone` is a record (update and delete refused by trigger, delete and truncate revoked); the three tables lose delete and truncate for the runtime role (`retained-history-grants.test.ts` lists them).
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, one current permit per TYPE): the generated indexes `permit_current_uq` and `permit_type_no_uq` are on the type VERSION id, so a new revision of the regulation in the middle of a permit's life would have let a second current permit and a repeated number in. The migration adds the same rules on the CODE: `permit_code_current_uq` (one ISSUED or EXEMPTED permit per code and dossier), `permit_code_open_uq` (one application on its way per code and dossier: a second permit of a type is a renewal or the next attempt after a rejection, never a parallel one), `permit_code_no_uq` (a number belongs to one type in the tenant, capitals and spaces around it do not make another number). `addPermit` on a type that is held (current or on its way) is 409 `PERMIT_ALREADY_CURRENT`; the decision that would make a second current permit is the same error; a repeated number is 409 `PERMIT_NO_DUPLICATE`. A current permit and an application may coexist in the database (a renewal in preparation): only two currents or two applications are refused.
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, type version): a permit keeps the id of the permit type version it was written under for good (trigger). `renewPermit` writes the replacement under the version of the code in force TODAY (inclusive days), not under the old one: a regulation that changed is applied to the new paper; if no version of the code is in force the renewal is 422 `PERMIT_TYPE_NOT_EFFECTIVE`. The old permit is SUPERSEDED and keeps everything it says. `addPermit` takes a version id; a draft, a version that has not begun or one whose last day has passed is 422 `PERMIT_TYPE_NOT_EFFECTIVE`, a version that is not of the tenant is 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` `permit_type_id`.
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, applicability): `createPermitDossier` plans a PLANNED, required permit for every type version in force that day whose `applies_to_rule` applies, reading exactly the keys of ST-PMT-001 (`project_group_keys` against `project.project_group`, `work_types` against the business keys of the project type and the sector, inclusive investment bounds as exact decimals). A fact the project does not have yet (no group at CONCEPT, no kind of works) does NOT leave a rule out: a permit missing from a dossier is a legal risk nobody sees, one too many is a click. The business owner should confirm this reading with the legal reviewer (PMT 12, 3 hours).
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, unconfirmed types): a type that still has a `cxn_ref` is planned and FLAGGED (the read gives `cxn_ref`, the screens show the badge and the count); `createPermitDossier` never raises `PERMIT_TYPE_UNCONFIRMED` (declared in the contract, PMT 7): the whole starting catalogue is a working assumption, so refusing would block every dossier until the lawyers confirm. The code stays in the contract and the error registry for the day a tenant wants the stricter rule.
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, readiness): `permit_dossier.readiness_pct` is the cache of PMT 3.1 step 5: required TYPES (by code) covered by a permit valid today (first and last day inclusive) or an exemption, over the required types, two decimals; the dossier is COMPLETE at 100 and OPEN otherwise (CLOSED stays closed; no command closes a dossier yet). Every command that changes a permit recounts it in the same transaction and moves the dossier version only when the figure changed. A permit that merely passes its last day does not recount it: the `permit-expiry` job (ST-PMT-004) must call `PmtDossierStore.refreshReadiness`; the read lines count `issued_count`, `expiring_count` (within 60 days) and `satisfied_count` on the day of the read, so the screens are right between runs. A type required once stays required when the next attempt follows a rejection (the new permit inherits `is_required`).
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, milestones): SUBMITTED moves PLANNED to SUBMITTED, ACKNOWLEDGED SUBMITTED to IN_APPRAISAL, SUPPLEMENT_REQUESTED and SUPPLEMENT_SUBMITTED toggle IN_APPRAISAL and SUPPLEMENT_REQUESTED, APPRAISAL_MEETING and DECISION are recorded in IN_APPRAISAL without a move, HANDED_OVER in ISSUED without a move; anything else is 409 `PERMIT_STATE_INVALID`. A step that happened needs its day (not in the future, not before the step before it); every milestone moves the permit version (If-Match). The first SUBMITTED fixes `expected_decision_on` = submitted day + `default_processing_days` of the type version when the officer gave none. BR-PMT-04 says working days of the tenant calendar; that is R2 (PMT 12), R1 counts calendar days.
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, decision): IN_APPRAISAL only. ISSUED needs the scan (422 `PERMIT_DOCUMENT_REQUIRED`: a document version of the tenant that is not infected), a number (trimmed, 100 characters), `issued_on` (default: the first day, else today; not in the future), `valid_from` (default: the issue day) and either `no_expiry` or `valid_until` (default: the first day plus the months of validity of the type version, last day inclusive); a last day already in the past is accepted (what is on paper is recorded; the expiry job turns the status). REJECTED needs a reason, takes no number or days, and leaves the reason as the note of the DECISION milestone (the table has no column for it). Both write a DECISION milestone.
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, renew and revoke): `renewPermit` takes ISSUED or EXPIRED permits that have a last day (a permit without an end has nothing to renew: 409), the new last day must be today or later and later than the old one, the new permit is ISSUED today with the number and the scan, names the old one (`supersedes_id`), keeps authority, person in charge and the required flag, and the answer's `resource_id` is the NEW permit. `revokePermit` takes ISSUED permits only (an expired one is renewed or replaced), with the reason and the decision document (new column).
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, scope): the commands need the permission (`permit.manage` for the dossier, `addPermit` and milestones, `permit.record` for decision, renewal and revocation: PMT 2 gives the officer both and the leader the exemption) within a grant that reaches the project: the unit that owns it, the unit that invests in it, the unit that keeps the dossier, or a PROJECT grant. A dossier or permit of another tenant is 404; one of the tenant outside the grant is 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`. The reads (`get_resources_permit_dossier`, `get_resources_permit_dossier_id`, `get_resources_permit_id`, CR-PMT-02) use the same units with `permit.read`: a dossier or permit outside the grant is a 404. No system role holds `permit.manage` or `permit.record` today except TENANT_OWNER (`permit.*`): the tenant defines a role for the officer, or the role seeds of ADM/IAM gain one.
- ST-PMT-002 (audit and events): audit actions `pmt.dossier_opened`, `pmt.permit_planned`, `pmt.permit_milestone_recorded` (the note is free text, so the row says `has_note`, not what it says), `pmt.permit_issued`, `pmt.permit_rejected`, `pmt.permit_renewed`, `pmt.permit_revoked`. Events `pmt.dossier.opened.v1` (with the planned permit ids), `pmt.permit.planned.v1` (one per permit planned by the dossier, and one for `addPermit`), `pmt.permit.milestone_recorded.v1`, `.issued.v1`, `.rejected.v1`, `.renewed.v1` (with `superseded_permit_id`), `.revoked.v1`, each with permit, dossier, project, type code and revision, status, `valid_until`.
- ST-PMT-002 (decision, web): the notice of a decision, a renewal or a revocation sits above the forms of PMT-02 (the command moves the permit out of the status that offers the form, so the form is gone when the answer comes); the renewal notice leads to the new permit. Browser flow `apps/web/e2e/pmt-dossier-flows.spec.ts` (3 tests) and the visual regions of PMT-01 (title) and PMT-02 (back link and title) pass in the browser lane; the fixture users are `permitOfficer`, `permitManager` and `permitReader`.
- ST-PMT-002 (not done): the "gates blocked" column of PMT-01 (needs `evaluatePermitGates`, ST-PMT-004); `declarePermitExemption` and PMT-06 (the exemption path; the table check, the trigger and the readiness already count EXEMPTED); the expiry job and alerts (ST-PMT-004); a dossier per component from the screen (the command takes `project_component_id`, the screen opens project-level dossiers only); a document picker (the scan is typed as the id of a document version, as in CTR).

## DOC-1 ST-DOC-007, 008, 009 (compose and review, issue-request and reference ports, documents signed outside the system) open points

- ST-DOC-009 (DOC-09 and the upload door): `uploadDocumentFile` (CR-DOC-01, `POST /document-files`, `doc.file.upload`) puts a PDF into the shared upload pipeline and answers the key, the SHA-256, the pages and the verdict of each signature; DOC-09 (`SignedUploadScreen`) reads the file with it and then calls `uploadLeaderSignedDocument` or `registerExternallySignedDocument`. The permission is judged without a unit (the file belongs to no unit until a command attaches it), so a grant on one unit is enough to upload; the unit scope is checked by the two commands. The roles that hold `doc.outgoing.upload_signed` or `doc.outgoing.register_external` must also hold `doc.file.upload` (tenants define their roles: nothing is seeded). `receiveIncomingDocument` (ST-DOC-011) uses the same door. The DOC-09 screen files a PDF only: scans of incoming documents are DOC-11.
- ST-DOC-007 review is not run by the ADM approval engine (KERNEL-044 / ST-ADM-012): the submit moves the document to IN_REVIEW directly, `workflow_instance_id` stays empty, `review_due_on` is stored but nothing reminds the reviewer, and no notification reaches them. When the engine takes `OUTGOING_DOCUMENT` the commands call it, the history table (`outgoing_document_event`) stays the record the screens read.
- Signer authority (BR-DOC-08, decisions): the matrix in force on the day of the command is resolved for (document type, `payload.amount_vnd`, drafting unit). No rule is a refusal (422 `SIGNER_NOT_AUTHORIZED`, reason `DOA_RULE_NOT_FOUND`), so a tenant has to publish a matrix before any document can be sent for review. The signer must hold the approver role of the rule over the drafting unit (an Active grant of the role in its window, scope containing the unit); KT., TM., TL., TUQ. need a delegation that is in force, was given to the signer, carries `doc.outgoing.sign`, covers the unit, from a delegator who holds that role. The check runs at submit and at approval (the matrix version is written to the history); the signing start only re-checks the delegation (a running approval keeps the matrix it started with, ADM resolver contract). The amount is what the payload declares (`amount_vnd`, whole VND); for a document a source module asked for it is the module's value, for a person's draft it is typed by the drafter: a rule for the drafter's own figure needs a second check by the reviewer, who sees it on DOC-04.
- State secret (BR-DOC-20): the refusal reads the members of the payload that declare a level (`secrecy_level`, `security_level`, `do_mat`, `state_secret`... with Mat, Toi mat, Tuyet mat in any spelling or case). The OCR label detection of scans (CXN-DOC-08) belongs to the incoming flow and is not done. The refusal is a 422 and writes a DENIED audit row with the path of the member, never the value.
- Free drafting: `template_code` absent takes `<TYPE>-DEFAULT` or else the first published template of the type in force; `content_blocks` is merged into the payload under that name (`{{content_blocks.x}}` reaches it); there is no rich text editor (DOC.md says placeholder form).
- A cancelled draft sets its document anchor to `EXPIRED` (the checks `document_kind_chk` and `document_number_chk` of V002 now let EXPIRED carry no symbol), which frees the source reference and hash for a new request. A SIGNED document can still be cancelled (the signature is dropped); a NUMBERED or SEALED one ends only by voiding its number (ST-DOC-012).
- Return from SUBMITTED sets `document.current_version_id` to null and cancels the open signing sessions (the provider is told after commit); the file version of the locked PDF stays in the history. Approving again on the same day gives the same bytes and re-points the document at that version instead of writing a second one (the render is deterministic and the object key holds the digest).
- DOC-02: the signer and the delegation are typed as membership ids (no operation lists the people who may sign), the document type list is read from the templates in force (no operation lists document types), the unit list needs `organization_unit.read`. The preview is a `renderDocumentPreview` document of no unit, so a drafter with a grant on a branch of the tree cannot download it (the screen says so): `renderDocumentPreview` should accept the drafting unit, or the preview should be shown without a stored file.
- DOC-03 shows the last eight characters of the drafter and the signer: DOC has no member directory read. DOC-04 shows the payload members that are text, the recipients and the history; the SHA-256 of the final PDF is the one of the current version.
- `classification` of a draft is always INTERNAL (`DocOutgoingCreate` has no classification member); the version inherits it. PERSONAL or CONFIDENTIAL drafts need the member.
- ST-DOC-008 (decisions): `payload_ref` is a key of the DOC file port under `tenants/<tenant>/` (the file port is the in-memory MOCK until the object storage of KERNEL-024 is bound); the hash is computed from the parsed payload (`payloadSha256`, canonical), so a module writes any JSON formatting it likes; `signer_hint.drafting_unit_id` is required (a request names no unit otherwise); `requestDocumentIssue` needs `doc.issue.request` over the whole tenant (403 for a branch grant); a refusal is committed in its own transaction and answers 422; `STATE_SECRET_NOT_SUPPORTED` joins the four reject codes of DOC.md section 3 F2 (allowed by `doc_issue_request_reject_code_chk`); the consumer ignores (and counts) an event that is not well formed instead of failing the delivery; `DocumentIssueConsumer` is wired into `createOutboxConsumers` through `docIssue` and `bootstrap.ts` (two small edits of shared platform files). `mne.report.issue_requested.v1` has no producer yet: the payload shape is the one of `doc.issue_requested.v1`.
- ST-DOC-008 AC-ST-DOC-008-3: the command that issues a sealed document (`issueOutgoingDocument`, ST-DOC-010) does not exist, so the chain test drives the last step through `OutgoingIssuance` (the service the command and the external registration share: SEALED to ISSUED, the request ISSUED, `doc.issued.v1` once). ST-DOC-010 calls `OutgoingIssuance.issue` after its verification gate.
- ST-DOC-009 (decisions): the upload verifies with `STRICT` and without a revocation port (NOT_CHECKED: no OCSP or CRL adapter exists); a signing time must be proven by an RFC 3161 timestamp, so a signature with only the time the signer declared is `SIGNING_TIME_UNPROVEN` (a tenant policy `LENIENT_TIME` does not exist yet). The signed content is compared as a prefix: the uploaded file must keep every byte of the locked PDF and add to it; a file saved again by a tool that rewrote the whole document is `SIGNED_CONTENT_MISMATCH` (comparing extracted page text would accept it, and needs a text extractor that DOC does not have). The upload needs the signature in the field `sig_leader` (role LEADER) made with the registered personal certificate of the named signer (`SIGNER_NOT_AUTHORIZED`, reason `SIGNER_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`: the registry has no code of its own for it); the drafter does not upload the signed file of their own document (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`). The leader's signing time becomes `leader_signed_at`. The external registration needs two signatures, the last covering the file to its end, takes the number from the file's form fields when it has them (a different declared number, symbol or date is 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`, reason `DECLARED_DIFFERS_FROM_FILE`) and writes the document ISSUED (route EXTERNAL_UPLOAD, version stage ORG_SEALED, `drafter` and `clerk` the registering clerk, no signer, layout profile ND30_A4_STANDARD); a MANAGED register asks for the next number (no manager override: `DocExternalRegistration` has no reason member), a MIRRORED one for a free number (VOIDED numbers count as taken).
- ST-DOC-009 (not done): the manager's deviation from the next number of a MANAGED register; a stored `LENIENT_TIME` policy per tenant; revocation checks of the signer certificates; the check that the signer named on DOC-09 is the one who signed (the screen takes no declared signer: the server compares the signature with the registered personal certificate of the signer of the document).
- Shared files touched (`apps/api/test/e2e-stack/outgoing-seed.ts` also writes the signed sample files and the register ZEXT for DOC-09): `apps/api/src/workers/outbox-consumers.ts` and `apps/api/src/bootstrap.ts` (the DOC issue consumer), `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/doc/index.ts`, `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts`, the two i18n catalogues (`doc.draft.*`, `doc.editor.*`, `doc.list.*`, `doc.review.*`), the authorization matrix (`matrix-commands.json`, `negative-matrix.ts`), the isolation lists (`isolation.test.ts`, `channels/api.ts`, `channels/database.ts`), `packages/db/test/retained-history-grants.test.ts`, `apps/api/test/e2e-stack/serve.stack.ts` and `apps/web/e2e/support/e2e-fixtures.json` (the DOC people and the DoA matrix of the stack), `apps/web/e2e/visual.spec.ts`, `apps/web/package.json` (the new spec in `e2e` and `e2e:flows`).

## CTR-1 (BOQ and guarantees, ST-CTR-002 and ST-CTR-003) open points

- ST-CTR-002 (decision, bulk import prior art): `packages/kernel/src/tabular-file.ts` is reused as it is (CSV and XLSX by the bytes, the limits, the zip-bomb guard); `master-import-store.ts` is NOT reused. That store stages a batch, keeps it, and applies it in a second step by a second person, because master data is governed by change requests. A BOQ is a draft of one revision, edited by its officer and checked as a whole at submit (the 100-percent rule), so the import is one command: every row is judged, none is written until all pass, the result and the hash are kept in `boq_import` (append-only). What is shared with MDM-1: the reader of the file, the upload pipeline (`FileService`: type by bytes, scan, private storage), the idempotency of a file by SHA-256, the row error list.
- ST-CTR-002 (decision, one command, two bodies): `importBoqLines` takes the lines as JSON, or a `multipart/form-data` file (CSV or XLSX; `mode` is a query parameter) that `BoqFileGuard` judges (size 413, bytes 422 `FILE_TYPE_NOT_ALLOWED`, scan 422 `FILE_NOT_CLEAN`) and reads before the command runs; the lines and the SHA-256 of the file become the JSON body, so validation, idempotency, audit and events are those of the JSON form. CTR-04 reads a CSV in the browser and sends JSON (the typed client has no upload for this operation). The contract change that documents the file body is staged in the scratchpad folder `ctr-002-pending-read/README.md` (also the list filters of `get_resources_boq_line`).
- ST-CTR-002 (decision, idempotency by file): an import is recorded in `boq_import` with the SHA-256 of the file (or of the canonical lines when none was given), its mode and its sequence in the revision. An APPEND of a hash already imported since the last REPLACE answers the first import (200, `duplicate: true`, same `import_id`); a REPLACE of the hash that is the latest import changes nothing; a REPLACE after other imports puts the lines back. The version of the revision does not move for a repeat.
- ST-CTR-002 (decision, totals): lines never add up to MORE than the amount of the revision (422 `BOQ_TOTAL_MISMATCH` with the three figures, nothing written, at import); being SHORT is allowed (APPEND in several passes) and shown. `CtrBoqStore.assertBalanced(tx, revisionId)` is the check for `submitContractForReview` (ST-CTR-004): it needs at least one line and a difference of exactly 0. The database holds the amount of a line (`boq_line_amount_chk`, the exact product rounded by the rule of the line) and refuses a line in a revision that is not a draft.
- ST-CTR-002 (decision, stable items): `boq_item` is per contract, unique by key. A line with a key of an item that another revision prices must keep the unit (row fault `unitChanged`: a different unit is a different item); an item that no line prices yet (a draft being put right, or a cancelled one) takes the unit and wording of the new line. An item left without any line by a REPLACE is CANCELLED (never deleted: the runtime role cannot) and is active again when a line names it.
- ST-CTR-002 (decision, units and members): a unit must be an active `UNIT` entry of master data (row fault `unitUnknown`); `party_contractor_id` must be an active member of the joint venture of the contract (`partyUnknown`) and is stored as `party_id`. The default retention of a line that names none is 5 percent, a constant labelled `BOQ_RETENTION_RULE` (`CTR.RETENTION`, awaiting CXN-EXE-04): the screen and every answer say how many lines took it. When the owner of the business signs the rule it moves to rule data (MDM) and the constant goes.
- ST-CTR-002 (limits): 2,000 lines per import (the command body limit is 1 MiB); a larger BOQ is appended in passes. CSV only in the browser (5 MB); XLSX through the API.
- ST-CTR-003 (decision, rules not in the plan): the release of an ADVANCE guarantee needs the advance recovered or another ACTIVE ADVANCE guarantee of at least the amount still owed (422 `RELEASE_CONDITION_NOT_MET`); the other kinds need evidence and a person other than the registrant. Releasing a PERFORMANCE guarantee of a contract in force is allowed (UAT-AC310 does it); a rule on the stage of the contract belongs to rule data pending CXN-EXE-04 and CXN-02. Warranty releases are not tied to the warranty obligation of AST (not built).
- ST-CTR-003 (decision, maker-checker): the registrant cannot verify (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`, also in the database: `guarantee_verification_maker_checker_chk`) and cannot release (`guarantee_release_decision_chk`). The replacement created by an extension has the extender as its registrant, so a third person (or the other accountant) verifies and releases it. `verifyGuarantee` and `releaseGuarantee` are in the approver list of the maker-checker matrix.
- ST-CTR-003 (decision, extension): an ACTIVE guarantee only; the old row is SUPERSEDED at once with the expiry it had (a trigger keeps what a guarantee says), the new row is PENDING_VERIFICATION with `extended_from_id`. If the replacement is REJECTED the old one stays SUPERSEDED and the contract has no live guarantee of that kind: the registration of a new one (or a new extension, which needs an ACTIVE row) is the way out. A rule that keeps the old row ACTIVE until the replacement is verified would contradict the state guard of the operation (ACTIVE to SUPERSEDED, with the new row PENDING_VERIFICATION); it is a question for the owner of the business.
- ST-CTR-003 (decision, the job): `guarantee-expiry` ticks hourly and works in the 06:00 hour of Vietnam time (`JOB_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `GUARANTEE_EXPIRY_HOUR`); days are counted in calendar days in Vietnam from the date of `expires_at` there, so 23:59 on the 1st is still the 1st. One reminder per threshold 30/15/7/0 (the smallest threshold the days left have reached; a missed day sends the threshold of the day it runs), remembered in `last_alert_days`, which does not change the version of the guarantee. The day after the expiry the guarantee is EXPIRED once with `ctr.guarantee.expired.v1` (event key per guarantee). Reminders are `ctr.guarantee.expiring.v1`, registered in `notification_event_type` by V1383 and turned into in-app notifications by the notification core through a role-in-scope workflow per tenant (docs/notification-event-types.md; test `apps/api/test/ctr/guarantee-expiry-notification.test.ts`). Nothing seeds that workflow: a tenant that has none gets no notification (`notification_no_workflow_total`).
- ST-CTR-003 (cross-group, ISS): the issue for an expired advance guarantee with an advance owed is opened by the intake consumer of ST-ISS-005 from `ctr.guarantee.expired.v1` (`issue_required`, `source_module: CTR`, `source_reference`); the payload holds no number, no issuer and no person. Until that consumer exists the job emits the event and no issue is created. `requestAdvance` (PAY) asks `CtrGuaranteeStore.holdsActive(tx, contractId, 'ADVANCE', at)` to answer 422 `ADVANCE_GUARANTEE_REQUIRED`.
- ST-CTR-003 (observability): gauges `ctr_guarantee_expiring{days}` (cumulative: within 30, 15, 7, 0 days) and `ctr_advance_guarantee_at_risk`, alert `CTR_ADVANCE_GUARANTEE_AT_RISK` (product channel, log adapter, owner contract-office, runbook under docs/runbooks/alerts).
- CTR (data model additions of V1382 and V1383, for the data dictionary): `boq_import` (new, append-only), `guarantee.verified_by/verified_at/verification_method/verification_evidence_id/release_reason` (new columns), constraints and guard triggers listed in the migration headers.
- CTR (reads): the guarantee reads give the staff the registrant, the verifier, the method and the evidence; a guest contractor sees the paper and the statuses only; `advance_outstanding_vnd` only to a reader of the ledger (`ledger.read`). The BOQ reads give a guest contractor the lines of its own contract without the stable item id.
- CTR (browser lane): `apps/web/e2e/ctr-flows.spec.ts` seeds its own contracts straight in the database (foreign keys off, as the stack does) and adds the users `ctrOfficer`, `ctrAccountant`, `ctrChecker`, `ctrReader` (e2e-fixtures.json and serve.stack.ts). CTR-01, CTR-02 and CTR-25 of ST-CTR-001 still have no browser flow.
- CTR (UAT-PLAN-16): the plan says 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` for a virus signature or an executable renamed `.xlsx`; the shared pipeline answers 422 `FILE_NOT_CLEAN` / `FILE_TYPE_NOT_ALLOWED` (ST-KERNEL-024). Same status, nothing written; the UAT row should name the codes.

## PAY-1 (Treasury statement import, ST-PAY-006) open points

- ST-PAY-006 (decision, layout): the real statement layout is unknown (R-PAY-04), so `createTreasuryStatementFormat` reads a WORKING ASSUMPTION (`ASSUMED-2026.1`, `docs/pay-treasury-import.md`): voucher number, voucher date, project code, Treasury office code, whole VND amount, booking time, optional direction and account. The headers are the column map (`DEFAULT_TREASURY_COLUMNS`), the adapter stays PROPOSED, the Treasury API MISSING; the screen says so beside every result. When the agency gives samples the map and the rules of `judge` change, and the version string with them.
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, project code): the project code of a row is matched against `project.code` (the internal code), not `national_project_code`. If the Treasury writes the national code, change `projectOf` in `pay-treasury-statement-adapter.ts`.
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, unreadable file): a file that cannot be read is 422 `TREASURY_IMPORT_SCHEMA_INVALID` and is rolled back with its core `FAILED` try, so the connector is NOT stopped by a wrong upload and no `FAILED` import is kept. The core's FAILED path (connector `FAILED` until the reset of MDM-24, an outbox `file_import.failed`) is not used by PAY. If the business wants the stop (a changed layout is a signal), the command needs a persist-then-answer-422 path that the command interceptor does not have.
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, amount mismatch): `confirmTreasuryManually` lists 422 `AMOUNT_MISMATCH_OPENED` in the contract, but the item that is opened must stay, and a 422 rolls the transaction back. It answers 200 with status `EXCEPTION` and `reconciliation_item_id`. `matchTreasuryTransaction` (ST-PAY-007) has the same listed code and needs the same decision (or the persist-then-422 path above).
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, scope): `importTreasuryStatement`, `getTreasuryImport` and `receiveTreasuryCallback` need their permission on the WHOLE tenant (a file covers every project and its identity can be consumed once, so a branch grant could shadow it for a wider one); PAY.md section 2 says "PROJECT/UNIT" for the reconciliation officer, which would need a per-project quarantine (`PROJECT_OUT_OF_SCOPE`) and a file identity that is per scope. The reads of transactions and the confirmation by hand are project-scoped.
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, owners): no module holds "the reconciliation officer of a project", so the owner of every item is the importer (`ownerFor` is not given), due in three days. `openGapItems` opens an item for a row refused for a gap in master data (`CROSSWALK_MISSING`, `CROSSWALK_TARGET_INVALID`, `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND`); a malformed row has no item, its owner on screen is the importer.
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, two registers of differences): the core item (`file_import_reconciliation_item`) and the PAY item (`reconciliation_item`, kind `DUPLICATE_CONFLICT`) both exist for a conflict. ST-PAY-007 (`resolveReconciliationItem`) must close both, or read the core's; until then the core item stays OPEN. The PAY item exists only for rows whose project is known (it needs `project_id`).
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, a corrected file): a quarantined row is taken by a corrected file with other bytes; the same bytes answer `ALREADY_IMPORTED`. A "retry the quarantined rows of this import" command would need a new operation.
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, refunds): a `REFUND` row of a file and a `REFUND` callback are refused (`REFUND_UNSUPPORTED` in the quarantine, 422): `treasury_transaction_refund_chk` demands `reversal_of_id`, which neither carries. They belong with `reversePayment` (ST-PAY-008).
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, callback): the signature covers the JSON array of the eight fields of the contract (the contract has the signature in the body, not in a header); there is no freshness window (the event carries the booking time, not a send time), replay is stopped by the inbox key (provider, event id). Keys come from `secret://pay/treasury/<provider>` through the shared `WEBHOOK_SECRETS` (environment `MACHVON_SECRET_PAY_TREASURY_<PROVIDER>`); none is configured, so production refuses every event (adapter MISSING). The body is kept by reference through `WEBHOOK_PAYLOADS`.
- ST-PAY-006 (decision, manual confirmation): the request must be `SENT_TREASURY`; the confirmer is not its creator or submitter (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`, application level: the database has no column for who confirms); one live (`UNMATCHED` or `MATCHED`) confirmation per request, an `EXCEPTION` one may be superseded. `authz/maker-checker.ts` (`APPROVER_COMMANDS`) is NOT extended (its seeding needs a payment request chain); the rule is tested in `pay-treasury-transaction-store.test.ts`, `treasury-transaction.test.ts` and `pay-flows.spec.ts`.
- ST-PAY-006 (upload and list, CR-PAY-01 applied): `uploadTreasuryStatementFile` (multipart, `treasury.import`, whole tenant; judged by its bytes, read as a table, header checked against the layout with `checkTreasuryLayout`, scanned, stored; answers `layout_ok` and `missing_columns`) and `listTreasuryImports` (whole-tenant read, empty otherwise) are implemented; the import takes the optional `file_name`. `UploadedFile` of `sas/theme-assets.ts` carries the cleaned `name` of the part for it. PAY-13 has the file picker, the layout verdict before the import and the list of earlier imports. The name of a file picked through `importTreasuryStatement` without `file_name` is `statement-<sha12>`.
- ST-PAY-006 (not done): the Temporal workflow of TK-006-2 and the virus scan inside the import (the import is synchronous: at most 5,000 rows in one transaction, the scan is the one of the upload pipeline); the metric `pay_treasury_import_rows{outcome}` is written to the `PAY_METRICS` sink, which no worker binds yet (no-op); no alert rule; `getTreasuryImport` shows at most 500 lines (the counts are those of the whole file).
- ST-PAY-006 (shared files touched): `packages/db/src/index.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/registry.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/sas/theme-assets.ts` (the optional `name`), `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts`, `apps/web/src/i18n/{vi,en}.json`, `apps/api/test/authz/{matrix-commands.json,negative-matrix.ts}`, `apps/api/test/isolation/{isolation.test.ts,suite.ts,channels/api.ts,channels/database.ts}`, `apps/api/test/conformance/command-conformance.test.ts` (the count of write operations), `packages/api-client/test/{types,command-surface}.test.ts` (the multipart upload), `packages/db/test/retained-history-grants.test.ts`, `apps/api/test/e2e-stack/serve.stack.ts` (roles `E2E_TREASURY_*`, `seedPayFixtures`), `apps/web/e2e/support/e2e-fixtures.json`, `apps/web/e2e/visual.spec.ts`, `apps/web/package.json`.

## LND-1 (sections, parcels and recovery notice timers, ST-LND-001) open points

Built (screen LND-02, `lnd-flows.spec.ts` and the visual region included): `docs/lnd-parcels.md`; migration `V1680__lnd1_section_parcel_deadline.sql`; kernel `land-timers.ts`; rule type `LND_TIMER` (master-types, baseline, policy, dictionary); stores `lnd-*` in `packages/db`; API `apps/api/src/modules/lnd/`; web `apps/web/src/screens/lnd/`.

1. **LND-02 is complete.** The three reads and the upload door (CR-LND-01) are implemented: `listLandParcels`, `listLandSections`, `uploadLandParcelFile` (the door scans, reads the table only after the scan, refuses a file with a column a parcel must not carry with 422 `IMPORT_SCHEMA_INVALID` and removes it again). The sections list takes `cursor` and `limit` from the contract but answers one page (a project has few sections); a project with thousands of sections would need paging.
2. **Contract: no operation records the decision to recover.** `LndParcelStore.recordRecoveryDecision` (422 `NOTICE_PERIOD_NOT_ELAPSED` before `due_on`, the timer `SATISFIED` on the day) is tested but nothing calls it; the database check `land_parcel_notice_chk` is the only guard that runs today. The plan approval and the enforcement stories decide who calls it.
3. **Job `land-deadlines` is not built.** A notice timer has no breach (the 90/180 days are the earliest day a decision may be taken, not a duty to decide), so this story starts no alert. The alerts at T-7 and T, the BREACHED state and the ISS issue of BR-LND-03 belong to the payment and posting timers (`PLAN_POSTING_30`, `PAYMENT_30`, `COMPLAINT_RESOLUTION`), which the later LND stories start; the rule data for all five timers exists already.
4. **Working days.** BR-LND-01 counts calendar days and so does the baseline rule. The rule can say `WORKING` with a `calendar_code` (counted on the published ADM calendar); no tenant uses it until the business owner decides (CXN-12). A notice rule keeps `days` at 90 or 180 because `land_parcel.notice_days_applied` allows no other value; a change of the law to another number is a migration.
5. **Schema drift to feed back into the design.** `land_parcel.notice_document_version_id` (the evidence of the notice, with its foreign key and the all-or-none check `land_parcel_notice_evidence_chk`), the text checks, `land_parcel_boundary_geometry_chk`, `land_deadline_subject_timer_chk`, the three guard triggers and the end of `DELETE` for the runtime role are not in `schema_reference.sql`.
6. **MDM screens.** The rule type `LND_TIMER` has no panel in the catalogue screens of MDM (it works through the generic change request and publication; `RulePanel` lists a rule under a catalogue and none names it). Adding it touches the MDM web files and is left to the MDM owner.
7. **Scope choices to confirm with the business owner.** A parcel command is judged by the unit tree of the unit that answers for the parcel or a grant on its project (not by the units of the project); a section by the project; an import by a grant over the whole project or tenant (a unit grant is refused, `docs/lnd-parcels.md`). `land.manage` over a unit lets an officer declare a parcel of that unit in any project of the tenant: no relation between a unit and the projects it works on is modelled yet.
8. **`address_text` is free text.** A person's name written in it cannot be detected. The form says not to; the DPO should decide whether the field stays (CXN-EXE-06).
9. **Not built here (other stories):** households and their personal data, inventory, compensation plans, payments, complaints, enforcement, resettlement, stage progress, the read model `getLandProgress`, `recordSectionHandover`, `scope_units_guard` and the `household-retention` job.

## BID-1 (procurement plan, ST-BID-001) open points

- ST-BID-001 (decision, what a plan is): `createProcurementPlan` makes a DRAFT revision of a project with all its packages in one transaction (`procurement_plan`, `procurement_package`, `procurement_plan_event`; migration V1343). A plan is never edited: the contract has no update or cancel operation, so a new `createProcurementPlan` on a project that has a DRAFT RESTARTS it (the draft becomes CANCELLED with its packages, history step REPLACED, the new plan is the next revision); a plan IN_REVIEW blocks a new one (409 `PLAN_STATE_INVALID` naming it), an APPROVED plan is revised by a new plan whose `supersedes_id` is the plan in force. At most one plan in work per project (partial unique index) and one APPROVED (V002). Returned plans (`RETURN`) go back to DRAFT and are also restarted rather than edited. Contract gap: `updateProcurementPlan` and `cancelProcurementPlan` (If-Match, DRAFT only) would make this less blunt; the unit owner decides whether replacing another planner's draft is acceptable (today any planner of the project scope may).
- ST-BID-001 (decision, the budget check, BR-BID-02): at `submitProcurementPlan` and again at the approval, the budgets of the packages are added per allocation line (exact decimals) and each sum must not exceed `allocation_line.amount_vnd`; a line counts only when its capital plan is PUBLISHED and the line is of the project of the plan (else 409 `ALLOCATION_LINE_INVALID`); the sum is of the packages of THIS plan, so a revision lists all packages again (the plan it replaces is SUPERSEDED at the approval and no longer counts). The refusal 422 `PACKAGE_BUDGET_EXCEEDS_ALLOCATION` names every line over its limit (`lines[]`: allocation_line_id, allocated_vnd, packages_vnd, excess_vnd). CAP `getCapitalAvailability` is not implemented yet and its reserved/committed figures would lower the limit: when it exists, the check should read it instead of the line amount (one place: `BidPlanStore.assertWithinAllocation`). The line rows are locked FOR SHARE; a plan total is checked by the database at commit (deferred trigger) to equal the sum of its packages.
- ST-BID-001 (decision, maker-checker): the approver is neither the maker nor the person who submitted (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`; CHECK constraints `procurement_plan_sod_chk` and `procurement_plan_submitter_sod_chk` as well); a decision of any kind (approve, return, reject) is taken by that rule. An approval rests on a document of the tenant that is a decision (`doc_type_code = 'QD'`, template DOC-QD-PHE-DUYET-KHLCNT of the DOC pack) and EFFECTIVE; none or one not in force is 422 `DECISION_DOCUMENT_NOT_EFFECTIVE`, one that does not exist or is no decision is 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`. Return and reject need words (`comment`); a document is accepted only with an approval. The words are shown to people who hold `bid.plan.manage` or `bid.plan.approve` only. Not done: the DoA matrix (ADM-15) is not consulted for who may approve a plan of what value (the module document says "theo DoA"); the approver is whoever holds `bid.plan.approve` in the scope of the project.
- ST-BID-001 (decision, scope): the commands are judged by the project scope of the permission (`bid.plan.manage`, `bid.plan.approve`; the unit of the project or its investor unit, or the project itself); a plan or project outside the scope is 404 for the caller (as in CAP drafting), not 403. Reads: `bid.read` for the plans, `procurement_package.read` for the packages; plans in work (DRAFT, IN_REVIEW, CANCELLED) and their packages are shown to people who hold either planning permission only; a package outside any plan (awarded elsewhere) is shown like a package of a plan in force.
- ST-BID-001 (decision, lifecycle of a package): `procurement_package.status` follows its plan: DRAFT, IN_REVIEW, PLANNED (plan APPROVED, `approved_decision_id` set), SUPERSEDED, CANCELLED; the tender stories move it on (AWARDED is what CTR fixtures already use). "Only a package of an approved plan is organised for selection" (BID-03 rule) is for those stories to enforce on `status = 'PLANNED'`. A package keeps its money, code, funding line, method and period for good (trigger `procurement_package_guard`); only status, `approved_decision_id` and `external_package_code` follow. Package code is unique per project revision (V002 index). `planned_start_period` is a quarter or a month (`2026-Q4`, `2026-11`).
- ST-BID-001 (assumption, legal): the list of selection methods (`OPEN_BIDDING`, `LIMITED_BIDDING`, `DIRECT_APPOINTMENT`, `COMPETITIVE_QUOTATION`, `DIRECT_PURCHASE`, `SELF_EXECUTION`, `COMMUNITY_PARTICIPATION`, `SPECIAL_CASE`) is a working assumption from Luật Đấu thầu 22/2023/QH15; legal must confirm it (it is one list in `packages/db/src/bid-plan-input.ts`, the column has no CHECK because CTR fixtures write 'OPEN'). The stages of a project that take a plan are PIPELINE, BUDGETED, IMPLEMENTATION, SUSPENDED (as CAP drafting); BID 3 says "PIPELINE or later".
- ST-BID-001 (events, audit): `bid.plan.drafted.v1`, `bid.plan.submitted.v1`, `bid.plan.approved.v1` (with `package_ids`, `decision_document_id`, `superseded_plan_id`); a return, a rejection and a restart leave an audit row and a history step, no event (the contract names none). Events and audit rows carry ids and exact amounts, never titles or words.
- ST-BID-001 (contract gaps for the orchestrator, staged in the scratchpad folder ST-BID-001-pending-read): (1) `BidPlanCreate.packages` items are `type: object`: the item schema (nine fields, `additionalProperties: false`) is enforced by hand in `readPackages` and the typed client takes one cast (`contractBodyOf` in `apps/web/src/screens/bid/plan-hooks.ts`); (2) the package list has no `plan_id` and `project_id` filters, the screens read all pages and filter; (3) the responses of `listProcurementPlans` and `get_resources_procurement_package*` are untyped objects; (4) `ALLOCATION_LINE_INVALID`, `PROJECT_STAGE_INVALID` and `DECISION_DOCUMENT_NOT_EFFECTIVE` are raised by submit and decide too but are listed under create only (`x-error-codes`); (5) no update and cancel of a draft (above), no `getProcurementPlan` by id.
- ST-BID-001 (not done): milestones (`procurement_milestone`), import of the national bidding network (`external_package_code` is only kept), the allocation-line picker of BID-16 is a free identifier with suggestions from `get_resources_allocation_line` (a planner without `allocation_line.read` types it; a read of the lines of a project by a planner permission is missing), BID-03 shows the planned period for "Hạn" (no deadline column exists before the tender milestones).

## DOC-1 (ST-DOC-010: verify-and-issue gate and distribution) open points

- ST-DOC-010 (decision, revocation is honest): `issueOutgoingDocument` verifies both signatures again from the stored bytes (`issue-gate.ts`, `STRICT`, with the revocation port). A revocation that the port could not answer (no OCSP or CRL adapter is bound, so `UnavailableRevocationPort` answers UNKNOWN today, or the responder did not answer) is NOT read as good: the answer is 503 `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` with the reason `REVOCATION_UNAVAILABLE`, the document stays SEALED and nothing is announced. The registry has no code of its own for it. Consequence to settle with the owner [CXN-DOC-07]: until an OCSP/CRL adapter is bound, no sealed document can be issued through this command in a real deployment (the external registration, ST-DOC-009, issues without a revocation check and is unaffected; the in-system signing needs a certification authority adapter that does not exist either). A tenant policy that accepts an unknown revocation with a recorded reason is not built.
- ST-DOC-010 (decision, findings to codes): a hash of the stored file that differs from the one of its version, a broken digest, a revoked certificate, a missing or doubled signature or a seal that does not cover the end of the file are `SIGNATURE_VERIFICATION_FAILED` (the findings go in the answer); a change after a signature that the signature does not allow is `INCREMENTAL_CHANGES_NOT_ALLOWED`; a chain that does not end at a trust anchor `CERTIFICATE_CHAIN_UNTRUSTED`; a signing time without an RFC 3161 timestamp `SIGNING_TIME_UNPROVEN`. The new verification is stored next to the earlier ones (`document_signature`, the next `verification_no`) so DOC-07 shows the one the gate judged.
- ST-DOC-010 (decision, unconfirmed template): a document whose template or layout profile still waits for its legal confirmation (`rendersWatermarked`, now one function in `@machvon/db`) needs `unconfirmed_override_reason` (at least 20 characters after trimming) from someone who holds `doc.template.confirm_legal` over the WHOLE tenant (403 `SCOPE_DENIED` otherwise, like the template commands); a reason for a template that needs none is 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` (`OVERRIDE_NOT_NEEDED`). The reason and the person are kept on the document (`V1223` constraint, never rewritten), in the audit row and as `template_unconfirmed` in `doc.issued.v1`; the file is not rendered again and keeps its watermark.
- ST-DOC-010 (decision, `distribute_now`): issuing with `distribute_now` also needs `doc.distribution.manage` over the drafting unit (403 `SCOPE_DENIED` before anything is verified) and sends the document to every recipient printed on it, the inbox of the unit for a unit of this system and the paper handover for the others.
- ST-DOC-010 (decision, recipients and channels): a dispatch goes only to recipients the document names ("Noi nhan" is the legal list; 422 `RECIPIENT_INVALID`, reason `NOT_ON_DOCUMENT`), at most 500 rows per document. Channels of the first release: `INTERNAL` (the inbox of a unit of this system: DELIVERED at once), `PAPER_HANDOVER` (SENT; the receipt is the acknowledgement) and `EMAIL` through `EmailChannelPort` (`DOC_EMAIL_CHANNEL`). No mail transport exists, so the bound adapter says PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE and the row is FAILED with that code and can be sent again; nothing is reported as sent that was not. The LGSP channel waits for ST-DOC-021 and is refused by name (`CHANNEL_NOT_SUPPORTED`). An adapter must only queue the message: it runs inside the transaction of the command.
