- ST-DOC-010 (decision, aggregate status): the document is DELIVERED when one row is DELIVERED or ACKNOWLEDGED (a receipt on a SENT row delivers it at the instant of the receipt) and ACKNOWLEDGED when at least one recipient must acknowledge and all of them have (`document_distribution.acknowledgement_required`, a column V1223 adds: the design says "mandatory recipients" but the table had no column for it; default true). Both moves, their history rows and `doc.delivered.v1` / `doc.acknowledged.v1` happen in the command that caused them, once. A resend finds the row (unique per document, lower-cased recipient name and channel) and writes nothing; `retryDocumentDistribution` sends a FAILED row again.
- ST-DOC-010 (decision, read): there is no list operation for the distributions of a document, so `getOutgoingDocument` carries `distributions` (an allowlist of fields, at most 500 rows), `renders_watermarked` and `unconfirmed_override_reason`. The response of that operation is an untyped object, so no contract change was needed. A paged `GET /outgoing-documents/{id}/distributions` is a better read once documents have many recipients.
- ST-DOC-010 (contract gaps for the orchestrator): (1) `DocDispatch.recipients` items are `type: object` without members, so the generated type admits no member and the typed client takes one cast (`recipientBody` in `apps/web/src/screens/doc/distribution/hooks.ts`); the members read are `kind`, `name`, `code`, `unit_id`, `channel` (INTERNAL, EMAIL, PAPER_HANDOVER) and `acknowledgement_required`. (2) `issueOutgoingDocument` can answer 503 `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` and 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`, `dispatchOutgoingDocument` 422 `RECIPIENT_INVALID` with a reason member; neither is in `x-error-codes` of the operation. (3) `doc.delivered.v1` and `doc.acknowledged.v1` are announced by the issue (with `distribute_now`), the dispatch, the receipt and the retry, whichever moves the document.
- ST-DOC-010 (job, not scheduled): `recheckIssuedRevocation` (`revocation-recheck.ts`, the `doc.revocation-recheck` job of the design) asks again about the documents in force whose latest verification is older than a day or not good, stores the new verification and raises `DOC_ISSUED_SIGNATURE_REVOKED`; like the register jobs it is a function over the transaction of one tenant that the platform scheduler has to call once per tenant (no scheduler registers DOC jobs yet). It also verifies only the sealed file; the long-term validation (DSS and document timestamp, `ISSUED_LTV`) is not built.
- ST-DOC-010 (not done): the metric `doc_issue_latency_seconds` is the time from the seal to the issue (a gauge: the sink has no histogram); no email delivery receipts, no recall notice to recipients (ST-DOC-019), no incoming side of the internal channel (the inbox of the unit is only the row).
- ST-DOC-010 (tests): the chain test of ST-DOC-008 (`issue-requests.test.ts`, UAT-AC410) now issues through the real command (`FlowWorld.issueSealed`); the isolation suite has a distribution of each tenant (`DOCUMENT_FIXTURES.distributionId`) and the authorization matrix a distribution of tenant A (`distributionId`). Browser: `apps/web/e2e/doc-issue-flows.spec.ts` (6 flows, users `docIssuer`, `docLegal`, `docIssueOnly`, `docReader`); the stack answers "not revoked" for the test authority (`DOC_REVOCATION` override) and keeps the real unconfigured email adapter.

## PMT-1 (ST-PMT-003: permit exemption) open points

- ST-PMT-003 (decision, who proposed): the design says the approver is not "the proposer" without defining the proposer. A permit has no proposer column, so the proposer is the person who created the permit (opened the dossier or added the permit) and whoever last changed it (`updated_by` before the exemption): neither can declare its exemption (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`, also held by the trigger of `V1182` for every role but the owner). A leader who recorded an unrelated milestone is therefore not an approver of that permit either; the cost is accepted for the stricter reading of BR-PMT-04. Whether the people who recorded earlier milestones count is not decided by the design.
- ST-PMT-003 (decision, type): the type version the permit was written under decides `exemption_allowed` (422 `EXEMPTION_NOT_ALLOWED`); the conditions of the construction law [CXN-EXE-02] stay data of the type, not code. A planned or submitted permit can be exempted, any other status is 409 `PERMIT_STATE_INVALID`; a type that already has a current permit in the dossier is 409 `PERMIT_ALREADY_CURRENT`.
- ST-PMT-003 (what it writes): status EXEMPTED with the trimmed basis (at most 2000 characters, `permit_exempt_basis_chk`) and the decision document (readable, not infected), a DECISION milestone dated today that names the document, the readiness of the dossier counted again (an exemption covers its type already), the audit row `pmt.permit_exempted` (ids and states, not the text of the basis) and `pmt.permit.exempted.v1`. The gate evaluation (`evaluatePermitGates`, requirement VALID_OR_EXEMPT) is a later story; the readiness count and the PMT-02 summary already treat EXEMPTED as covering.
- ST-PMT-003 (scope, web): `permit.exempt` is held by the leader of the unit tree or project (not whole-tenant), so the command acts inside the grant like the other permit commands. PMT-02 offers the form to someone who holds the permission for a PLANNED or SUBMITTED permit of a type that allows it; the server still refuses the proposer. Fixture users `permitLeader` and `permitProposerLeader`; browser flow `apps/web/e2e/pmt-exemption-flows.spec.ts` (3 tests).
- ST-PMT-003 (not done): the exemption of a gate (`grantPermitGateWaiver`, PMT-06 second half) and the revocation of an exemption are other stories; the decision document is a version id typed on the form like the other PMT scans (no document picker).

### ST-CTR-006 (the chain of changes: tiers per project and ChangeEvent/PCO/CO requests, slice CTR-1)
- ST-CTR-006 (decision, no tiers set): a project that has set no `change_chain_config` refuses the first request with 422 `CHANGE_STAGE_NOT_ALLOWED` and `reason: TIERS_NOT_CONFIGURED`; the spec lists only the wrong-tier case, and there is no default count because a default would lock a number the owner never chose.
- ST-CTR-006 (decision, the contract): a request is raised, promoted and assessed on an ACTIVE or SUSPENDED contract (409 `CONTRACT_STATE_INVALID` with `contract_status` otherwise); a suspended contract may still need a variation such as more time. To be confirmed with the business: whether a SIGNED contract that is not yet active may take one.
- ST-CTR-006 (decision, the baseline): the request names a revision of the contract that is `EFFECTIVE` (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` on `baseline_revision_id` otherwise); a revision pending its effective date is not a baseline, because the change is priced against what is in force.
- ST-CTR-006 (decision, the assessment): `evaluateChangeRequest` is for a PCO or a CO (422 `CHANGE_STAGE_NOT_ALLOWED`, `reason: STAGE_NOT_EVALUATED`, for the first notice of an event); an `EVALUATED` request may be assessed again until it is decided, each time under the version it shows. The spec's body has no scope note although the acceptance text and the field list say "scope": `scope_impact_note` (optional) was added to `CtrEvaluateChangeRequest`.
- ST-CTR-006 (decision, promotion): promoting marks the request `PROMOTED` with the reason and raises the next tier as a new request under it (same contract, baseline, kind of event and estimate; its starting amount is the assessment if there is one, else the estimate); a request has one child (`change_request_one_child_uq`); a CO is not promoted (422, `reason: LAST_TIER`). A withdrawn or promoted request is over; the database keeps it as it is.
- ST-CTR-006 (decision, funding): an assessment above zero names the line of capital (`allocation_line_id`, 422 `ALLOCATION_UNAVAILABLE` with `reason: ALLOCATION_LINE_REQUIRED` without it) and CAP is asked through `CapitalCommitments.assertCanAbsorb` (new, `CapAvailabilityStore.assertCanAbsorb`): a line not in force is `NOT_IN_FORCE`, a policy that blocks the amount carries its reason; a warning is not a refusal at this point, the commitment will ask for it to be accepted when the contract changes (ST-CTR-007 and the commitment of the change). A decrease or no change asks nothing.
- ST-CTR-006 (migration): V1386 adds the assessment columns (`evaluated_by`, `evaluated_at`, `funding_impact_note`, `scope_impact_note`, `evaluation_allocation_line_id`, `status_reason`), the checks (`change_request_evaluated_chk`, `_stage_status_chk`, `_parent_chk`, `_submitted_chk`, `_text_chk`), the unique child index, the guard that keeps a request in its place and moves its status along the edges only (`change_request_guard`), a stronger `change_chain_config_guard_tg` (it replaces the baseline one, which refused only a change of the count: the new one refuses every `UPDATE` and `DELETE` of a locked configuration with 23514 `CHANGE_TIERS_LOCKED`) and `REVOKE DELETE, TRUNCATE` on both tables from the runtime role. The baseline trigger and its function are dropped in the same migration; the new function keeps the old name of the trigger so the acceptance text (`change_chain_config_guard_tg`, SQLSTATE 23514) still holds.
- ST-CTR-006 (contract): the `x-error-codes` of the five commands are completed (`CHANGE_STAGE_NOT_ALLOWED`, `CHANGE_STATE_INVALID`, `CONTRACT_STATE_INVALID`, `ALLOCATION_UNAVAILABLE`, `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `SCOPE_DENIED`, `VERSION_CONFLICT`); `getChangeChain` answers `CtrChangeChainView` (tiers, requests with `actions`, `actions.configure` and `actions.open`) instead of an open object; `scope_impact_note` is added to the assessment. `get_resources_change_request` keeps the generic page schema. `contracts:compat` accepts all of it (additions only).
- ST-CTR-006 (guest): a guest contractor reads the id, code, tier, status, contract and dates of the requests of its own contract, no `reason`, no figures, no people and no `actions`, and not the tiers (`config` is absent); the spec's wording ("hides the internal assessment that is not approved") is read as: until the decision story exists nothing of the assessment is shown to a guest at all. To be revisited with ST-CTR-007, which approves a CO.
- ST-CTR-006 (web): CTR-20 (`TiersScreen`), CTR-07 (`RequestScreen`, one request per contract at a time on the form, the list of the contract below) and CTR-19 (`ChainScreen`, a column for each tier). Each opens without a project or a contract and asks for one (a picker), like the other CTR screens; CTR-03 links to CTR-07 for the staff of a contract that is being performed. The mockup of CTR-08 (compare and approve an annex) is the decision story. Browser flows `apps/web/e2e/ctr-change-flows.spec.ts` (4 tests; users `changeOwner`, `changeOfficer`, `changeEvaluator`, `changeReader`); visual regions CTR-07, CTR-19, CTR-20 (the frame and the title).
- ST-CTR-006 (open, UAT matrix): `UAT-AC307` and `UAT-AC308` are covered in the database, the API and the browser; `UAT-AC309` (the decision of a CO that creates a revision) belongs to ST-CTR-007.
- ST-CTR-006 (superseded by ST-CTR-007): the decision of a CO, deferred above, is built there.
- ST-CTR-006 (open, reviews): the authorization and migration reviews the story requires were not run (no subagents were allowed); the scope (404 outside, 403 inside without the permission), the guest, the lock and the edges of the status are covered by tests, not by an independent reader. Run both before the story is called done.

### ST-CTR-007 (the decision of a change order, effective-dated revisions and the timeline, slice CTR-1)
- ST-CTR-007 (decision, who): the people who may not decide a CO are everyone who raised or promoted a request of its chain, whoever assessed it, and for an approval whoever made the decision document; the rule holds for return and reject too (they can withdraw). The spec says only "other than the maker"; the chain is read as the maker.
- ST-CTR-007 (decision, the revision): the approval makes the next revision (kind CHANGE_ORDER, PENDING_EFFECTIVE) that follows the latest revision in force or waiting, so several changes can wait for their days; their first days must be strictly increasing (a revision is in force up to, and not including, the first day of the next, `valid_until` is exclusive, and the baseline CHECK is `valid_from < valid_until`). The value of a CO is the value of that revision plus the approved amount, never zero or below, never below the floor of BR-CTR-06 (accepted, or paid net of the advance recovered).
- ST-CTR-007 (decision, the BOQ): the decision body in the spec has no BOQ lines although the acceptance text re-prices a line. `boq_lines` (optional, the lines of an import) was added to `CtrContractChangeDecision`; absent, the BOQ of the revision it follows is copied. The lines never add up to more than the value, so a decrease must send the lines that shrink (422 BOQ_TOTAL_MISMATCH); an increase may leave the BOQ short of the value and complete it by an import while the revision waits (V1387 lets the BOQ of a pending CHANGE_ORDER be written; CTR-04 offers the import). The job does not require the BOQ to equal the value. The web form takes the lines as JSON: a table editor for them is a later improvement.
- ST-CTR-007 (decision, the effective day): the day defaults to today and may not be before it (422 RETROACTIVE_NOT_ALLOWED); the retroactive path (`approveRetroactiveAdjustment`, `retroactive = true`) is ST-CTR-016 (R2). `contract_revision_retroactive_chk` already refuses `retroactive` on any revision that is no change order.
- ST-CTR-007 (decision, the job): `revision-effective` runs hourly at every hour instead of once at 00:05: it is idempotent, makes good a missed night and takes revisions in the order of their days; on a normal day the effect is the same within the hour of midnight. It leaves a revision pending (counted, gauge `ctr_revision_overdue`, alert `CTR_REVISION_OVERDUE`) while the contract is not ACTIVE/SUSPENDED or while its value is below the floor of the day; it does not re-judge the capital.
- ST-CTR-007 (decision, delegation): the size of the change (absolute) is judged against bands of the document type `CTR_CHANGE` when the tenant configured any (`unconfigured: ALLOW`, like the result of a selection); the spec names no limit for a CO. To be confirmed with the business: whether a change order should take the `CTR_CONTRACT` bands on the new value instead.
- ST-CTR-007 (decision, the capital): an approval that adds to the contract asks CAP whether the line the assessment named can take the increase (`assertCanAbsorb`, nothing committed); the revision's own commitment of the delta, and the release of a decrease, are not written (the ledger has no source kind for a change and no release). The job emits `ctr.commitment.changed.v1` with the new value and the previous one for CAP to reconcile; the consumer is not in this story.
- ST-CTR-007 (migration): V1387 adds the three foreign keys of the links (V002 had the columns only), the checks (a change order makes one revision and a revision comes from one change order; `retroactive` only on change orders; a decided change order has who, when and why; `APPROVED` and `resulting_revision_id` go together), the unique index per change order, the index for the job, the guard of revisions and the new BOQ write rule, and closes an APPROVED change order like a promoted one.
- ST-CTR-007 (contract): `boq_lines` added to the decision; `x-error-codes` of the decision completed (and `ctr.change.returned.v1`, `ctr.change.rejected.v1` added to its events); `CtrChangeRequestLine` gains `actions.decide` and `resulting_revision_id`. The revision DTO gains `supersedes_id`, `change_request_id` and `change_request_code`. Additions only.
- ST-CTR-007 (web): CTR-08 (`DecisionScreen`) and CTR-26 (`RevisionsScreen`); the board links a CO to CTR-08; the workspace links to CTR-26; CTR-04 offers the import to a change order that waits. Browser flows in `apps/web/e2e/ctr-change-flows.spec.ts` (users `changeApprover`, `changeAll`); visual regions CTR-08 and CTR-26.
- ST-CTR-007 (open, other stories): AC-2 speaks of acceptances created on 25 July and 2 August snapshotting 2,000,000 and 2,100,000: the acceptance (`createAcceptance`) is ST-CTR-009. The rule it needs is built and tested here (`CtrRevisionStore.pricingRevisionAt`, `unitPriceAt`); the snapshot itself is not. The members' shares of a joint venture are not re-split when the value changes (BR-CTR-08 is judged when the contract leaves the draft only).
- ST-CTR-007 (open, reviews): the money, authorization and migration reviews the story requires were not run (no subagents were allowed). The floor, the people of the chain, the delegation, the guard of revisions and the job are covered by tests, not by an independent reader. Run all three before the story is called done.

### ST-CTR-008 (measurements: recording with cumulative rules, corrections, CTR-10, slice CTR-1)
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, the period): one live (draft, sent or approved) measurement of an item in a period at a time (409 `MEASUREMENT_STATE_INVALID` with `reason: PERIOD_ALREADY_MEASURED`); a rejected or superseded one frees the period. The spec is silent; the alternative (several records per item and period, summed) would make "the measurement of the period" ambiguous for the acceptance (ST-CTR-009), and a second record is a correction anyway.
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, zero): `recordMeasurement` refuses a quantity of zero (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`) and a negative one (422 `CUMULATIVE_DECREASE_WITHOUT_CORRECTION`); `correctMeasurement` accepts zero, which withdraws the quantity of the period.
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, statuses of the errors): the story says 422 `PERMIT_GATE_BLOCKED` (AC-3, UAT-AC369) and 409 `MEASUREMENT_IN_ACTIVE_ACCEPTANCE` (AC-1); the registry lists the opposite as the primary status of each (and allows both). The commands answer what the acceptance text says; the table of CTR.md and `openapi.json` say the same. `PmtGateStore.assertPasses` still answers 409 to the other callers.
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, the cap): what counts against the quantity of the item is draft + sent + approved, for the whole contract; a rejected or superseded measurement does not. The approval judges the cap again against the revision in force **now** (a change order that lowered the quantity under what is waiting refuses the approval with 422 `QUANTITY_EXCEEDS_BOQ`; the maker can give it back). A correction is judged against the revision in force now too, and an item the revision in force no longer prices has quantity zero.
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, the cumulative): `quantity_cumulative` = approved before + this period, computed at record and again at approval (the only two moments it can change); it is a reading aid, the cap is judged on the live total. A SUBMITTED measurement that is later approved after another one was approved first gets the cumulative of the order of approval, not of the order of recording.
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, the reference): the unique index of the generated schema (`measurement_reference_uq`, all but SUPERSEDED) was dropped and recreated (V1388) to release the number of a REJECTED measurement as well, so the supervisor can record again with the same form. An override reason on a record keeps the duplicate number for good (the index ignores it).
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, the contract status): record and send need ACTIVE; a correction needs ACTIVE or SUSPENDED (the work is stopped, the books are still put right); a decision does not look at the status of the contract, so what was sent before a suspension can still be decided.
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, evidence): `evidence_refs` are `{ document_version_id }` of clean versions of the tenant (the schema had free objects; the compat allow-list records the change); a draft may have none, a sent one must have at least one, a correction must send its own. The decision's `evidence_ids` are documents, like the other decisions. `note` of `submitMeasurement` is kept as the reason of the audit row.
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, the stable item): `recordMeasurement` names the stable BOQ item, which the BOQ read hid from every guest. It is now shown to a guest who holds `measurement.record` over the contract (the rule is per row, by the place of the contract); a guest who only reads the BOQ still does not see it (the test of the import is unchanged).
- ST-CTR-008 (decision, visibility): a DRAFT is read only by its maker (the exposure note of the read says "ẩn bản chưa nộp của bên khác"); the list and the read take the caller as `viewer`.
- ST-CTR-008 (contract): `CtrMeasurementLine` and `CtrMeasurementPage` type the two reads; `contract_id` filter on the list; `x-error-codes` completed for the five commands; `ctr.measurement.returned.v1` and `.rejected.v1` added to the decision. `compat-allow.json` lists the narrowing of the evidence items and the scaffold response of the read by id (nothing consumed them).
- ST-CTR-008 (migration): V1388 adds the identity, sent, evidence, number and text checks, replaces the unique index of the number, the guard of the edges and the immutability of what was approved, and revokes `DELETE`/`TRUNCATE`.
- ST-CTR-008 (web): CTR-10 (`MeasurementScreen`, with the record, correct and decision dialogs); the workspace links to it; the period picker needs `reporting_period.read` (otherwise the identifier is typed). The parent screen CTR-09 (site diary) is another story; the back link goes to its route. Browser flows `apps/web/e2e/ctr-measurement-flows.spec.ts` (users `measurementRecorder`, `measurementApprover`, `measurementReader`); visual region CTR-10.
- ST-CTR-008 (open, role pack): no role of the baseline carries `measurement.*` yet, for the supervisor or for the guest contractor; a guest role needs `measurement.record/submit/read`, `boq_line.read` and `reporting_period.read` to use the screen as designed. The tenant defines the roles until the pack of CTR exists.
- ST-CTR-008 (open, offline): the offline queue of `recordMeasurement` with a client operation id is MOB-63; the command is idempotent by key already.
- ST-CTR-008 (open, reviews): the money and authorization reviews the story requires were not run (no subagents were allowed). The cap under concurrency, the cumulative, the maker-checker, the guest scope and the visibility of drafts are covered by tests (database, API, browser), not by an independent reader. Run both before the story is called done.

### ST-BID-007 (procurement petitions with data-driven deadlines, BID-20, slice BID-1)
- ST-BID-007 (decision, the rule): the deadline is the master rule type `BID_PETITION` (keys `PROCESS`, `RESULT`), modelled on `LND_TIMER`: days, calendar or working days of a named ADM calendar, with its legal basis and `CXN-BID-02`. The baseline carries 7 **calendar** days for both kinds (the stricter reading of the spec's "7 working days", so no petition is answered late on a count that is longer than the law's); a tenant that counts working days publishes a version with its calendar. `RULE_OWNER_MODULES` gained `BID` and the `CXN` pattern accepts `CXN-BID-02`. The story is DONE_UNCONFIRMED until CXN-BID-02 is confirmed.
- ST-BID-007 (decision, the error codes): a missing rule reuses `TIMER_RULE_UNCONFIRMED` (registered for LND, same meaning). `PETITION_OUT_OF_TIME` is not defined by the spec beyond its name; it means here: the day received is ahead of today, or a petition about the result arrives with no approved result, before the day it was approved, or after the days the rule (optional `filing_days`) gives from that day. A petition about the process has no window. To be confirmed with Legal together with CXN-BID-02.
- ST-BID-007 (decision, the answer): `resolution_document_id` was required by the schema (a missing field is 400); it is optional in the schema now and the command answers 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` without it, as the acceptance criterion says (relaxing a required field is compatible). The document only has to exist in the tenant and differ from the petition's own; it is not required to be an issued outgoing document (the reply goes out through DOC; the link between the two is not built here). `outcome` `ESCALATED` passes the petition to the level above and the level above then answers it with `RESOLVED`; `PROCESSING` and `WITHDRAWN` exist in the table and its guard but no command sets them.
- ST-BID-007 (decision, the conflict): "an evaluator involved in the petition" is read as: a member with a declaration of conflict that stands between them and the contractor of the petition or its package (`blockingDeclaration`, the same test the contract uses) - 409 `CONFLICT_OF_INTEREST_BLOCKED`, not 403, the registered status of the code. The evaluators who signed the evaluation report but declared nothing are not stopped; whether they should be is a question for the business (the report signers are not structured data before ST-BID-012).
- ST-BID-007 (decision, who logs): the petition is logged by the planner on behalf of the contractor (`bid.petition.manage`); a guest contractor does not lodge one in the system in this story. The contractor of a petition must exist in the tenant; whether it took part in the bidding is not asked.
- ST-BID-007 (decision, the reminder): one reminder for each petition, the first time a pass finds it overdue (`overdue_notified_at`), to the recipients that the notification workflow of `bid.petition.overdue` resolves for the package; no workflow is published by default (the event is consumed and no notification is made until a tenant publishes one). The list and the screen show the overdue state from the clock at once.
- ST-BID-007 (contract): `listProcurementPetitions` (CR, `bid.read`) with `BidPetitionLine` / `BidPetitionList`; `x-error-codes` of the two commands completed; `bid.petition.escalated.v1` added.
- ST-BID-007 (web): BID-20 (`PetitionScreen`) embeds the declarations panel of BID-05, where conflicts are declared and decided; documents are typed by identifier (there is no document picker yet, like BID-18). Browser flow `apps/web/e2e/bid-petition-flows.spec.ts` (users `petitionManager`, `petitionConflicted`, `petitionAuthority`, `petitionReader`); visual region BID-20.
- ST-BID-007 (open, reviews): the legal-template review the story requires was not run (no subagents were allowed), and the rule data waits for CXN-BID-02.

### ST-QMS-004 (inspection request, schedule and result with the regime of the day, QMS-04, slice QMS-1)
- ST-QMS-004 (decision, the regime): the regime is the master rule type `QMS_REGIME` (keys `ND-06-2021`, `ND-207-2026`), modelled on `BID_PETITION`: required signer roles, the roles of the completion, a checklist for each kind of inspection, with its legal basis and `CXN-07`. The regime in force on the **day asked for** (`requested_for`) decides, and the inspection keeps the version of the rule, a snapshot of the roles, the checklist and whether the regime waited for its confirmation. The checklists and the date the replacement regime starts (2027-01-01) in the baseline are working assumptions of the platform, not the text of a decree; both regimes are flagged `awaiting_confirmation`. The story is DONE_UNCONFIRMED until CXN-07 is confirmed.
- ST-QMS-004 (decision, the error codes): `REGIME_NOT_EFFECTIVE` (422) is new: no regime is in force on the day asked for. `PERMIT_GATE_BLOCKED` is answered 422 by the construction commands (`assertGateOpen`, extracted from the measurement store into `permit-gate-guard.ts`), as the acceptance criterion says; the commands that cannot go on at all keep 409.
- ST-QMS-004 (decision, the checklist): an answer is `{code, outcome: PASS|FAIL|NA, note?}`. The rules the server holds: every item of the template answered once and nothing else; a PASS has no failed item; a FAIL or CONDITIONAL has at least one; a CONDITIONAL states its condition in the note of each failed item (422 `CHECKLIST_INCOMPLETE`, `reason` in the extensions). The result of an inspection is written once; a recorded inspection is kept as it is by the trigger `guard_inspection`.
- ST-QMS-004 (decision, what follows a failure): the story says a failed result "drives the next step". The non-conformance report is a later QMS story; this one only carries `failed_item_codes` in the event `qms.inspection.result_recorded.v1` (codes, never the words of a note). "Cannot be edited after acceptance minutes exist" holds from the result on: the acceptance minutes (ST-QMS-005) will refer to a recorded inspection.
- ST-QMS-004 (decision, who reads): the read needs the new permission `inspection.read` over the scope of the contract; the guest contractor roles need request and read to see their own requests. The list hides nothing else of the inspection from a reader.
- ST-QMS-004 (contract): `listInspections` and `getInspection` (CR, `inspection.read`) with `QmsInspectionLine` / `QmsInspectionList`; the checklist of the result gets its item schema; `x-error-codes` of the four commands completed; `compat-allow.json` lists the breaking items. Registered in the authz and isolation suites; the members of the browser stack are 815-817.
- ST-QMS-004 (web): QMS-04 (`InspectionScreen`) lists the inspections with the regime, the roles that sign and the warning `[CXN-07] chưa xác nhận`; the diary and the paper are typed by identifier (there is no picker yet); the participants come from the members the caller may read (`membership.read`), and the screen says so when they cannot be read. Browser flow `apps/web/e2e/qms-inspection-flows.spec.ts` (users `inspectionRequester`, `inspectionSupervisor`, `inspectionReader`); visual region QMS-04.
- ST-QMS-004 (open, reviews): the authorization and the legal-template review the story requires were not run (no subagents were allowed); the gate-blocked path is proved by the API test, not by the browser (the stack seeds no permit gate).

### ST-QMS-005 (quality acceptance minutes, signatures and the event to CTR, QMS-05, slice QMS-1)
- ST-QMS-005 (decision, what is signed): the minutes store `content_hash`, the SHA-256 of their canonical content (sorted keys: the minutes, the contract, the inspection, the number, the scope, the paper, the roles, the result and the answers of the checklist). Nothing about the minutes changes after they are drafted (trigger), so the hash cannot drift; every signature is over that hash (BR-QMS-02), a database trigger refuses one over another hash or for a role the minutes do not require, and approval and signing recompute the hash from the rows and refuse (`CONTENT_CHANGED`) if it differs. A signature is never updated or deleted.
- ST-QMS-005 (decision, who signs which role): the spec says the caller names the role and the server checks that the role is required, but not whose role it is. Read as BR-QMS-05 needs it: a guest of the contractor of the contract (or of a party of its joint venture) signs as `CONTRACTOR` and as nothing else; the staff signs as `SUPERVISOR`, `OWNER` or `DESIGNER`; a guest of another firm signs as none (403 `SCOPE_DENIED`, `detail: signer_role`, added to the codes of the operation); one person signs once for a set of minutes (409 `ALREADY_SIGNED`), so one person cannot be the supervisor and the owner. Which staff member is the supervisor of this contract is not modelled (the roles are not tied to people yet); the permission `qacceptance.sign` over the contract is the right to sign.
- ST-QMS-005 (decision, signature methods): BR-QMS-03 and UAT-AC357 fix the worth of a signature: `DIGITAL_CERT` is a `LEGAL_E_SIGNATURE` and needs `cert_serial`; the in-app methods are an `INTERNAL_RECORD` and have none (422 `SIGNATURE_METHOD_NOT_ACCEPTED` otherwise; `qms_signature_effect_chk` keeps the table to it). The screen says "internal record, not a digital signature". Open: the rule data QMS.SIGNATURE (which methods a tenant accepts) is built with the site diary (ST-QMS-001) and both will read it; the certificate is not verified through the DOC adapter yet (it does not exist), so a serial is taken as given; whether a certificate signature is legally enough waits for CXN-EXE-05.
- ST-QMS-005 (decision, the serious non-conformance): `quality_nonconformance.blocks_acceptance` (V002) and a status other than `CLOSED` block the minutes of the whole contract, at drafting and at approval, judged before the state (UAT-AC361 approves minutes that are only drafted and expects `OPEN_CRITICAL_NCR`). The spec does not tie an NCR to an item for this rule; ST-QMS-006 gives the NCR its inspection and may narrow it. The NCR itself, its corrective actions and its closing are ST-QMS-006's; the browser stack and the tests insert them directly.
- ST-QMS-005 (decision, scope and inspection): the scope must fit the kind of inspection (see docs/qms-acceptance.md); the roles that sign are the inspection's own snapshot, so the completion has the designer by the inspection and not by a second lookup. The approval also needs the contract to be `ACTIVE` (409 `CONTRACT_STATE_INVALID`, added to the codes); drafting needs the same.
- ST-QMS-005 (decision, reject and revoke): `reject` is the other decision of `decideQualityAcceptance` and moves `SIGNING` to `REJECTED` with the reason (audited, no event; the spec names no event for it); whoever drafted the minutes may reject them (only approving is barred). Revoking is refused while an approved acceptance holds the minutes, read from `acceptance.quality_acceptance_id` (V002) and status `APPROVED`; CTR sets the column in ST-CTR-009 and its consumer of `qms.quality_acceptance.revoked.v1` unlinks a draft acceptance (AC-2 says "CTR unlinks the acceptance"): that consumer is ST-CTR-009's, here the event is emitted and the guard is in place.
- ST-QMS-005 (contract): `listQualityAcceptances` and `getQualityAcceptance` (CR, `qacceptance.read`) with `QmsAcceptanceLine` / `QmsAcceptanceList`; `x-error-codes` of the four commands completed (no breaking change). Registered in the authz and isolation suites; the members of the browser stack are 818-821 (the flow makes 820 a guest of the contractor of the seeded contract).
- ST-QMS-005 (web): QMS-05 (`AcceptanceScreen`); the paper of the minutes is typed by identifier (the DOC render port the story assumes does not exist; there is no picker yet). `CommandFrame` is shared with QMS-04. Browser flow `apps/web/e2e/qms-acceptance-flows.spec.ts` (users `acceptanceDrafter`, `acceptanceOwner`, `acceptanceContractor`, `acceptanceReader`); visual region QMS-05.
- ST-QMS-005 (open, reviews): the authorization review the story requires was not run (no subagents were allowed); the story is DONE_UNCONFIRMED until CXN-07 is confirmed.

### ST-CTR-009 (acceptance of work with price snapshot and quality gate, CTR-12, slice CTR-1)
- ST-CTR-009 (decision, when a line counts): `acceptance_line.active` defaults to true in the table, but two acceptances containing the same measurement must be able to wait (UAT-AC314 starts from two sent acceptances and expects exactly one approval, by the unique index `acceptance_line_measurement_uq WHERE active`). A line is therefore written **inactive** with its draft acceptance and becomes active when the acceptance is approved (and inactive again when it is revoked): "active" means "counts as accepted". The guard `guard_acceptance_line` keeps it to that, and the earlier measurement tests that wrote active lines directly were adapted. The pre-check (409 `MEASUREMENT_ALREADY_ACCEPTED` naming the ids) is made first; the index decides when two approvals race (30 rounds, in the store and over HTTP).
- ST-CTR-009 (decision, the price): the price of a line is the line of the item in the revision whose dates contain the day of the acceptance (`accepted_on`, the Vietnam date it is drawn up; `EFFECTIVE` or `SUPERSEDED`), because a revision has one line for an item (V1382); the retention percentage and the rounding rule come from that line, and the line keeps them (UAT-AC58: a later revision changes none of it). No revision in force on the day is 422 `REVISION_NOT_EFFECTIVE`. An acceptance is of one period (`period_id`) and has at most one line for an item and a party.
- ST-CTR-009 (decision, the minutes of quality): the spec says CTR receives `qms.quality_acceptance.approved.v1` and links the minutes (UAT-AC313 step 2). A consumer would leave the link stale when the minutes are revoked, so the approval **reads QMS's state when it decides** instead: the minutes named on the acceptance serve while approved; otherwise the latest approved minutes of the contract whose inspection one of the measurements was made against (`measurement.inspection_id`) are linked at approval. None is 422 `QUALITY_ACCEPTANCE_REQUIRED`. Submit refuses (the same code) only minutes that were named and can never serve (rejected or revoked): the acceptance may wait for minutes, as UAT-AC313 shows. If a consumer is wanted later for display, it can set the link ahead of the decision.
- ST-CTR-009 (decision, the serious non-conformance): "no open critical NCR on the item" is read as: not closed and `blocks_acceptance`, and about the work (one of the measurements, or an inspection of one of the items) or about no work in particular (the contract in general). One about other work does not stop the acceptance. ST-QMS-005 judges the minutes of quality at the level of the contract; the two can be aligned when ST-QMS-006 gives an NCR its item.
- ST-CTR-009 (decision, the ledger): "exactly one RECOGNIZE_ACCEPTANCE ledger entry per line" belongs to PAY (ST-PAY-001 consumes the event; the story's assumption says so). This story updates `contract.accepted_total_vnd` (CHECK `contract_ledger_order_chk`) and the event carries a line for each measurement with the keys PAY's ledger uses; the exactly-once of the ledger is proved there. The revoke guard reads `payment_request.claims_acceptance` (V002) and is tested with rows until ST-PAY-003.
- ST-CTR-009 (decision, the people): the decision cannot come from whoever drafted or sent the acceptance (`assertNotProposer`; the database keeps `decided_by <> submitted_by`); revoking cannot come from whoever sent or approved it. The decision schema allows `return` (the state guard of the operation says SUBMITTED to APPROVED, REJECTED or DRAFT): it clears who sent it. `acceptance_no` is NT-nnn for the contract. Rejecting and sending back are audited with the reason and send no event; the spec's events are drafted, submitted, approved and revoked.
- ST-CTR-009 (contract): the generic reads `get_resources_acceptance` and `get_resources_acceptance_id` are typed (`CtrAcceptanceLine`, `CtrAcceptancePage`, filter `contract_id`) with the breaking items listed in `compat-allow.json` (as for the measurements); `x-error-codes` of the four commands completed. Registered in the authz and isolation suites; the members of the browser stack are 822-825.
- ST-CTR-009 (web): CTR-12 (`AcceptanceScreen`); documents are typed by identifier (there is no picker yet); `CommandFrame` of QMS is shared. Browser flow `apps/web/e2e/ctr-acceptance-flows.spec.ts`; visual region CTR-12.
- ST-CTR-009 (open, reviews): the money and the authorization reviews the story requires were not run (no subagents were allowed); the story is DONE_UNCONFIRMED until CXN-07 is confirmed.

### ST-PAY-001 (cumulative ledger, lock order and acceptance recognition, PAY-10, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-001 (decision, what is new in the database): V002 already had the two tables with their row level security, the four CHECKs, the key of a balance and the single reversal of an entry. V1562 adds what makes it a ledger: the application role loses UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE on the entries (and DELETE/TRUNCATE on the balances), a trigger stops anyone else from changing or deleting an entry, an entry must belong to the balance it names and a reversal must be the exact opposite of what it reverses, and a balance must be the sum of its entries **at every commit** (deferred constraint triggers on both tables, so either may be written first). The spec's "append-only guards" and "tenant-leading indexes" were the missing half; the indexes of the identifiers were in V002 already, so only the two ordering indexes are new. The migration is numbered V1562: V1560 and V1561 are the Treasury import of the same block.
- ST-PAY-001 (decision, state not order): the consumer recognises an acceptance only while it is `APPROVED` and takes it back only while it is `REVOKED`, read under the lock of the contract, and takes the amounts from `acceptance_line` rather than from the payload. The outbox does not promise that an approval and a revocation of one acceptance arrive in order across consumers; reading the state makes the result the same either way, and the amounts cannot disagree with the table. The spec's "per acceptance line" is read as per balance (one for each item and party), because the key of `cumulative_ledger` is `(contract, item, acceptance, party)` and an acceptance has one line for each measurement, several of which can be of one item.
- ST-PAY-001 (decision, lock order): contract row first, then the balances in the order of their identifiers (the rank of `ledger-registry.ts`). The spec's chain "contract -> ledger rows by boq_item_id -> advance rows -> CAP" is built as far as this story reaches (the contract and the balances); the advance rows and CAP are taken by the claim and payment stories, which call `PayLedgerStore.post` under the same first lock. Ordering the balances by their identifier rather than by item is the order the registry's `lockLedgerRows` already guarantees for every ledger of the product.
- ST-PAY-001 (decision, the header): the totals on the contract (accepted, requested, paid, advance, retention) and their CHECKs were in V002 already (TK-PAY-001-2). `accepted_total_vnd` is moved by CTR when it approves or revokes; this story reads it to say whether the balances reconcile with it (`reconciled`) and moves only the version of the contract, so a cached page of an older version is never served. The requested, paid and advance totals are moved by the claim and payment stories together with their entries.
- ST-PAY-001 (decision, guests): the ledger is for the staff who hold `ledger.read`. A guest of the contractor is refused with 403 `SCOPE_DENIED` even with the permission on the contract (the spec: "a guest contractor never receives ledger fields"); a contract outside the scope is 404 like any other.
- ST-PAY-001 (decision, the cache): the page is cached per process under `t:{tenant}:pay:ledger:{contract}:v{version}:{question}`; the version moves with every entry, and any `pay.*` event about the contract drops the keys under its prefix (the consumer handles them, including the two it emits itself). A second process is correct because of the version, not because of the drop. `SummaryCache` gained `deletePrefix`.
- ST-PAY-001 (contract): `getCumulativeLedger` answered `additionalProperties: true`; it is typed (`PayCumulativeLedger`, `PayLedgerLine`, `PayLedgerEntry`, `PayLedgerBalance`, `PayLedgerContract`), no field was removed, so no entry in `compat-allow.json`. `x-error-codes`: 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`, 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`.
- ST-PAY-001 (observability): the gauge `pay_ledger_lock_wait_seconds` (alert `PAY_LEDGER_LOCK_WAIT` above 5 s) and the counter `pay_value_exceeded_total{rule}` are emitted by `PayLedgerStore` when a `MetricsSink` is given; `createOutboxConsumers` still passes none (as for the other consumers), so they reach an exporter when ST-KERNEL-050 wires one. The alert `PAY_LEDGER_CHECK_VIOLATION` is raised by the consumer through the alert sink it already has. Runbooks in `docs/runbooks/alerts`.
- ST-PAY-001 (web): PAY-10 (`LedgerScreen`), read-only; the filters are the server's (`boq_item_id`, `acceptance_id`) and the choices are those of the lines read so far; `keepPreviousData` keeps the page in place while a filter is applied. Browser flow `apps/web/e2e/pay-ledger-flows.spec.ts` (the ledger is written to the database as the consumer leaves it, with a claim and a reversed payment); visual region PAY-10.
- ST-PAY-001 (open, performance): the budget of UAT-AC343 is declared in the API test (3,000 balances, p95 5 s cold and 1 s warm over HTTP in the test stack). The spec says "thousands of ledger rows"; the number is ours and not a measured production figure.
- ST-PAY-001 (open, reviews): the money, authorization and migration reviews the story requires (chief accountant and an architect, 6 to 10 hours) were not run; no subagents were allowed. The story lists no CXN, so it is DONE, with those reviews open.

### ST-PAY-002 (payment request draft with server-computed money, PAY-01, PAY-02, PAY-03, CTR-13, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-002 (decision, recovery per tranche): the formula of BR-PAY-04 is applied to each tranche of the advance of a party with its own threshold (`contract_advance.recovery_threshold_pct`, 80 by default) and the results are added: for one tranche it is exactly the formula of the spec, and it stays right when a party has been advanced twice at different thresholds. `G` (the gross asked for before) is the sum of `cumulative_ledger.requested_vnd` of the party across the contract; a line of the acceptance without a party is counted under the one active party of the contract (a joint venture whose line names none is 422 `PARTY_REQUIRED`).
- ST-PAY-002 (decision, the cap): recovery plus retention never go above the gross of a line (the line CHECK of V002), so the recovery asked for by the formula is shared by the largest remainder with each line capped at what it can carry and the rest shared again among the others; what cannot be carried is left for the next request (the spec does not say, the CHECK forces the choice, and the alternative of refusing the request would make an advance already past its threshold block every payment). `advance_recovery_extra_vnd` is read as recovery on top of the formula, within the room the tranches have left, shared over the parties by the largest remainder on that room.
- ST-PAY-002 (decision, the tax adjustment): it belongs to the header (`payment_request_line_net_chk` of V002 has no tax), so the lines add up to `net - tax`; it needs `tax_adjustment_basis` (CHECK) and a basis given without an adjustment is not kept.
- ST-PAY-002 (decision, drafts and claims): the spec says several drafts of one acceptance may exist and the claim is taken when the request is sent; creating is still refused with 409 `ACCEPTANCE_ALREADY_CLAIMED` when another request already holds the claim (it could never be sent). The other drafts are a warning (`OTHER_DRAFTS_FOR_ACCEPTANCE`) in the answer and a count on the detail (hidden from a guest of the contractor). The request number is unique in the contract and never overridable.
- ST-PAY-002 (decision, who may let a duplicate invoice through): UAT-AC60 says an approver "has the right to approve exceptions" but the permission registry has no such permission. It is read as `payment.verify` (the right of the checker of PAY) over the contract, held together with `payment.document.attach`, by a person other than whoever drew the request up (`assertNotProposer`). A reason for a number that was not used before is refused (422 `NO_DUPLICATE`). A duplicate in another contract is the warning `DUPLICATE_INVOICE_OTHER_CONTRACT` and also in the audit row.
- ST-PAY-002 (decision, the line of capital): at drafting it must be the project's, in force (it has a balance), of a plan year no earlier than the year the acceptance was drawn up and still open (the end of its year, or the day an extension of the appropriation reaches); whether it has enough left is judged by the port `CapAvailabilityStore` when the request is sent (UAT-AC342 steps 2 and 3, the story of the submission). The spec's "remaining limit of the line" field of PAY-02 is not shown: the options carry the amount of the line as the plan states it.
- ST-PAY-002 (decision, kinds): the API takes ADVANCE (from a planned `contract_advance`, gross is its amount, no lines), INTERIM and FINAL (a final request names a settlement of the contract); the form of PAY-02 draws INTERIM only, because the advance and the settlement are asked in their own screens (PAY-11 and the settlement stories). The contract states of BR-PAY-09 are in `pay-request.ts`.
- ST-PAY-002 (decision, the database): V1563 holds what the application could be bypassed on: the edges of the status, amounts fixed once sent, lines that are lines of the acceptance and worth what it says, lines that add up to the header at the commit, clean documents, a request never deleted, a document never changed. Fixtures of earlier stories that wrote a payment request directly were corrected where the new rules require it (a request number for a returned one, `RETURNED` instead of `CANCELLED` without a reason).
- ST-PAY-002 (contract): `PayPaymentCommandResult` (the figures and the warnings on top of `CommandResult`), `PayPaymentRequest`, `PayPaymentRequestPage`, `PayPaymentLine`, `PayPaymentDocument`, `PayPaymentWarning`; the two generic reads are typed with the breaking items listed in `compat-allow.json` as for the measurements and acceptances. UAT-AC323 step 4 expects 400 `INVALID_REQUEST` for a body with `gross_vnd`; the platform answers 400 `FIELD_NOT_ALLOWED` for a field outside the allowlist (the code the other commands already give), and the test says so.
- ST-PAY-002 (web): PAY-01, PAY-02, PAY-03 and CTR-13 (`ValuationScreen`, a read of the same request, with the price in force and the retention rate on each line); documents are typed by identifier (no picker yet). Browser flow `apps/web/e2e/pay-payment-flows.spec.ts` (the capital line, the contract and the approved acceptance are written to the database); visual regions PAY-01, PAY-02, PAY-03, CTR-13.
- ST-PAY-002 (open, reviews): the money review the story requires (3 to 5 hours with the chief accountant) was not run; no subagents were allowed. The golden cases are those of the spec. The story is DONE_UNCONFIRMED until CXN-01 (national project code) and CXN-02 (advance cap and threshold) are confirmed.

### ST-PAY-003 (sending, checking and returning a payment request, PAY-03, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-003 (decision, the acceptance is recognised on demand): sending recognises the acceptance on the ledger if the consumer has not yet (`PayLedgerStore.recognize`, idempotent under the lock of the contract), so the ledger never depends on the order of the events and a request can be sent the moment its acceptance is approved.
- ST-PAY-003 (decision, room on the line of capital): the CAP reserve/commit calls arrive with ST-CAP-011, so the check of UAT-AC342 is allocated less disbursed less the nets of the requests in flight (SUBMITTED, VERIFIED, SENT_TREASURY) on the line, 422 `ALLOCATION_UNAVAILABLE` with reason `INSUFFICIENT`. It is replaced by the CAP call when that story lands.
- ST-PAY-003 (decision, retention): `retention_hold` rows are written with `release_condition` COMPLETION (the spec names no other until the settlement story); a return deletes them (V1564 lets a hold be deleted only when its request is RETURNED and nothing was released) and writes `RETENTION_RELEASE`, which is not a reversal kind, so the entries of a second round pair with the holds of that round by time.
- ST-PAY-003 (decision, returns): a request `SENT_TREASURY` is returned only when the Treasury returned it (`returned_by_treasury`), so the screen offers return for SUBMITTED and VERIFIED only; the Treasury callback story sets the flag. Revising a returned request makes it a draft again (ST-PAY-002), sending it takes the claim again.
- ST-PAY-003 (decision, maker-checker): the checker is neither the person who drew the request up nor the one who sent it (`assertNotProposer`); the same person may hold payment.create, payment.submit and payment.verify and still be refused on their own request.
- ST-PAY-003 (decision, the alert): the spec asks for a lock wait above 2 s (p95); the gauge `pay_ledger_lock_wait_seconds` is now set by the writers of the ledger and by sending, checking and returning, and `PAY_LEDGER_LOCK_WAIT` fires above 2 s (it was 5 s).
- ST-PAY-003 (found by a test): a request returned, sent again and returned again left its second hold on the ledger, because the first `RETENTION_RELEASE` of the request counted as the release of every hold; a release now counts only for the holds made before it. Covered by the second return in `pay-claim-store.test.ts`.
- ST-PAY-003 (open): reviews of money, authorization and migration were not run (no subagents); the UAT-AC320 concurrency was run over the store with 30 rounds and over HTTP with two keys, not with a load of 50 rounds of two threads over HTTP.

### ST-PAY-004 (advance request conditions, cap and linear recovery per member, PAY-07, CTR-06, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-004 (decision, the rule data): the cap and the threshold are the rule type `PAY_ADVANCE_CAP` (key = the category of the contract, tiers by the value of the contract) and the days of a land clearance advance `PAY_ADVANCE_GPMB`, both platform rules read by date with the version stored on the advance (`cap_rule_version_id`). The baseline carries only the fixture of UAT-AC326 (CONSTRUCTION from 10bn at 20 percent, CONSULTING at 30 percent, threshold 80, GPMB 30 days), flagged `awaiting_confirmation` with CXN-02 and CXN-12; a category or a value with no row is 422 `ADVANCE_RULE_UNCONFIRMED` (new code) rather than a number the platform made up. The story is DONE_UNCONFIRMED until the legal owner signs the figures.
- ST-PAY-004 (decision, what counts under the cap): BR-PAY-10 says "paid and requested"; a planned advance holds its place too (otherwise two accountants could plan beyond the cap before either is sent), a cancelled one gives it back, and a recovered one still counts (the cap is on what was advanced, not on what is owed).
- ST-PAY-004 (decision, ADVANCE_OUTSTANDING_PRIOR): the spec names the code and no condition; UAT-AC326 (a second tranche of 500m is allowed while the first, 1.5bn, is still owed) rules out "any earlier tranche owed". It is read as the overdue rule of PAY 3.2 step 4: a land clearance advance past its deadline, or a contract advance whose member has already asked to be paid up to the threshold and still owes it.
- ST-PAY-004 (decision, requestAdvance is a plan): it creates the advance PLANNED and nothing else (UAT-AC325: no journal entry until it is paid); the payment request of the ADVANCE kind then takes it to REQUESTED when sent (ST-PAY-003 code, tested here) and back to PLANNED when returned. PAID, `paid_on` and the deadline of a land clearance advance are set by the Treasury story; V1565 lets only REQUESTED go to PAID and sets the amount paid once.
- ST-PAY-004 (decision, a new read): the screens need a list, which the contract does not have (`/resources/advance_ledger_entry` stays internal): `listAdvances` (`GET /advances`, `advance.read`) is added to the contract and to this story, with typed answers for `getAdvanceRecoveryPlan` and the three commands.
- ST-PAY-004 (decision, a joint venture): the recovery of a member depends on its own work value, its own advance and the gross it asked for (UAT-AC327 is a test over the real flow with two members: 450m, 450m and 187.5m); `getAdvanceRecoveryPlan` reads the gross of a member from the cumulative ledger, counting lines that name no party under the one active party of a sole contract.
- ST-PAY-004 (test infrastructure): the foreign-attempt specs of the isolation suite are now sent a batch of 32 at a time; with about a hundred and ten requests at once the listener of the test machine refused connections (ECONNREFUSED) as soon as four more were added.
- ST-PAY-004 (open): the form that asks for an advance (PAY-11) and the board of overdue advances with their alerts (PAY-12, the `advance-overdue` job) are ST-PAY-011; the Treasury confirmation that pays an advance (DISBURSE, `paid_on`, the deadline) is ST-PAY-005/006. The reviews of money and of the legal rule were not run (no subagents).

### ST-PAY-005 (Treasury payment forms via DOC and send-to-Treasury tracking, PAY-04, PAY-14, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-005 (decision, templates): the platform ships no template of the four forms (the decree appendix is not verified, CXN-08): they are the tenant's own templates in the DOC library, found by `form_code`, and DOC's rule decides (`TEMPLATE_UNCONFIRMED` while the template or its layout waits, `TEMPLATE_NOT_EFFECTIVE` when none is in force). The tests and the browser flow create confirmed fixture templates labelled as such.
- ST-PAY-005 (decision, one call or four): UAT-AC337 asks for three forms and expects one drawn and two refused; a command answers one status, so a call that names several forms draws all or none and the refusal names the form (`form_code`); a client that wants the partial result of the UAT calls once per form.
- ST-PAY-005 (decision, place): the place-and-date line of a state document needs a place and the platform holds none for an organisation unit; `place` is a required field of the call (the contract gains it, with the allow-list entry ST-PAY-005, the operation having no client).
- ST-PAY-005 (decision, forms and returns): forms are drawn for the figures of a checked request, so a return supersedes the standing forms (the DB test and the claim store); the event is `pay.payment_form.generated.v1` (the contract named `requested`, which describes an asynchronous request to DOC that this synchronous path does not make; `requested` stays for the settlement forms).
- ST-PAY-005 (decision, who sends): sending is its own permission (`payment.treasury.send`); the person who recorded it is kept (`treasury_sent_by`) but, unlike the check, there is no maker-checker rule on it in the spec.
- ST-PAY-005 (test infrastructure): direct updates of a request to SENT_TREASURY in older tests now go through `AcceptanceWorld.sendToTreasury` (four standing forms, the day, the person), because the guard of the table asks for them; rows inserted already in that status (the Treasury import fixtures) are not touched.
- ST-PAY-005 (open): the legal-template and money reviews were not run (no subagents); the settlement forms (`generateSettlementForms`, PAY-15) are another story; the content of the forms and the mapping await the accountant's sign-off (docs/pay-treasury-forms.md).

### ST-PAY-007 (matching Treasury transactions to payments and the differences, PAY-05, PAY-06, PAY-09, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-007 (decision, a refusal that is a record): `matchTreasuryTransaction` must answer 422 `AMOUNT_MISMATCH_OPENED` and also keep the difference it opened, but a command that throws rolls back. The command pipeline gains `scope.refuseAfterCommit(error)`: the work and its audit row commit, the refusal is stored as the answer of the idempotency key (so a replay gets the same 422) and is thrown after the callbacks. It is general, unit-tested in command-interceptor.test.ts, and used by no other command yet.
- ST-PAY-007 (decision, what settling puts on the books): a `PAYMENT` entry per balance of the ledger for the *gross* of the balance (UAT-AC335: B01 2bn, B02 1bn; the recovery and the retention were booked when the request was sent), `contract.paid_total_vnd` once; the advance recovered is not touched again. An advance request pays its advance (PAID, `paid_vnd` = the gross asked, `paid_on`, `contract.advance_paid_vnd`, an `advance_ledger_entry` DISBURSE); the last day of a land clearance advance comes from the rule `PAY_ADVANCE_GPMB` of the day it was paid, and stays empty if the rule is not there (nothing is made up).
- ST-PAY-007 (decision, the day of the disbursement, CXN-03): `payment_request.treasury_paid_on` is the Vietnam date of the `confirmed_at` the Treasury gave (a guard of the table checks it), carried by `pay.payment.settled.v2` (TK-007-7); v1 stays for the consumers that exist. Both are emitted by the same command, once each. The consumers (CAP DISBURSE, DIS, MNE) are other groups' stories.
- ST-PAY-007 (decision, the plan year, AC-4): the allocation line was judged at the time of sending; the settlement judges again only that the line is of the plan year the request carries (PLAN_YEAR_MISMATCH, also a guard of the table). It does not re-check the room on the line: the Treasury has already paid, and refusing to record it would leave the books behind the bank.
- ST-PAY-007 (decision, the owner of a new difference): a difference opened by the match is owned by the person who matched, due in three days (the same as the one opened by a confirmation by hand), until `assignReconciliationItem` gives it to someone; `owner_membership_id` and `due_at` are never null.
- ST-PAY-007 (decision, `note` of resolve): the contract table lists `note*`, but UAT-AC12 expects 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` when it is missing, while a missing required field of a body is 400 `INVALID_REQUEST` everywhere. The schema keeps `note` optional (the description says it is needed) and the store refuses an empty one.
- ST-PAY-007 (decision, new reads): `getReconciliationItem` (PAY-06 reads one item) and `getTreasuryTracking` (PAY-09, permission `treasury_transaction.read`) are added to the contract; the plan lists them under the story.
- ST-PAY-007 (decision, write-off): there is no separate permission for the approver in the contract (`reconciliation.resolve` carries the three resolutions); the rule is the person (not the owner) and the document, in the store and in the table (`reconciliation_item_write_off_chk`). A role that separates who may write off from who may explain is a change request.
- ST-PAY-007 (open): what becomes of a request whose difference has been explained or written off is not in the spec: it stays SENT_TREASURY (the Treasury paid another amount), and is settled only by a transaction of exactly its net. A corrective path (return by the Treasury, reversal, or an adjustment of the net) belongs to ST-PAY-008 (reversal). The reviews of money and of authorisation were not run (no subagents).

### ST-PAY-008 (reversing a settled payment against a confirmed refund, PAY-08, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-008 (decision, required fields): the contract table lists `reason*`, `approval_document_version_id*` and the refund as the three things a reversal needs, but UAT-AC331 expects 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` without a reason, 422 `REFUND_NOT_CONFIRMED` with a reason and no refund, and only then the approval; a missing required field of a body is 400 everywhere else. The schema keeps all three optional (the descriptions say each is needed) and the store asks in the order of the UAT (reason, refund, approval, person).
- ST-PAY-008 (decision, who may reverse): `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED` for the person who matched the payment (`settled_by`) or sent the file to the Treasury (`treasury_sent_by`): four eyes on money that goes back. The spec names the error but not the people; in a small agency where one person does both the check is a change request (a role, not a code change).
- ST-PAY-008 (decision, the refund): it must be a REFUND whose `reversal_of_id` is the transaction that settled this request, for exactly the net, of the same project and not matched yet. A partial refund is not a reversal here (the entries undo whole payments); a request settled by several transactions does not exist (one matched transaction per request).
- ST-PAY-008 (decision, the entries): the reversal posts `PAYMENT_REVERSAL` before the release of the claim, so the paid amount never lies above the requested one. The release of retention names no entry (as when a request is returned), so the screen pairs it with the hold on the same item that has the opposite figure. The entries say a fixed sentence; the person's words stay on the request and in the audit trail.
- ST-PAY-008 (decision, an advance): the payment of an advance request is reversed too (the spec only names the entries of an acceptance payment): the advance goes back to PLANNED, an `advance_ledger_entry` REVERSE_DISBURSE undoes the DISBURSE and the contract no longer shows the money out; nothing may have been recovered. V1568 adds the edge PAID to PLANNED to the guard of the advance, with the amount paid cleared.
- ST-PAY-008 (decision, retention): a reversal is refused (409 `PAYMENT_STATE_INVALID`, `RETENTION_RELEASED`) when part of the retention of the payment has already been released by CTR; the hold goes only while none of it was released (the guard of V1564, extended to a reversed request). How CTR gives back a retention that was paid out and then refunded is not in the spec.
- ST-PAY-008 (open): `pay.payment.reversed.v1` is emitted; the consumers that undo the DISBURSE (CAP, ST-CAP-006) and take the amount back in DIS and MNE are other groups' stories. The trace of the whole chain (`getPaymentChain`, PAY-17) is ST-PAY-0xx. The reviews of money and of authorization were not run (no subagents).

### ST-CAP-006 (postings of capital from payment events, nightly check of the balances, CAP 3.4)
- ST-CAP-006 (decision, no double commitment): the spec says a payment commits at the sending, but the activation of a contract already commits its whole value to the line (ST-CAP-005). A commitment again for each payment would count the same capital twice (the availability control and the check `allocated >= committed + disbursed` would refuse a line that is fully committed by its contract). A request therefore commits only when the contract holds no commitment on the line (`covered` otherwise); the settlement turns the commitment into a disbursement either way (`UNCOMMIT` then `DISBURSE`).
- ST-CAP-006 (decision, reversal): the reversal takes the `DISBURSE` back and, when the contract's own commitment paid it, also the `UNCOMMIT`, so the contract owes the amount again (the acceptance can be claimed again); the spec names only the reversal of the disbursement.
- ST-CAP-006 (decision, the posting date): the entries are dated by the moment the Treasury confirmed the payment (`confirmed_at` of `pay.payment.settled.v2`, whose Vietnam date is `treasury_paid_on`) and by the confirmation of the refund for a reversal.
- ST-CAP-006 (decision, state over order): the consumer judges by the status of the request under the lock of the balance, so an event read late or twice changes nothing; V1368 adds `source_ref_seq` to the key of an entry so a request that is returned and sent again can commit again.
- ST-CAP-006 (decision, the incident): the incident of the drift is the alert `CAP_BALANCE_DRIFT` (gauge `cap_balance_drift_vnd`) with the audit row and the event `cap.balance.drift_detected.v1`. An issue in ISS is not opened (CAP is not an issue source: `issue.source_module` and the intake would need CAP and a CAP category in master data); the event carries what the intake reads.
- ST-CAP-006 (open): the reserved amount (`reserved_vnd`) is not compared by the nightly check and `CONSUMED reservation hook` of TK-CAP-006-1 is not built, because reservations post no entries yet; the consumers of DIS and MNE for `pay.payment.settled.v2` and `pay.payment.reversed.v1` are other groups' stories; the review of money was not run (no subagents).

### ST-PAY-009 (settlement of a project, deadline by project group, exception close, PAY 3.5)
- ST-PAY-009 (decision, the figures): the spec says only that the figures come from the ledger. `settled` is the gross paid, `outstanding` is the accepted work not paid plus the retention still held (the debt that waits for the warranty, as in UAT-AC66), `unrecovered_advance` is the advance disbursed less recovered from the journal of the advance. A settlement of a year reads the payments of that year and the balances at its end. Whether the agency counts the retention as a debt of the settlement is a question for the accounting owner.
- ST-PAY-009 (decision, stage of the project): the whole project is settled when it is COMPLETED, a year or a contract while it is IMPLEMENTATION, SUSPENDED or COMPLETED (the spec only says 422 `PROJECT_STAGE_INVALID`). Closing a settlement does not move the project to SETTLED: that is the lifecycle of PRJ, which can consume `pay.settlement.closed.v1`.
- ST-PAY-009 (decision, the deadline rule): `PAY_SETTLEMENT_DEADLINE` is a platform rule type (key `VERIFY_DEADLINE`) with one row of days for each of the four groups. The baseline carries the fixture of UAT-AC339 flagged `awaiting_confirmation` (CXN-09), like the caps of the advance; "the rule is switched off" of the UAT is a version that is not in force on the day of the submission.
- ST-PAY-009 (decision, project group): `PROJECT_GROUP_MISSING` is a guard the table makes nearly unreachable (a project past its concept must have a group: `project_group_required_chk`); it answers for a project that was stopped after losing its group.
- ST-PAY-009 (decision, who): whoever sent the settlement does not verify it (the spec); the person who approves the exception or closes is neither who opened it nor who sent it (the spec names only "the maker"; both are the preparers). The exception is approved once, on a VERIFIED settlement, and is not taken back; the close accepts `decision: "approve"` only (the schema shares its `decision` enum with other commands; return and reject have no meaning for a settlement).
- ST-PAY-009 (decision, the day of the submission): it may not be after today in Vietnam (a settlement is not sent in the future); earlier days are allowed, as the filing may be recorded late.
- ST-PAY-009 (decision, the reminders): the reminders at 30 and 7 days are events with an audit row (for the notification module to consume when its templates exist); only the overdue settlement opens an issue in ISS (BR-ISS-07 allows one open issue for a source object, so a reminder as an issue would hide the late one). A settlement first found late is reported late and the reminders it missed are not sent afterwards.
- ST-PAY-009 (open): the references to the conclusions of AUD (`audit_finding_refs`) are kept as identifiers without checking them against AUD (it has no table of conclusions yet); `generateSettlementForms` and PAY-16 are another story; the consumers of `pay.settlement.*` events (PRJ, DIS, MNE) are other groups' stories; the reviews of money, authorization and the legal rule were not run (no subagents).

### ST-PAY-010 (trace of a payment, PAY-17)
- ST-PAY-010 (decision, the contract of the answer): the schema is a free object, so the answer is nodes with `type`, `id`, `version`, `revision`, `status`, `label`, `amount_vnd`, `superseded`, `facts` and edges with a relation, plus the totals; a Treasury transaction has no version (the table has none) and says so with `version: null`. The tree of the screen is a list of the kinds in the order of the chain, each node saying how many it leads to, rather than a drawn graph.
- ST-PAY-010 (decision, who sees it): the right is `payment.chain.read` over the project; a guest contractor gets 404 whatever the grant (the chain shows the payments and the Treasury of the owner, not a contractor's to read), the same answer as a project that is not there.
- ST-PAY-010 (decision, what counts): the totals count the requests that were sent, checked, with the Treasury or settled; a draft, a cancelled, a returned and a reversed request are shown in the chain and are not in the total. The chain is cut at 500 nodes a kind and 50 contracts, and says which kinds were cut.
- ST-PAY-010 (open): the review of authorization was not run (no subagents).

### ST-QMS-006 (non-conformances and corrective actions, QMS-07)
- ST-QMS-006 (decision, the reads): the design has no list or detail operation for non-conformances, only the generic `get_resources_quality_nonconformance` (+ `_id`), so those carry the board: the newest first with a keyset cursor (creation time in microseconds and id), filters `contract_id`, `status`, `severity`, each line with its actions and `allowed` computed for the caller (the web shows a button for exactly these; the server judges each command again).
- ST-QMS-006 (decision, the status): it is derived from the actions (`statusFromActions` in the kernel) and every command bumps the version of the non-conformance, so every event has its own aggregate version and a stale `If-Match` on assign/close/reopen is caught. Evidence and verification carry no `If-Match` (the contract says so); they lock the non-conformance and its action instead.
- ST-QMS-006 (decision, the evidence): a photo must be of the same contract and `CLEAN` (422 `FILE_NOT_CLEAN`, added to `x-error-codes` of the operation); a document must be `CLEAN` (422 `DOCUMENT_NOT_CLEAN`). The contract text says "geotag photo"; the geotag checks of the photo (`check_geofence`, `check_summary`) are not required here.
- ST-QMS-006 (decision, the fixtures): V1462 makes the status of a non-conformance move one edge at a time, so the tests of the acceptances that need a non-conformance closed or with another severity set the stage with `ncrStatement`/`closeNcr` (superuser, triggers off) in `packages/db/test/support/ncr-fixtures.ts` instead of updating it as the owner.
- ST-QMS-006 (open): nothing writes `root_cause` yet (the screen and the view hide it from a guest contractor, ready for the command that will); the automatic non-conformance from a failed material test (QMS-06) and the consumer of `qms.ncr.raised.v1` at CRITICAL that opens an issue in ISS (ISS-11) are not built; a non-conformance that is overdue raises nothing yet (no job).
- ST-QMS-006 (open): the review of authorization was not run (no subagents).

### ST-CTR-011 (completion of a contract, CTR-03)
- ST-CTR-011 (decision, the guards): the order is the minutes, the non-conformances, the measurements, then the day, so the person is told the first thing in the way; the identifiers are the first twenty of each kind. The day cannot be before the day (Vietnam) the minutes were approved, which the contract does not say.
- ST-CTR-011 (decision, who completes): the right is `contract.complete` over the contract; the same conflict-of-interest check as the other commands of the life of a contract applies, and no separate approver is asked for (the decision is the approved minutes). No delegation limit of the matrix is applied to a completion.
- ST-CTR-011 (decision, the summary): the staff of an ACTIVE contract get `completion` and `actions.complete` in the summary, read on every request (it is not part of the cached detail).
- ST-CTR-011 (open): the acceptance criterion says AST receives `ctr.contract.completed.v1` and proposes creating a warranty, without creating one when no handover exists; AST (ST-AST-001) does not exist yet, so only the event is published. Its consumer belongs to the AST stories.
- ST-CTR-011 (open): the rule data of the quality regime waits for CXN-07 (the story cites it), so the story is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the review of authorization was not run (no subagents).

### ST-AST-001 (handover and punch list, AST-01 and AST-02)
- ST-AST-001 (decision, the state machine): the design allows the decision from PUNCH_PENDING and INSPECTING only, but nothing moves a handover to INSPECTING and a handover with no defect would then need a defect to be accepted; a handover with no list yet (DRAFT) can be approved as it is (the as-built dossier and the independence still apply) and rejected, and only a handover with a list is given back. A decided handover is final.
- ST-AST-001 (decision, assets): `ASSET_NOT_REGISTERED` is in the list of errors of the decision, but `registerAsset` requires an ACCEPTED handover, so the two cannot both hold; the acceptance does not wait for assets (ST-AST-002 registers them after it). The O&M manual and the warranty certificate are kept when given and not required (the CHECK and the error list ask for the as-built dossier only).
- ST-AST-001 (decision, the data): `defect_photo_id`, `fixed_by/at/note`, `verification_note`, `reopen_count` and the decision note and evidence are added to the tables (V1520) so that the photo of the defect is kept apart from the evidence that it was put right and the verifier can be told apart from the contractor; the count of critical items and the snapshot are written by a database trigger, not by the command.
- ST-AST-001 (decision, scope): the decision and the reads are judged by the contract or by the receiving unit (a grant on that unit's subtree); the permission checks in the views use the same two places.
- ST-AST-001 (decision, who): the waiver is refused to the person who put the item on the list, the acceptance to the drafter of the handover, the verification to the person who said the item was put right (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`). The design has no field for who "said" it other than `fixed_by`.
- ST-AST-001 (open): the NCR link of a punch item (`nonconformance_id`) is not set by any command; the ISS consumers of the punch items that are overdue, the geotag checks of the photos and the mobile screen MOB-66 are not built; a handover that is given back or rejected emits no event (the design lists none).
- ST-AST-001 (open): the story cites CXN-07, so it is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the review of authorization was not run (no subagents).

### ST-AST-002 (asset dossier, AST-03)
- ST-AST-002 (decision, the value): the assets of a contract are summed over the assets that are not disposed and come from the contract of the handover; the contract row is locked first so that two registrations cannot both pass; the refusal carries `total_vnd` and `contract_value_vnd`.
- ST-AST-002 (decision, the national code): `external_asset_id` is never generated and never looked up; it is typed by hand at the transfer and the screen says so until it is set (the integration with the national system of public assets is not part of this story).
- ST-AST-002 (decision, the statuses): IN_OPERATION and DISPOSED are in the guard of the database and the kernel, but no command of this story moves an asset there; the stories that need them (warranty, disposal) add the commands.
- ST-AST-002 (decision, what is shown): the value, the basis and the total under the contract are masked unless the caller holds `asset.manage` or `asset.transfer`.
- ST-AST-002 (open): the story cites CXN-09, so it is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the review of authorization was not run (no subagents).

### ST-AST-003 (warranty obligation and closure, AST-04)
- ST-AST-003 (decision, the grade of the work): the model has no grade of construction work (cấp công trình) anywhere (the project has an investment group A/B/C, not a grade), yet the minimum term depends on it; `createWarrantyObligation` gains an optional `construction_grade` (additive to openapi.json) and the obligation stores it. A work whose grade is not stated is held to the longest minimum, so the unknown never lets a shorter term through. Where the grade should live in the model (project, contract or asset class) is for the construction business owner to say (CXN-EXE-04).
- ST-AST-003 (decision, the rule): the minimum terms are the new MDM rule type `AST_WARRANTY` (keys SPECIAL, I, II, III, IV; attribute `min_months`; owner module AST, added to the owners of rule data), platform-owned, baseline 24/24/12/12/12 flagged `awaiting_confirmation` with CXN-EXE-04: trial figures, not regulation. No rule in force is 422 `NO_EFFECTIVE_RULE`. The obligation stores the version of the rule, so the screen can say it waits for confirmation.
- ST-AST-003 (decision, expiry): the job that marks obligations EXPIRED is another story; an ACTIVE obligation whose last day has come counts as expired in the reads, the status filter and the closure, so the closure does not wait for the job. The database allows ACTIVE → CLOSED for the same reason.
