- ST-AST-003 (decision, open claims): a claim is open unless it is VERIFIED or CLOSED (a disputed or an unverified repair blocks the closure); the claim commands (file, acknowledge, fix, verify) are ST-AST-004, which also decides how a VERIFIED claim becomes CLOSED. The closure locks the obligation before it counts the claims and the claim guard of the database locks it while it writes, so the two cannot both pass.
- ST-AST-003 (decision, the list): the design has no list of obligations except the one that expires soon (`listExpiringWarranties`, for the job story) and the table has no generic read, so AST-04 reads a new `listWarrantyObligations` (additive to openapi.json); `listExpiringWarranties` is left to ST-AST-004.
- ST-AST-003 (decision, the date): `starts_on` is not tied to the date of the completion minutes: the story does not say the warranty must start on it, and the project office may have agreed a later start; the term and the end are what the rule judges.
- ST-AST-003 (open): the consumer of `ast.warranty.closed.v1` in CTR (release of the retention on `WARRANTY_END` and of the warranty guarantee, UAT-AC392 step 4) is ST-CTR-010/012/013; until then the event is only announced. The story cites CXN-EXE-04, so it is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the legal-rule and money reviews were not run (no subagents).

### ST-CTR-010 (release of the retention, CTR-21)
- ST-CTR-010 (decision, the condition of a hold): the payment writes holds with the condition COMPLETION (ST-PAY-003); the acceptance tests of the story speak of holds with WARRANTY_END. The release evaluates whichever condition the hold carries; who sets WARRANTY_END, and from which rule (`CTR.RETENTION`, CXN-EXE-04: the percentages and the conditions are rule data that does not exist in the repository yet), is still open. WARRANTY_END means every warranty obligation of the contract is closed and there is at least one; MANUAL_DECISION means a clean document is attached, now or by an earlier release.
- ST-CTR-010 (decision, the maker): the hold has no proposer column, so "the proposer is not the approver" is applied to the person who sent the payment request the money was held from (`created_by` of the hold), in the command and in a CHECK of the database. The workflow approval by a leader (u.lanhdao of the fixture) is not part of the command.
- ST-CTR-010 (decision, replay): AC-ST-CTR-010-2 says the new key after success answers 409 RELEASE_EXCEEDS_HELD, AC-ST-CTR-010-1 and the error catalogue say 422; the catalogue governs (422). A hold with nothing left answers RELEASE_EXCEEDS_HELD before the check of the version, so the replay with the old If-Match gets the business answer and not a conflict of versions.
- ST-CTR-010 (decision, the list): there is no generic read of `retention_hold`; CTR-21 reads a new `listRetentionHolds` (`GET /contracts/{id}/retention-holds`, additive to openapi.json, guarded by `ledger.read` like the cumulative ledger, a contractor of the contract is refused). The release itself pays nothing: the payment of released retention is a payment request of kind RETENTION_RELEASE (PAY).
- ST-CTR-010 (decision, the job): `retention-due` reminds once per hold on the morning its earliest day comes (new column `due_notified_on`, the event type `ctr.retention.due.v1` registered for the notification core); it does not check the condition, because the reminder is about the day.
- ST-CTR-010 (open): FORFEITED is in the statuses and the guard but no command forfeits a hold (termination with a decision, a later story). The story cites CXN-EXE-04, so it is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the money, authorization and legal-rule reviews were not run (no subagents). The test of the consumers of the outbox and the count of the timers of the workers were out of date since ST-CAP-006 and ST-CTR-010 (the consumer `cap-payment-ledger`, the new job) and are corrected here.

### ST-CTR-012 (closure of a contract, CTR-03)
- ST-CTR-012 (decision, the settlement): the command takes the settlement it rests on (`settlement_revision_id`, required by the contract) and judges it: a CONTRACT-scope settlement of this contract or a PROJECT-scope settlement of its project; an ANNUAL one serves no closure. Only a CLOSED one passes; the exception of the settlement is the only exception the advance guard accepts (the story says "an approved exception" and PAY has no other).
- ST-CTR-012 (decision, the retention): "released or transferred to warranty" is read as: every hold with something held is released, or waits for the end of the warranty (`WARRANTY_END`) while a warranty obligation of the contract that is not closed runs. Once the warranty is closed the rest has to be released. There is no separate act of transferring retention to the warranty (the obligation carries `retention_amount_vnd`, but the hold stays the money).
- ST-CTR-012 (decision, the inbox consumer): the task names a consumer of `pay.settlement.closed.v1`; the command reads the state of the settlement (as the consumers of PAY do: the state is read, not the event), so the event would have nothing to maintain. The notification that tells the leader a contract may now be closed is not built; it belongs with the notification rules of the contract workflow.
- ST-CTR-012 (decision, no migration): the edges to CLOSED and the record of the change are in V1380 (ST-CTR-004); the guards need the state of other modules and stay in the command, as the completion does. The aggregates of the settlement, the advance and the retention are read inside the transaction that has the contract locked.
- ST-CTR-012 (open): the story cites CXN-EXE-04, so it is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the money and authorization reviews were not run (no subagents). ST-CTR-004 and ST-CTR-011 fixtures that seeded COMPLETED contracts without the fields of the completion (`change-chain` API test) were corrected.

### ST-LND-002 (affected households, LND-03)
- ST-LND-002 (decision, what is stored in the clear): the columns `head_name` and `address_text` of V002 stay and hold the masked forms (initials; only the last part of the address), and `national_id_masked` and `phone_masked` are new; the sealed values are in `head_name_enc`, `address_enc`, `national_id_enc`, `phone_enc` (V1683 adds `head_name_enc`, `address_enc` and the two masked columns, with a check that a sealed value and its masked form come together). So no readable personal value is left in a column that is not sealed, and the list reads no key.
- ST-LND-002 (decision, the cipher): AES-256-GCM, a key per tenant and purpose derived (HKDF) from the master key of `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEYS`, the ciphertext bound to tenant, table, column and row, the id of the master key in the header (rotation: the highest id seals, any held id opens). The binding of the master keys to the KMS or OpenBao (R-LND-04) is not in the repository and is not part of the story; a process without keys fails closed (503 `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`) and still serves everything that holds no personal data.
- ST-LND-002 (decision, the reading): the design names `lnd.pii.read` and says every opening is audited, but has no operation for it; `revealHouseholdIdentity` (`GET /affected-households/{id}/identity?reason=`, additive to openapi.json) is a GET and not a command so that the personal data is not kept in the idempotency record of a command; it wants a reason (10 to 300 characters, the "reveal-with-reason" of TK-LND-002-4), writes the audit row (fields and reason, no values) in the same transaction as the reading, and counts `lnd_pii_decrypt_total`.
- ST-LND-002 (decision, the scope): a grant on a project or on a contract reaches no household (a household belongs to a unit); the setting `app.scope_units` is put in every transaction from the grants that carry the permission, and the identity number and the telephone are hidden whole per unit from a caller whose `lnd.pii.read` does not reach the unit of the household. The owner role of the tables is also under the policy (the tests put the setting first).
- ST-LND-002 (decision, the consent): `consent_record_id` is a logical reference to the ConsentRecord of IAM, which has no table that fits here yet; the identifier is checked for its shape and for being present when the basis is `CONSENT`, not against a table. The data requests of the data subject (`handleHouseholdDataRequest`, LND-13) and the erasure by anonymising are another story; the tables cannot be deleted from by the runtime role.
- ST-LND-002 (decision, the duplicate): the same identity number in a project is `HOUSEHOLD_DUPLICATE` through the HMAC of the number per tenant and project (the unique index of V002); the same pair of household and parcel is `LINK_DUPLICATE` before the shares are added up, so the answer does not depend on the room left on the parcel.
- ST-LND-002 (open): the story cites CXN-EXE-06 and CXN-13, so it is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the privacy, authorization, migration and tenant-isolation reviews were not run (no subagents), and the DPIA (R-LND-02) is for the data protection officer (`docs/lnd-households.md` has the table of where each piece of data lives). The inventory (LND-04) and the data requests (LND-13) are other stories.

### ST-LND-003 (data subject requests, LND-13)
- ST-LND-003 (decision, what a request does): the design lists the kinds and says only that a deletion is partly refused when the data is a payment voucher that must be kept. The effects are chosen so that every outcome is defensible to a data protection officer: ACCESS and RECTIFY are recorded as served; WITHDRAW_CONSENT erases the optional data (telephone, address) of a household registered on that consent and is refused for another basis; RESTRICT stops the sealed data being opened (`409 PROCESSING_RESTRICTED`, a new code in the registry); ERASE FULFILLED anonymises the household unless a payment was made to it (`409 RETENTION_LEGAL_HOLD`), ERASE PARTIAL erases the telephone and the address. The legal reading of "must be kept" (which records, for how long) is CXN-EXE-06 and waits for the owners; today any `PAID` or `DEPOSITED` payment holds the household.
- ST-LND-003 (decision, the table): the story lists no table, but the reason has to be kept ("ghi lý do"), so the requests are rows of a new append-only table `household_data_request` (V1684) under the same unit policy as the household, not text in the audit trail; the audit row carries no reason, because the reason is free text that may hold a name.
- ST-LND-003 (decision, unverified): `requester_verified: false` is refused for every kind and every outcome (UAT-AC381 step 4 is an access); a refusal of an unidentified person is therefore not recorded as a request. The UI also stops it before it is sent.
- ST-LND-003 (decision, `dsr_open`): a request is handled in one command, so the flag `dsr_open` of V002 is never set by it; it stays for a workflow with an open request (a later story).
- ST-LND-003 (open): lifting a restriction, the statutory response deadline (open legal question in the story), the export of the data to the person (portability) and the correction of a field are not in the story. The story cites CXN-EXE-06, so it is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the privacy and authorization reviews were not run (no subagents).

### ST-LND-004 (inventory, LND-04)
- ST-LND-004 (decision, the price decision): the table keeps `price_decision_ref` (text) and the design says the version of the decision is stored, but there is no catalogue of the decisions on land prices anywhere. They are master data of the tenant, a new type `LND_PRICE_DECISION` (key: the reference; `title`, `issuing_authority`; not rule data, because the decisions are issued by the province and not by the platform, `TENANT_ONLY`), read by the date of the survey through `MasterRules.read` like every dated rule; the record keeps the version id and the revision (two new columns). The unit price stays typed by the officer: the platform judges whether the decision was in force, not the price. The generic catalogue commands of MDM create a decision; no catalogue screen was designed for it.
- ST-LND-004 (decision, the answer): `confirmInventory` takes the outcome of the household (`CONFIRMED` or `DISPUTED`), the day it answered and a note that an objection needs. Both are judged once, by someone other than the person who counted (`SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`, also a CHECK), and a judged record is final; the replacement of a record by a newer count is a later command (`SUPERSEDED` is reachable in the database, from every state, and nothing leaves it).
- ST-LND-004 (decision, the states around): a confirmation moves the household from REGISTERED to INVENTORIED (and from DISPUTED back to INVENTORIED when no other record is objected to), an objection moves it to DISPUTED, and the first confirmed record moves the parcel from SURVEYED to INVENTORIED (the state machine of the module). The parcel goes to DISPUTED only by a complaint (ST-LND-008). Each of these moves is part of the command's transaction and is said in the audit row.
- ST-LND-004 (decision, the read): the design has no read for LND-04, so `listInventoryRecords` (`GET /land-inventory-records?household_id=`, `land.household.read`, additive to openapi.json) lists the records of one household; the description is read only inside the unit of the household.
- ST-LND-004 (open): AC-ST-LND-004-2 (a plan item on an unconfirmed record is refused) is enforced by the plan stories against the status `CONFIRMED`; there is no plan item yet to test it on. The story cites CXN-12, so it is DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the privacy, authorization and migration reviews were not run (no subagents).
- ST-LND-005 (decision, the totals): the totals of a plan are kept by the command that adds items, incrementally and under the lock of the plan, because a caller who sees only some units cannot sum all the items; a trigger lets them change only while the plan is a draft, and a CHECK ties them to the generated grand total. `revisePlan` therefore requires the caller to see every item (403 SCOPE_DENIED otherwise): a partial copy would be another plan.
- ST-LND-005 (decision, the items): a compensation item copies amount, quantity, unit price and price decision (with revision) from its CONFIRMED record; an expired decision is judged at the survey day (ST-LND-004), not at the day of the plan (UAT-AC382 step 3). A record is used once per plan (other plans may reuse it). A support or a resettlement item is a policy amount in whole dong with a legal basis. The screen adds one item per submit; the command takes up to 200.
- ST-LND-005 (decision, scope): the plan is scoped by project (`land.plan.write`), its items by the units of the households (`land.read`/`land.plan.write`); a grant on a project reaches no household. `listCompensationPlans` and `listCompensationPlanItems` are additive to openapi.json (the design has no read for LND-05).
- ST-LND-005 (decision, revising): allowed from POSTING, POSTING_CLOSED and APPROVED; the old revision becomes REVISED and keeps its items; the new one is a DRAFT with revision + 1, `supersedes_id` and `revision_reason`. Approval, posting and payment are ST-LND-006/007.
- ST-LND-005 (open): CXN-12, so DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the privacy, authorization, tenant-isolation and money reviews were not run (no subagents).
- ST-LND-006 (decision, the opinions): the design has no table for the opinions of the households and no command to handle one, yet requires that the approval waits for them: `compensation_plan_feedback` (V1687) keeps each opinion once, with its channel, the unit it came from (RLS `scope_units_guard`), and when and by whom it was handled; `handlePlanFeedback` and `listPlanFeedback` are additive to openapi.json (CR-LND-05). A handled opinion is final; opinions are recorded while POSTING and handled while POSTING or POSTING_CLOSED.
- ST-LND-006 (decision, days): the days of the posting and of the payment are the rules PLAN_POSTING_30 and PAYMENT_30 in force on the day the posting starts and on the approval day, with their version stored on the timer and on the plan; `posting_started_on` is not in the future and not before the plan was drafted (a posting dated back would shorten what the households were given); the end is the day the timer falls due and closing is allowed on that day. Open: whether a posting may start on a day earlier than the day of the command for a plan that was posted on paper first needs the owner of the rule (CXN-12).
- ST-LND-006 (decision, status code): UAT-AC377 step 5 expects 422 PLAN_STATE_INVALID for approving a plan that was never posted; the command table and the state machine of LND.md write 409 PLAN_STATE_INVALID, and the registry lists 409 with 422 as another status. 409 is used everywhere, as for revisePlan; the UAT text should be corrected by its owner.
- ST-LND-006 (decision, scope of the approval): the approver holds a grant on the project, which reaches no household, yet needs to know that every record is confirmed and every opinion handled and to move the parcels and households: three SECURITY DEFINER functions answer a word or a count (see docs/lnd-posting.md); each answers only for the tenant of the transaction and restores the scope of the caller. The drafter-is-not-approver rule is a CHECK on `approved_by` as well as `assertFourEyes`.
- ST-LND-006 (decision, subjects): parcels move INVENTORIED to PLAN_DRAFTED (items added), PLAN_POSTED (posting) and PLAN_APPROVED (approval), households INVENTORIED to PLAN_LISTED (posting); those objected to, paid or enforced stay where they are; a revision leaves them. Cancelling a plan before approval is not in this story.
- ST-LND-006 (open): CXN-12, so DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the legal-rule, authorization, privacy and tenant-isolation reviews were not run (no subagents). The alerts at T-7 and T of the timers are the alerting stories' work.
- ST-LND-007 (decision, late reason): UAT-AC378 needs the reason of a payment made after its day and `LndConfirmCompensationPayment` has no field for it: `late_reason` is added to the request (optional, additive) and to the table (V1688); `compensation_payment_status_chk` keeps that a paid payment has a reason exactly when it was paid after its day, and what each status holds.
- ST-LND-007 (decision, limits): `PAYMENT_EXCEEDS_PLAN` is judged per household against what the plan holds for it (the whole plan follows, and the database holds the plan total against any other writer through `plan_payment_totals`, which sees every unit); a payment is due after the approval and not after `payment_due_on`; a plan is completed when no payment waits and everything it holds was paid or deposited (a plan paid in part stays PAYING). Open: whether a household may be paid more than its items (an arrear) needs a rule of the owner of CXN-12.
- ST-LND-007 (decision, advance): the story speaks of a suggestion of recovery that PAY would make from the event, and UAT-AC383 and the error codes of the command (`ADVANCE_RECOVERY_EXCEEDED` on the confirmation, the payment left unpaid) need the answer in the command: a payment that names `advance_id` recovers it through `PayAdvanceStore.recordRecovery` in the same transaction, with PAY's own trail. The caller must hold `land.payment.write` over the contract; an advance of another project is refused.
- ST-LND-007 (decision, personal data): the account of a payment is accepted at scheduling, sealed (BR-LND-07) and shown masked; no command opens it (open: a reveal with `lnd.pii.read` and an audit row when the payment channel needs it). The trail and the events carry the payment, the plan, the advance, the amount and the dates, never a household or an account.
- ST-LND-007 (open): the daily job that sends the alerts at T-7 and T, breaches the timers the day after and raises the issue with a code and no name (UAT-AC378 steps 1 and 3) is not in a story of the plan; the data it needs is stored (`alert_level_sent`, `overdue_flagged_at`, `listUpcomingLandDeadlines`). A payment is not cancelled by a command. CXN-12 and CXN-02, so DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the money, authorization and privacy reviews were not run (no subagents).
- ST-LND-008 (decision, the words): the table of the complaints holds what the complainant said and the reply, both personal data in the unit of the household (policy `scope_units_guard`); `owner_unit_id` of the request must be the unit of the household (the field is in the contract and the household decides it); the trail and the events carry the number `LC-nnnnnn`, the subject and the dates; the event for the issue intake is `lnd.complaint.filed.v1` with the members of the common contract and the subject code; `linkComplaintIssue` is registered with the intake so that `issue_id` is written in the transaction that opens the issue (it widens the scope of that one statement, as the intake acts for the system). UAT-AC384 asks for the group "Giá bồi thường" in the issue: the intake reads no subject today, so the issue holds the kind of problem and the number only (open: map the subject to the issue category, ISS-11).
- ST-LND-008 (decision, the answer): `resolveLandComplaint` answers from RECEIVED, UNDER_REVIEW or ESCALATED; accepted and rejected need a reply document (CHECK `land_complaint_resolved_chk`), an escalation does not; the timer ends by the day of the answer (an escalation ends it); there is no command for UNDER_REVIEW and CLOSED, which the table and the guard allow.
- ST-LND-008 (decision, the parcels): the state machine of the module puts a parcel in DISPUTED by a complaint: filing holds the household's parcels that are not yet paid (`land_complaint_hold`, with the state each had), an accepted or rejected answer gives them back unless another complaint holds them, an escalation keeps them. Open: a parcel disputed while its payments complete is not moved to PAID until the hold is released and the payments are looked at again.
- ST-LND-008 (open): CXN-12, so DONE_UNCONFIRMED; the privacy review was not run (no subagents); the number of a complaint is `LC-` and six digits, not the `LC-A-xxx` of the UAT text.
- ST-DOC-002 (decision, the export): the ledger export is made in the browser from every page of `listDocumentRegisterEntries` (no new server operation): scope lines, one row per number, and a last line with the SHA-256 of every byte above it, so the file can be checked later; the unit appears by its id (the screen does not read the unit names). Open: a server-side signed export for ledgers too large for a browser, if the records office asks for one.
- ST-SAS-003 (decision, the status job): the stored status follows the clock one step at a time (a pass that finds a license behind by several steps tells each step, each with its own event and audit row, instead of one jump), the entitlement cache is told once per cause met in the pass, and the job leaves Suspended and Revoked licenses alone; `licenseStatusAt` stays the read-time truth, so a gap between two passes never grants rights. Open: no alert if the job has not run for more than five minutes beyond the generic JOB_FAILED alert; the notification for `sas.license.status_changed.v1` (tenant owner told on Grace/Expired) is for the notification story.
- ST-ADM-006 (decision, rank in the directory): the directory tells the reader per row whether the lifecycle commands are open (`can_manage`), by the same rule the commands judge (BR-IAM-05), so the screen never offers what the server would refuse with ROLE_ESCALATION_DENIED; the commands still decide, and the last-owner rule (LAST_TENANT_OWNER) is told by the command, not predicted. A server that says nothing leaves the buttons to the command.
- ST-ADM-006 (open): the seat line needs `subscription.read`, which a unit administrator does not hold by default, so only roles that read the subscription see it; if unit administrators should see seats, the permission catalogue needs a narrower read (sign-off of the role matrix).
- ST-ADM-007 (decision, closing a contract): a guest grant that covers several contracts is narrowed to the others instead of expiring (the guest keeps the contracts still in force); the guest is ended only when no grant remains. Both closed and terminated contracts take the access away at once (the design names contract.closed; a terminated contract is no longer in force either). Open: the contract module does not yet publish an event when a contract is suspended; a suspended contract leaves guest access as it is.
- ST-KERNEL-013 (decision): a vulnerability is named by the package that holds the advisory (not by the packages that depend on it), and an audit that only counts vulnerabilities without naming them fails with the counts and cannot be excepted; exceptions are capped at 90 days and carry an owner. The images of the SBOM come from the Dockerfiles (there are none yet) and the pub packages from the lockfiles.
- ST-KERNEL-013 (open): the daily workflow was not run here (it needs GitHub Actions, the repository variable SUPPLY_CHAIN_OWNER and the `supply-chain`/`security` labels, and pulls the OSV scanner image `ghcr.io/google/osv-scanner:v1.9.2`, whose tag was not checked offline); the image scan of built images (trivy) waits for the first Dockerfile.
- ST-KERNEL-030 (decision, Dart responses): the sample responses come from the contract (every member, the simplest value of its type), not from recordings of the API; the Dart test therefore proves the generated classes read and write back every shape of the contract and name a member that does not match, while a mismatch between the running server and the contract is the job of the TypeScript types the server is written against (`Schemas`) and of the breaking-change detector. Open: recorded responses of the real API (from the e2e stack) as a second source of fixtures; the typed Dart methods still return `Future<Object?>` and do not call the decoders yet.
- ST-KERNEL-030 (decision): an explicit null on an optional member reads as absent and is not written back (the two mean the same in this contract); `type: [string, null]` and `nullable: true` both read as nullable.
- ST-KERNEL-025 (open): `continueDrill` is the rule that the BI module must call for every level of a drill (ST-BI-*); the API of the drill does not exist yet, so the rule is proved in the kernel with the same ports as the scheduled export.
- ST-AUD-001 (decision): `coordinator_user_id` stores the membership id of the coordinator, as the other modules store the person who acts.
- ST-AUD-001 (decision): a target of the scope that is missing or belongs to another tenant is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, not the registry's `422 SCOPE_OUT_OF_TENANT`, so the answer does not tell the tenants apart (BR-IAM-01, the negative matrix); the registry entry stays for the stories that name it.
- ST-AUD-001 (decision): the hold of a mixed scope has kind RESOURCE; it is placed on start and released on cancel by the system (`WORKER_MEMBERSHIP_ID`) in the same transaction, which keeps the four-eyes check of `legal_hold`.
- ST-AUD-001 (open): V1320 extends `legal_hold_blocks` for dossiers, but DOC-013 must call `assertNotOnHold` before it disposes of a dossier. The list of recommendations that are overdue and the other AUD reads belong to later AUD stories and must reuse `engagementScope`.
- ST-AUD-001 (open): the PLANNED → conclusion and closure 409s are proved in the kernel state machine (`nextEngagementStatus`), as the operations belong to later AUD stories. The scope items of the OpenAPI stay open (a tightened schema was a breaking change for `contracts:compat`), so the client casts them in `apps/web/src/screens/aud/hooks.ts`. The system roles of SAS/IAM do not yet hold `aud.*`; the e2e stack grants them to its own roles.
- ST-AUD-002 (decision): a place named by a finding or a recommendation that is in the tenant but outside the scope of the engagement is `422 TARGET_OUT_OF_ENGAGEMENT` (a new registry code); one that is missing or of another tenant is `404`, the same answer, so the tenant boundary is not probed.
- ST-AUD-002 (decision): the financial total is read under `FOR UPDATE` of the conclusion row and the trigger `audit_recommendation_total_guard` holds the same rule at the table; a cancelled recommendation does not count, so ST-AUD-003 (cancel, reassign) and ST-AUD-007 (amended conclusions) may free or move money through the same guard.
- ST-AUD-002 (open): `supersedes_id` and a second conclusion of an engagement are `409 CONCLUSION_REVISION_INVALID` until ST-AUD-007, which owns the amended conclusions; `recordAuditConclusion` then has to move the recommendations of the old revision (SUPERSEDED) and relax `ENGAGEMENT_EDGES.recordConclusion`, which only leaves ONGOING and AWAITING_CONCLUSION.
- ST-AUD-002 (open): the due day of a recommendation (BR-AUD-06, no later than the implementation deadline of the conclusion) and its owner belong to ST-AUD-003 (`assignAuditRecommendation`); `listAuditRecommendations` already carries `owner_unit_id`, `overdue` and `status` filters for AUD-06 and AUD-07, and the overdue flag is written by the job of that story.
- ST-AUD-002 (open): the conclusion document is not tied to one engagement (nothing refuses the same document recorded for two engagements); the registry has no code for it, so it is left to the review of the legal rules rather than invented here.
- ST-AUD-002 (decision): a finding of an amount (`amount_vnd`) is not added up against the total of the conclusion: only the financial recommendations are (BR-AUD-05); the findings state what was found.
- aud screens (fix): `isDay` of the engagement screens threw a RangeError for a month 13 or a day 0 instead of refusing the day; it now answers false, with a test.
- ST-AUD-002 (open): DONE_UNCONFIRMED because it rests on CXN-AUD-01 (the deadlines are read from the conclusion, never hard-coded); the legal owners confirm the item and the status moves to DONE. The code needs no change when they do.
- ST-AUD-003 (decision): the owner of a recommendation is the person it was given to, or when none was named anyone whose grant of `aud.recommendation.execute` reaches the owner unit; an owner sees what was given to them without a grant over the engagement (`recommendationScope`). `NOT_OWNER` is 422, as the registry has it (the answer to a stranger is still 404 when they do not see it at all).
- ST-AUD-003 (decision): the owner of an object of evidence that is not in the scope of the caller is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, not the registry's `422 OWNER_NOT_FOUND`, so that the answer does not tell tenants apart (BR-IAM-01, the negative matrix); evidence is any document that DOC holds EFFECTIVE (the "clean attachment" of BR-AUD-09 is an EFFECTIVE ATTACHMENT document, whose scan is DOC's).
- ST-AUD-003 (decision): a second action may be planned while IN_PROGRESS (the edge `plan` leaves IN_PROGRESS where it is); the report needs at least one DONE action and none PLANNED; a return clears the one who reported the work, so the next report names its own reporter.
- ST-AUD-003 (decision): a deadline extension with a decision document (BR-AUD-06) is not in this story: `AudAssign` has no field for one, so the day may not pass the deadline of the conclusion. ST-AUD-007 adds it with the amended conclusions. `create_issue` (the linked ISS issue) is `422` until R2.
- ST-AUD-003 (open): `is_overdue` is not written by anything yet (the job `aud.recommendation-overdue` and the escalations belong to ST-AUD-006); the screens compute the days late from `due_on` and the status, and the filter `overdue` reads the column the job will write. The reopening of a closed recommendation when a recovery is reversed (`reopen`) belongs to ST-AUD-004; the table already allows the edge.
- ST-AUD-003 (open): DONE_UNCONFIRMED because it rests on CXN-AUD-01 (the days are read from the conclusion); the review of authorization (2-3 hours) is not run.
- ST-AUD-004 (decision): `recovered_vnd` is kept by the trigger `audit_recovery_link_apply` under the lock of the recommendation; no code path writes it, and the reconcile job proves the sum of the lines equals it.
- ST-AUD-004 (decision): by hand only TREASURY_REMITTANCE and MANUAL_EVIDENCE are accepted and each needs an EFFECTIVE document; PAY_RECOVERY and PAY_DEDUCTION come from PAY only. A reversal on a CLOSED engagement writes the line but does not reopen the recommendation (`reopened_at` marks the ones that were).
- ST-AUD-004 (decision): what the ledger cannot take from PAY (over-recovery, a reversal with nothing to reverse, a recommendation that is not financial) is kept once for each event and kind in `audit_recovery_exception` and shown on AUD-08; the consumer ignores a recommendation that is not in the tenant.
- ST-AUD-004 (decision): `GET /audit-overview` and `GET /audit-engagements/{id}/recovery-links` are additive operations the story needs for AUD-01 and AUD-08; the watch list of the overview is limited to 50 engagements, the most overdue first.
- ST-AUD-004 (open): PAY must publish `pay.recovery.recorded.v1` and `pay.recovery.reversed.v1` carrying the recommendation id and the amount; the consumer is ready and tested against that shape, but the publishing side belongs to the PAY group. Until then only manual lines reach the ledger.
- ST-AUD-004 (open): the exceptions stay OPEN; a command for the accountant to resolve them is not in the story (status RESOLVED exists in the table). The reviews of money and authorization (2.5-4 hours) are not run.
- ST-AUD-005 (decision): `AudFollowUpReport` gets two optional members, `place` and `drafting_unit_id`: DOC needs the agency and the place of issue in the head of every document and no record of the tenant holds the locality. The unit defaults to the only unit the engagement is about; the place is a dash when nobody says it (the DOC drafter completes it before the report goes out). A tenant profile with its locality would remove the member.
- ST-AUD-005 (decision): the closure asks for an ISSUED report of every conclusion that stands (not SUPERSEDED), as the plan says, and not for one issued after the last recommendation was closed; a stricter rule (a report as of the day of the last closure) is a question for the inspection coordinators.
- ST-AUD-005 (decision): the figures are computed from the state of the day: closed ones by the Vietnam day of `verified_at`, recoveries by `recorded_on` of the ledger; the history of the other statuses is not kept, so a recommendation that is not CLOSED now counts as it is now for any day, and a report for a day long past is only as exact as that.
- ST-AUD-005 (decision): an issue approved by the person who asked for the report is ISSUED with no approver (the table keeps the two apart) and raises AUD_REPORT_EXCEPTION; DOC itself may allow it.
- ST-AUD-005 (open): DONE_UNCONFIRMED because it rests on CXN-AUD-02 (the form and the deadline of the report); the template is watermarked until a lawyer confirms it. The reviews of the legal template and of money (1-2.5 hours) are not run. The file store of DOC is the in-memory adapter until KERNEL-024; the payload is lost with it on restart (the report keeps the hash and the figures).
- ST-AUD-005 (open): AUD-10 shows `report_due_on` of the conclusion but no alert fires when it passes; the deadlines and the escalations belong to ST-AUD-006. The e2e stack has no DOC drafter, so the browser flows write DOC's answers to the outbox as DOC writes them; the request event is proved against the real DOC consumer in `apps/api/test/aud/report.test.ts`.
- ST-DIS-003 (decision): `plan_version_id` of `ReportDraft` is optional: the server finds the plan version in force at the end of the period (`DisReportStore.effectivePlan`) and a named one that differs is 422 `PLAN_VERSION_NOT_EFFECTIVE` with `effective_plan_version_id`. The contract no longer requires it.
- ST-DIS-003 (decision): the members of the later stories (`construction_vnd`, `acceptance_vnd`, `treasury_submitted_vnd`, `preparing_dossier_vnd`, `physical_progress_pct`, `narrative`) are refused 422 until ST-DIS-004, which should copy `report_revision.measurement_basis` (kept by V1042 with the default YTD) to the progress snapshot.
- ST-DIS-003 (decision): the list answers `summary` (`filtered`, `scope`, `is_filtered`, `filters`) so DIS-01 can say what a figure is the total of; `plan_id` filters by the capital plan of the plan version and `plan_version_id` by the version. A report type must match the type of its period (WEEKLY with a WEEK).
- ST-DIS-003 (open): `get_resources_funding_snapshot_line` and `get_resources_funding_snapshot_line_id` are in the contract with the scaffold answer of a period snapshot (`snapshot_id`, `as_of`, `items`) and are not exposed: the cells of a draft are read with the report (`funding_lines`) under `report_revision.read`, and the line reads belong to the period snapshot of ST-DIS-009. The orchestrator should either drop them from the story or define their answer.
- ST-DIS-003 (open): a draft after the soft delete of an earlier report, and the submit and accept steps, are ST-DIS-007 and later; the status labels of those states are already on DIS-01.
- ST-DIS-003 (open): DONE_UNCONFIRMED because the date that counts as disbursed (CXN-03, the booking date of the Treasury) is not signed by the business owner; the cumulative is entered by the investor and reconciled with PAY by later stories.
- ST-DIS-004 (decision): a save of a draft names what it changes: the volumes, `narrative` and `attachments` are replaced when named and kept when left out, so each step of the draft (DIS-03, DIS-04, DIS-05, DIS-06) saves what it shows with the same command. Naming any volume asks for the four amounts together. `ReportDraft` now types `narrative` (`ReportNarrative`) and `attachments` (`ReportAttachmentRef`).
- ST-DIS-004 (decision): the contract had no way to put a file on a report, and the story names a kernel upload with a scan as a cross-group request. DIS got its own upload door `uploadReportFile` (`POST /report-files`, `report.edit`) on the shared pipeline (purpose `document`, 25 MB), like the other modules; a save links the file by id and hash after checking the store. The multipart parser of the upload guards now reads the headers as UTF-8 so a Vietnamese file name comes out as it was typed (it was read as Latin-1).
- ST-DIS-004 (decision): the review is part of the read of a live draft (`review` on `getReportRevision`), not a new operation; the errors carry the problem code the server answers and the warnings their own code. Warnings that "may require a reason by policy" have no policy store yet: none requires a reason; it is a question for ADM when a policy for the report exists.
- ST-DIS-004 (decision): the Treasury gap warning compares the cumulative reported with the Treasury payments booked in the window of the basis (BR-DIS-12): PERIOD the period, YTD from 1 January of the fiscal year, PROJECT_TO_DATE all time, to the last day of the period, on the Vietnam day, refunds (`reversal_of_id`) taken off. The threshold of 5% and the windows are assumptions until CXN-03 is signed.
- ST-DIS-004 (open): the REGISTERED schedule row of next month on acceptance (AC-ST-DIS-004-3 "when the draft is later submitted and accepted") belongs to the decision story (DIS-006): here the registration is kept with its CTMTQG subset on the cells.
- ST-DIS-004 (open): the files of a report cannot be downloaded or previewed yet; the reviewer of DIS-07 needs a presigned download of a file of a submitted report.
- ST-DIS-004 (open): TK-DIS-004-4 (the contract and the sync scope for MOB-14, MOB-18, MOB-19 and MOB-20): the commands and the DTO are the same as the web's and carry `client_operation_id`; the offline queue and the sync buckets belong to the mobile group.
- ST-DIS-004 (open): money review (`review.required: money`) is not run: the lines of BR-DIS-01, BR-DIS-06 and the 203.8% ratio are for the finance reviewer; DONE_UNCONFIRMED because the basis and the 5% gap rest on CXN-03.

- ST-DIS-005 (decision, the hash of a draft): `source_year` of a cell is not part of `draftHash`: it is read from the allocation when a save does not name it, so the hash of what was sent and the hash of what is stored (the one a send seals) must not differ by it. Cost: a replay of a `client_operation_id` with only another `source_year` is answered as the same report. Open: hash the cells after the allocation defaults are resolved, in `createDraft`, `saveDraft` and `ReportController.create`, and put `source_year` back (Codex review, P2).
- ST-DIS-006 (assumption, UNDER_REVIEW): the table lets SUBMITTED move to UNDER_REVIEW and a reviewer decides from it, but no command sets it: the design says "when a reviewer opens the report" and a read must not change a report. ST-DIS-007 (withdrawal: "not yet UNDER_REVIEW") needs to say whether the opening is a command of its own.
- ST-DIS-006 (assumption, maker-checker): always on; there is no tenant setting that turns it off. The denied attempt is the audit row `command.denied:post_reports_id_decision` the command pipeline writes for every 403.
- ST-DIS-006 (assumption, evidence): `evidence_ids` of a decision are kept as given (at most 20, each once); R1 has no store to check them against.
- ST-DIS-006 (open, flaky, not mine): `packages/db/test/work-calendar-store.test.ts` "serialises two publications at the same time" failed once with STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED in a full run under load and passed 3 of 3 alone; the race of two publications of the same year decides who is newer.
- ST-DIS-006 (open, web): the unit suites of the web app at 5 s per test time out under the load of a full parallel run (ten files in one run, none in the next); they pass alone. A `testTimeout` for the suite, or fewer workers in the gate, is for whoever owns the web gate.
- ST-DIS-007 (assumption, a withdrawn report): WITHDRAWN has no way out in the state machine of the design, and a project would never report that period again (a revision exists, so a new draft is refused). A correction starts from a WITHDRAWN revision as from a returned one (`post_reports_id_corrections`, the reason is required): the table does not change, only the source of the correction (kernel and web). To be confirmed with the capital owner.
- ST-DIS-007 (assumption, a deleted draft): a draft that is deleted keeps the place of its revision, so a new draft of the project and the period is not made until it is restored; a deletion that is never restored stays in the table after the 30 days. What purges it is for the retention story.
- ST-DIS-007 (assumption): every maker holding `report.withdraw` on the project may withdraw, not only the person who handed the report in (DIS.md says "maker (chính sách)").
- ST-DIS-007 (open, read model): the list of the drafts that were deleted (`deleted=true`) runs on the clock of the database (`now() - 30 days`) while the restore is judged on the clock of the module; they agree in production and the tests date their deletions by the database.
- ST-DIS-007 (open, flaky, not mine): `apps/api/test/pay/cumulative-ledger.test.ts` "reads a ledger of thousands of rows within the budget" compares the median of the warm reads with the median of the cold ones (15.52 ms against 15.52 ms) and fails one run in three on this machine; the bound is a timing assertion, not a behaviour. It passes or fails the same with and without the DIS work.
- ST-DIS-008 (assumption, layout): only the synthetic template DIS-WEEKLY-1 is read until the real workbook is sampled (D04); the registration for next month is not in it and stays as DIS-05 left it.
- ST-DIS-008 (assumption, ownership): a batch of the bulk import is read and applied by the person who made it (404 for anyone else), because its faults name projects of their scope; a second person who has to take the batch over makes their own dry run of the same file.
- ST-DIS-008 (open, framework): the batch table of the design is the record of this import; the file import framework of the kernel (`docs/file-import.md`, claims of business keys for ever) is not used, because a weekly report is claimed by project and period. If the capital owner wants one quarantine and one reconciliation list for every file the system takes, the DIS import would move onto it.
- ST-DIS-009 (assumption, formulas): the split of the plan by origin (`LOCAL_ADDED`, `CENTRAL_SUPPORT`, `REVENUE_INCREASE` are local, the rest the Prime Minister's), the 5% threshold of the Treasury coverage, the Treasury counted from 1 January of the fiscal year up to the cutoff by the Vietnam day, and the Treasury figure carried once on the year plan of the central budget are proposals until D03 is signed (CXN-03); every snapshot says `PROPOSED_PENDING_SIGNOFF` until the five glossary terms of the domain DISBURSEMENT are PUBLISHED.
- ST-DIS-009 (assumption, who builds and freezes): every snapshot holds the whole tenant, so building needs `snapshot.build` and freezing `snapshot.freeze` over the whole tenant (403 `SCOPE_DENIED` otherwise); the official snapshot (`PERIOD_CLOSE`) is built once the period is no longer open. A net of the Treasury below zero for the year gives no rate (`NO_DATA`) and `PARTIAL` coverage. A build that was never frozen is replaced by the next one of the same period and kind and becomes FAILED (the status list of V002 is kept).
- ST-DIS-009 (open, PAY): the Treasury source is `treasury_transaction.confirmed_at` read as the Vietnam day; PAY is to give `treasury_paid_on` (cross-group request of the story). The weekly job `dis.snapshot-weekly` (build and freeze after the lock, Temporal) and the 1,000-project performance test (AC-ST-DIS-009-5, `UAT-PLAN-11`) wait for the platform slice and the staging dataset; the build already reads a kind of data at a time, not a project at a time.
- ST-DIS-009 (open, read model): `listPeriodSnapshots` is an additive read (the TODO of ST-DGV-002 asked for one): the header of a snapshot (row count included) is readable by anyone who holds `disbursement.read`, only the lines are scoped. `forecast_eac_vnd` is null until ST-DIS-018.
- ST-DIS-010 (assumption, unit of a group): DIS-12 groups by the unit captured in each line of the snapshot (the owner unit of the project when it was built), not by the investor unit of the register of today; if the capital owner wants "chủ đầu tư" as the investor, the line of the snapshot has to capture `investor_unit_id`.
- ST-DIS-010 (assumption, carried over): the rate of the capital carried over divides what was reported by the plan of that capital (`bucket=CARRYOVER`), because the Treasury does not split a payment by source; BR-DIS-05 names `D_carryover` and waits for D03.
- ST-DIS-010 (assumption, order of the reasons): BR-DIS-08 names the denominator first, so a project with no plan is `ZERO_DENOMINATOR` even when nothing was paid; the snapshot lines built from now on follow the same order (the kernel `ratesOf` changed with this story).
- ST-DIS-010 (open, cache): the cache key `t:{tenant}:dis:rates:{snapshot}:{scope_hash}` of the task is not used; the read is a few sums over the lines of one frozen snapshot, indexed by snapshot. A cache belongs with the performance test of ST-DIS-009 (AC-5) once there is a staging dataset.
- ST-DIS-010 (open, read model): `getDisbursementRates` answers an aggregate, not a page of rows, so the isolation reachability test lists it with the reads that have no `items` (`LISTS_WITHOUT_ROWS`); the isolation fixture snapshot is now FROZEN so that the read has something to open on.
- ST-DIS-011 (assumption, the definitions): the five terms of ST-MDM-008 (`GIAI_NGAN_PERIOD`, `GIAI_NGAN_YTD`, `GIAI_NGAN_PTD`, `MAU_SO_KH_TTG_GIAO`, `MAU_SO_KH_TONG`) are the definitions the plan names `DISB_YTD`, `PERIOD_DELTA`, `PTD` and the two denominators; the snapshot reads them through `GlossaryStore.definitionStatus` (the list of its own that ST-DIS-009 had was removed). The treasury cutoff is part of every term, not a term of its own.
- ST-DIS-011 (open, export): AC-ST-DIS-011-1 asks that the PDF export of a snapshot states the pending sign-off in its footer; the export is ST-DIS-016, which has to print the `definition_status` the snapshot carries (and DIS-25 shows the label already).
- ST-DIS-011 (open, CI): the golden suite is labelled "[CHỜ KÝ D03]" in its name and fails the build on a regression of the proposed numbers; making the pipeline treat it as non-gating for the sign-off is a setting of the CI owner (there is no gate on it today).
- ST-DIS-011 (open, fixtures): the fixtures are data of `@machvon/contracts` (read by the suite, the screen and the sign-off pack `docs/dis-d03-signoff-pack.md`, which a test keeps in step); the QA owner still has to add FX-DIS-001..007 to `test_fixtures.json` (cross-group request of the story).
- ST-DIS-011 (note): the ranking of FX-DIS-007 is `rankUnits` of the kernel (BR-DIS-10, minimum plan 1 billion as an assumption); ST-DIS-013 builds the read and the screens over it.
- ST-DIS-011 (assumption, who reads): DIS-25 reads the definitions from the business glossary, so it needs `glossary.read` (a person with `disbursement.read` only is refused by the glossary and sees the refusal state); which roles of the leadership and the capital owner hold `glossary.read` is a matter of the role pack of IAM.
- ST-DIS-012 (assumption, thresholds and curve): EW-01 (10 points, critical at 20), EW-02 (3 days to explain), EW-05, EW-07 are assumptions awaiting the finance office, seeded as drafts for every tenant; the expected curve is a straight line over the days of the year and the rate EW-01 reads is the rate on the whole year plan; a project with no Treasury figure is silent for EW-01.
- ST-DIS-012 (assumption, rules of a unit or a tenant): R1 evaluates rules of the scope PROJECT only; `UNIT` and `TENANT` are refused with 422 on `scope_kind` until a release evaluates them.
- ST-DIS-012 (open, deadlines): EW-02 is evaluated when a close snapshot is frozen with the day of the evaluation as today; the deadline that passes with no snapshot after it is not noticed until one is frozen. The daily check belongs with the job platform (Temporal) and the weekly snapshot job of ST-DIS-009.
- ST-DIS-012 (open, PRJ): `warning.opened.v1` carries `auto_explanation` and the days to explain; the PRJ consumer that makes the explanation request and sets `explanation_request_id` / EXPLANATION_REQUESTED is not part of this story (the table has the state and its guard).
- ST-DIS-012 (open, who handles): `acknowledgeEarlyWarning` / `dismissEarlyWarning` are scoped by the project of the warning (`warning.handle` over the unit); a warning with no project (a later rule of a unit) would need its own scope rule. HOME-03 reads the warnings through `getHomeWarnings`, which belongs to HOME.
- ST-DIS-012 (note, gauge): the gauge `dis_warning_open_gauge{severity}` is set by the job after an evaluation and by the commands after a handling; no metrics sink is wired into the worker process yet (a no-op sink is used), as for the other DIS counters.
- ST-DIS-013 (assumption, comparison group): the comparison group is the type of the unit (`unit_type`) as it is today in the register, not as it was when the snapshot was built; a unit that changes type between two snapshots moves group. The first release ranks every group that has units (the task said one group); the minimum plan (1 billion dong) is a constant of the kernel, not tenant data yet.
- ST-DIS-013 (open, HOME): the read of the ranking (`getDisbursementRankings`, `GET /home/rankings`) and the screens are the HOME stories; `DisRankingStore.list` is the read they will use. Nothing here is exposed over HTTP.
- ST-DIS-013 (open, flaky, not mine): `packages/db/test/template-database.test.ts` "stops the shared server and removes its directory" took 321 s and failed in two full runs of the gate (it passes alone in 25 s); it stops and removes the embedded PostgreSQL directory, which Windows holds while other processes of the run still read it.
- ST-DIS-014 (assumption, actual of a week): the actual of a week is the difference of two cumulative Treasury figures of frozen close snapshots, so it is only as exact as the cutoff of each snapshot (the Treasury may book late into an earlier week; the correction shows in the week of the next freeze, not in the week it belongs to). The first frozen week of the year is counted from the first day of the year and the screen marks it.
- ST-DIS-014 (open, EW-09): the warning for two negative weeks in a row is the warning deepening story; this read gives it the differences it needs (`difference_vnd`).
- ST-DIS-014 (open, no filter by project on the screen): the API takes `project_id` and `unit_id`; DIS-24 filters by plan year only until a project picker exists on the DIS screens.
- ST-DIS-015 (assumption, overdue): overdue is a project with nothing handed in after the deadline; a report that came in late is submitted and counted as late, not as overdue (AC-ST-DIS-015-1 read as 7 submitted including 1 late, 3 overdue). Returned and withdrawn reports leave the project as a draft. See docs/dis-submission-board.md.
- ST-DIS-015 (open, scale): the board is tallied in memory from the projects in scope; move the counts to SQL if a tenant has tens of thousands of reporting projects.
- ST-DIS-015 (open, EW-02): the early-warning rule for a missing report after the deadline reads the same classification; wiring EW-02 to `submissionStateOf` is left to the warning deepening story.
- ST-DIS-015 (note, DIS-13): the monthly narrative starts the draft and edits the four sections; the volumes and the send remain the steps of the report (DIS-03/04/06). The monthly draft is created on the basis PERIOD.
- ST-DIS-015 (assumption, monthly deadline): a monthly period is a calendar month due on the 5th of the next month at the calendar deadline time; the rule is the constant MONTHLY_DEADLINE_DAY, not tenant data (the calendar definition has the weekly rule only). Make it an attribute of the calendar version when D03 fixes the real deadline. Found by the gpt-6-astra review: without it no monthly period could be generated.
- ST-DIS-016 (assumption, link life): the signed link lives 5 minutes (kernel MAX_PRESIGN_TTL_SECONDS), not the 15 minutes of the design; each download request issues a fresh one and judges the scope again. Raise the kernel cap or serve the file through the authenticated endpoint if 5 minutes proves short.
- ST-DIS-016 (open, FILES gateway): the public route GET /report-exports/{id}/file serves the bytes inside the API process because the FILES module has no storage gateway yet (MOCK adapters); move it to the gateway when S3 and ClamAV are bound.
- ST-DIS-016 (open, share and official layout): a file is only its maker's; the official TT 37/2025 layout through DOC and the sharing of a file are the R2 export story.
- ST-DIS-016 (note, record in the audit trail): the export has no table (tables: [] in the plan); its record is the audit row dis.report_exported, read back at download. If audit rows are ever purged by retention, an export older than the retention can no longer be downloaded (404) and is made again.
- ST-DIS-017 (open, mobile group): the Flutter offline queue, the conflict screen, MOB-13..21 and the tenant-switch guard on the device (AC-ST-DIS-017-2/3/5) are the mobile group's; this story fixes the server side and the contract pack (docs/dis-mobile-contract.md). See docs/dis-mobile-sync.md.
- ST-DIS-017 (open, PowerSync spike ST-KERNEL-002): the sync rules are PROPOSED; the syntax and the end-to-end proof of AC-ST-DIS-017-4 on a real service wait for the spike, and the component stays PLANNED (no restore evidence).
- ST-DIS-017 (open, MOB/ADM): nothing writes sync_membership_scope yet; the projection of the grants into it (and its end on revocation) is the MOB story with ADM events. The tests insert the rows.
- ST-DIS-017 (assumption, registration at acceptance): the REGISTERED rows of next month are made when the report is accepted (job dis.registration), not when it is submitted; a closed fiscal year or a project that no longer reports skips the registration and the audit row says why.
- ST-DIS-018 (assumption, D03): the 80% threshold of the reallocation candidate, the order forecast then registration then missing, and the Treasury figure of the snapshot as the base of the estimate are proposals awaiting D03 (CXN-03); the response and the screen say so.
- ST-DIS-018 (open, R2): the historical expected curve for a month with neither forecast nor registration needs a year of history; until R2 such a month is missing, the estimate is a lower bound and no reallocation is called for it (the linear default of the design is not applied).
- ST-DIS-018 (open, CAP-19): the candidates for reallocation are flagged on DIS-23 only; the proposal of a reallocation from them is the CAP story (CAP-19), which reads the same estimate.
- ST-DIS-018 (note, curve months): the months before the cutoff are read from the transactions live, the month of the cutoff from the frozen snapshot; a late booking dated before the cutoff moves the earlier months, not the figure that was frozen.
- ST-PRJ-006 (assumption, CXN-33): the due date of an explanation that a warning asks for by itself is three working days (MDM-18); it stays an assumption until CXN-33 is confirmed. Without a published work calendar the count is by weekdays and the audit row says WEEKDAYS_ASSUMED.
- ST-PRJ-006 (open, Kernel/Platform): the plan names a Temporal timer for each request ("overdue marking uses a Temporal timer per request"); the first slice has no Temporal, so the overdue mark is the periodic job prj-explanation-overdue (every 15 minutes). The behaviour is the same within the quarter of an hour; replace the job by the timer when Temporal is provisioned.
- ST-PRJ-006 (open, NOT): the in-app reminder before the due date is the NOT group's (cross-group request of the story); no reminder is sent from here.
- ST-PRJ-006 (note): an OPEN warning is acknowledged by the person who asks for its explanation (the database moves a warning OPEN -> ACKNOWLEDGED -> EXPLANATION_REQUESTED only); the acknowledgement note says so.
- ST-PRJ-007 (open, IAM): the plan asks for an IAM scope resolver that honours effective-dated unit changes; here the project changes unit on its day and the scope version of the members who gain or lose sight of it is raised, so the next decision is made on the new tree. A future-dated resolver is the IAM group's request.
- ST-PRJ-007 (note, schedule): the job prj-transfer-apply runs every 15 minutes and applies what is due by the Vietnam date, instead of one pass at 00:05; it is idempotent. The pages of the project bank cached by the API process (60 seconds) are not cleared by the job.
- ST-PRJ-007 (note, status): a returned request is stored as CANCELLED (the data model has no status for it); the reason says what to put right and the requester asks again.
- ST-PRJ-007 (contract): two reads were added to openapi.json for PRJ-20, getProjectTransferImpact and listProjectTransfers; they are listed in the module document when it is next revised.
- ST-PRJ-007 (open, DIS/PRJ scope): after a transfer, who may read the reports of the project before the effective day is judged by the units the project has now, not by the unit it had on the day of each report; the snapshots (including a rebuild of an earlier period) are attributed by the unit of the last day of the period. Giving each report its unit of the day in the scope predicates is a change across the report reads (a period-aware scope) and is left to the DIS group; until then the old unit loses the weekly reports of the project from the day of the transfer.
- ST-CAP-010 (open, ADM): AC-ST-CAP-010-2 (the proposal follows the approval workflow version bound at its start, with two approval levels) needs the workflow engine and DoA of ST-ADM-012; until then the proposal has one decision by a person who is neither its author nor its submitter, and the domain guard is the only judge.
- ST-CAP-010 (assumption, CXN-33 and CXN-13): the thresholds that suggest donors and recipients (an estimate under 80% of the plan, 15 points behind the curve) are assumptions; the planner chooses the lines, and the automatic suggestion is deepening.
- ST-CAP-010 (note, status): WITHDRAWN is in the data model and the guard but the contract has no command to withdraw a proposal.
- ST-CAP-010 (note, plan revision): the revision the application makes carries the author of the proposal as its maker, the submitter as its submitter and the approver as its approver (the audit rows of the plan name the applier), so that the four-eyes of the plan hold with the people of the proposal.
- ST-MNE-005 (decision, no new operation or table): the weekly quick report is a monitoring report on the form `CV3665-WEEKLY`; this story gives the form its cells in `form-pack.json` (the two rates and the two sums from the snapshot, the slow projects, the proposals), adds the DOC template `MNE-CV3665-WEEKLY` to the platform pack (so the form and the rule `WEEKLY_QUICK` are no longer skipped) and builds the screen MNE-07. The rates come from the same `rateOfSums` as DIS-19.
- ST-MNE-005 (decision, new field source): `SLOW_PROJECTS {limit}` (1 to 20, default 5) is the only source that is a text: the projects of the unit with an open early warning of EW-01 (OPEN, ACKNOWLEDGED, EXPLANATION_REQUESTED), the furthest behind first (`gap_points`), with the leader in charge now (as DIS-24 reads it). A figure with it or a text with another source is refused when the form is read.
- ST-MNE-005 (assumption, CXN-11): the cells of the weekly form are a working assumption until CV 3665/BTC-PTHT is confirmed; the weekly disbursement (difference of two closings, `weeklyActual` of DIS-24) is not a cell yet, the form holds the cumulative figures of the snapshot. The label and the watermark say CXN-11; the story is DONE_UNCONFIRMED.
- ST-MNE-005 (note): the list of the slow projects is the state of the warnings when the snapshot is attached; a later evaluation changes DIS, not the report that was handed in (the payload is frozen at the hand-in). A report whose snapshot was attached before a warning opened has to attach the snapshot again to see it.
- ST-HOME-001 (decision, no table): HOME owns none; `getCommandCenter` reads the frozen snapshot (the latest frozen PERIOD_CLOSE, or the one named) through `DisSnapshotStore.rateSums` and `rateOfSums`, the code of DIS-19, so the two screens cannot disagree; the counts (open warnings, overdue explanations, pending reallocations) are `HomeStore` reads of the tables of DIS, PRJ and CAP in the scope of the reader (project scope for projects, unit scope for units).
- ST-HOME-001 (decision, context token): each tile carries a token `{tenantId, snapshotId, scopeHash, metricCode, unitId, exp}` signed with HMAC-SHA256 over a purpose prefix (`home-context:`), TTL 30 minutes. The key is `ORCHESTRATION_OPTIONS.contextKey` (the one of background work, hence the prefix) or a key of the process when none is configured (tokens then die with the process, which the screen answers by asking again). `verifyHomeContext` is for ST-HOME-002.
- ST-HOME-001 (open, ST-HOME-002): the drill destination `/home/home-10?token=` has no screen yet; the links of the tiles point to it.
- ST-HOME-001 (open, HOME-01 filters): the fund and fiscal-year filters wait for a read model that carries them; `getCommandCenter` has `snapshot_id`, `unit_id` and `denominator_kind` only, so HOME-01 offers the unit (when the list of units can be read) and shows the year and the day of the snapshot. `denominator_kind` is accepted and echoed; both rates are always given.
- ST-HOME-001 (not built): the cache `t:{tenant}:home:cc:{snapshot}:{scope_hash}` of the task list (a decision for the load test D09), the push event `snapshot.frozen.v1` and the quality badge from DGV (ST-HOME-007: until then `quality_badge` is NOT_ASSESSED and the new-snapshot banner is fed by a poll once a minute that never changes the figures on its own), and `home_load_ms`.
- ST-HOME-001 (assumption, CXN-03 and CXN-33): the formulas of the tiles are proposals under CXN-03 until the business owner signs them (D03); the staleness limit of 8 days is the story's own.
- ST-HOME-002 (decision, token): the token of ST-HOME-001 now names the member (an opaque id) and how far down the drill has gone (`projectId`, `lineId`); a token of another member or another tenant is 400 INVALID_REQUEST, one that is old is 409 SNAPSHOT_EXPIRED with the snapshot, the metric, the unit and the project in the problem body (the signature is good, so they are the server's own). The scope hash is the one of the reader now; a token made under another scope is read over the present scope and the answer says `scope_changed`.
- ST-HOME-002 (decision, authorization): each hop judges the unit (unit scope) and the project (project scope) again and the cell by the lines of the project in the snapshot; what is refused is 403 SCOPE_DENIED that names nothing and is recorded as `home.drill_denied` (DENIED, resource the snapshot, the metric and the level in the diff), with `AuditWriter.recordDenied` in its own transaction.
- ST-HOME-002 (decision, children): the unit children are the units that own lines (flat, under the unit), not the organisation tree; the cells are the lines of the year plan (the carried-over cells are not of the plan the rates divide by). The reconciliation is checked at the server over every child, as big integers, whatever page is shown; a ratio says RATIO.
- ST-HOME-002 (open, DOC and PAY): the hop from a Treasury transaction to the document (DOC `document_version`) and the sensitive-read audit of it wait for a DOC read endpoint (cross-group request); the Treasury transactions are read from the table of PAY (`treasury_transaction`) up to the end of the cutoff day (Vietnam time).
- ST-HOME-002 (open, BI): a token below the unit is not renewed by itself (the screen goes on from the unit and says so): renewing a deep token needs an endpoint that issues one for a project, a decision for the BI library that shares this token (cross-group request).
- ST-HOME-002 (not built): the metric `home_drill_denied_total`, and the drill of the ranking and warning figures (they have no tile in the center yet).
- ST-HOME-003 (decision, no table): `getHomeWarnings` reads the warnings of DIS (`DisWarningStore.list`, now with `statuses`, `snapshotId` and `id` filters) in the project scope of `home.read`, and adds the leader in charge now, the rule and its threshold, the explanation and, for the profile, the evidence (the cells of the project in the snapshot the warning was opened on) and the history (`warningHistory` of the kernel). `getDisbursementRankings` reads the ranking that ST-DIS-013 computed, with the names of the units, a table for each group and each denominator.
- ST-HOME-003 (open, contract): `rule_code` and `warning_id` of `getHomeWarnings` are not in `openapi.json` (it has `snapshot_id`, `severity`, `cursor`, `limit`); the server takes them and the web client types the query of the profile as a query of the list. They are to be added to the contract with the next revision (the profile could also be a read of its own).
- ST-HOME-003 (open, BR-HOME-06): the policy `ranking_visibility` (ALL or OWN_ONLY) has no tenant setting yet; it is a provider (`HOME_RANKING_VISIBILITY`) that answers ALL when none is bound. With OWN_ONLY a unit keeps its rank among all.
- ST-HOME-003 (open, limit): warnings of a unit (not of a project) are not listed (the project scope of DIS-23); the profile shows the member ids of the history, not their names.
- ST-HOME-003 (correction of ST-HOME-001, BR-HOME-05): the overdue explanations now include the ones answered whose day is past and that are not concluded, and the reallocation proposals that wait are the submitted and the approved ones not yet applied; the store tests were extended.
- ST-HOME-004 (decision, no table): `getMyWork` collects, by the membership of the person who asks, the approvals that wait (ADM `approval_task` Pending), the explanations owed (PRJ: requested, returned, overdue) and to conclude (answered, asked by the person), the reports returned or draft past the period (DIS), and the monitoring reports of the unit of the person (MNE, drafts and returned of the first revision due within 14 days or late); the order is `orderWork` of the kernel (late, near within 7 days, the rest, then the day, the kind, the id).
- ST-HOME-004 (open, ADM): the approval has no screen to open it from (the inbox of ST-ADM-012 is later): the card has no link. The workflow engine keeps `document_type` of the instance as the only name of what is approved.
- ST-HOME-004 (assumption): the obligations of MNE are of the unit of the membership of the person (the story names no per-person assignment); a report is the work of whoever created or handed it in (the DIS assignment of a report to a person is not kept); the number of days of "near" (7) and of the look ahead for obligations (14) are the story's own.
- ST-HOME-004 (open, contract): `getMyWork` returns `items`, `counts` and `next_cursor` (the contract names only `cursor` and `limit` and no body schema).
- ST-HOME-005 (decision, no operation): the story names no operation and the contract has no HOME funding read, so HOME-07/08 read `listAppropriations` (`capital.read`) and `get_resources_allocation_line` (`allocation_line.read`) through the web and group them in `funding-model.ts`; published appropriations of one year, CTMTQG from the `ctmtqg_subset_vnd` of the allocation lines of published plans, grouped by appropriation (the register's `capital_plan_id` is optional and not used) (inside the amount of the line, never added), nothing known is "no data" and never zero.
- ST-HOME-005 (open, contract): a HOME operation with the figures grouped, and the scope of the reader applied by the server, replaces the two CAP reads; `v_home_funding_structure` of the plan is not in the schema. Until then a reader needs `capital.read` besides `home.read`.
- ST-BI-001 (decision): a metric is data with an allowlisted expression tree (`bi-metric.ts`); the platform syncs `metrics/*.metric.yaml` through a JSON bundle (`scripts/bi-metrics-bundle.mjs`, run in lint) read from the object store by hash; same commit and bundle again is `NO_CHANGE`, an older bundle from a revert commit applies (V1580 changes the unique index to commit and hash); a refused sync is kept as a `REJECTED` run in a transaction of its own.
- ST-BI-001 (open, contract): BI-15 has no read of `bi_metric_sync_run` (no operation lists runs): the bundle hash and the error show "no data yet" and the counts are derived from the revisions. A list operation of the runs is a later change of the contract.
- ST-BI-001 (open, MDM): `glossary_term` of a platform metric is validated and hashed but not linked (`glossary_term_id` empty) until the MDM glossary can be looked up by code. The exclusion of two published revisions of one code over the same days (effective-range registry, `metric_definition` stays PENDING) is for ST-BI-003 with the publisher.
- ST-BI-001 (open, dependency): `js-yaml` is used by `scripts/bi-metrics-bundle.mjs` for the YAML files but is not declared in `package.json` (it arrives through another dependency; adding it needs a registry the sandbox could not reach). The dimensions of a dataset are read from `bi_dataset.join_paths.dimensions` until ST-BI-002 fixes the format. The bundle is stored through the file service with purpose `document` and type `text/plain`.
- ST-BI-002 (decision): a version is the record of one frozen close snapshot of DIS with the quality run of DGV (badge, linked run, quarantined count); one FROZEN version per dataset and period, the earlier ones SUPERSEDED and kept; nothing new is made for the same content (checksum). Built by the consumer of `snapshot.frozen.v1` and `dgv.quality_run.completed.v1` and by `buildBiDatasetVersion`; `BUILD_ALREADY_RUNNING` from a BUILDING row or an advisory lock.
- ST-BI-002 (open, contract): the contract had no read of datasets or versions, so `listBiDatasets` and `listBiDatasetVersions` (`bi.query`) were added to openapi.json for BI-07 and BI-18 (`buildBiDatasetVersion` answers 202 as the contract says).
- ST-BI-002 (open, DGV): DGV adds up what each rule quarantined (a row two rules quarantine is counted twice) and does not keep which rows, so the count is an upper bound and the certified rows a lower bound (`certified_row_count_min`); the compiler of ST-BI-004 must exclude them once DGV keeps their identity. Only `DS-DISB` (period snapshots) is buildable; the other `bi_dataset` source kinds wait for their sources. The metrics (ST-BI-001) read `bi_dataset.join_paths.dimensions` and DS-DISB declares `project, unit, fund_source, budget_level, bucket`.
- ST-BI-003 (decision): closed by default in `BiPolicyStore.applicable` (403 ACCESS_POLICY_MISSING, never an empty list); predicates are a small allowlisted tree over the dimensions of the dataset, ANDed with the scope of the reader at query time (a policy narrows, never widens); activation by someone else retires the policy it replaces (`activate`, SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED).
- ST-BI-003 (open, dependency): the part of AC-ST-BI-003-1 that reads data (only in-scope rows returned, `account_no` absent from results and exports) and the query with a `sql` field (AC -2, FIELD_NOT_ALLOWED on `post_dashboard_queries`) belong to the query compiler of ST-BI-004; the decision that activates a policy (`decideBiPublication`) is ST-BI-006 (R2), so until then a policy is activated through the store only. The preview as a user of BI-08 needs the query. No sample policy is seeded: with none, every dataset is closed.
- ST-BI-003 (open, contract): `listBiAccessPolicies` (`bi.policy.manage`) was added to openapi.json for BI-08.
- ST-BI-004 (decision): the `snapshot_id` of the BI API is the id of the dataset version of a period (the FK of `drill_context`), and the DIS close snapshot it was made from supplies the lines; a query is one certified metric, at most one dimension, SQL written from allowlists (`DISBURSEMENT_FIELDS`, `DISBURSEMENT_DIMENSIONS`); ratios from sums, distinct counts apart, Unclassified as a group, null with a reason never 0; drill tokens HMAC under a per-tenant key (`kid`), 30 minutes, contexts kept server-side, the right to read judged again on every step; closed by default on the policies of ST-BI-003.
- ST-BI-004 (open, not built): the asynchronous job (202) above a cost threshold and the per-tenant rate limit (429) of F3; a result is cut at 500 groups (`truncated`) and `cursor` is accepted and not used; the cache is in the process (a shared cache with invalidation belongs to the platform cache service); `DrillQuery` has no `as_of_period`, the period is the version's. Only `DS-DISB` is queryable.
- ST-BI-004 (decision, metric): the null policy of the metric is applied by the query (`ZERO_IF_NO_ROWS` gives zero only when the group has no line at all; lines that have no figure are still no data). A column a policy hides or masks (by its name in the table, by the name of a field that reads it or by the name of a dimension) is not summed either: the metric over it is refused as `DIMENSION_NOT_IN_DATASET`. An identifier field (DU_AN, DON_VI) is counted, never summed. A path that is open keeps the revision of the metric it began with when a newer one replaces it.
- ST-BI-004 (open, DGV): the rows DGV quarantined cannot be left out of the sums (no identity kept); `quarantined_count` is an upper bound and is shown, with `feeds_certified: false` for a FAILED or STALE version.
- ST-BI-004 (assumption): BI-02 ("my dashboards") is the board of the certified metrics, because dashboards are R2 (ST-BI-005); the part of AC-ST-BI-003-1 that reads data (only in-scope rows returned, hidden columns absent) is covered here by the query tests (policy predicates, hidden dimensions).
- ST-HOME-005 (assumption): the cut-off (*Thời điểm chốt*) is the day of the decision that gave the capital (`decision_document.issued_on`), the only date the register exposes; the register does not expose when a revision was published.
- ST-LLM-001 (decision): the registry rules are the kernel's (`llm-registry`) and the database holds the same ones (V1140: the baseline check of a PUBLISHED policy, the unique index of a model without a version); a policy is published only if it is no wider than the baseline matrix (BR-LLM-01) and keeps the self-hosted-only line of important data; stricter is allowed; exactly one policy stays PUBLISHED. A provider that takes more than public data needs its data agreement to be activated; a draft dated in the future cannot be published before its date. The reads `listAiProviders`, `listAiModels` and `listAiClassificationPolicies` were added to the contract with the new permissions `llm.provider.read` and `llm.policy.read` (to be granted with the role matrix, ST-IAM).
- ST-LLM-001 (open, for ST-LLM-005): `ai_request.policy_version_id` is not null, but a tenant with no published policy has no row to point at: the gateway must record the baseline policy (a revision made by the system) before the first request. `models.status` goes to EVAL_PENDING and APPROVED with the evaluation (ST-LLM-004); activation of a SUSPENDED provider is allowed here and the suspend command comes with the kill switch (ST-LLM-006).
- ST-LLM-002 (decision): the secret of a virtual key lives only in the vault (`secret://tenants/{tenant}/litellm/{key}`); the application keeps the fingerprint and the references, and no view, audit row, event, log or screen carries a secret or a reference. LiteLLM and the vault are behind `LiteLlmAdminPort` and `SecretVaultPort` (kernel); `MockLiteLlm` and `InMemoryVault` stand in until the services are deployed. A rotation leaves the old key ROTATING for 24 hours and the `llm-key-sweep` job (every 5 minutes) revokes it; V1141 lets only the ACTIVE key hold an alias. New contract read `listAiVirtualKeys` with the permission `llm.key.read` (to be granted with the role matrix).
- ST-LLM-002 (DONE_PENDING_ENV): the deployment of LiteLLM and Langfuse (self-hosted on domestic cloud), the adapters of the real LiteLLM admin API and the vault, and the restore test that lets `infra/components/litellm-langfuse.json` leave PLANNED (AC-ST-LLM-002-3, UAT-NFR-OPERABILITY-01) need a real environment. The runbook and the alert rules exist; the component stays PLANNED until a restore is done on a clean host and recorded as evidence.
- ST-LLM-003 (decision): money is exact (decimal strings of up to eight places held as BigInt, cost in dong rounded half up); a request is held against every budget of its scopes with the counters locked one at a time in the order tenant, unit, project, user; a settlement is one per provider request (the attempt is unique by `provider_request_id`, the reservation is locked first); an actual cost above the hold is an exception that blocks the scope; a request with no usage is PENDING_RECONCILE and keeps its hold, and the job `llm-reservation-expiry` (every 5 minutes) moves a lapsed RESERVED one there. V1142 fixes the baseline (a tenant budget could be made twice; the check that cached tokens never exceed the input made the SEPARATE semantics impossible).
- ST-LLM-003 (open): verification of a price by a second person (BR-LLM-13) has no operation in the contract and `pricing_version` keeps no declarer: prices are shown as not verified; the approval task for a budget that does not stop needs a workflow instance (the store answers APPROVAL_REQUIRED, the gateway records the wait); the quotas (`max_requests_per_day`, `max_concurrent`) are enforced at admission by ST-LLM-005; `reconcileAiInvoice` and `post_budgets_id_reconcile` are not built; LLM-09 shows reservations and totals, the detail of each attempt comes with ST-LLM-005.
