New contract reads `listAiBudgets` and `listAiPricingVersions` (permission `llm.finops`).
- ST-LLM-004 (decision): a prompt is approved by someone other than its author and is then immutable (the database refuses the update too; approval moves the label PRODUCTION and retires the previous holder); datasets are numbered versions of synthetic or anonymised cases (nothing above internal) and need an injection case to be used; an evaluation is one profile × dataset × route, signed by someone other than who started it, PASS only over passing metrics; a run the runner could not finish is kept as FAILED and cannot be signed; a profile is released only when every model is APPROVED, the effort is supported and every route has a current run signed as passed, by someone other than its drafter, with the ids of those runs in the audit row; the international route takes public data only; one PUBLISHED profile per task (partial unique index, V1144); the new contract parameter `profile_version_id` of `get_resources_ai_eval_run`.
- ST-LLM-005 (decision): the class of a request is the highest class of its sources (document versions, clean and of the tenant) and is never said by the caller; the route is the policy in force (none: the baseline, international off) ∩ the routes of the profile ∩ approved, priced models that take the class, the international route only for public data (checked again before each call, counted in `llm_international_non_public_total`, refused by the database too); the budget is held before a model is called and settled attempt by attempt; an answer that fails the checks is asked for once more from the same model and is not sent to another; only hashes and counts are kept, never the text of an input or an answer; a request refused by the policy or the budget is kept with its audit and its refusal is the answer for the same key (`refuseAfterCommit`); a request with no policy published names the nil uuid in the route decision; V1145 makes `ai_request.policy_version_id` nullable and adds the guards.
- ST-LLM-005 (open): the models are `MockAiGateway` until the adapter through LiteLLM with the virtual key of the tenant is deployed, and the call is made inside the transaction of the command (to be split into admission, call and settlement for a real provider); quotas (`max_requests_per_day`, `max_concurrent`) have no acceptance criterion and move to ST-LLM-006; the ACL of each source and fields with a class (not whole documents) are not checked; the SAS entitlement and the kill switch are other stories; the approval task of a soft budget is still left to the workflow; the Langfuse trace id is not set; the eval spend of ST-LLM-004 is still not taken from a budget.
- ST-LLM-006 (decision): a kill switch is for one scope (tenant, provider, model, profile, task) and one is engaged at a time per scope; new requests of the scope are refused with 503 at the door and before every attempt, Postgres is read each time (no cache between: the table has a handful of rows); what waited is cancelled and its hold given back, what was sent is cancelled when the provider can and is otherwise marked killed_pending and its answer thrown away; a switch is released only by another person and does not engage again; the decisions of people are written once (the database refuses change and delete, except that the command fills applied_operation_id/applied_resource_version once), and a command that uses an answer of the AI asks AiDecisionStore.require in its own transaction. V1146 also lets a request be CANCELLED with no route and makes ai_request.killed_pending. New contract reads listAiKillSwitches and listAiDecisionRecords (llm.audit.read).
- ST-LLM-006 (open): BID, PAY, DIS and OMNI do not call AiDecisionStore.require/markApplied yet (the owners of those modules adopt the port); the stop of a request that the gateway runs in its own transaction waits for the adapter that splits admission, call and settlement; the Redis cache of the design is not built (not needed to meet five seconds today); the SAS entitlement check of the gateway is another story.
- ST-LLM-004 (open): the runner is `MockEvalRunner` until ST-LLM-005 calls the models through LiteLLM; the spend of an evaluation (`cost_vnd`) is kept on the run but not taken from a budget; the act of rolling back (`ROLLED_BACK`) and routing/fallback (LLM-05) belong to ST-LLM-005/006; the comparison of two models (LLM-13) and the playground (LLM-11) are other stories; the template of a prompt is uploaded through the document store, the screen takes its key and hash.

### ST-QMS-001 (site diary, slice QMS-1)
- ST-QMS-001 (decision, the states): `DRAFT → CONTRACTOR_SIGNED → SUPERVISED → OWNER_CONFIRMED → LOCKED`, and `SUPERSEDED` once a correction of a locked diary is locked. The contractor signs over one hash of the canonical content (sorted keys, numbers as strings), the contract, the day and the diary it corrects, so a signature over other content is void (`valid=false`). Triggers `guard_site_diary` and `guard_site_diary_signature` (V1463) repeat the order in the database; `machvon_app` cannot delete a diary.
- ST-QMS-001 (decision, who signs): the standing of the signer is judged on the membership, not on the role alone: the contractor as a member of the contractor of the contract, the supervisor and the owner as staff of the procuring entity (`rolesSignableBy`); a person signs a diary once (`qms_signature_person_diary_uq`). A refusal for the wrong role is 403 `SCOPE_DENIED` with detail `signer_role`.
- ST-QMS-001 (decision, one diary per day): one main diary per contract and day; a second save of the same day without the version it was based on is 409 `DIARY_DAY_EXISTS`, a save based on an older version is 409 `DIARY_SYNC_CONFLICT`; a retry with the same `client_operation_id` answers the first answer. A correction is a new diary (`-R{n}`) with `correction_of_id`; one is open at a time.
- ST-QMS-001 (decision, the value of a signature): an in-app confirmation is an `INTERNAL_RECORD`, a certificate a `LEGAL_E_SIGNATURE` (BR-QMS-03); until the business settles CXN-EXE-05 every view says `awaiting_confirmation: true` and the screens show the note. The CXN is a constant in the kernel (`DIARY_SIGNATURE_VALUE`) to be replaced by the rule.
- ST-QMS-001 (open): the lock after three working days without a supervisor is a scheduled job and an alert that are not built; `resolveDiaryConflict` and the conflict copies of an offline client (ST-QMS-002), `getDiaryCoverage` and the photos (ST-QMS-003) are other stories; the serial of a certificate is kept as given and is not checked against the DOC adapter.
- ST-QMS-001 (decision, operations of updates): the operation of an update of a draft (`saveSiteDiaryDraft` or `reviseSiteDiaryDraft`) is kept in `site_diary_operation` (append-only, one row per operation of the tenant, with the version it made), so that the retry of a successful update with another key of the request is told what it did and not `DIARY_SYNC_CONFLICT`. A day whose diary was replaced by a correction is not a day with no diary: saving it answers 409 `DIARY_STATE_INVALID`.

### ST-QMS-002 (offline site diary, slice QMS-1)
- ST-QMS-002 (decision, the copy of a device): a save over a draft that the device had not read at its version commits a copy (`conflict_of_id`, `CONFLICT_PENDING`) and then answers 409 `DIARY_SYNC_CONFLICT` (`refuseAfterCommit`), so the conflict is stored and shown and nothing is overwritten; the list shows the diary of the day once and the copies hang from it. `resolveDiaryConflict` keeps the diary, takes the copy or merges, and keeps what was set aside in `conflict_discarded`; a settled copy is kept as it is. V1464 adds the columns and tightens the triggers (a copy is never signed).
- ST-QMS-002 (decision, the place): the server refuses a place (`content.location`) without `content.geo_consent: true` (422) instead of dropping it silently, because dropping would change what the contractor signs; the device must not send one without consent.
- ST-QMS-002 (open, DONE_PENDING_ENV): the Flutter app (QMS-09: offline form, queue of commands with `client_operation_id`, conflict screen, permission of location, wipe of the data of the previous tenant on a tenant switch), the PowerSync sync rules per tenant and contract scope and the revocation test (a removed membership stops syncing), the PowerSync runbook and its backup/restore test and alert, and `post_sync_batches` (ST-MOB-005) are not built here: there is no mobile client or PowerSync service in this environment. UAT-AC367 (no location without consent, wipe on a tenant switch) is proved on the server for the first half (the diary refuses it) and waits for the device for the second.
- ST-QMS-002 (open): the settling is not offered as a guided merge of the fields (the person writes the merged content); a copy of a device for a diary that was signed meanwhile can only be set aside (`KEEP_PRIMARY`), and the diary of the day is never written over once signed.

### ST-QMS-003 (site photo register, slice QMS-1)
- ST-QMS-003 (decision, flags not verdicts): the four checks (time, place, repetition, EXIF) are flags for review; `check_summary` is PASS or WARN, FAIL is never produced because an infected file (`FILE_NOT_CLEAN`) or a wrong hash (`HASH_MISMATCH`) is refused and not registered. The server reads the EXIF itself from the stored file (`readExif`, JPEG only, no dependency) and the hash from the bytes.
- ST-QMS-003 (decision, the place): a place is kept only with the consent to it, the one in the EXIF included (`site_photo_place_chk`: no coordinates, accuracy or source without `geo_consent`); a device that sends a place without the consent is refused with `GEO_CONSENT_REQUIRED` so that the person knows it was not kept. The place is judged against `project_site_zone` (new, V1465: centre and radius, several per project); a project with no zone has `MISSING`.
- ST-QMS-003 (decision, contract): `get_resources_site_photo` and `get_resources_site_photo_id` (photo.read) are added to openapi.json for the register of QMS-08, which had no list; the purpose `site_photo` is added to the upload purposes (images only, 15 MiB). Photos are append-only (trigger, no DELETE for the runtime role).
- ST-QMS-003 (open, DONE_PENDING_ENV): the Flutter app QMS-10 (camera, consent prompt, checksum, resumable upload, no coordinates in the app storage without consent: UAT-AC367 on the device), the presigned upload to the object storage and the real antivirus; nothing in this environment uploads a photo from a phone.
- ST-QMS-003 (open): the pHash of the design (Hamming distance up to 6) is not computed, only the SHA-256 (an image library has to be approved in `supply-chain.json`); the stored file keeps its own EXIF GPS when there is no consent (the file is not re-encoded); PRJ has no command to draw the zones of a site (rows of `project_site_zone` are written by the owner of the data); QMS-08 lists the places as coordinates and has no map tiles.

### ST-DOC-011 (incoming documents, slice DOC-1)
- ST-DOC-011 (decision, state secret): a title, symbol or sender that declares TỐI MẬT, TUYỆT MẬT, "độ mật: MẬT" or "(MẬT)" is refused (`STATE_SECRET_NOT_SUPPORTED`) and audited as `DENIED` without the text; the check is on the words the clerk types, it does not read inside the PDF (no OCR here), so a secret level only in the body of the file is the clerk's judgement (BR-DOC-19).
- ST-DOC-011 (decision, duplicates): a suspected duplicate is the same sender, original symbol and day written on the letter in the scope of the caller; it is refused with the id of the other letter and taken in only with a reason of at least ten characters, which is kept with the link.
- ST-DOC-011 (decision, overdue): late days are working days by the calendar `VN-STD` (weekdays where none is published); the hourly job `markOverdueIncoming` marks once and emits `doc.incoming_overdue.v1` once; `late_days` is kept when the letter is resolved. The job is a function, not yet scheduled by the worker runtime (the scheduler of the jobs belongs to the platform slice).
- ST-DOC-011 (open): filing (`FILED`) and the link to the outgoing response from DOC-13 are ST-DOC-013; the channels EMAIL, LGSP and PORTAL and the OCR/auto-classification of a scanned letter are ST-DOC-014; DOC-13 does not offer `response_outgoing_id` and takes the handler as a user id typed by the assigner (no picker of the members of a unit yet).
- ST-DOC-011 (decision, the file): the PDF of a letter is kept in the store the downloads are served from (`DOCUMENT_FILE_PORT`, key with the digest), and `getDocumentVersionDownload` / the version reads use `versionScope`: the unit that issues the document or, for an incoming letter, the unit of its register, the unit it was given to, and whoever took it in. The right asked for the download is still `doc.outgoing.read` (the contract names it); a role for incoming letters has to hold it as well as `document_version.read`. A narrower right for incoming files (and its own read operation) is for the contract owner.
- ST-DOC-011 (decision, the job): `incoming-overdue` is on the scheduler of the API process (`standardJobs`, hourly); the reminder is `doc.incoming_overdue.v1` (registered for notifications in V1225) addressed to every membership of the handler, or, for a letter given to a unit with no one named, to the memberships that may work letters in that unit; the resource is `doc.incoming` so that `PermissionResourceAccess` asks for `doc.incoming.read`. Configuring a tenant's workflow with `role-in-scope` ignores the explicit recipients (a workflow choice).
- ST-DOC-011 (open): a letter whose handler has no membership that is active any more is marked overdue and announced to nobody until it is reassigned; the register picker of DOC-13 offers what the server names when several are open (no list of registers is read there); the intake takes the type as typed when the list of types cannot be read; the response document of a resolution is not offered on DOC-13.

### ST-DOC-013 (dossiers with provisional retention, slice DOC-1)
- ST-DOC-013 (decision, contract): `fileIncomingDocument` took a `DocDossierItem` (`document_id`), which names no dossier; its body is now `DocIncomingFiling` `{dossier_id, ordinal?}` (the letter is the one in the path); the two changes are accepted in `compat-allow.json`.
- ST-DOC-013 (decision, disposal): AC-ST-DOC-013-1 speaks of a disposal proposal; `proposeDossierDisposal` belongs to ST-DOC-017, so this story provides what it will use (`disposalBlocker` / `assertDisposable` in the kernel, `DocDossierStore.holdActive`, and `disposal_blocker` on `getDossier`) and proves the order (provisional 422, legal hold 409 also after the retention, not expired 422, not closed 409). ST-DOC-017 maps them to the responses.
- ST-DOC-013 (decision, retention): a rule is chosen only when it is PUBLISHED, of the category and in force on the day of closing; an unconfirmed rule never counts (the dossier is provisional with no rule). `FROM_ISSUED` counts from the latest `issued_on` of the documents of the dossier. The order of the rule sets is the default one (TT 74/2025 first, a design assumption CXN-05); a per-tenant order is not configurable yet.
- ST-DOC-013 (open): a dossier closed while no rule was confirmed stays provisional and PERMANENT; the trigger lets one later command settle it once, but no command does yet (ST-DOC-017: `handoverDossierToArchive`, `proposeDossierDisposal`, `decideDossierDisposal`, `createDocumentRetentionRule`, `publishDocumentRetentionRule`, `confirmDocumentRetentionRuleLegalBasis`), and the consumers of `legal_hold.placed/released` are not needed (the hold is read when the dossier is read). DOC-17 and DOC-18 (screens) are ST-DOC-017.
- ST-DOC-013 (open): an archivist files documents of the units under their grant of `doc.dossier.manage` (the document of another unit is as good as none); the filing of a document of an incoming letter without a register follows the rule of `incomingScope`. The outgoing document goes to FILED by the store directly (no kernel state machine for outgoing exists yet to carry that step).

### ST-DOC-014 (overview of the documents, slice DOC-1)
- ST-DOC-014 (decision, contract): `getDocumentOverview` (`GET /document-overview`, `doc.overview.read`) is added to openapi.json; the story named no operation. It is a read with no parameters.
- ST-DOC-014 (decision, overdue): "overdue" means late now (assigned or processing); the `overdue` filter of `listIncomingDocuments` and `is_overdue` of the reads now agree with the tile. A letter resolved late is no longer overdue (it keeps `late_days`); before this story the filter listed it.
- ST-DOC-014 (decision, scope): a tile whose kind the viewer may not read is `null`, not 0; the overview itself needs `doc.overview.read`, held apart from the rights to read documents.
- ST-DOC-014 (open): the interoperability tile is 0 until ST-DOC-021; the queue of "waiting for the seal of the agency" counts the state NUMBERED (the design has no separate seal queue); counts are read on demand, with a reload button, not pushed.

### ST-DGV-003 (quality incidents and exceptions, slice DGV-1)
- ST-DGV-003 (decision, people): in DGV the owner, steward, assignee and verifier are memberships (as in ST-DGV-001); the story's "user" is the membership. The handler must hold `data.quality.remediate` on the whole tenant.
- ST-DGV-003 (decision, the waiver that ends): the exception does not go back to OPEN in place; a new OPEN exception points at the waived one (`reopened_from_id`, unique), so that the decision and its reason stay as they were written (the story says QE-1 reopens "with reopened_from_id"). The incident behind it is REOPENED once.
- ST-DGV-003 (decision, rerun): "a run that passes" is the latest completed run of the dataset finished after the incident was resolved, with `PASS` for the rule (same code in the same contract) in it.
- ST-DGV-003 (decision, contract): `DgvExceptionDecision` replaces the generic body of `post_quality_exceptions_id_decision`; the notification types `dgv.incident.sla_breached.v1` and `dgv.exception.reopened.v1` are registered (V1022) with the resource `quality_incident` so that `quality_incident.read` lets the person see the reminder.
- ST-DGV-003 (open, done in ST-DGV-004): the quality run opens an incident per failed rule and, for a QUARANTINE rule, now writes the exception rows of the records it kept out (up to 50 sample ids). The export of exceptions does not exist (the masking is on the read). The 4/24/72/240-hour SLA and the 180-day waiver are working assumptions until the data owner signs.

### ST-DGV-004 (source mappings, the gate of a batch, reconciliation; slice DGV-1)
- ST-DGV-004 (decision, sides): A is `PAY.treasury_transaction` (payments minus refunds per project up to the last day of the month, Vietnam clock), B is the `disbursed_reported_vnd` of the frozen `PERIOD_CLOSE` snapshot of the month (`reporting_period` of type MONTH whose start date is in the month asked, as `YYYY-MM`). The only sources and basis accepted are those two and `VND, ngày Kho bạc thanh toán`; anything else is 422 `BASIS_MISMATCH`. The comparison is whole-tenant, so the command and the reads need the right on the whole tenant.
- ST-DGV-004 (decision, result): `MATCHED` only when no project differs; `WITHIN_TOLERANCE` only when every project is on both sides within the tolerance (BR-DGV-09 compares totals; this is stricter on purpose: offsetting differences are still differences). 202 is kept for `runReconciliation` although the answer is already computed (the id reads at once).
- ST-DGV-004 (decision, incident): a `DIFFERENT` reconciliation has one HIGH incident (24 h) owned by the owner of the dataset, with `reconciliation_id` and no `rule_id` (exactly one of the two); the open incident of the same code and month is reused; it closes after a later reconciliation of that code and month that is not `DIFFERENT`. The dataset is `dataset_id` (new optional field of `DgvReconRequest`) or the only DIS dataset the caller sees.
- ST-DGV-004 (decision, quarantine): a QUARANTINE rule that did not pass lists the sample ids it keeps (at most 50) as OPEN exceptions of the owner of the dataset; `quarantined_count` keeps the whole count.
- ST-DGV-004 (decision, mappings): a unit is one of dong, thousand, million, billion (code or Vietnamese words); a rejected mapping can be proposed again (the unique index covers proposed and confirmed only); the decision is by a member other than the proposer who holds `data.mapping.approve` on the whole tenant (the data owner of the story is not tied to the owner of the dataset yet).
- ST-DGV-004 (open): DIS, PAY and MDM do not call `DgvMappingStore.gate` when they read a batch (they import through their own stores); the gate, its table and DGV-14 are ready for them. The gate counts rows as the caller says.
- ST-DGV-004 (open): `MAX_DIFFERENCES` (1000) is a working assumption; the projects of a difference are ids, not names (the project name is a read of PRJ). The notification of an incident of a reconciliation to its owner is not sent (the sweep tells about lateness only). DGV-14 shows validation and publish stages as counts of batches only; a richer pipeline view waits for the pipelines.

### ST-IAM-006 (profile, sessions and devices self-service, slice IAM-1)
- ST-IAM-006 (decision, profile): `user_account` is global to the person, so a change in one tenant is a change in all of them; it is reached only through `my_profile` and `update_my_profile` (V1242), which take the membership of the caller and find the user from it in the tenant of the transaction. The phone cannot be cleared (the contract has no "none"); the email is not editable here.
- ST-IAM-006 (decision, sessions): the session of the request is not revocable here (422 `SESSION_CURRENT`, new code); revoking a session opened on a device removes that device with it ("signs out" in AC-3); a repeat of a revocation changes nothing and is recorded as `session.revoke_repeated` / `device.revoke_repeated` (a command must write an audit row). The reason of 10 characters is the `ReasonOnly` body of the contract; the screen offers a default.
- ST-IAM-006 (decision, devices): `device_registration` keeps its V002 shape (a new live row when a device signs in again); the push token reference is nulled on revocation (V1242 makes the column nullable, checked as null exactly when revoked).
- ST-IAM-006 (open): the Keycloak back-channel logout is a port (`IdentityLogout`, `NoIdentityLogout` by default) wired through `session.identityLogout`; the real call needs the identity provider (DONE_PENDING_ENV). The deny-list acts at once in the process that revoked and within its 5 s negative cache elsewhere (the Redis deny-list of IAM 3.4 is still to come).
- ST-IAM-006 (open): the devices are registered by the notification service (ST-NOT-006, not built); there is no command here to register one. The alert thresholds for the failure modes of the story (observability task) are not written.

### ST-ADM-012 (approval workflow engine, slice ADM-1)
- ST-ADM-012 (decision, no Temporal): Temporal is not in the repository, so the engine follows the definition in the database, one transaction per decision (locks on the task and the instance). `workflow_instance.temporal_workflow_id` is written as `appr:{tenant}:{instance}` so a Temporal runner can take over. Domain events are written to the outbox as the design says (`adm.workflow_instance.started.v1`, `adm.approval_task.decided.v1`, `.completed.v1`, `.cancelled.v1`, `adm.approval_task.reassigned.v1`, `adm.workflow_guard_rejected.v1`).
- ST-ADM-012 (decision, tasks): a DoA step gives a task to every member whose grants reach the approver role of the band (capped at 20 per step); the approvals needed are the larger of `min_approvals` and the levels of the band; the people who approved an earlier step are left out of a DoA step. A maker who holds a task has it reassigned, and one who only tries to decide for someone else is refused without moving the task.
- ST-ADM-012 (decision, errors): new codes `WORKFLOW_ALREADY_RUNNING` (409) and `NO_ELIGIBLE_APPROVER` (422); `WORKFLOW_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `WORKFLOW_NOT_DRAFT`, `TASK_ALREADY_DECIDED`, `APPROVER_NOT_AUTHORIZED` and `DOA_RULE_NOT_FOUND` were in the registry already. `return` and `reject` need a reason of 10 characters.
- ST-ADM-012 (decision, final command): the five business types are bound to test doubles that accept (`FinalCommandRegistry`); each owning module calls `register(businessType, handler)` with its real command (XM-ADM-01). The handler runs in the transaction of the decision with the key `wf:{instance}:final:{seq}` and answers `APPLIED` or `GUARD_REJECTED`.
- ST-ADM-012 (open): the notification of a new task is not sent (NOT-004..006). The SLA timers, `cancelWorkflowInstance`, `reassignApprovalTask` and the counters were built in ST-ADM-013. A definition has no `name` (the contract draft has none); ADM-06 shows the revision and the business type.

### ST-ADM-013 (operations on a running approval and the SLA, slice ADM-1)
- ST-ADM-013 (decision, no Temporal timers): the SLA is a job (`approval-sla`, every 5 minutes, `ApprovalStore.sweepSla`) over the pending tasks, not a Temporal timer per task: a task is judged by the pure rule `slaAction` of the kernel, once at `remind_at` (80 percent of the working time to `due_at`, counted when the task is created on the calendar `WORKDAY_VN`, or 80 percent of the elapsed time when no calendar is published) and at the deadline as its step says. A reminder is at most 5 minutes late. The same pass can be moved to a Temporal activity without changing the rule or the columns. The Temporal backup/restore test and the alert on a stuck approvals queue belong to ST-KERNEL-044 (the owner of that capability).
- ST-ADM-013 (decision, operator rights): both commands need `adm.workflow.operate` through a grant on the WHOLE tenant (as the reads that show every task); a grant on a unit subtree is 403 `SCOPE_DENIED`. A reason of 10 characters and the `If-Match` of the task (reassign) or of the instance (cancel) are required.
- ST-ADM-013 (decision, who may be given a task): the new assignee passes the same test as an escalation target (`canHold`): a member who exists, is not the maker (judged on the person), has no pending task or approval in the step already, did not approve the step before a DoA step, and is authorised by the rule of the step as their grants are now. A SPECIFIC step therefore cannot be reassigned outside its list of members; widen the list by publishing a new revision. The one exception is the escalation target of the step (`escalate_to`), who decides the task they were escalated whatever the rule says (named by the definition itself, which a second administrator published).
- ST-ADM-013 (decision, EXPIRE): an expired task is `Expired`; the instance ends `Completed` with the outcome `EXPIRED` only when the approvals given plus the tasks still open cannot reach what the step needs. No final command is called for it (there is none for a lapse): the owning module has to react to `adm.workflow_instance.completed.v1` with `outcome: EXPIRED` and `command: null` (cross-module request XM-ADM-01, with the reaction to `cancelled.v1`). Until then the record stays in its in-review state.
- ST-ADM-013 (decision, cancel does not touch the record): `cancelWorkflowInstance` ends the instance and its tasks; the record in the owning module is left as it was and the module is told by `adm.workflow_instance.cancelled.v1` (`by: OPERATOR`). The module that withdraws its own record keeps calling `ApprovalService.cancel`.
- ST-ADM-013 (decision, outcome constraint): V1306 replaces the check on `workflow_instance.outcome` (V002) by one that also allows `EXPIRED`.
- ST-ADM-013 (gap, notifications): `adm.approval_task.reminded.v1`, `.escalated.v1` and `.expired.v1` are events and audit rows only; nothing turns them into a notification to the approver or the operators until NOT-004..006 (the notification workflows) take them. The alert `ADM_APPROVAL_OVERDUE` and the screen ADM-18 are what shows a late task today.
- ST-ADM-013 (gap, ADM-18): the monitor lists pending tasks only (no history of reassigned, cancelled or expired tasks, no filter by unit or assignee, no link to the record, whose screen belongs to the owning module); the member chosen for a reassignment comes from the active staff and the server judges the rest, so a person who cannot decide the step is offered and refused with a reason.
- ST-ADM-013 (gap, metrics sink): `AppModule` now gives the ADM controllers the sink of `options.policy.metrics` (`ADM_METRICS`), but `bootstrap.ts` passes none yet (the exporter is ST-KERNEL-050), so in production the counter `workflow_guard_rejected_total` (and `audit_export_*` of ADM-016) is silent until a sink is bound there; the gauges and the counter of the job `approval-sla` use the sink of the workers, which has the same gap. The tasks open when V1306 runs get `remind_at` from the migration (80 percent of the time from creation to the deadline, a time-of-day approximation of the working-time point).
### ST-ADM-014 (time-boxed delegation of approval authority, slice ADM-1)
### ST-ADM-015 (reorganisation of organisation units, slice ADM-1)
- ST-ADM-015 (decision, no Temporal): the saga keeps its state in `unit_reorganization` and `unit_reorganization_step` and is carried out by the job `unit-reorganization` (every minute), one step per transaction, each found by what is left to do; the key `reorg:{id}:{step}:{item}` of the design is the row of each step. The "future-dated timer" is the job waiting for the effective date (Vietnam date). Moving to Temporal later keeps the steps and the table.
- ST-ADM-015 (decision, CR-ADM-16): `openapi.json` had no read for the reorganisations, which ADM-16 needs; `get_resources_unit_reorganization` and `get_resources_unit_reorganization_id` were added in place, permission `iam.unit.reorganize.read` on the whole tenant (list and detail with the steps). The three permissions are the tenant's: `iam.unit.reorganize`, `.approve` and `.read` are checked on the whole tenant, not on a subtree (the reorganisation reaches units, members and projects across the tree).
- ST-ADM-015 (decision, "stays Draft"): AC-2 says the refused proposal stays Draft. The contract has no operation to edit a draft, so a draft left behind by a refusal could be neither corrected nor sent; a refused proposal (422 `REORG_MAPPING_INCOMPLETE` with the missing ids) stores nothing, and the user proposes again. The state Draft exists for `return`: a proposal sent back; **there is no operation to correct and resubmit it** (needs one in the contract); until then a returned proposal is proposed afresh.
- ST-ADM-015 (decision, kinds): MERGE (2+ old units into one), SPLIT (one into 2+ existing units, a default successor or a mapping of every member and project), DISSOLVE (units into one that takes over, old units Dissolved) and RENAME (one into one, usually a new unit). TRANSFER is in the contract enum and is refused (422): it is the move of a unit, `moveOrganizationUnit` of ADM-01. A new unit is only the sole successor (its id does not exist at proposal, so a mapping cannot name it); a split goes to existing (possibly Planned) units.
- ST-ADM-015 (decision, what moves): every live member of the old units (Active, Suspended and Invited: a member reactivated or accepted later must not appear in a unit that no longer exists; an Ended membership stays as history), every grant over an old unit **of anyone** (not only of the members who move): UNIT_SUBTREE and UNIT_ONLY scopes, with the old unit replaced by the successor (the sole one, else the default, else all); a grant of another kind (TENANT, PROJECT, CONTRACT) is untouched. The old grant Expires (`effective_until` = the moment it is replaced, never before it began); the new one starts then (a grant is never backdated, even for a reorganisation dated earlier). Projects not CLOSED move; closed ones stay with the old unit as history. Documents, tasks and other records keep pointing at the old unit (the succession table says where it went).
- ST-ADM-015 (open, XM-ADM-02): PRJ does not accept a transfer from a service actor with the reorganisation's id, so the port is unbound in production (`UnboundProjectTransferPort`, 503 `PROJECT_TRANSFER_UNAVAILABLE`) and the saga **stops at the first project**: a unit that holds projects is not reorganised until PRJ binds the port (`reorganizationProjects` of the worker dependencies). The tests use a recording double that moves the owner. The port is answerable for the transfer: the step does not look again at who owns the project, and the key `reorg:{id}:project:{project}` makes a retry harmless on the PRJ side.
- ST-ADM-015 (gap, ADM-16): the wizard chooses the default successor of a split; it does not map single members or projects (the server supports `mapping.memberships` and `mapping.projects`), and the preview counts the members that move but not the projects (the screen has no project directory read to count them by unit); the server checks the whole mapping on submission and says how many have no successor. The list shows all proposals; it has no filter by state.
- ST-ADM-015 (closed, a race with an invitation): `MemberStore.unitOf` now reads the unit row `for share` (held to the commit of the invitation) and the retirement step looks for live memberships of the old units again after it has locked the row; a person invited in that window stops the saga (Failed, UNIT_VALIDITY_INVALID) and approving it again moves them. Still open: `reactivate` of a membership of a unit that is already retired.
- ST-ADM-015 (gap, notification and names): nothing tells the members that they moved or the approver that a saga failed beyond the alert `ADM_UNIT_REORGANIZATION_FAILED` and the screen; `iam.org_unit.reorganization_applied.v1` and the bulk `iam.access.changed.v1` are events until NOT-004..006 take them. The old units keep their names (the history); the screens that list units show a Superseded unit as it is.
- ST-ADM-015 (shared files touched): `packages/db/src/{unit-reorganization-store,jobs,index}.ts` (+ `packages/db/test/jobs.test.ts`), `packages/kernel/src/{adm-reorganization,index}.ts`, `apps/api/src/workers/workers.ts` (+ the two lists of the jobs in `apps/api/test/workers`), `apps/api/src/modules/adm/index.ts`, `Design/MACHVON_Design/03_Engineering/{openapi,error_codes}.json` and the generated contracts (new code `PROJECT_TRANSFER_UNAVAILABLE`), the authorization matrix (`proposeUnitReorganization`, `decideUnitReorganization`, fixture `reorganizationId`, the state hash) and the isolation suite (`REORGANIZATION_FIXTURES`), `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts` and the two i18n catalogues (`adm.reorg.*`), `alerts/rules`, `docs/runbooks`.
- ST-ADM-014 (decision, CR-ADM-15): `openapi.json` had no read for `delegation_grant`, which ADM-04 needs; `get_resources_delegation_grant` and `get_resources_delegation_grant_id` were added in place (as CR-ADM-06 did), permission `iam.delegation.read`. Without that right on the whole tenant a caller reads only the delegations they gave or received, so the right is needed to read even one's own; the delegate of a role that has only the approval right sees what was delegated to them through getMyAccess.
- ST-ADM-014 (decision, who delegates for whom): the caller is the delegator (the schema of `DelegationCreate` has no delegator field). ADM 3.5 also lets the tenant administrator delegate for an absent delegator with a document; that needs a new field and a document reference in the contract and is not built. Revoking another person's delegation needs `iam.delegation.create` AND `iam.delegation.read` on the whole tenant (a leader with a tenant-wide approval role does not become an administrator by holding the create right alone).
- ST-ADM-014 (decision, what is delegated): only business permissions the delegator holds with their own grants, over a scope their grants cover (a unit or a unit with the ones below it); `iam.*`, `adm.*` and a bare `*` are refused by the rule (the `system.*` permissions are the platform operators' and a tenant delegator does not hold them). Only `approval.decide` and `doc.outgoing.sign` can be delegated (kernel `DELEGABLE_PERMISSIONS`): they are the ones something reads (the approval engine with the door of the decision; the signing authority check of ST-DOC-007, where the delegate must hold their own signing grant, the delegation being the authority to sign for the delegator); another permission is 422 `UNSUPPORTED` until a consumer reads delegations for it. A new consumer adds its permission to that list. The scope of a delegation is stored as `{kind, ref_ids}` and the approval engine and the signing check read `UNIT_ONLY` and `UNIT_SUBTREE` against the unit of the approval; a `PROJECT` scope (which the contract enum allows) is refused with 422 until they can match it, and `CONTRACT` is not in the contract.
- ST-ADM-014 (decision, start): a delegation may start up to 5 minutes in the past (the clock of the client); it is Active at once when it starts then and Scheduled when later, and the job puts a Scheduled one in force within a minute of its first instant.
- ST-ADM-014 (decision, the job): `delegation-lifecycle` ends a delegation when its delegator's grants no longer cover what it passed on (the same `delegatorHolds` test as the command, so a revoked or lapsed source grant, a lapsed membership or a unit that was removed ends it within a minute). A decision never relies on the job: `ApprovalStore.decide` judges the delegation and the delegator's grants live. The list of the delegator is therefore at most a minute behind the truth.
- ST-ADM-014 (gap, document prefix and notification): the prefix "TUQ." of a text issued under a delegation waits for CXN-DOC-01 (DOC); only `on_behalf_of_id` and `delegation_id` are recorded. Nothing tells the delegate that they received a delegation, or that it ended, until NOT-004..006 take `iam.delegation.created.v1`, `.revoked.v1` and `.ended.v1`.
- ST-ADM-014 (gap, directory rights): the form of ADM-04 chooses the delegate and the unit from the member directory and the unit list (`get_resources_membership`, `get_resources_organization_unit`), which need their own read rights; a leader who may delegate (`iam.delegation.create`) but cannot read them sees the form say so (`adm.delegation.form.lookupFailed`) and cannot complete it. The tenant gives the roles that delegate (and the operators of ADM-18, whose reassignment dialog reads the same directory) the right to read the directory and the units; a lookup that needs no such right (a delegate picker served by the delegation permission) is a contract change not made here.
- ST-ADM-014 (gap, observability): `delegation_active_total` and `delegation_source_ended_total` are emitted by the job with the sink of the workers (no production sink yet, see the note of ST-ADM-013); no alert rule was added because a delegation that ends by itself is a normal event, not a failure.
- ST-ADM-014 (shared files touched): `packages/db/src/{delegation-store,jobs,index}.ts` (+ `packages/db/test/jobs.test.ts`), `packages/kernel/src/{adm-delegation,index}.ts`, `apps/api/src/workers/workers.ts` (+ the two lists of the jobs in `apps/api/test/workers`), `apps/api/src/modules/adm/index.ts`, `Design/MACHVON_Design/03_Engineering/openapi.json` and the generated contracts, the authorization matrix (`createDelegation`, `revokeDelegation`, fixture `delegationId`, the state hash) and the isolation suite (`DELEGATION_FIXTURES`), `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts` and the two i18n catalogues (`adm.delegation.*`), `docs/runbooks/jobs.md`.
- ST-ADM-013 (shared files touched): `packages/db/src/{approval-store,approval-sla-job,jobs,index}.ts` (+ `packages/db/test/jobs.test.ts`), `packages/kernel/src/{adm-workflow,index}.ts`, `apps/api/src/workers/workers.ts` (+ the two lists of the jobs in `apps/api/test/workers`), `apps/api/src/modules/adm/approval.controller.ts`, the authorization matrix (`cancelWorkflowInstance`, `reassignApprovalTask`, fixture `workflowInstanceId`) and the isolation suite, `apps/web/src/routes/screens/index.ts` and the two i18n catalogues (`adm.monitor.*`), `alerts/rules`, `docs/runbooks`.

### ST-SAS-011 (billing run and e-invoice, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-011 (decision, CR-SAS-14): `openapi.json` had no read of invoices, which SAS-09 needs; `listTenantInvoices` (`GET /system/tenants/{tenantId}/invoices`, `system.tenant.read`) and the schemas `Invoice`, `InvoiceLine`, `TenantInvoices` were added, and the generic reads `get_resources_invoice_line(_id)` now return `InvoiceLine`. The owner of SAS.md should add them to the operation list of 3.7.
- ST-SAS-011 (decision, billing period): `runBillingCycle` bills the subscriptions whose period is exactly the one named; the daily job bills the period that closed last. SAS.md says "mỗi (subscription, period)" without saying whether the plan fee is in advance or in arrears; arrears was chosen because the use of the period is part of the invoice. A business owner should confirm.
- ST-SAS-011 (gap, overage price): the price of a unit beyond the limit of a METERED feature is the new column `plan_feature.overage_price_vnd`, and the plan catalogue contract (`PlanFeature`, ST-SAS-007) does not carry it, so a plan made through the API has none and its use is never billed. A change request on the plan catalogue (SAS-06) must add the field to the schema, the validation and the screen.
- ST-SAS-011 (gap, buyer and VAT): the buyer snapshot is the code and name of the tenant; the tax code, address and e-mail of the buyer (needed by the provider) are not modelled (CXN-INV-02), and the VAT category of every line is the plan's (default NOT_SUBJECT, CXN-INV-01). The real provider adapter stays PROPOSED until a contract exists (D05/D06); the MOCK works the number out from the reference key (the same in every process), but its numbers are not those of a tax authority.
- ST-SAS-011 (gap, sending and payment): Issued -> Sent (the e-mail and the lookup link to the buyer), the payment webhook and the match of payments (`matchBillingPayment`, `receiveEInvoiceWebhook`: ST-SAS-012 and later), the adjustment and replacement invoices (SAS-24) and the reminders to the Tenant Owner at 1/15/30 days past due (NOT-004..006) are not in this story. PastDue goes back to Active only when the payment story does it.
- ST-SAS-011 (gap, metrics sink): the gauge `einvoice_issue_failed_invoices` is sent to the same sink as the other job gauges (ST-KERNEL-050 binds it in bootstrap).

### ST-SAS-012 (payment webhooks, inbox and reconciliation, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-012 (decision, CR-SAS-15): `openapi.json` had no read of the payments that wait, which SAS-10 needs; `listBillingPayments` (`GET /system/payments`, `system.tenant.read`) and the schemas `BillingPayment`, `BillingPaymentList` were added, and `Invoice` now has `payments`. The owner of SAS.md should add them to the operation list of 3.7.
- ST-SAS-012 (decision, where a payment lives): SAS.md says the inbox row is written under PLATFORM_OPERATOR and moves to the target tenant when matched. The inbox row stays with the platform tenant (it is the record of what the provider told us); the **payment** is the thing that moves: it is recorded under the platform tenant and `billing_payment_adopt` (a security-definer function, the only way to delete a payment) replaces it with the tenant's row (`source_inbox_event_id` keeps the link) in one step, so that `UNIQUE (provider, provider_txn_ref)` holds for the whole platform.
- ST-SAS-012 (gap, partial and excess payments): a payment settles an invoice only for exactly what is outstanding (SAS.md: "số tiền phải khớp số còn phải thu"). A part payment, an over payment or one payment for two invoices waits as PendingReview for ever: the `PartiallyPaid` state of the invoice and the reversal link (`reversal_of_id`, state Reversed) exist in the tables and have no operation yet. The "maker-checker for a difference above 0 VND" of the summary line of `matchBillingPayment` is not built: a difference is refused, not approved. A business owner should say what a person may accept (a tolerance, a bank fee) and who approves it.
- ST-SAS-012 (gap, real providers): the three providers are MOCK. The signature scheme is the platform's timestamped HMAC-SHA256 (`X-Signature`, `X-Timestamp`); a real bank or gateway signs its own way (a scheme per provider, as the channel adapters have one), and the keys come from the environment until the vault of the platform exists (ST-KERNEL, `MACHVON_SECRET_PLATFORM_WEBHOOKS_<PROVIDER>`).
- ST-SAS-012 (gap, e-invoice callback): it is applied while the callback is answered and again on every redelivery, not by a worker; an invoice that is not committed yet (the provider answered faster than the transaction) is a 404 and the provider's retry finds it. A provider that never retries would leave the invoice PendingIssue until the worker's own tries end (IssueFailed) and a person issues it again, which then finds the invoice at the provider by the reference key.
- ST-SAS-012 (gap, reminders): the reminders to the Tenant Owner at 1, 15 and 30 days past due and the notice when a payment is matched go through the notification core (NOT-004..006); the events `sas.payment.matched.v1`, `sas.invoice.overdue.v1` and `sas.subscription.recovered.v1` are published for it.

### ST-SAS-013 (adjustment and replacement invoices, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-013 (decision, receivable): SAS.md 3.7 item 4 gives the receivable as "the originals not replaced + the adjustments + the replacements - what was paid". Taken literally, an original that was adjusted and then replaced would count its adjustment on top of its replacement. The adjustments of an invoice that was replaced are left out (the replacement is the whole invoice again, with what they said in it); an accountant should confirm (CXN-INV-02).
- ST-SAS-013 (decision, adjustment limits): an adjustment may not take more than the invoice still stands for (its total and the adjustments issued so far) and must change something; a replacement must be for more than nothing, so that replacing is not cancelling. The contract names 422 `EINVOICE_ADJUSTMENT_INVALID` for these and gives no more; the problem carries the reason and the amount the invoice stands for. A correction of an adjustment, and an adjustment of a replaced invoice, are refused (422).
- ST-SAS-013 (decision, payments of corrected invoices): the reconciliation settles an **Adjusted** invoice for what its adjustments left of it (its total plus the adjustments issued or waiting for the provider, less what was paid), and it stays Adjusted (the table lets it go to Replaced only); a **replacement** is paid for its own total less what was paid on the invoices it replaced, back along the chain; an adjustment is never paid by itself (it is folded into the invoice it adjusts) and a note that names one, or a replaced invoice, goes to a person. Still open: an Adjusted invoice is never marked Overdue by the sweep (the table lets Adjusted go to Replaced only), so a subscription whose only unpaid invoice is adjusted does not become past due; the status chain of SAS.md 3.7 would have to allow Adjusted to Overdue.
- ST-SAS-013 (gap, tax decree): the fields the correction asks for (reason, the agreement document) and the rule that a buyer's agreement is needed are placeholders for CXN-INV-02 until an accountant confirms the text of the decree; the provider adapter has no call for the correction yet (the MOCK issues it like any invoice), so the link of a correction to the original at the tax authority is not sent.
- ST-SAS-013 (observability): no new failure mode needs an alert: a correction is a command with a refusal, and what happens to its invoice afterwards is the `SAS_EINVOICE_ISSUE_FAILED` alert of the issue.

### ST-SAS-015 (controlled support access, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-015 (decision, the token): the story assumes the special token `act=support` is issued through the IdP; the platform issues it itself, through `issueSupportAccessToken` (an operation added to the contract for it: `POST /support-access/{id}/token`, control plane, `system.support.request`), because the tenant token issuer is the only one whose tokens the tenant plane verifies. The operation is not in SAS.md 5 (the table of operations); the change request is to add it there. A token lasts at most five minutes and ends with its grant.
- ST-SAS-015 (decision, who may approve): SAS.md names TENANT_OWNER and TENANT_ADMIN. TENANT_OWNER holds the permission through its patterns; TENANT_ADMIN gets `tenant.support.approve` and `support_access_grant.read` in the seed and by V1489 for the tenants that exist. A write needs two different people: with one owner and no administrator a tenant cannot give a write access, on purpose.
- ST-SAS-015 (decision, lapse and start): a request nobody decided on within seven days lapses (Expired); an approval may put the start of the access at most seven days ahead; the window is fixed by the approval that completes the grant, so the minutes asked for count from the start. SAS.md fixes none of these; they are the smallest values that keep an old request from being approved by mistake.
- ST-SAS-015 (decision, scope): a grant names modules by the first tag of the operations in `openapi.json` (generated as `MODULES`) and may name resources: a request then reaches only a path that holds one of the ids (a list is out of reach). An operation without a tag is out of reach of every grant. The scope of `resource_ids` is the path, not the rows: a read that returns other rows of the same module through a filter is not narrowed.
- ST-SAS-015 (gap, the operator's reads): the operator (control plane) cannot list the grants of a tenant (the reads are the tenant's), so SAS-19 shows them the request form and the answer of the command only; a read for the control plane would need a change request.
- ST-SAS-015 (gap, notification): SAS.md says the banner tells the Tenant Admin; the screen shows it, but no notification is sent when a request arrives or a grant starts (NOT-004..006 own the delivery). `sas.support_access.requested.v1` is emitted for it.
- ST-SAS-015 (observability): the gauge `support_access_active` and the alert `SAS_SUPPORT_ACCESS_ACTIVE`; the counter `authz_decision_total{reason="SUPPORT_GRANT"}` counts the support tokens the grant did not let in.

### ST-SAS-016 (tenant data export and offboarding, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-016 (decision, the window): SAS.md 3.2 gives `contract_end_date` and `export_window_days` (30–180, default 90 [assumption]) and says "trong cửa sổ" without saying when the window starts. It starts at the later of the end of the contract (the end of that day, UTC) and the moment offboarding starts, so a tenant is not cut off from its data before its contract ends and one whose contract ended long ago still gets the whole window. `resumeTenant` brings an Offboarding tenant back only inside the window.
- ST-SAS-016 (decision, the version of the export): the contract asks `post_tenants_id_export` for an If-Match and a tenant has no version it can read. The If-Match is the number of exports the caller has seen (`exports_version` of `listTenantExports`), so a double submit by two people is a 409 `VERSION_CONFLICT`. A change request should say so in SAS.md 5.
- ST-SAS-016 (decision, delivered): "tải xong → Delivered" is read as every file of the manifest, the manifest included, downloaded once through its link (the bytes went out); a link lives five minutes and is asked for again each time, the export itself 72 hours.
- ST-SAS-016 (decision, what is exported): every table with row-level security and a `tenant_id`, found in the catalogue at the time of the export, so a table added later is exported without a change; never the stored answers of commands, the events not yet relayed, the sessions and devices of people or the export jobs; columns whose name says secret, password, private key, token hash, token fingerprint or API key are left out and listed in the manifest. BUSINESS_DATA_ONLY also leaves out the tables of access, audit, notifications and legal holds (a prefix list in `sas-offboarding.ts`); the business owner should confirm the split.
- ST-SAS-016 (decision, the operations): four operations were added to the contract because the story's screen cannot work without them and the story lists none: `listTenantExports`, `getTenantExportManifest`, `downloadTenantExportFile` (a signed link, as the report export does it) and `listSystemTenantExports` (the state of the leaving tenant for the System Admin, with the legal holds in force). The change request is to add them to SAS.md 5.
- ST-SAS-016 (gap, JSONL only): the format is `JSONL_CSV_BUNDLE` but only JSON lines is written; the CSV of each table is not (it would double the bundle). The attachments are copied into the export (so they can be encrypted); a very large tenant is not handled: an export larger than 512 MiB ends as Failed (`ExportTooLarge`) because a file is kept in memory while it is made. Streaming and parts are the next step.
- ST-SAS-016 (gap, System Admin exports): SAS.md 3.10 says TENANT_OWNER or System Admin may ask; the operation is on the tenant plane (`tenant.export`), and a platform operator cannot reach the data of a tenant except through a support grant, which never gives `tenant.export`. A System Admin who must export for a tenant whose owner is gone needs a change request (a control-plane operation, with the consent and the audit of BR-SAS-16).
- ST-SAS-016 (gap, reminders and purge): the reminders to the owner at day 0, 30, 60 and seven days before the window ends (the `TenantOffboardingWorkflow`) are the notifications' (NOT-004..006); the event `sas.tenant.status_changed.v1` carries what they need but no reminder is sent. There is no operation or job that purges an archived tenant: the retention job purges by policy and skips legal holds, and removing the rest is a manual procedure with a deletion certificate (docs/sas-offboarding.md).
- ST-SAS-016 (observability): the gauge `tenant_export_pending` and the alert `SAS_TENANT_EXPORT_PENDING`; the audit actions `tenant.export_*`, `tenant.offboarding_started`, `tenant.archived`.

### ST-SAS-017 (tenant domains with DNS verification, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-017 (mock, DNS and certificates): the DNS lookup (`DomainProofPort`) and the certificate authority (`CertificatePort`, the plan's `AcmePort`) are the MOCK pair `sharedMockDomainPorts()` (`packages/db/src/domain-ports.ts`), the same for the commands and the job in one process; a real resolver (DNS over HTTPS or the system resolver) and an ACME client are to be bound (D05/D06) before a production domain is attached. Until then no domain is really proved.
- ST-SAS-017 (assumption, TLS lifetime): the authority port does not report when a certificate ends, so `TLS_LIFETIME_DAYS = 90` is assumed [assumption]; the renewal at 14 days left follows from it.
- ST-SAS-017 (decision, the hold): "giữ chỗ 90 ngày" is read as the hostname being held against every tenant, the one that released it included, so a name released by mistake is not taken by a second add within 90 days. UAT-AC152 step 5 has "B thêm lại" (another tenant), which this satisfies; holding it against the same tenant too is the stricter reading.
- ST-SAS-017 (decision, the token): the TXT value is `machvon-verify=<token>` at `_machvon-verify.<hostname>`; the platform keeps only the SHA-256 of the token and shows it once. A lost value is recovered by adding the same hostname again: the tenant's own custom domain that waits for its proof, or Failed, gets a new token and a new week (the old token stops working); a separate "show the challenge" operation was not in the story and is not added.
- ST-SAS-017 (decision, the sign-in): the web sign-in is not in this repository yet; the selection takes the host the page was opened on from the header `X-Tenant-Host` (and `host` on the candidates), a hostname the caller states, because the API cannot see the browser's address behind the gateway. A host proves nothing about the caller: it only narrows which tenant they may choose, so a wrong host makes the choice fail, never widens it. The web sign-in sends it from the address of the page (`globalThis.location.hostname`; a host that is not a name, such as localhost, chooses nothing); the mobile sign-in has no host and sends none.
- ST-SAS-017 (decision, the operations): `listTenantDomains` was added to the contract because screen SAS-05 cannot work without a list and the story names three commands only; the `host` query of the candidates, `preselected` and the header `X-Tenant-Host` of the selection serve the second acceptance criterion.
- ST-SAS-017 (decision, the provisioning path): `TenantRegistry.ensureDomain` (the DNS_SUBDOMAIN step) refuses a name that **another** tenant released less than 90 days ago (409 `DOMAIN_TAKEN`, `RESERVED`) but lets the tenant that released it take it back, so a provisioning run again after its compensation still works. The `add` command holds the name against the same tenant too (see the decision on the hold).
- ST-SAS-017 (decision, a resolver that fails): a lookup that throws (timeout, outage) is not a proof that is gone: the weekly recheck leaves the domain as it is and does not move its time of last check, so the next pass looks again; only an answer without the token fails a domain. `verifyTenantDomain` treats a failed lookup like a missing record (422 `DOMAIN_UNVERIFIED`) and does not fail the domain.
- ST-SAS-017 (gap, the platform domain): `MACHVON_PLATFORM_DOMAIN` (default `machvon.vn`) tells which names are the platform's own subdomains; the list of reserved labels (`www`, `api`, `admin`…) is a short fixed list in `sas-domain.ts` and is not configurable.
- ST-SAS-017 (observability): the gauge `tenant_domain_failed_total` and the alert `SAS_DOMAIN_FAILED`; the audit actions `tenant.domain_*`.

### ST-NOT-004 (event catalogue and notification workflow designer, slice NOT-1)
- ST-NOT-004 (gap, the minimum set of event types): the plan names `report.*`, `approval.*`, `guarantee.expiring`, `explanation.requested` and `deadline.near` as the first types. The catalogue holds the nine that the modules registered in their migrations (`ctr.guarantee.expiring.v1`, `doc.incoming_overdue.v1`, `bid.petition.overdue.v1`…). The `report.*` and `adm.approval_task.*` events exist but their payloads do not carry the `notification` member (docs/notification-event-types.md §2: resource, deep link, recipients), so registering them with a schema here would either accept payloads the core cannot use or refuse the ones the modules send. Their owners register them when they make the events notifiable; `explanation.requested` and `deadline.near` have no emitter yet.
- ST-NOT-004 (decision, publish body): `post_notification_workflows_id_publish` keeps the `Decision` body of the contract (the same as the replay of a notification): `decision` must be `approve`, the reason is audited. The reason for overriding the quiet hours is part of the definition (`quietHoursOverrideReason`), not of the publication.
- ST-NOT-004 (decision, the business type): a notification workflow's business type is its event type (the unique indexes of `workflow_version` then give one line of revisions and one Published revision for each event type); `business_type` in the draft is optional and must say the same.
- ST-NOT-004 (decision, the catalogue is read through a function): `notification_event_type` has no right for the application role, so the tenant plane reads the catalogue through `notification_event_catalogue()` (V1442), which gives everything but the payload schema.
- ST-NOT-004 (gap, templates): the contract lists `TEMPLATE_INVALID` for the publication, but the templates are the next story (ST-NOT-005): until then a channel other than the inbox only has to name a template code of a valid shape. NOT-005 adds the check that the template exists, is Active and fits the channel and the classification of the event type.
- ST-NOT-004 (gap, no edit of a draft): the story names a create and a publish only; a draft is replaced by writing another one. An `updateNotificationWorkflowDraft` is not added.
- ST-NOT-004 (observability): no gauge or alert (writing and publishing a workflow is interactive and has no failure that needs paging); the audit actions `notification.workflow_created` and `notification.workflow_published` and the event `not.workflow.published.v1` carry the tenant and the ids, never the definition.

### ST-NOT-005 (multi-channel templates with approval and test send, slice NOT-1)
- ST-NOT-005 (decision, a test is not a notification): the spec says a test send is `is_test = true` and not counted in the metrics. Making a test a `notification_instance` would need a workflow version, an outbox event with sample variables and the metrics of the delivery engine (twenty places) to know about tests. A test send is therefore a one-off call of the channel adapter after the commit, with an audit row of numbers only: nothing is created, so nothing is counted, and the column `is_test` stays for the campaigns' own dry runs.
- ST-NOT-005 (gap, push and inbox tests): an inbox or push template is rendered and judged by a test send, not delivered: the inbox has no row for a test and a push needs a registered device (ST-NOT-006, ST-NOT-007).
- ST-NOT-005 (gap, provider templates): a ZNS or WhatsApp template can be written and sent to review (`PendingProvider`) but the registration with the provider and its approval by webhook are the external-channel story (R3); until then `decideTemplateRevision` answers 409 `TEMPLATE_PROVIDER_PENDING` and a provider template is not tested.
- ST-NOT-005 (decision, the list of test recipients): "danh sách thử của tenant" had no operation; two were added (`listNotificationTestRecipients`, `setNotificationTestRecipients`) with a table (V1443). The screen adds a person by the id of their membership: a picker that searches members needs a members read the template managers do not hold.
- ST-NOT-005 (decision, the template check of a workflow): the check that a workflow names a template that exists and is Active for its channel (`TEMPLATE_INVALID` on publication, ST-NOT-004) is now possible; wiring it into the publication of a workflow is left to the story that first sends from a workflow template, so no workflow that works today is refused by a change in this story.
- ST-NOT-005 (assumption, the variables): the schema of a variable is `{ type, classification }` with the type `money` for an amount of money; the contract left `variable_schema` a free object. Existing templates written before this story (the delivery tests') have no classification and are not re-judged; only a revision written or tested now is.
- ST-NOT-005 (observability): no gauge or alert (writing, reviewing and testing a template is interactive); the audit actions `notification.template_created|submitted|decided`, `notification.test_sent` and `notification.test_recipients_set` carry ids and counts, never the content.

### ST-NOT-006 (push device registration, slice NOT-1)
- ST-NOT-006 (mock, the vault): the push tokens are kept by `InMemoryPushTokenVault` (MOCK) under `secret://push/{tenant}/{device}`; the vault of the platform (OpenBao) replaces it by providing `PUSH_TOKEN_VAULT`. A process restart loses the tokens until then, and the apps register again at their next start.
- ST-NOT-006 (gap, mobile): the Flutter app (the permission prompt MOB-05, the refresh of the token, the cleanup on a change of tenant) is not in the repository: there is no `apps/mobile`. The mobile tasks of this story are DONE_PENDING_ENV with the mobile stories (MOB-001..); the generated client `packages/mobile-api-client` already has `registerDevice`.
- ST-NOT-006 (gap, orphan tokens): the token is removed from the vault after the commit of the revocation, when the reference has already left the row. A vault that fails at that moment leaves a token that nothing refers to. A sweep of the vault against the live references (and the alert for it) belongs to the story that wires the real vault.
- ST-NOT-006 (decision, token rotation): V1445 lets `token_fingerprint` change while a device is live; V1242 held it as part of what a device is, which made a refreshed token impossible to record.
- ST-NOT-006 (decision, the tenant switch): the server cannot know the tenant a person switches from, so the client revokes the device of the tenant it leaves (`revokeMyDevice`) before registering in the next; the same device is a registration for each membership.
- ST-NOT-006 (observability): no gauge or alert; the audit actions `device.registered|updated|revoked` carry the platform and the permission state, never the token, its fingerprint or the public id.

### ST-NOT-007 (FCM and APNs adapters, slice NOT-1)
- ST-NOT-007 (mock, unverified): `PushChannelAdapter` over `PushProviderPort` follows the public documentation of FCM and APNs, and the only ports are `MockPushProvider` (MOCK, registered outside production only). The adapters stay MOCK/unverified until a sandbox spike against the real providers (credentials, FCM v1 HTTP, APNs HTTP/2 with the token key); the lists of error codes in `push-port.ts` are to be checked in that spike.
- ST-NOT-007 (gap, no receipts): a push provider answers "accepted" only, so a push stays `ProviderAccepted`; there is no delivery receipt and nothing marks the notification read. `lookup` is `UNDETERMINED`.
- ST-NOT-007 (gap, mobile): the Flutter handlers of the silent message (read `/me/entitlements` again) and of the opaque link are not in the repository (no `apps/mobile`); they are DONE_PENDING_ENV with the mobile stories.
- ST-NOT-007 (decision, no variable in a push): a push template uses no variable at all, even a public one, instead of allowing the public and internal ones: a lock screen is read by anyone near the phone, and the only text that is certain to be safe is a finished sentence. SMS keeps its public and internal variables. The dispatcher does not fill any variable of a push either, so a template that predates the rule cannot leak one.
- ST-NOT-007 (gap, one account per channel): the silent push uses the first Active `PUSH_FCM`/`PUSH_APNS` channel account of the tenant; a tenant with several accounts for one provider is served by one of them.
- ST-NOT-007 (observability): `push_devices_revoked_total{reason}` and `push_silent_total{channel, outcome}` are incremented; no alert is written for them.

### ST-OMNI-001 (channel accounts, slice OMNI-1)
- ST-OMNI-001 (mock, the providers): the only `ChannelVerifierPort` implementations are `MockChannelVerifier` (outside production) and `UnconfiguredChannelVerifier` (production refuses every account with `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`). Real verifiers (credential check, webhook registration, challenge) wait for the contracts with the providers (D06); the secrets are read from the vault by the verifier, which is the vault of the platform (OpenBao) once it is deployed.
- ST-OMNI-001 (gap, credentials): the story connects accounts by the reference of a secret that is already in the vault; nothing here writes the secret there (OMNI-05 has no field for the secret itself, on purpose). Rotating a credential is putting a new value under the same reference and verifying the account again. A screen or command that stores the secret belongs with the vault.
- ST-OMNI-001 (gap, crash between question and record): if the process stops after the inbox row of the verification request and before the answer is recorded, the account stays pending with `verify_requested_at` set and no worker looks at it again. OMNI-06 hides Verify while a request waits, and offers it again once the request is older than a minute (the command renews it). A sweep that re-sends such requests by itself belongs with the real verifiers.
- ST-OMNI-001 (decision, no re-enable): a Disabled account is final (OMNI 4 has no way back); a new account is connected. Disabling does not Suppress the queued attempts in the same transaction: the dispatcher does it at their turn (`CHANNEL_ACCOUNT_DISABLED`), so a command that closes an account with a long queue stays short.
- ST-OMNI-001 (gap, one external id for two tenants): the unique index is per tenant. The lookup of the webhook (`channel_accounts_by_external_id`) answers only Active accounts, so two tenants that both verify the same external id make a receipt for it ambiguous; verification does not check across tenants (it cannot see them, and saying so would tell a tenant that another uses the id). The webhook already refuses an ambiguous receipt.
- ST-OMNI-001 (gap, other omni screens): OMNI-01..04 and OMNI-07..11 (conversations, contacts, consents, routing policies, the inbox of the integration) and the rest of OMNI are later stories; only the accounts are here. The conversations will have to check the account at send time as the delivery engine does.
- ST-OMNI-001 (observability): alerts `OMNI_CHANNEL_CREDENTIAL_EXPIRED` and `OMNI_CHANNEL_VERIFICATION_FAILED` (events, with the tenant, channel and account id; no external id, no secret); the metric `notification_delivery_attempts_total{outcome=credential_expired}`; audit actions `omni.channel_account_connected|updated|disabled|verification_requested|verified|verification_failed`.

### ST-MOB-005 (offline command queue, server side, slice MOB-1)
- ST-MOB-005 (open, DONE_PENDING_ENV): the app (the encrypted local store, the queue with QUEUED/SENDING/ACCEPTED/REJECTED/CONFLICT/UNKNOWN/DISCARDED, retry with backoff, MOB-41 and MOB-43) and the tests on a device (kill the network at each step: UAT-AC502 on a phone) are not in this repository (there is no `apps/mobile`). The server side is built and tested: batches, receipts, lookup, retention.
- ST-MOB-005 (decision, the batch calls the platform's own API): each queued command is sent through the public API of the server itself, with the credentials of the batch and the key of the command, instead of calling the stores of five modules from one controller. So every rule of the operation applies and none is copied, at the price of a call to itself per command (up to 50 per batch, each in its own transaction). The batch holds one connection while it runs, so the batches in flight are limited by slots (`SYNC_MAX_CONCURRENT_BATCHES`, default 3 for a pool of 10; the setting has to stay under half the pool, nothing checks it against the real pool size). The address is the local port of the connection, never the Host header. `SYNC_OPERATION_INVOKER` can be replaced by an in-process dispatcher if the cost shows.
- ST-MOB-005 (gap, five command types): `report.submit`, `report.draft.save`, `issue.create`, `site_diary.append`, `measurement.record` only (the enum of the contract). The payload of each is the body of its operation; a command the operation refuses is `rejected` with its code. The tests use stand-ins for the operations (the contract-valid bodies of `createIssue` and `recordMeasurement` pass the real request validation; the operations themselves are tested in their own stories).
- ST-MOB-005 (gap, `server_copy`): a conflict answers the `server_version` and the code only. The app reads the server copy through the read operation of the module (the contract has an optional `server_copy` that is never filled).
- ST-MOB-005 (gap, UNKNOWN metrics and alert): the queue length and the UNKNOWN rate are states of the phone, which the server cannot see; the server counts the answers (`sync.batch_submitted` audit with counts, `sync.command_*` events). The alert on a high UNKNOWN rate waits for the app to report it.
- ST-MOB-005 (decision, no If-Match): `post_sync_batches` and `post_sync_attachments` take no `If-Match` (they change no record that has a version); the parameter is removed from the contract (compat-allow, ST-MOB-005/009).

### ST-MOB-009 (chunked attachment upload, server side, slice MOB-1)
- ST-MOB-009 (open, DONE_PENDING_ENV): the chunk uploader of the app (persisted progress, resume, compression, the size and type checks on the phone, MOB-19) and the tests on a phone (network flapping, app killed mid-upload) are not in this repository. The server side is built and tested.
- ST-MOB-009 (mock, the antivirus): the scan is `MockScanPort` (the EICAR string) and runs inside the completion command. With a real scanner (ClamAV) a large file would hold the transaction for the scan; the completion should then move the attachment to SCANNING and return, with a consumer that scans and settles it (the status path and the event are already shaped for it).
- ST-MOB-009 (gap, abandoned uploads): an upload that is never completed stays UPLOADING and its chunks stay in the storage. A job that removes the uploads older than a day and their chunks is not written.
- ST-MOB-009 (decision, links of 5 minutes): the presign lifetime is capped at 5 minutes by the kernel, so the links of the missing chunks are asked for again (`get_sync_attachments_id`) as often as an upload takes longer than that; there is no operation to send a chunk through the authenticated API (the signed link is the way).
- ST-MOB-009 (gap, memory): the file is put together in memory (at most 50 MiB) when it is completed; the storage port has no streaming read.
- ST-MOB-009 (gap, linking): `SyncAttachmentStore.linkableKey` is the guard; no module takes a phone attachment yet (the report, issue and diary operations of the five command types take file versions of DOC), so nothing calls it outside the tests.
- ST-MOB-009 (decision, no If-Match): `post_sync_attachments` and `post_sync_attachments_id_complete` take no `If-Match` (compat-allow ST-MOB-005/009).

### ST-NOT-010 (channel limits, cost and overview, slice NOT-1)
- ST-NOT-010 (assumption, prices): the price of a message is the constant `CHANNEL_UNIT_COST_VND` (SMS 800, ZNS 300, WhatsApp 1,000 VND) until the price lists of the providers are in the SAS plan; the budget is a budget of estimates. The meter events go to SAS already (`notification.email|sms|zns`), so the billing side sees the count.
- ST-NOT-010 (gap, fallback): the spec lets a message over its limit fall back to a channel that costs nothing; here it waits for the next day or month. The fallback belongs with the channel chain of the workflow (NOT-06).
- ST-NOT-010 (gap, no WhatsApp/Zalo meters): the meter catalogue has e-mail, SMS and ZNS only; WhatsApp is priced for the budget but not metered in SAS.
- ST-NOT-010 (decision, the period of the overview): attempts are placed in the period by when they were made (`created_at`), not by when they ended; an attempt made at the end of the period and not ended yet is not in it, and appears in the next read.
- ST-NOT-010 (observability): the attempts held by a limit are counted by `notification_delivery_attempts_total{outcome=held_by_limit}`; no alert is written for a budget that is used up or a queue that waits for it (the figures are on NOT-04/NOT-15).
- ST-NOT-010 (mock, SMS): `SmsHoursAdapter` is the only SMS adapter; the sending hours are the working assumption of 07:00 to 21:00 (CXN-SPAM-01 pending), not configurable per tenant.

### ST-NOT-009 (broadcast campaigns, slice NOT-1)
- ST-NOT-009 (assumption, from the plan): every campaign goes through approval whatever its size; needs the entitlement `notification.campaign` (grace and expired deny `manage` and `approve`; the reads stay open).
- ST-NOT-009 (assumption, size): a campaign has at most 5,000 recipients, because its notifications are made in the transaction of the approval (a bulk insert, not the engine one by one); a wider segment is 409 `SEGMENT_INVALID` and is split into campaigns. A launch in batches by a job is the way to lift it.
- ST-NOT-009 (assumption, validity): a notification of a campaign expires 7 days after the time of the campaign (the spec only says an expired message is not sent); NOT-10 shows it as the time plus 7 days.
- ST-NOT-009 (gap, time zone): the contract has one instant `scheduled_at`; the "by the time zone of the recipient" option of NOT 3.5 needs a field the contract does not have. The preference time zone of a person still moves the sends of a push or an SMS out of their quiet hours.
- ST-NOT-009 (gap, inbox): a campaign on the inbox (IN_APP) is refused (422 `TEMPLATE_INVALID`, field channel): the inbox is delivered by the insert and cannot wait for a time. It needs the notification to be inserted Scheduled and released by a job.
- ST-NOT-009 (decision, scope): the units of a segment are tested against the scope of whoever acts on create, preview, submit and cancel; the decision resolves the same segment without the scope of the approver. A maker who loses a unit after the preview is stopped at the submit, not at the approval.
- ST-NOT-009 (decision, system workflow): the notifications of campaigns hang on one published workflow `campaign.broadcast` per tenant, made by the first approval (published by that approver, written by the maker, so the check of V002 holds). It shows in NOT-06 and gives NOT-03 the category `campaign`, which appears only after the first campaign of the tenant.
- ST-NOT-009 (decision, reads): `notification.campaign.read` is new (the approver needs to see what waits); NOTIFICATION_ADMIN has it through `notification.*`. Whoever lacks the permission on the whole tenant reads the campaigns they made.
- ST-NOT-009 (observability): no metric and no alert for campaigns yet (the trail, the event `not.campaign.approved.v1` and the screens carry them); a campaign stuck in Sending beyond the validity of its notifications is the case to alert on.
- ST-NOT-009 (UI): NOT-09 offers every Active template of an outside channel (all pages are read) and every unit the person can read (`get_resources_organization_unit`; without that permission only roles can be chosen).

### ST-OMNI-002 (contact directory, slice OMNI-1)
- ST-OMNI-002 (decision, shared identity): the spec says two contacts must hold "a verified identity with the same hash", but the table is unique by (tenant, channel, hash), so one identity cannot belong to two contacts. The merge therefore asks that each contact holds a Verified identity with the same hash, on different channels (the same number on the phone of one and on the WhatsApp of the other); the identity named in the body is one of them and the other is found by the hash.
- ST-OMNI-002 (decision, merge keeps identities): a merged contact keeps its identities and its consents, the surviving contact shows them and lookups follow `merged_into_id`; nothing is copied or moved except the conversations. This is what keeps the consents from adding up and what R3's unmerge will need.
- ST-OMNI-002 (gap, sending the code): no SMS or mail provider yet (D06); the sender is a MOCK port outside production and unconfigured in production, where an identity is verified by the provider or by a document. A code is asked for again by adding the same identity again (the contract has no operation to ask for one).
- ST-OMNI-002 (gap, consent lookup): the lookup of the consents of a contact "through the merge chain" belongs to ST-OMNI-003 (the consents of outside contacts); this story leaves the consent rows with their first subject.
- ST-OMNI-002 (decision, supervisor): `ADMIN_DOCUMENT` is allowed to whoever holds `omni.contact.merge` (OMNI_SUPERVISOR); `PROVIDER_VERIFIED` is allowed on Zalo and WhatsApp to whoever manages the directory, as the inbound worker (ST-OMNI-004) is not there yet to write it.
