- ST-OMNI-002 (gap): no operation blocks or unblocks a contact, or revokes an identity (V002 ties Verified to its method and moment, so a Verified identity cannot be Revoked); unmerge is R3.
- ST-OMNI-002 (observability): no metric or alert yet for the directory (the trail and the event `omni.contact.merged.v1` carry the merges); the lock of an identity after five wrong codes is the case to alert on.
- ST-OMNI-002 (decision, scope): the directory is the tenant's (a contact has no unit), so reading and changing it takes the permission on the whole tenant, as for the channel accounts; an agent whose grant is on one unit does not reach it. Letting a unit-scoped agent see the contacts of the conversations of their unit needs a rule on the contacts of a conversation (ST-OMNI-004).

### ST-OMNI-003 (consent of the contacts, slice OMNI-1)
- ST-OMNI-003 (decision, proof): the reference of the proof is required for `CHANNEL_OPT_IN` and `PAPER_SCANNED` (they leave something to show) and optional for `IN_APP` and `WEB_FORM`, where the capture itself is the proof; it stays with the consent and is on neither the trail, the event nor any read.
- ST-OMNI-003 (decision, merge): a consent keeps the contact that gave it; `latestForContact` takes the latest word among the contact and the contacts merged into it. The consents are not added up, and a contact merged away takes none; the survivor does.
- ST-OMNI-003 (gap, sending): the message to a contact (ST-OMNI-005, `sendConversationMessage`) must ask `latestForContact` before it is taken up, and a withdrawal must Suppress the waiting sends; the engine does that for members only today. The STOP/HUY keyword rule waits for the inbound messages (ST-OMNI-004).
- ST-OMNI-003 (gap, expiry): no job expires a consent that has a term (`Expired` exists in the table; nothing sets it for a contact).
- ST-OMNI-003 (decision, scope): the consents, like the directory, are the tenant's: a grant on one unit alone neither takes, withdraws nor reads them (the list is empty for it, as for the consents of members).
- ST-OMNI-003 (observability): no metric or alert yet; the trail and `not.consent.changed.v1` carry the changes.

### ST-OMNI-004 (conversations and assignment, slice OMNI-1)
- ST-OMNI-004 (decision, closed): a Closed conversation is final (OMNI.md 4); a message on its thread opens a new conversation that points to the last closed one (`previous_conversation_id`), and the thread is unique only among the conversations that are not Closed (V1287 replaced the unique index of V002).
- ST-OMNI-004 (decision, resolve): resolving a New or an Assigned conversation is 409 (OMNI.md 4), so the guard allows `resolve` only from InProgress and PendingCustomer. The commands themselves (resolve, reopen, close, open by the agent) are ST-OMNI-005/007.
- ST-OMNI-004 (decision, roles): the agent holds `omni.conversation.reply` and `omni.conversation.convert`, not `omni.conversation.*` (it carried the assignment). OMNI.md 4 says "SUPERVISOR/AGENT (tu nhan)" for the assignment and OMNI.md 2 says reassigning is the supervisor's; the self-claim by an agent is not built and waits for a decision (a permission to claim, or a rule on assigning to oneself).
- ST-OMNI-004 (decision, sender): the identity of a sender is marked `PROVIDER_VERIFIED` on Zalo OA and WhatsApp only (the provider authenticates the sender); a mail and a text message leave it Pending.
- ST-OMNI-004 (decision, name): a contact made by a message takes the name the provider gives, else the masked identity; class `OTHER`.
- ST-OMNI-004 (decision, text): the text of a message is kept in `channel_message.body_text` (up to 20000, classification PERSONAL), read only through `get_resources_channel_message/{id}`; the list carries none. OMNI-01 does not show it: the thread is OMNI-02 (ST-OMNI-005).
- ST-OMNI-004 (gap, queue): `queue_unit_id` is null (the queue of the whole tenant) until the routing policy (ST-OMNI-006) sets it; the SLA deadlines (`sla_first_response_due_at`, BR-OMNI-06) are not set for the same reason.
- ST-OMNI-004 (gap, attachments): attachments of an inbound message (quarantine, virus scan) are not read; the port carries text only.
- ST-OMNI-004 (gap, unmerge): a message of a contact merged away goes to the survivor; there is no unmerge (R2).
- ST-OMNI-004 (gap, close job): nothing moves a Resolved conversation to Closed after 7 days (ST-OMNI-007).
- ST-OMNI-004 (observability): no metric or alert yet; the trail (`conversation.opened`, `.reopened`, `.assigned`, `contact_consent.withdrawn`) and `omni.conversation.assigned.v1` carry the changes.

### ST-OMNI-005 (replies, notes, resolve and reopen, slice OMNI-1)
- ST-OMNI-005 (decision, sending): the message is queued in `channel_message` (OUTBOUND, Queued) and sent by `ConversationSender` in the pass of the NOT dispatcher through the same adapters; it does **not** go through `notification_instance` and `delivery_attempt` (those are made for members, with preferences, quiet hours and plans). The cap and the budget of the channel account (ST-NOT-010) apply to a reply all the same: the messages are counted with the attempts in `DeliveryLimits` (`channel_message.sending_at` and `cost`), and one that would go over waits for the next day or month; a reply has the priority NORMAL, so it is held by money like any ordinary message.
- ST-OMNI-005 (gap, address): the provider adapter of a channel where the thread is not the sender (a mail, a text message) cannot answer from a hash. The answer is a sealed copy of the address kept for the conversation (the master keys of the personal data exist), to be made when the first real adapter is contracted; until then the in-memory adapters accept the handle.
- ST-OMNI-005 (decision, address): an adapter is given `external_thread_id` as the place to answer; the address or number of a contact is never stored (only its hash and mask), so the adapter of a channel must make a thread handle it can answer to.
- ST-OMNI-005 (decision, support consent): an answer for support is grounded without a consent while the window of the person is open (a channel without a window is always open); a withdrawn consent stops it all the same. Where the consent is missing outside the window, WhatsApp says `OPT_IN_REQUIRED`, the rest `CONSENT_DENIED`.
- ST-OMNI-005 (decision, attachments): the adapters carry text, so an attachment (a clean PUBLIC or INTERNAL version of a document) goes as an opaque link `/d/{id}` after the words, like a link; a CONFIDENTIAL one is refused as an attachment and may be a link.
- ST-OMNI-005 (decision, version): the version of the conversation is asked only when a message moves its state; resolve and reopen always ask it.
- ST-OMNI-005 (gap, links): `/d/{id}` is opaque and the web route shows the metadata of the version the server lets the person read and offers its download through `getDocumentVersionDownload` (the permission and the scan of that operation apply; no viewer yet). The PDP check that the agent may read what they link belongs to `linkConversationResource` (ST-OMNI-007); a message links documents of the tenant only.
- ST-OMNI-005 (config): `WEB_BASE_URL` (an origin, no path) is new and optional; without it a message that carries a link is not sent. The deployment must set it where agents send documents.
- ST-OMNI-005 (gap, templates): the screen OMNI-02 sends free text; sending an approved template outside the window is API-only (no picker of templates yet).
- ST-OMNI-005 (gap, SLA): the first answer of the conversation is not recorded for the SLA (BR-OMNI-06, ST-OMNI-006).
- ST-OMNI-005 (gap, STOP): a stop word ends the consents at once, but a message already queued is failed only when the dispatcher takes it up (within one pass), not at the moment of the withdrawal.
- ST-OMNI-005 (observability): no metric or alert for the failed replies yet; the status and the code are on the message.

### ST-OMNI-006 (routing policy, SLA in working time and canned replies, slice OMNI-1)
- ST-OMNI-006 (decision, scope): the policies and the replies are the tenant's: `omni.policy.manage` on the whole tenant reads and changes them (the supervisor of one unit does not), as the directory and the consents.
- ST-OMNI-006 (decision, publication): a version can be published only if its calendar is published for the year of today (422 `POLICY_INVALID` on `calendar_code`); publishing a version that is not a Draft is 409 `POLICY_STATE_INVALID` (new code, not in OMNI.md).
- ST-OMNI-006 (decision, window): the deadlines are counted on the default working window of ADM (08:00-12:00, 13:00-17:00, BR-ADM-08), the one of the worked example (Friday 16:50 + 30 minutes is Monday 08:20); ISS keeps its own window.
- ST-OMNI-006 (decision, rules): a rule narrows by one or more channels and by words found in the **first message** only; a later message does not route again. Keywords are kept folded (no accents, no case). A reply of the contract's `classification` enum was widened to the five classes so that a confidential class is a 422 on the field and not a refusal of the shape of the request.
- ST-OMNI-006 (gap, breach): the job that raises the SLA breach every minute and tells the supervisors through NOT is not built; nor is the measure of the first answer (the first outbound minus the first inbound, in working time, BR-OMNI-06) and the rate of OMNI-10 (BR-OMNI-09).
- ST-OMNI-006 (gap, use): the agent cannot yet pick a canned reply in the composer of OMNI-02 (the replies are made and read here); a reply cannot be retired by the API (there is no operation for it, only the state in the table), and has no language field.
- ST-OMNI-006 (gap, calendar): a conversation of a year with no published calendar has no deadline and nothing says so to anyone; the calendar of the next year must be published before the year ends.

### ST-CTR-014 (scorecard of a contractor and log of its violations, slice CTR-1)
- ST-CTR-014 (decision, rule): `CTR.SCORECARD` is a constant of the kernel (weights 30/25/20/10/15, thresholds 90/75/60) and `weights_rule_version_id` stays empty until the rule engine holds rules (R-CTR-05); the thresholds are an assumption awaiting the signature of the business owner (CXN-EXE-01) and CTR-23 says so.
- ST-CTR-014 (decision, revisions): an evaluation is made `SUBMITTED` (there is no draft command); a second one for an approved period is a new revision that supersedes the approved one when it is approved; a period has one evaluation open at a time (409 `EVALUATION_DUPLICATE`). The scorecard filters evaluations by the day they were made, not by the period label.
- ST-CTR-014 (decision, violations): the scorecard lists violations of every status (the confirmer needs the recorded ones) and counts only the confirmed ones; the sanction is chosen at the confirmation and rests on a clean, non-sealed document of decision, or on the one a termination left on the violation.
- ST-CTR-014 (gap, lifecycle): appeal and revocation of a violation have no command yet (the table and its guard allow `CONFIRMED` to `APPEALED`/`REVOKED`); the national database export of ST-CTR-015 is not built; the list of the contractors that lead to CTR-23 is not built (the screen reads `?id=<contractor>`).

### ST-ISS-006 (weekly matrix of the issues, slice ISS-1)
- ST-ISS-006 (decision, snapshot): BR-ISS-08 says to use the weekly snapshot once the period is locked; the job `issue-weekly-snapshot` and its table do not exist, and the matrix is read from the history of the issues (the events carry the status they moved to), which gives the same figures for a week that is over. If a snapshot is wanted for audit it must be written from this read.
- ST-ISS-006 (decision, attribution): an issue is counted for the unit and the group it has today, so an issue moved between units counts for the unit it is in now in every week; the identity `closing = opening + opened - resolved` holds for each issue, hence for each row.
- ST-ISS-006 (gap, BI): the matrix is not offered to BI; the overdue count uses the deadline the issue has today (an extension moves it for the past weeks too).

### ST-CTR-016 (retroactive adjustment, slice CTR-1) - deferred
- ST-CTR-016 (deferred, design): `approveRetroactiveAdjustment` is not built yet. It needs decisions the specification leaves open: the change order with a past effective day is refused by `decide` (422 `RETROACTIVE_NOT_ALLOWED`) and the adjustment is said to act on that same request, so the state it acts on and the revision it makes (retroactive, effective from the past) are not defined; the PAY ledger has no entry kind for an adjustment and its sign rules (`cumulative_ledger_entry_forward_sign_chk`) forbid the negative delta of a price that falls; and how the money difference of an acceptance is priced (quantity times the new price minus the snapshot) and the check against an approved settlement (`SETTLEMENT_ALREADY_APPROVED`) need the business owner. To be decided before it is built.

### ST-AST-005 (link of an asset to the unit that operates it, slice AST-1)
- ST-AST-005 (decision, link): a link is made `LINKED` straight away (the DRAFT of the table is not a state a command leaves it in) and is never changed; replacing one means suspending it, which has no command yet, so an asset keeps its first link until then. The O&M contract must be a contract of the same project as the asset; the plan is a clean version of a file of the tenant.
- ST-AST-005 (gap, integration): the code of the asset in the system of the operator is a note typed by hand; there is no adapter (R3).

### ST-CTR-015 (export of the records of the contractors, slice CTR-1)
- ST-CTR-015 (decision, columns): the columns of the file are the map `CONTRACTOR_EXPORT_COLUMNS` of the kernel until CXN-EXE-01 gives the format of the national database; changing it changes the hash, so the new file is a new batch.
- ST-CTR-015 (decision, scope of a file): a file holds what the person who asked for it was allowed to read; the batch is read and marked as submitted only by the people who asked for its file (`contractor_export_access`, written by every call, repeats included) and by whoever holds `contractor.export` on the whole tenant. A caller of a narrower scope never gets the file of a wider one: other records are another hash.
- ST-CTR-015 (decision, period): evaluations are exported by the day they were approved and violations by the day they were confirmed (the day each became a decision), not by the label of the period or the day the violation happened.
- ST-CTR-015 (gap, files): the file of an export that lost a race to an identical one stays in the store unreferenced (the object store has no remove on the file service); `CONFIRMED`/`FAILED` of a batch have no command (nothing comes back from the national system).

### ST-OMNI-007 (links of a conversation and its conversion into an issue, slice OMNI-1)
- ST-OMNI-007 (decision, conversion): the issue is made in process by `IssIssueStore.create` in the transaction of the command, with source MANUAL (the ISS source enum has no OMNI), severity MEDIUM (the contract names none) and the owner unit of the project; one issue for a conversation, marked `converted` on its link so an issue linked by hand does not count. `due_date` and `assignee_membership_id` of the contract are refused with 422 instead of ignored: ISS takes the deadline from the SLA policy and has its own assignment.
- ST-OMNI-007 (decision, scope): a link needs the permission to read the record held at its place; a conversion needs `omni.conversation.convert` at the place of the project (OMNI-09 offers only those projects). An agent scoped to a project sees a conversation only while it is assigned to them (the visibility rule of ST-OMNI-004).
- ST-OMNI-007 (gap, unlink): a link cannot be removed; nor can an issue be unlinked after a conversion. The list of the conversations of an issue is not offered (no index on `resource_links` yet).
- ST-OMNI-007 (gap, close job): nothing moves a Resolved conversation to Closed after 7 days, and the statistics of OMNI-10 are not built.

### ST-PRJ-010 (risks of a project, slice PRJ-1)
- ST-PRJ-010 (gap, job): the daily job `prj.risk-due` (warnings for critical or overdue risks, BR-PRJ-07) is not built; HOME-03 gets the critical risks from `project.risk_registered.v1` (severity CRITICAL from 15) only, and a risk that becomes critical by an update is announced by `project.risk_updated.v1`.
- ST-PRJ-010 (gap, MDM): the master type `RISK_CATEGORY` has no catalogue definition (fields, screen) yet; a risk names an `EFFECTIVE` entry of that type, so the entries have to be created by the master-data tools first (story MDM-010).
- ST-PRJ-010 (decision, stage): a project takes risks in every stage; the module document does not say that a settled, rejected or cancelled project must refuse them.
- ST-PRJ-010 (decision, code): the code of a risk may be renamed by an update to a free one (the contract lists `RISK_CODE_DUPLICATE` on the update); the code stays unique inside the project.
- ST-PRJ-010 (gap, issue): the link to an issue is the identifier on the risk, checked to be an issue of the same project; the issue does not list its risks, and the caller needs no right on the issue to link it.
- ST-PRJ-010 (gap, owner): PRJ-16 takes the owner and the linked issue as identifiers typed by hand (no directory of members is offered to the screen).

### ST-PRJ-008 (revisions of a project, slice PRJ-1)
- ST-PRJ-008 (gap, initial revision): revision 1 (`INITIAL`) is written DRAFT with the project and nothing approves it when the project is admitted to the project bank; the first adjustment therefore supersedes nothing and the history shows r1 as a draft.
- ST-PRJ-008 (decision, effective day): `effective_from` may not be after today, because an approval is applied to the project at once; a decision that takes effect later would need a job like `prj-transfer-apply`.
- ST-PRJ-008 (gap, group): the project group (QTQG/A/B/C) suggested by the thresholds is not worked out again when the total changes.
- ST-PRJ-008 (gap, consumers): nothing consumes `project.revised.v1` (MNE obligations); the legal dossier PRJ-04 lists the documents of the revisions only, not those of the stage changes, the appraisal or the transfers.
- ST-PRJ-008 (decision, stages): a project takes revisions from PIPELINE to COMPLETED; before the project bank the dossier is edited directly, and from SETTLED on it no longer changes.
- ST-PRJ-008 (decision, scope): a scope revision (`ADJUST_SCOPE`) is only recorded on the revision, the project having no scope column; the investor and decision-authority units are not checked against the permission of the proposer.

### ST-PRJ-009 (work breakdown and milestones, slice PRJ-1)
- ST-PRJ-009 (decision, contract): the module document lists no way to change what a milestone waits for after it is written, yet UAT-PLAN-58 saves a dependency between existing milestones, so the command `setMilestoneDependency` and the two reads `listProjectComponents` and `listProjectMilestones` were added to the contract.
- ST-PRJ-009 (decision, delay): a delay is counted from the baseline day in calendar days (done: the day it was done; open: the planned day or today once it has passed), as the story says; the status `DELAYED` is the one a person sets when moving the planned day, and `delayed` on a line is the computed flag.
- ST-PRJ-009 (gap, components): a component cannot be edited or retired after it is written, and the sum of the children is not set against the value of their parent.
- ST-PRJ-009 (gap, dependencies): a milestone may be done before the milestone it waits for; the module document does not forbid it.
- ST-PRJ-009 (gap, consumers): nothing consumes `project.milestone_updated.v1`; the milestones are not offered to the dashboards.

### ST-CAP-014 (capital ledger explorer, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-014 (decision, scope): an entry of a project is read in the scope of the caller over the project; an entry of no project (the appropriation of a source) only by a holder of `capital.ledger.read` on the whole tenant, because the module document gives the ledger a tenant data scope but the story asks for scoped entries only.
- ST-CAP-014 (decision, balance): the balance is the sum of the entries by the effect of each kind (the table of `cap-ledger.ts`: appropriated, allocated, reserved, committed, disbursed); `CapBalanceReconcileJob` keeps its own SQL copy of the allocated, committed and disbursed effects, to be replaced by this table.
- ST-CAP-014 (gap, snapshot): the balance at a day is worked out from the entries on every read; no snapshot of a closed day is kept.
- ST-CAP-014 (gap, reservations): the job that expires a reservation and releases it is ST-CAP-011; the screen shows whatever reserve and release entries exist.

### ST-SAS-020 (release flags, slice SAS-1)
- ST-SAS-020 (decision, cohort): a tenant is in the cohort named like its region, because the tenant has no cohort attribute and the module document gives no operation to assign one; a cohort of its own would need a column and a command.
- ST-SAS-020 (decision, declaration): the flags are declared in the code (`DECLARED_FLAGS`) and written Off by the migration; the `PUT` changes a flag and never adds one, because the contract of the update carries no description and a flag key is part of the code that reads it. The two declared flags stand for the R2 features SAS-21 and SAS-23.
- ST-SAS-020 (decision, retired): a retired flag is on for no tenant and is final; the module document does not say whether a retired flag reads as on or off, and the code path it decided is gone by then.
- ST-SAS-020 (gap): no code reads a flag yet (`getMyFeatureFlags` is for the client); the metrics and alert thresholds the story's tasks mention (`tenant_id` telemetry on flag evaluation) are not built.

### ST-DGV-007 (glossary bound to datasets, slice DGV-1)
- ST-DGV-007 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the bindings back, yet DGV-02 must warn of a deprecated binding and offer the revision in force, so the read `listDatasetGlossaryBindings` was added to the contract.
- ST-DGV-007 (decision, deprecation): no consumer is written for the deprecation: the revision bound is read through the term at every read, so the warning is always current and there is nothing to keep in step.
- ST-DGV-007 (decision, index): the unique index of V002 on (dataset, column, term) allowed a dataset to be bound to the same term twice (a NULL column differs from every other NULL); V1024 replaces it with one that treats the whole dataset as a column of its own.
- ST-DGV-007 (gap): no unbind; coverage is not set against the columns of the dataset (they are in the data contract of ST-DGV-005, not built); nobody is notified when a bound revision is deprecated.

### ST-DGV-009 (certification of data products, slice DGV-1)
- ST-DGV-009 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the products, yet DGV-18 shows them with their conditions, so the read `listDataProducts` was added to the contract, and the error `PRODUCT_STATE_INVALID` (409).
- ST-DGV-009 (decision, who): the owner of the product may not certify it nor mark it provisional (the document says only "≠ owner" for the certification); the owner may revoke their own product, which is the safe direction.
- ST-DGV-009 (decision, lines): NOT_ASSESSED to CERTIFIED is allowed directly (the UAT does it); PROVISIONAL cannot be marked again, a certified product is revoked or renewed, and a revoked one is final. The expiry is a transition of the job, not a decision.
- ST-DGV-009 (decision, run): "the latest run PASSED" is the badge of BR-DGV-04, so a run older than the freshness SLO of the dataset (STALE), a WARNING and a hard fail all refuse with QUALITY_NOT_PASSED.
- ST-DGV-009 (decision, event): the expiry announces `dgv.product.expired.v1`, an event the document does not list, because BI has to learn that the badge changed and `dgv.product.certified.v1` is the event of a decision.
- ST-DGV-009 (gap): no command creates or edits a data product; no consumer of the two events; the contract is read as the status of `dataset_contract` (ST-DGV-005 is not built).

### ST-QMS-008 (diary coverage, slice QMS-1)
- ST-QMS-008 (decision, late): "ký muộn = số ngày làm việc từ record_date đến chữ ký nhà thầu > 1" (BR-QMS-08) is read as: the working days after the day of the diary, up to and including the day of the first signature of the contractor; one is allowed; the days beyond are `late_days`. The first signature counts, so a correction signed weeks later does not make the diary late.
- ST-QMS-008 (decision, today): working days after today are not counted (they are not missing yet), so a range that reaches past today is counted through today (`through`).
- ST-QMS-008 (decision, calendar): a year of the range without a published WORKDAY_VN calendar is refused (422 CALENDAR_NOT_EFFECTIVE) rather than counted by the weekdays, since the coverage is "by the work calendar"; the lateness of a signature in a year with no calendar falls back to the weekdays like the deadline of a letter.
- ST-QMS-008 (decision, job): the job `diary-coverage` counts the month up to yesterday and sets the gauge `qms_diary_missing_days{tenant_id}`; the document says only "cảnh báo thiếu nhật ký" and names no recipient, so it tells no one.
- ST-QMS-008 (gap): no notification of a missing diary and no alert rule over the gauge.

### ST-BID-013 (contractor history, slice BID-1)
- ST-BID-013 (decision, scope): the scorecards and contracts shown are those of the contracts the caller reads (`contractor.performance.read`, as on CTR-23), not all of the tenant's: the document says BID calls the interface of CTR, so CTR's scope applies. An evaluator with only `bid.contractor.read` sees the restrictions and no scorecard.
- ST-BID-013 (decision, restrictions): the restrictions are listed whole, in force or not, with an `in_force` flag by the Vietnamese date; the decision document and the note are left to BID-02, which already hides them from those without the full right.
- ST-BID-013 (decision, cache): the read goes to CTR every time, so the consumer of `ctr.contractor_evaluation.approved.v1` that "refreshes the cache" has nothing to refresh and is not written.
- ST-BID-013 (gap): the evidence SCORECARD of an analysis finding waits for ST-BID-009.

### ST-CAP-011 (fund reservation, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-011 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to list the reservations, yet CAP-18 is a table with a countdown, so the read `listFundReservations` (`capital.read`, as the availability read) was added to the contract.
- ST-CAP-011 (decision, term): an earmark runs at most 366 days and its last instant must lie ahead; the document sets neither. A change of a reservation is a release and a new one.
- ST-CAP-011 (decision, year): a reservation is refused in a soft-closed or locked plan year (as a commitment), while a release or an expiry is not: capital returns to the line whatever the state of the year.
- ST-CAP-011 (decision, expiry): the job marks the status EXPIRED and posts a RELEASE entry with the reason `EXPIRED`, and announces `capital.reservation_released.v1` with status EXPIRED (the document lists only created and released).
- ST-CAP-011 (decision, reconcile): the nightly balance check compares the reserved column too, since reservations now post entries.
- ST-CAP-011 (gap): the consumption of a reservation by a payment request of PAY is the payment consumer (ST-PAY); no other module consumes the events yet.

### ST-QMS-007 (material testing, slice QMS-1)
- ST-QMS-007 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the tests, yet QMS-06 has to show them, so the reads `listMaterialTests` and `listMaterialContracts` (`material.manage`, the right of the supervisor who registers the samples; the second lists the contracts being performed so that the form does not need the right to read contracts) were added; the commands also state `CONTRACT_STATE_INVALID`, `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` and `SCOPE_DENIED`.
- ST-QMS-007 (decision, non-conformance): the specification says the non-conformance of a failed test takes a seriousness "by rule" and states no rule, and gives no person and no term; it is major (so it does not hold back the quality acceptance), answered for by the person who took the sample and due fourteen days after it is raised; two constants of the kernel (`FAILED_TEST_SEVERITY`, `FAILED_TEST_ACTION_DAYS`).
- ST-QMS-007 (decision, report): a result with no report is refused by the shape of the request (`report_document_version_id` is required), so the answer is `INVALID_REQUEST` rather than the `VALIDATION_FAILED` of the scenario; the database constraint `material_test_result_chk` stands behind it.
- ST-QMS-007 (gap): `ACCEPTED` and `REJECTED` have no command; the screen takes the report and the bill item as typed codes.

### ST-AST-004 (warranty claims, slice AST-1)
- ST-AST-004 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the claims, yet AST-05 has to show the board, so the read `listWarrantyClaims` (`warranty.read`) was added; the obligation view now names the contractor of the contract (`contractor`) because a claim is addressed to it; the commands also state `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, `SCOPE_DENIED` and the evidence errors.
- ST-AST-004 (decision, term): the contractor has seven days to answer, counted from the day the defect was reported (R-AST-03 leaves the term to the rules of the tenant or the terms of the contract, neither stated); it is one constant of the kernel (`CLAIM_RESPONSE_DAYS`).
- ST-AST-004 (decision, lifecycle): a disputed repair is put right again by the contractor (`recordWarrantyFix` from `DISPUTED`) and verified again, so a dispute is not a dead end; the verifier is neither the person who reported the defect nor the one who said it was put right; flagging a late answer does not change the version of the claim.
- ST-AST-004 (decision, issue): the intake of ISS gains the event `ast.warranty_claim.overdue.v1` (source module AST, category key `WARRANTY` that the tenant keeps in master data, like the other categories) and the claim keeps the issue it opened.
- ST-AST-004 (gap): the text says a late answer also suggests a violation to CTR; only the issue is opened. `REPAIRING` and `CLOSED` have no command. The evidence of a claim or a repair is typed as a code in the screen.

### ST-AST-006 (evaluation trigger, slice AST-1)
- ST-AST-006 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the triggers, yet AST-07 has to show them, so the read `listEvaluationTriggers` (`evaluation.trigger.manage`) was added; the commands also state `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, `SCOPE_DENIED` and, for the first, `HANDOVER_NOT_ACCEPTED`.
- ST-AST-006 (decision, day): a trigger after a handover falls due no earlier than the day the handover was accepted (the day in Vietnam) plus 12 or 24 months, and a later day may be chosen; the other kinds are not set for a day that has gone. One trigger exists for a project, a kind and a handover (the unique index of V002), so a second manual trigger on a project needs a handover to be named.
- ST-AST-006 (decision, stage): a trigger is set once the project is under way (`IMPLEMENTATION` to `CLOSED`); the trigger of the closed settlement needs `SETTLED` or `CLOSED`.
- ST-AST-006 (decision, reminder): the one who set the trigger is told when it falls due, through the notification service.
- ST-AST-006 (gap): the text says the system proposes a trigger when a handover is accepted or the settlement is closed; nothing sets one by itself (the screen proposes the day). `CANCELLED` has no command. MNE has no read of its evaluations, so the screen takes the code of the evaluation as typed.

### ST-BI-005 (dashboards and immutable versions, slice BI-1)
- ST-BI-005 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the dashboards, yet BI-01 lists them and BI-03 opens one, so the reads `listDashboards` and `getDashboard` were added with the permission `bi.query` (the one `listBiDatasets` uses); the scaffold reads `get_resources_dashboard_version(_id)` (`dashboard_version.read`) are implemented as well. The drill-in schema of the layout is not in the contract (`layout_json` is an object), so it is fixed here: `{columns, widgets[]}` with tiles `{id, kind, title?, metric_version_id, dimension_code?, grid{x,y,w,h}}`, 12 columns, at most 100 tiles, no overlap.
- ST-BI-005 (decision, visibility): a dashboard is seen by its owner and, once published to the library, by the whole tenant; the shares of ST-BI-007 widen it. Only the owner saves a version until the EDIT share exists (a dashboard the caller does not see is 404, one in the library is 403).
- ST-BI-005 (decision, owner): the owner is a membership id, as every `created_by` of the module.
- ST-BI-005 (decision, status): a version is saved as DRAFT and only the publication of ST-BI-006 moves it; the dashboard keeps pointing at the newest saved version, whatever its state; an ARCHIVED dashboard takes no version (409 `STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED`, the registry's code for a transition that does not exist).
- ST-BI-005 (decision, test control): the isolation suite used `get_resources_dashboard_version_id` as the id-bearing operation that no case covers; it is implemented now, so the negative control moved to `get_resources_outbox_event_id`.
- ST-BI-005 (gap): the tiles of a saved layout are drawn with live data in BI-04 and on the board of BI-02, not inside the builder; no drag-and-drop (the grid is edited by numbers, with the preview).

### ST-BI-006 (certification and publication approval, slice BI-1)
- ST-BI-006 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the requests, yet BI-11 is an inbox, so the read `listBiPublicationRequests` was added with the permission `bi.publish` (the approver's); the `x-domain-events` of `decideBiPublication` and `post_dashboards_id_publish` now list the events they emit (`bi.dashboard.published.v1`, `bi.policy.activated.v1`).
- ST-BI-006 (decision, reproducibility): "rerun on the latest snapshot gives the same result as the stored one" has no stored result in the data model, so the check is the rerun of the metric over the latest frozen version of its dataset against what the snapshot stored: the lines found against `period_snapshot.row_count`, and the groups of every dimension of the metric adding up to the whole (a distinct count is only compared by rows). If a stored figure per certification is wanted, it needs a column and the story to say when it is written.
- ST-BI-006 (decision, object): for `DASHBOARD` the `object_id` of the request is the id of a version (the state machine moves `dashboard_version`, not the dashboard); the baseline `post_dashboards_id_publish` takes the dashboard id and works on the version it points to.
- ST-BI-006 (decision, authority): the metrics and the policies are the tenant's, so asking for them and every decision needs the right over the whole tenant; a version of a dashboard is asked for by the owner of the dashboard. The author of the object cannot approve either, on top of the one who asked (the database has the checks of V002 for both).
- ST-BI-006 (decision, migration): V1581 held a `SET NOT NULL` next to expand steps, which the expand-then-contract check refuses, so it moved to V1583 with its marker (nothing was applied yet).
- ST-BI-006 (gap): a request cannot be withdrawn (`WITHDRAWN` exists in the table and the guard, the plan has no command for it); publishing does not set the audience (sharing is ST-BI-007).

### ST-BI-007 (dashboard sharing without data access, slice BI-1)
- ST-BI-007 (decision, contract): the shares are read through `getDashboard` (a `shares` list the owner alone gets, with the names of the grantees) and not through a read of their own, because the module document has none and BI-17 only needs them next to the dashboard.
- ST-BI-007 (decision, reach): a `UNIT` share reaches the members of the unit and of the units under it (the same ancestor rule the queries use for the units of a requester); a `ROLE` share reaches the members who hold the role now; the right is judged on every read, so a revocation, a change of unit or a role that ended takes effect at once.
- ST-BI-007 (decision, edit): a share with `EDIT` lets the person save versions of the dashboard (the version stays theirs, and the owner or another asks to publish and a third approves); sharing, revoking and asking to publish stay the owner's.
- ST-BI-007 (decision, duplicate): a second active share for one grantee is 409 `STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED` (the unique index of V002), because no error code of the contract names it; a change of permission is a revocation and a new share.
- ST-BI-007 (decision, audience): the dashboard is `SHARED` while it has an active share and `PRIVATE` when it has none; a dashboard in the library keeps its audience.
- ST-BI-007 (gap): the grantee is given by id; the contract has no directory of people, roles or units to pick from, so BI-17 does not look the name up before the share.

### ST-DGV-008 (maturity rubric, evidence-based assessment and roadmap, slice DGV-1)
- ST-DGV-008 (decision, rubric): the module text asks for 8 pillars of 3 to 5 criteria with weights, descriptions of the levels 1 to 5, DCAM/DMM references, `min_coverage_pct` and `evidence_ttl_days` but fixes neither the pillar codes nor the shape of `pillars_json`; the platform takes the eight pillars of document 08 as GOVERNANCE, METADATA, QUALITY, INTEGRATION, SECURITY, LINEAGE_BI, AI_READINESS and VALUE_COST (UAT-AC494 names QUALITY), the weights of the pillars add up to 100 and the weights of the criteria are relative (BR-DGV-08 divides by their sum). The rubric thresholds (70% coverage, evidence life) need the sign-off of the Data Owner (docs/dgv-maturity.md).
- ST-DGV-008 (decision, what counts): BR-DGV-08 speaks of ASSESSED criteria; a criterion counts only when a reviewer accepted it and its evidence has not run out, and a NOT_APPLICABLE counts out of the fraction only when a reviewer accepted the reason (otherwise an assessor could raise the coverage by excluding criteria). A published assessment is scored on its day of publication so that it does not change as evidence ages.
- ST-DGV-008 (decision, states): there is no command to submit an assessment, so the first record puts it IN_REVIEW; `post_maturity_assessments_id_publish` takes `approve` (publishes), `return` (back to DRAFT) and `reject` (REJECTED, which frees the period). The reviewer named on the assessment is the only reviewer; the publisher is not the assessor.
- ST-DGV-008 (decision, versions): `If-Match` of `recordMaturityEvidence` is the version of the row of the criterion (the assessment is the path), so that two records of the same criterion cannot overwrite each other unseen; `reviewMaturityEvidence` takes the version of the row it decides on.
- ST-DGV-008 (decision, roadmap): a milestone is ACHIEVED by a published assessment of the whole tenant for the same rubric code (any revision) whose pillar level (exact, not rounded) reaches the target; IN_PROGRESS is in the table and is not written yet; MISSED is by the job `dgv-maturity-sweep` and is final. The target must be above the pillar level of the latest published assessment of the tenant.
- ST-DGV-008 (decision, reads): the contract had no read for the rubrics or the milestones, so `listMaturityRubrics` (`GET /dgv/maturity-rubrics`) and `listMaturityMilestones` (`GET /dgv/maturity-milestones`) were added with `data.maturity.read`; the score response carries the assessed and not applicable counts, the share asked, the day it is scored on and every criterion. All maturity operations need their permission through a grant on the whole tenant (the data belongs to no unit).
- ST-DGV-008 (gap): a rubric version cannot be edited (a new one is drafted); the screens take the document of a rubric as JSON with a template; evidence is a record of a kind and a reference (no file is attached to it); an assessment of a domain or a data product does not prove the roadmap; the notification of the assessor when evidence expires and of the owner of a missed milestone is by the outbox payload `notification.recipients` and depends on the notification templates being registered.

### ST-DGV-005 (data contracts with the approval of the consumers, slice DGV-1)
- ST-DGV-005 (decision, ODCS): the module text pins "v3.0.x" as an assumption and the repository carries no JSON Schema file of ODCS; the platform accepts `v3.0.0` to `v3.0.2`, checks the fields it stands on with the paths of the standard and keeps the rest of the document (`docs/spikes/DGV-SPK-ODCS.md`). To be confirmed by Data/Platform.
- ST-DGV-005 (decision, consumers): the module text says "every registered consumer" and no registry of consumers exists; the consumers of a dataset are those the **published** contract declares (a custom property `consumer` of the ODCS document) and the BI datasets built on it (`bi_dataset.dgv_dataset_id`), once each; the new revision's own declaration does not count, so dropping a consumer does not spare its owner. When the document and the platform name the same consumer (a BI dataset) the platform owner wins. A BI dataset built after the draft is asked at the publication (the approval is kept, 409 `CONSUMER_APPROVAL_PENDING`, audit `dgv.contract_consumers_added`). The owner of a consumer reads their own approvals through `listConsumerApprovals` (`GET /dgv/contract-consumer-approvals`), which needs only `data.contract.consume`.
- ST-DGV-005 (decision, states): there is no command to submit a draft, so a revision is IN_REVIEW as soon as it is drafted; one revision is open per dataset. The baseline decision `post_data_contracts_id_publish` acts on that revision: `approve` publishes (a breaking one needs the window, which only `publishDatasetContract` takes), `return` (RETURNED) and `reject` (REJECTED) close it so that another can be drafted.
- ST-DGV-005 (decision, compatibility): the classification of BR-DGV-07 is applied to the columns (removed or renamed, type not widened, made required or added required, key, unit, classification raised); an integer widened to a number and a precision or length widened with the same scale are not changes of type; a rename cannot be told from a removal and an addition, and both are breaking anyway. A change of meaning (a description) is not detected.
- ST-DGV-005 (decision, window): a breaking revision needs `migration_window_until` at least 30 days after `effective_from` (BR-DGV-07, an assumption); a window for a revision that is not breaking is 422. Published versions are immutable, so "r3 serves until the window ends" is worked out when they are read (`serving`, `serving_until`); a backward compatible revision replaces the one before it at once.
- ST-DGV-005 (decision, publish): the publication needs the dataset to have a retention policy (`data_contract_version.retention_policy_id` is NOT NULL), 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` naming `retention_policy_id` otherwise; the document is stored as a generated file (`dgv_contract`) and taken back if the command does not commit.
- ST-DGV-005 (gap): a revision cannot be edited (a new one is drafted); the consumers are not notified (the event `dgv.contract.drafted.v1` carries the count, the notification module may route it); DGV-19 lists the revisions in review through the list of contracts, with the approvals inside, as there is no read of the approvals alone.

### ST-BI-009 (query log, heavy queries as jobs and metric lineage, slice BI-1)
- ST-BI-009 (decision, heavy): the module text says a query over the cost or time threshold is a 202 job but gives no threshold; a query costs the lines of its snapshot times the weight of its dimension (project 8, unit and fund source 3, budget level and bucket 1, none 1) and is a job over 250,000 units (kernel `bi-query-cost`; the host may bind `BI_QUERY_COST_LIMIT`). The weights and the limit are to be tuned on real data.
- ST-BI-009 (decision, answer): the contract has no read for the answer of a job, so the same question asked again is answered 200 from what the job kept for an hour (`bi_query_run.result`, `result_expires_at`); the answer is the job's own and never leaves the reader and the scope it was made for (the key of the cache has them).
- ST-BI-009 (decision, status): a command answers with another status than the route declares through `CommandScope.respondWith`; the status is kept with the answer of the idempotency key and the replay has it too.
- ST-BI-009 (decision, scope changed): a job whose reader still has the right but whose scope version moved since the question ends DENIED `SCOPE_CHANGED` (the answer would be of another scope than the one asked under); the reader asks again and gets a job under the new scope.
- ST-BI-009 (decision, lineage): the records of a metric are the projects that have a line in the snapshot (nodes and edges written set-wise at the build), so the lineage of one metric over a tenant of 5,000 projects is cut at the node cap and says `truncated`; `getBiLineage` takes `direction` and `depth` (not in the contract before).
- ST-BI-009 (decision, visibility): the lineage never tells what the source would refuse: a dashboard version needs `bi.query` besides the sharing, a dataset of the catalogue `data.catalog.read`, a dataset of BI `bi.query` and a policy, a record the right to read its project; the right to read the lineage is the one the caller came through (`data.lineage.read` or `bi.lineage.read`).
- ST-BI-009 (decision, run read): the contract had no read for the state of a run, and a screen that asks the same question again to learn how a job ended would make a job each time one failed; `getBiQueryRun` (`bi.query`, the person's own runs only) is added and the screens look at the run, asking the question again only when it completed.
- ST-BI-009 (decision, pinned): the spec of a job keeps whether the question continues a drill (`pinned`), so a job reads the revision of the metric the drill was begun with.
- ST-BI-009 (gap): the drill (`GET /bi/drill`) and the refresh of a context still answer in the request whatever their cost; the lineage is written from now on (a version built before this gets its source when the same build is asked for again, and the edge dataset version → metric when the metric is next read); no command cancels a job (CANCELLED is in the table and the guard); the metric registry sync replaces the registry of the platform, so a metric left out of a bundle is retired (found by the tests of this story, not changed here).

### ST-BI-008 (exports and per-recipient schedules, slice BI-1)
- ST-BI-008 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to list the exports or the schedules, yet BI-16 and BI-12 are lists, so the reads `listBiExports` (`bi.export`) and `listBiExportSchedules` (`bi.export.schedule`) were added; the `x-domain-events` of the two commands now list `bi.export.ready.v1` and `bi.export.denied.v1`. The contract has no way to serve the file, so the signed link of the files service is served by `GET /bi/exports/{id}/file` (public, signature only), as DIS does for its reports.
- ST-BI-008 (decision, columns): V002 has no place for why a job was refused nor for when a schedule last ran, so `bi_export_job.denied_reason` and `bi_export_schedule.last_run_at` were added (the runner needs the second to run a schedule once for each of its times).
- ST-BI-008 (decision, reproducible scope): a job keeps the scope hash of its subject, and the link to its file is given only when the person's scope hash now is the same: a scope that changed, narrowed or widened, is not served a file cut under another one (AC-ST-BI-008-3 asks that no data outside the intersection of the old and the new scope is exported; refusing the old file and cutting a new one to the new scope gives that without keeping per-row scope).
- ST-BI-008 (decision, row cap): the 100,000 rows of the cap are the groups of the answer (a metric over a dimension), since the answer of a metric is its groups; a PDF holds at most 5,000, as the PDF of DIS does. A refusal over the cap is kept as a DENIED job (`EXPORT_ROW_CAP_EXCEEDED`, 422) and `truncated` is always false.
- ST-BI-008 (decision, classification): a dataset of class CONFIDENTIAL or PERSONAL is not exported at all (`CLASSIFICATION_EXPORT_DENIED`, 403): the module document allows it "where a policy allows" and an access policy has no attribute that says so. The attribute is the next step if those datasets are to be exported.
- ST-BI-008 (decision, link only): a `LINK_ONLY` schedule makes the same CSV for each recipient (the job table has no such format) and says in `bi.export.ready.v1` that the file is delivered behind the sign-in; the sending of the link or the file is the notification module's.
- ST-BI-008 (decision, no job): an export that no job can be made for (no frozen version of the period to read, so no dataset version to name) is 422 `SNAPSHOT_NOT_FOUND` and keeps nothing; in a schedule run it is skipped and told on the audit trail (`bi.export_schedule_skipped`).
- ST-BI-008 (decision, query engine): the reading of a metric (certified, snapshot, policies, hidden columns, scope hash) moved from the query controller to `BiQueryEngine`, so the screen, the drill and the export read through the same code, for a member who is in a request or not.
- ST-BI-008 (decision, permissions): an export is cut to the projects the person may query and may export, both (the two rights can have different scopes), and the scope the file is given under includes the policies over the dataset (which policy, which revision) and both scopes, so a policy replaced or a right narrowed or widened since the file was made stops the link.
- ST-BI-008 (decision, schedule revision): a schedule reads the revision of the metric it was made with (a superseded one is still read, as a dashboard reads its pinned revisions), because no command updates or retires a schedule; a request that someone makes now needs a certified revision.
- ST-BI-008 (gap): no command pauses, changes or retires a schedule or withdraws a job (the states are in the table and the guard); the recipients and the dashboard of a schedule are given by id; the notification of the recipient is not sent here.

### ST-DGV-006 (lineage graph, slice DGV-1)
- ST-DGV-006 (decision, contract): the batch of the contract has its edges as free objects, so an edge is fixed here as `{ from, to, observed_at? }` where an end is `{ node_id }` (an existing node) or a description of the node (`node_kind`, `ref_module`, `ref_id` or `ref_key`, `display_name`, `classification`), made when it is not there; the answer of the graph is `{ start, direction, depth, truncated, nodes[], edges[] }` and a restricted node is `{ key, restricted, level }`.
- ST-DGV-006 (decision, identity): the unique index of V002 on a node does not see two nodes with no id (a null is not equal to a null), so V1026 indexes the identity with the nulls turned into values.
- ST-DGV-006 (decision, visibility): who sees a node is the right to the object it stands for (dashboard, metric, dataset of the catalogue as the catalogue shows it, dataset of BI by policy, record by project: a record needs the right over the lineage and the right to read its project, even over the whole tenant); the sources, batches, tables and exports name no data of their own and are seen by whoever reads the lineage when they are public or internal; personal and core data only over the whole tenant. A graph whose start the person may not see is 404, not a restricted node, so that the existence of the node is not told.
- ST-DGV-006 (decision, errors): a batch that names a node of another tenant by its id is 422 `LINEAGE_NODE_INVALID` and, as for the queries, the answer does not tell it from a node that does not exist (the matrix of the negative authorisation tests has it as an id of the body).
- ST-DGV-006 (gap): the pipelines, the builder of the snapshots of BI, the imports and the exports are to post their batches (adoption by the other groups, as the story says).

### ST-DOC-017 (archive handover, disposal proposals and retention rules, slice DOC-1)
- ST-DOC-017 (decision, handover): CLOSED goes to HANDED_OVER and, in the same command, to ARCHIVED (HANDED_OVER is the step the archive passes through, so the version moves twice); the outgoing documents FILED in the dossier become ARCHIVED with a line in their timeline, incoming letters stay FILED.
- ST-DOC-017 (decision, rejection): the module text sends a rejected disposal back to ARCHIVED; a dossier proposed from CLOSED never was archived, so the proposal records the state it came from (`disposal_from_status`) and a rejection returns the dossier there. The proposal can be made from CLOSED, HANDED_OVER or ARCHIVED.
- ST-DOC-017 (decision, status codes): `DOSSIER_NOT_CLOSED` is 422 in the registry (the kernel said 409 before; it follows the registry now); a dossier whose disposal is proposed or decided is 409 `STAGE_TRANSITION_DENIED`, the code of the registry for a transition that does not exist.
- ST-DOC-017 (decision, council document): the document of the decision must exist in the tenant and be issued (not a draft) or received; nothing more is judged about it. A hold placed after the proposal also stops the approval.
- ST-DOC-017 (decision, rules): the years of a rule are those of its class (PERMANENT none, Y70/Y20/Y10/Y5 their own) and may be left out of the request; a rule that starts the same day as another of the set and category is an overlap even between drafts (the unique index of V002); publishing checks the overlap against the published ones again, and the exclusion constraint is the last word under concurrency.
- ST-DOC-017 (decision, scope): the drafting, publication and confirmation of the rules belong to the whole tenant, like the templates (DOC 2); the confirmation is the right `doc.template.confirm_legal` as the contract says.
- ST-DOC-017 (decision, provisional): confirming a rule does not settle the dossiers that were closed before (UAT-AC421 keeps DS-421 provisional while DS-421b takes the rule); the guard of ST-DOC-013 still lets one later command settle a provisional dossier once, and none exists.
- ST-DOC-017 (decision, job): `doc-handover-due` runs hourly and reminds at 30, 7 and 1 days (a day that has passed is the last stage); the stage sent is kept in `handover_reminded_days`, written without moving the version of the dossier.
- ST-DOC-017 (gap): rules are not edited or retired; the receipt of the archive is a free reference; the NOT VALID checks of V1229 judge only the rows written from now on.

### ST-DOC-016 (layout profile governance and comparison of template revisions, slice DOC-1)
- ST-DOC-016 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the layout profiles, yet DOC-16 lists them, so the reads `listDocumentLayoutProfiles` and `getDocumentLayoutProfile` (`doc.template.read`) were added; the comparison of DOC-15 reads two `getDocumentTemplate` answers, which now carry the names of the drafter, the publisher and the legal confirmer.
- ST-DOC-016 (decision, seal): "the seal covers a third of the signature of the leader (±0.03), on its left" is judged on the boxes of the profile (`signatureLayout`), as the width of the seal box that lies over the box of the signature divided by the width of the signature box; the rendered PDF puts the signature widgets from the same boxes.
- ST-DOC-016 (decision, appearance): the line under the signature (ISO 8601 +07:00) is drawn as the appearance of the signature field when the signature is prepared; the zone is the fixed offset of Vietnam, like the rest of DOC.
- ST-DOC-016 (decision, scope): the library of layouts belongs to the whole tenant, like the template library (DOC 2).
- ST-DOC-016 (gap, CXN-DOC-01): the PNG images of the signature and of the seal are not drawn (no image assets); the legal confirmation of a profile has no command and its original text is not confirmed.

### ST-DOC-015 (replacement and correction documents, slice DOC-1)
- ST-DOC-015 (decision, draft): the module document says the command makes a draft with the relation REPLACES (or CORRECTS); the draft copies the type, template, layout, recipients, subject and content of the issued document, is signed in person (a delegation is not carried over) and the correction words are stored in the content under `correction`. How a template prints them is for the legal review of the templates.
- ST-DOC-015 (decision, open): a replacement (or a correction) is open while its draft is neither issued nor cancelled; one of each kind can be open at a time, and a cancelled draft frees the next. The relation of a cancelled draft is kept (relations are kept for good).
- ST-DOC-015 (decision, states): a corrected document stays in force and can be corrected again, replaced or recalled (on a basis only, DOC 4: "REPLACED/CORRECTED → RECALLED không có căn cứ mới"); a replaced document is final, in the database too (V1227). Recalling a corrected document was added to ST-DOC-012's recall for that.
- ST-DOC-015 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the relations, yet DOC-19 shows a timeline, so `getOutgoingDocument` carries `relations` (the answer was an open object).
- ST-DOC-015 (gap): the system actor of the table of states is the transaction of the issue itself (the membership of the one who issues), not a separate worker; the effect is the same and happens once.

### ST-CAP-013 (allocation scenarios that never touch the ledger, slice CAP-1)
- ST-CAP-013 (decision, method): the module document names no method of simulation; a scenario is a set of assumed moves (`assumptions.moves`: a line of the plan, the receiving project, a whole amount) judged against constraints (`min_remaining_vnd`, `max_move_vnd`, `max_total_vnd`) and against the balance of each donor line (what is reserved, committed and disbursed), the way a reallocation proposal is. Other assumptions (a rate of disbursement, a new appropriation) are not modelled.
- ST-CAP-013 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the scenarios, yet CAP-09 lists them, so the read `listAllocationScenarios` (`capital.read`, scoped by the unit of the base plan) was added; the answer carries `actions` (run, promote) for the caller.
- ST-CAP-013 (decision, infeasible): a move to a project that holds no line on the same appropriation is `SCENARIO_CONSTRAINT_INFEASIBLE` (`NO_RECIPIENT_LINE`) in a scenario, where a proposal says `TRANSFER_UNBALANCED`; the run keeps nothing when it is infeasible.
- ST-CAP-013 (decision, promotion): promotion judges the scenario again against the plan and the balances as they are then, needs a frozen input snapshot (optional at creation, required to promote) and draws the proposal as the one who promotes; its code is `SCN-` and the code of the scenario.
- ST-CAP-013 (gap): `ARCHIVED` is in the table and no command enters it; the two-year retention after archiving ([GIẢ ĐỊNH] in CAP 5) is a job of retention, not done here.

### ST-MNE-009 (investment evaluation tied to the project stage, slice MNE-1)
- ST-MNE-009 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the evaluations, yet MNE-08 lists them, so the read `listMneEvaluations` (`mne.read`) was added; the answer carries `actions` (submit, decide) for the caller.
- ST-MNE-009 (decision, status): the document has PLANNED → IN_PROGRESS → SUBMITTED but the contract has no command to start an evaluation, so submit starts from PLANNED, IN_PROGRESS or RETURNED; IN_PROGRESS stays in the table and the guard.
- ST-MNE-009 (decision, stages, CXN-13): INITIAL in IMPLEMENTATION within 12 months of the day the project entered it (status_changed_at, Vietnam time), MIDTERM in IMPLEMENTATION, FINAL in COMPLETED or SETTLED, IMPACT in CLOSED, AD_HOC anywhere, as the document lists; a refusal names the kind and the stage.
- ST-MNE-009 (decision, SoD): the decider is neither the planner nor the submitter (the table only forbade the planner); returning records the decision and submitting again clears it, so the one who returned may resubmit and a third person decides.
- ST-MNE-009 (decision, link): an approved evaluation is linked to the overall report (no project) due soonest that is still DRAFT, IN_REVIEW or RETURNED in the unit that owns the project or above it; with none, the link stays empty and is not made later.
- ST-MNE-009 (gap): the report that reads its linked evaluations is ST-MNE-002 territory and was not changed here.

### ST-PAY-012 (settlement forms and calendar, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-012 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the forms of a settlement, yet PAY-16 lists them, so the read `listSettlementForms` (`settlement_revision.read`) was added; `generateSettlementForms` takes the `place` of the place-and-date line as the Treasury forms do and the 17 codes as an enum, and answers with the forms drawn (two changes against the contract allowed in `compat-allow.json`: the operation had no client).
- ST-PAY-012 (decision, scope): the forms are drawn for a settlement that is VERIFIED (UAT-AC340 starts there) and of a year or a project; a settlement of one contract has none. The forms live in `payment_form_instance` (the table already carries the settlement as a subject) and not in a table of their own.
- ST-PAY-012 (decision, decision form): 10/QTDA is drawn like any form through DOC and, besides, an outgoing document is drafted for it (subject PAY, the settlement), for the unit that owns the project, with no recipient or signer; the review, signature and issue are DOC's own steps and the closing of the settlement already needs the document issued (ST-PAY-009).
- ST-PAY-012 (decision, calendar): the calendar is read from the list of settlements (the days left and the stage are counted on the screen in Vietnam time); the reminders themselves are the job of ST-PAY-009, so no new job or event was added.
- ST-PAY-012 (gap, CXN-09): the originals of the forms 01–05/QTNĐ and 01–12/QTDA and the mapping of their fields are not confirmed; the platform draws only from the templates that DOC holds confirmed, and refuses the rest by name.

### ST-PAY-011 (overdue advances with escalation, slice PAY-1)
- ST-PAY-011 (decision, levels, CXN-12): the warnings (7 days before the deadline, the deadline, the day after it, 7 days after it) follow BR-PAY-11 as the module document gives them; the deadline of a land clearance advance is the one the payment fixed (30 days after it was paid). The rule is `packages/kernel/src/pay-advance-alert.ts` and changes in one place when the business owner answers.
- ST-PAY-011 (decision, contract advance): an advance of a contract is overdue (level 3) from the day it is found past the threshold of recovery with a balance owed, or on a contract that is completed or terminated, and is escalated a week later; it has no level 1 or 2 because the document gives it no deadline.
- ST-PAY-011 (decision, escalation): level 4 raises the open ISS issue of the advance (the one level 3 asked for) to level 2 of ISS with the reason `ADVANCE_OVERDUE_ESCALATED`; when no issue is open the alert still goes and the advance stays on PAY-12, and a calendar year that is not published leaves the issue as it is, as the SLA job does.
- ST-PAY-011 (decision, plan): `acknowledgeAdvanceAlert` stores one plan (note and day) on the advance and may be given again; the audit trail keeps every plan. The job does not bump the version of the advance, so a screen is not made stale by an alert.
- ST-PAY-011 (decision, screens): PAY-11 does not take the guarantee: the server picks the smallest one that covers the advance, so a client cannot name a guarantee that is not the member's; PAY-12 reuses the dialog of the recovery of PAY-07.
- ST-PAY-011 (gap): `pay.advance.alert.v1` carries the `notification` member but no workflow of NOT is published for it, so nobody is told in the application yet; the issue and its escalation do tell the leaders through ISS.

### ST-LND-011 (enforcement of a land recovery, slice LND-1)
- ST-LND-011 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the cases, yet LND-09 lists them, so the read `listEnforcementCases` (`land.read`, as the complaints and the allocations) was added; each case says what the caller may do with it (`allowed`).
- ST-LND-011 (decision, procedure, CXN-12): the steps (persuasion, notice, decision forming the enforcement team, decision to enforce, execution, completion), the document every decision needs, the planned and executed days and the reasons that block an opening are working assumptions; the rules live in `packages/kernel/src/lnd-enforcement.ts` and change in one place when the business owner answers.
- ST-LND-011 (decision, parcels): a parcel that is approved, paid or disputed may have a case opened (it needs a recovery decision, a household of the parcel, an approved plan and the money offered, paid or deposited); the execution puts the parcel under enforcement, so a second case for it is refused (422 PARCEL_STATUS) once the first is completed.
- ST-LND-011 (decision, approval): every move is made by somebody other than the person who drew the case up, the suspension and the resumption included (403 SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED); a suspended case goes back to the step it was suspended from or to the one after it, never to the persuasion.
- ST-LND-011 (gap): the rounds of persuasion are not recorded (the persuasion is one step), and a completed case has no side effect on the contract (CTR) or the intake (ISS); no consumer reads `lnd.enforcement.started.v1` or `lnd.enforcement.advanced.v1` yet.

### ST-LND-012 (resettlement allocation, slice LND-1)
- ST-LND-012 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the allocations or the items waiting for a unit, yet LND-12 lists the first and the form needs the second, so the reads `listResettlementAllocations` and `listResettlementCandidates` (`land.read`, as the complaints) were added.
- ST-LND-012 (decision, item): the document makes `plan_item_id` optional in the table; the acceptance criterion says an allocation requires an approved plan item, so the command refuses a request without it (422 HOUSEHOLD_NOT_ELIGIBLE, reason NO_PLAN_ITEM). A plan that is approved, being paid or paid stands; a land item gives a plot, a house item a house or a flat.
- ST-LND-012 (decision, unit): the unit that answers is the unit of the household (as for a complaint); one item gives one unit (a second unique index); a declined allocation gives the unit code back.
- ST-LND-012 (gap): handing a unit over (`HANDED_OVER`, `handed_on`) and the household declining it have no command in the contract; the states exist in the table and are guarded, nothing moves them.

### ST-MDM-010 (merge, unmerge and survivorship rules, slice MDM-1)
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, contract): the module document names no way to read the rules or the merges, yet MDM-35 and MDM-36 list them, so the reads `listSurvivorshipRules` (`GET /survivorship-rules`) and `listMasterMerges` (`GET /master-merges`) were added to the contract, both under `master.read`.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, references): a merge points again the ACTIVE crosswalks and the contractor links of the merged record; the references held by a version, and the historical ones, are not moved and are read through the alias (`alias_json`).
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, separation of duties): the person who merges must not have made either record (403 `SELF_APPROVAL_DENIED`); the change request is created by the system member and applied by the person who merged, so it carries no decision document (`master_cr_publish_chk` is relaxed for MERGE and UNMERGE).
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, namespace): a record of the platform namespace, or of a type whose policy is PLATFORM_ONLY, cannot be merged by a tenant (403 `POLICY_LOCKED`).
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, day): a version cannot end the day it starts, so a merge of an entry whose version began today is refused (409) and made from the next day; the merge itself always takes effect today, so the references, the versions and the status of both records change together and no read ever sees half of it.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, attributes): the `label` of an entry is settled as an attribute like the others; an unsettled attribute is a 409 `MERGE_CONFLICT` (nothing is written), an override of an attribute that was not in conflict is a 422.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, unmerge): the answers of the steward for an attribute the survivor edited after the merge are `KEEP` and `REVERT`; a merge cannot be undone while the survivor is itself merged away or not in force.
- ST-MDM-010 (gap): a survivorship rule cannot be edited (a new revision is drafted) and the rules do not reach the version-level references; merging more than two records is done as a chain of merges.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, rules): a revision published for a later day leaves the one in force deciding until then (several revisions can be PUBLISHED, never two for the same day); a draft that a later published revision overrides from its own day cannot be published.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, keys): an attribute of an entry called `label`, or starting with `~`, is settled under its name with one more `~`, so it never shares a key with the label of the entry.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, content): the version a merge or an undo writes is checked by the rules of its type (`validateMasterContent`) like any version, keeps the end date of the version it follows (the entry that comes back gets the end date it had before the merge, kept in `alias_json.merged.valid_until`), and an entry whose version has run out by the day of the merge cannot be merged (409).
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, alias): `MasterStore.search` with a `business_key` follows the merges of `master_merge` that stood on `as_of` (from their day until the day they were undone) to the last survivor when the entry of that key has no version in force on `as_of`; no other read path was changed.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, contractors): repointing a contractor to the survivor, or back, advances its version and update metadata like the crosswalks, so a verification made against the contractor as it was read is refused.
- ST-MDM-010 (gap): a merge cannot be undone on the day it took effect when the survivor's version began that day (a version cannot end the day it starts, so the survivor could not take its attributes back before tomorrow, and the two entries must come back together); the steward undoes it from the next day.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, references): the rules of a catalogue type (`tenantLookup`) read the key of an entry that was merged away as the entry it was merged into, so the entries that name it by key (e.g. a decision authority naming a decision type) stay valid; the stored keys are not rewritten.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, rule data): merging or undoing the merge of a rule type (BR-MDM-09) needs the person to be an approver of that type (403 `SCOPE_DENIED`), as approving or publishing a change does; attributes are free-form, so names such as `__proto__` or `constructor` are kept as ordinary keys.
- ST-MDM-010 (decision, merge day): the rules in force and whether both entries are still alive are taken on the day of the merge (today); an entry whose version ends on or before it has run out (409).
- ST-MDM-010 (gap): issue intake (`IssIntake.open`) and `tenantLookup` / `searchMasterRecords` follow the key of a merged entry to its survivor (`liveRecordOfKey`); the other modules that read a catalogue entry by key straight from `master_record` (conversation links, AVC fund sources, the rule stores) are not yet alias-aware, so the merge of a type they read by key is for the steward to weigh.

### ST-ADM-020 (conversion of legacy data in batches, slice ADM-1)
- ST-ADM-020 (decision, contract): the module text names no way to read the batches or the errors of a dry run, yet ADM-13 lists them, so the reads `listMigrationBatches` (`GET /migration-batches`) and `getMigrationBatch` (`GET /migration-batches/{batchId}`, with the errors row by row) were added to the contract under a new permission `adm.migration.read`; the error CSV of the screen is made in the browser from that list.
- ST-ADM-020 (decision, mapping): decision D04 (source mapping) is open, so the platform knows two generic mappings (`GENERIC-1`, `GENERIC-CHILD-1`) and keeps the rows it converts in `migration_row` (business key, payload, amount numeric(24,0), references, batch, VALID or INVALID); a real source adds its mapping (and the table its rows go to) when D04 is decided.
- ST-ADM-020 (decision, workflow): the Temporal `MigrationApplyWorkflow` is stood in for by the job `adm-migration` (lots of 1,000 rows, a transaction each, upsert by business key, totals compared at the end); dry run and apply answer 202 and the screen reads the batch again while it is Validating or Applying. The job is scheduled only where the object store is bound.
- ST-ADM-020 (decision, file): `file_id` is the id of a file in the shared import file store (kind `import`), whose hash the server reads itself; the upload goes through the shared import pipeline (judged by its bytes, scanned) and the contract has no upload operation of its own for ADM-13, so the screen takes the id of an uploaded file.
- ST-ADM-020 (decision, states): the apply goes straight from `DryRunPassed` to `Applying` (the table of transitions of ADM.md 3.10), so `PendingApproval` and `Validated` exist in the table but nothing moves a batch to them; a dry run may be asked again for a batch that failed it or failed to apply; `ALREADY_IMPORTED` is the outcome of the job reaching a batch that is Applied already (no HTTP code).
- ST-ADM-020 (decision, dependents): a rollback is refused (409 `MIGRATION_DEPENDENTS_EXIST`) while a VALID row of another batch refers (through its mapping) to a VALID row of this one; the rows of a later batch that took over a business key are not the rolled-back batch's.
- ST-ADM-020 (gap): money controls are the number of rows and the sum of one amount column; per-currency or multi-column totals, and dependents in the modules that read the migrated rows, come with the mapping of a real source (D04).
- ST-ADM-020 (decision, upload): the registrar uploads through its own operation `uploadMigrationFile` (`POST /migration-batches/files`, `adm.migration.manage`), which uses the shared guard of the import files (10 MiB) and answers with the id to register by, the hash, the columns and the row count; a file of up to 100,000 rows still has to fit that limit of bytes.

### ST-LLM-007 (knowledge collections and ACL-filtered retrieval, slice LLM-1)
- ST-LLM-007 (decision, contract): the module text names no way to read the collections or their items, yet LLM-16 lists them, so the reads `listAiKnowledgeCollections` and `getAiKnowledgeCollection` (with the items, the hash of the access list and `stale`) were added to the contract under a new permission `llm.knowledge.read`. Retrieval has no operation of its own (the gateway and the analyses use `AiKnowledgeService.retrieve`).
- ST-LLM-007 (decision, access list): `document_version.acl_json` is `{}` for every document the application makes and cannot be written by it, so the ACL of retrieval is the document module's own: the scope of the reads of document versions (`versionScope` over the unit that governs the document, with the grants of the asker). The `acl_hash` of an item is the hash of what governs the reading (classification, issuer unit, and for a letter the register unit, the unit it was given to and who took it in); a chunk is offered only while the document still hashes to it, so a change of the governing unit or of the classification withholds the document from everybody until the item is indexed again (the job `llm-knowledge-reindex`, every five minutes), and a revoked grant takes the documents away at once. The recusal of an evaluator is the caller's `only`.
- ST-LLM-007 (decision, embedding): the model of a collection must be APPROVED, declare `capabilities.embedding = true` and be cleared for the highest classification the collection takes; the embedding itself is the port `AiEmbeddingPort` (a deterministic stand-in until the gateway goes through LiteLLM); with pgvector the distance is `<=>` over an HNSW index created only where the extension exists, in the TEST profile it is the cosine computed in SQL over plain arrays.
- ST-LLM-007 (decision, text): the text of a version is read from the pages the OCR of the document module recorded (`document_page_ocr`), its hash checked; a version with no page recorded cannot be indexed (422). The retrieval log (`llm.knowledge.retrieved`) keeps the hash of the question, the chunks and the versions returned.
- ST-LLM-007 (gap): a document with no issuer unit (and no incoming letter) is read through knowledge by nobody who holds the right over units, only by a grant on the whole tenant; the recusal of an evaluator is expressed by the caller (BID) through `only`; the stored texts of chunks that an index replaced stay in the object store (a sweep of the kind `ai-chunk` is for the retention job).
- ST-LLM-007 (decision, controls): indexing, retrieval and the indexing again by the job go through the same controls as the gateway before a text is embedded - the model APPROVED, its provider ACTIVE (503 `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`), and no switch of the emergency stop for the tenant, the provider, the model or the task type `EMBEDDING` (503 `AI_KILL_SWITCH_ENGAGED`); a retired collection answers nothing (409); a failure of the job sends the item to the back of the queue (`failure_code`), the texts a try stored are taken away when it does not commit and the ones it replaced when it does, and a retrieval whose texts were just replaced asks the chunks again (three tries).

### ST-LLM-008 (reconciliation of provider invoices, slice LLM-1)
- ST-LLM-008 (decision, contract): LLM-21 lists the reports of the runs yet the module names no read for them, so `listAiInvoiceReconciliations` (`GET /ai-invoices/reconciliations`, permission `llm.finops`) was added; the run is `reconcileAiInvoice` and the decision of the owner of a budget is `post_budgets_id_reconcile`.
- ST-LLM-008 (decision, provider): the invoice names its provider through its reference: the reference is the code of a provider of the tenant or begins with it followed by a dash (the longest code wins); no provider found is 422 on `invoice_ref`. No upload of the invoice file is made by this story - the amount and the reference are typed by the finance officer.
- ST-LLM-008 (decision, tolerance): the difference is inside the tolerance when it is at most 0.50 percent of the invoice, above or below, judged with integers of 1e-8 (`|variance| x 200 <= invoice`); the tolerance is a constant of the module, not a setting. The period must be over in Vietnam time (409 `RECONCILE_PERIOD_OPEN`); a period is at most 366 days.
- ST-LLM-008 (decision, late usage): an attempt that timed out and was paid for later is counted by the attempt that paid for it and is marked reconciled; the cost of an attempt is never edited (a database trigger refuses it); a variance lists the attempts still waiting and is run again when their usage arrives (`run_count`); a reconciled report is final and asking again answers with it, audited as `replayed`.
- ST-LLM-008 (decision, budget): the decision of the owner of a budget on the reservations that wait for usage: `approve` releases the ones whose usage never came (settled for nothing), `return` and `reject` keep them waiting; `evidence_ids` names reservations that wait on that budget (none named = all).
